# Somatic Reality Inspire — catalog

A catalog of soma design and somaesthetic interaction design, centred on Kristina Höök's lineage: research prototypes, design methods, somatic practices, artworks, performances and theory that treat body and mind as one soma, felt from within. Fields: foundations (somaesthetics, phenomenology, East Asian body-mind thought), somatic practices, soma design methods, somaesthetic interaction design, affective loops and biodata, movement and somatic art, intimate and feminist soma design, health and care, social soma, and soma with machines, AI and XR. Compiled by Reality Design Lab as idea material for designers, researchers, artists and students. Each work lists its core idea, how it works, and links to its paper, video and images.

https://somatic.reality.design · 2026-09-29 · 334 creators · 440 works

## How an AI assistant should use this file

- Ground ideas in the specific works below and name the work and creator you draw on.
- Cite papers with the DOI / URL given here; do not invent references.
- Combine body senses, methods and fields across works to propose new directions.
- Do not invent details that are not stated here; the links are the reference.

## Foundations: Soma & Somaesthetics

The ideas under soma design: the body-mind as one living whole, felt from within, and the philosophy of cultivating it.

### Somaesthetics

Shusterman's discipline of the body as the site of sensory appreciation and self-cultivation.

#### Thinking through the Body: Essays in Somaesthetics — Richard Shusterman (2012)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: We think with and through the body; educating somatic sensibility is part of educating the mind.
- What it is: A collection of essays that extends somaesthetics to education, the art of living, East Asian self-cultivation, eroticism and urban experience.
- How it works: Essays combining philosophy, somatic practice and cross-cultural readings, including Confucian and Zen body cultivation.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Attunement & appreciation
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9781139094030 (Cambridge University Press)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qzKI9VJbEnY
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Shusterman

#### Body Consciousness: A Philosophy of Mindfulness and Somaesthetics — Richard Shusterman (2008)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Posture, balance & proprioception, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: Habits live in the body-mind; to change them you must first notice, through trained attention, how you actually move and feel.
- What it is: Shusterman reads Merleau-Ponty, Foucault, Beauvoir, Wittgenstein, James and Dewey to argue that explicit, reflective body awareness is worth cultivating, against the view that it disturbs spontaneous action.
- How it works: Philosophical critique combined with Shusterman's own Feldenkrais practice, using examples such as neck tension, breathing and posture.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Attunement & appreciation, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9780511802829 (Cambridge University Press)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LXBf2l_tUVI
- Images: https://covers.openlibrary.org/b/id/2469617-L.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Somaesthetics

#### Somaesthetics: A Disciplinary Proposal — Richard Shusterman (1999)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: The soma is a sentient, purposive body-mind; improving its felt awareness improves perception, thought and action together.
- What it is: The essay that named and defined somaesthetics as the critical study and meliorative cultivation of the body as the site of sensory-aesthetic appreciation and creative self-fashioning.
- How it works: Proposes three branches: analytic (theory of the body), pragmatic (methods such as Feldenkrais, yoga, Alexander) and practical (actually doing the disciplines).
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Attunement & appreciation, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.2307/432196 (The Journal of Aesthetics and Art Criticism 57(3))
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Uxe73YY56Zg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Somaesthetics

#### Bone, Breath, and Gesture: Practices of Embodiment — Don Hanlon Johnson (1995)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Breath, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: The somatic practices share one premise: attending to breath, bone and movement from within changes the whole person, not a body part.
- What it is: An anthology of first-hand writings by the founders of somatic practices, including Elsa Gindler, Charlotte Selver, Ida Rolf, Moshe Feldenkrais, F. M. Alexander, Bonnie Bainbridge Cohen and Emilie Conrad.
- How it works: Edited collection with Johnson's introductions placing each practice in the history of the somatics movement.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Attunement & appreciation
- Paper: https://openlibrary.org/works/OL4141346W (North Atlantic Books)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=HwSdcnPK8bQ
- Images: https://covers.openlibrary.org/b/id/777359-L.jpg
- Project page: https://www.donhanlonjohnson.com

#### Pragmatist Aesthetics: Living Beauty, Rethinking Art — Richard Shusterman (1992)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Whole body-mind
- Idea: Aesthetic experience is lived and bodily, so cultivating how we feel and move is itself an art of living.
- What it is: Shusterman's first major book reads Dewey's aesthetics against analytic philosophy and ends by proposing that the body and its disciplines belong inside aesthetics, the seed of somaesthetics.
- How it works: Philosophical argument drawing on Dewey's pragmatism, popular arts (rap, dance) and the ethics of self-fashioning.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Attunement & appreciation
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.5771/9781461641179 (Blackwell 1992; 2nd ed. Rowman & Littlefield 2000)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=orpesq31bIg
- Images: https://covers.openlibrary.org/b/id/643150-L.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Shusterman

#### Bodies in Revolt: A Primer in Somatic Thinking — Thomas Hanna (1970)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: A soma is the body perceived from within by first-person perception: one self-sensing, self-moving process, not a mind plus a body.
- What it is: Hanna's manifesto for 'somatic thinking' argues that Western culture is shifting to treat humans as living somas, bodies experienced from within, rather than as minds carried by machines.
- How it works: Philosophical essay drawing on Darwin, Nietzsche, Merleau-Ponty and phenomenology; Hanna later defined the term in 'What Is Somatics?' (1986) and built Hanna Somatic Education on it.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://openlibrary.org/works/OL3811159W (Holt, Rinehart and Winston)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=6PvvNq0E7Vs
- Images: https://covers.openlibrary.org/b/id/8365877-L.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Hanna

#### Art as Experience — John Dewey (1934)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Whole body-mind
- Idea: Experience is neither mind nor body alone but the undergoing and doing of a whole organism; aesthetic quality is that experience made full.
- What it is: Dewey's lectures on aesthetics describe 'an experience' as a felt, rhythmic whole that arises when a live creature engages its environment, and root art in everyday bodily life.
- How it works: Philosophical lectures (William James Lectures, Harvard, 1931) developing a pragmatist theory of experience, art and perception.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://www.penguinrandomhouse.com/books/296640/art-as-experience-by-john-dewey/ (Minton, Balch & Co. 1934; TarcherPerigee)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o3t4TEm6y_w
- Images: https://covers.openlibrary.org/b/id/260869-L.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_as_Experience

### Phenomenology of the Lived Body

Leib, flesh, felt sense, the absent body: Husserl, Merleau-Ponty, Sheets-Johnstone, Gendlin, Leder.

#### How the Body Shapes the Mind — Shaun Gallagher (2005)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Posture, balance & proprioception, Movement & kinesthesia, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Much of the mind works through a body schema we never notice; design can act on it without passing through conscious image.
- What it is: Gallagher separates the body schema, the pre-reflective system that runs posture and movement, from the body image, what we perceive and believe about our body, and shows how both shape cognition.
- How it works: Integrates phenomenology with neuroscience and clinical cases such as deafferentation (Ian Waterman), neonate imitation and schizophrenia.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1093/0199271941.001.0001 (Oxford University Press)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M7ghXdujfLE
- Images: https://covers.openlibrary.org/b/id/133614-L.jpg https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/2/26/ShaunGallagher.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaun_Gallagher

#### The Primacy of Movement — Maxine Sheets-Johnstone (1999)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Thinking in movement is real thinking; kinesthetic experience comes before and shapes language and reflection.
- What it is: Sheets-Johnstone argues that animate movement is the origin of our sense of self, agency and concepts: we are first movers, and we learn ourselves by moving.
- How it works: Phenomenological and evolutionary analysis drawing on dance, infant development and comparative biology.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Movement & expression, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1075/aicr.14 (John Benjamins (Advances in Consciousness Research 14))
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=zhYAqejZdzI
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maxine_Sheets-Johnstone

#### The Absent Body — Drew Leder (1990)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Pain & inner sensations, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Dualism grows from the body's own tendency to recede; noticing the body without waiting for pain is a practice to cultivate.
- What it is: Leder asks why the body, the ground of all experience, usually disappears from awareness, and shows how it 'dys-appears', returning as a problem, in pain, illness and breakdown.
- How it works: Phenomenological analysis of the surface body, the visceral depths and the body in pain, drawing on Merleau-Ponty and on Asian contemplative traditions.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Attunement & appreciation, Care & healing
- Paper: https://openlibrary.org/works/OL1807680W (University of Chicago Press)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=baUVUUmHnf8
- Images: https://covers.openlibrary.org/b/id/141745-L.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drew_Leder

#### Throwing Like a Girl: A Phenomenology of Feminine Body Comportment, Motility, and Spatiality — Iris Marion Young (1980)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Posture, balance & proprioception
- Idea: How we feel our own capacities is shaped by norms; the lived body is always a gendered, situated body.
- What it is: Young describes how women in a sexist society often move with hesitancy, use only part of the body and experience it as a thing, and traces this to social norms rather than biology.
- How it works: Feminist reading of Merleau-Ponty and Beauvoir, applied to everyday movements such as throwing, carrying and taking up space.
- Lens: Body politics, First-person & felt experience, Body-mind unity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1007/BF02331805 (Human Studies 3)
- Images: https://covers.openlibrary.org/b/id/7358974-L.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Throwing_Like_a_Girl

#### The Visible and the Invisible — Maurice Merleau-Ponty (1964)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Body and world are of one flesh; to feel is always also to be felt, which dissolves the line between subject and object.
- What it is: Merleau-Ponty's unfinished last work introduces 'the flesh', a single element of which perceiver and perceived, body and world, are both made, and the reversibility of touching and being touched.
- How it works: Manuscript and working notes edited by Claude Lefort after Merleau-Ponty's death in 1961.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Intercorporeality
- Paper: https://nupress.northwestern.edu/9780810104570/the-visible-and-the-invisible/ (Gallimard 1964; Northwestern University Press (trans. Alphonso Lingis, 1968))
- Images: https://northwest-press-us.imgix.net/covers/9780810104570.jpg https://covers.openlibrary.org/b/id/9791967-L.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maurice_Merleau-Ponty

#### Experiencing and the Creation of Meaning — Eugene Gendlin (1962)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Pain & inner sensations
- Idea: Meaning is felt in the body before it is said; attending to that felt sense lets precise words and new ideas emerge.
- What it is: Gendlin's philosophical work describes 'experiencing', the bodily felt flow that is always richer than words, and how new meaning forms when we attend to it and let it be carried forward.
- How it works: Philosophical analysis of the relation between felt experience and symbolisation; the basis for the Focusing practice (1978) and Thinking at the Edge.
- Lens: First-person & felt experience, Body-mind unity, Attunement & appreciation
- Paper: https://nupress.northwestern.edu/9780810114272/experiencing-and-the-creation-of-meaning/ (Free Press 1962; Northwestern University Press 1997)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Wu7C67LscYk
- Images: https://northwest-press-us.imgix.net/covers/9780810114272.jpg https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/b/ba/Gene_Gendlin_1088_PCE_conference_Germany.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Focusing_(psychotherapy)

#### Ideas II: Studies in the Phenomenology of Constitution — Edmund Husserl (1952)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Touch & pressure, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: The body I live is felt from inside as mine; touching and being touched at once shows it is both subject and object.
- What it is: The second book of Husserl's Ideas analyses how the lived body (Leib) is constituted: as the zero point of orientation, the organ of will and the bearer of localised sensations felt when one hand touches the other.
- How it works: Phenomenological description written 1912–1928 and published posthumously in Husserliana IV (1952); English translation by Rojcewicz and Schuwer (1989).
- Lens: Body-mind unity, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1007/978-94-009-2233-4 (Kluwer / Springer (Husserliana: Collected Works III))
- Images: https://covers.openlibrary.org/b/isbn/9780792307136-L.jpg https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/8/8d/Edmund_Husserl_1910s.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edmund_Husserl

#### Phenomenology of Perception — Maurice Merleau-Ponty (1945)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Movement & kinesthesia, Posture, balance & proprioception
- Idea: I do not have a body, I am my body: understanding happens first as motor intentionality, the body's 'I can'.
- What it is: Merleau-Ponty's main work shows that perception is carried out by a lived body with its own motor know-how, the body schema, which reaches into tools and space before any thought about them.
- How it works: Phenomenological analysis drawing on Gestalt psychology and clinical cases such as Schneider, phantom limbs and the blind man's cane.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.4324/9780203720714 (Gallimard 1945; Routledge (trans. Donald A. Landes, 2012))
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JFj8kWm_N-Y
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/9/98/Maurice_Merleau-Ponty.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phenomenology_of_Perception

### Embodied & Enactive Mind

Mind as lived, embodied action: Varela, Thompson, Rosch, Johnson, Lakoff, Noë, Damasio.

#### Ecology of the Brain: The Phenomenology and Biology of the Embodied Mind — Thomas Fuchs (2018)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Heartbeat
- Idea: The person, not the brain, feels and thinks; mind is a living body in relation.
- What it is: Fuchs argues against 'brain-centrism': the brain is an organ that mediates the relations of a living body with its environment and with others, not the seat of the mind.
- How it works: Phenomenological and biological argument using circular causality, homeostasis, body memory and psychiatric cases.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Intercorporeality
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1093/med/9780199646883.001.0001 (Oxford University Press)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=m4eB2VfvYwk
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Fuchs_(psychiatrist)

#### The Feeling Body: Affective Science Meets the Enactive Mind — Giovanna Colombetti (2014)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Heartbeat, Skin conductance & arousal
- Idea: Feeling is not added to cognition; the body is the feeling organ of a single, affective sense-making process.
- What it is: Colombetti argues that the whole living body is affective, that emotion is not a separate module but a dynamical pattern of the organism, and that bodily feelings can be background, not only objects of attention.
- How it works: Philosophical synthesis of enactivism, phenomenology and affective science, including psychophysiology and dynamical systems.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Affective loops
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.7551/mitpress/9780262019958.001.0001 (MIT Press)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=s3SaLKHBmxU
- Images: https://covers.openlibrary.org/b/id/8882071-L.jpg
- Project page: https://experts.exeter.ac.uk/1082-giovanna-colombetti

#### Enactive Intersubjectivity: Participatory Sense-Making and Mutual Incorporation — Thomas Fuchs, Hanne De Jaegher (2009)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Movement & kinesthesia, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Understanding others is not mind-reading but mutual incorporation: two bodies forming one felt system.
- What it is: Fuchs and De Jaegher describe social understanding as bodies in interaction: through coordinated movement and rhythm, each partner's body extends into the other's, creating meaning neither makes alone.
- How it works: Theoretical paper combining Merleau-Ponty's intercorporeality with dynamical systems and developmental studies of interaction.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Body-mind unity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1007/s11097-009-9136-4 (Phenomenology and the Cognitive Sciences 8(4))
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AiFuQD-ZWy4
- Project page: https://hannedejaegher.net

#### Mind in Life: Biology, Phenomenology, and the Sciences of Mind — Evan Thompson (2007)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Whole body-mind
- Idea: Where there is life there is mind; the felt body is the living organism experiencing itself, not a container for a mind.
- What it is: Thompson argues for a deep continuity of life and mind: the self-organising processes of living organisms already carry the basic structure of sense-making that experience develops.
- How it works: Combines autopoiesis and dynamical systems theory with Husserlian phenomenology of time, emotion and empathy.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://www.hup.harvard.edu/books/9780674057517 (Belknap Press of Harvard University Press)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=rG3daqNH700
- Images: https://covers.openlibrary.org/b/id/412723-L.jpg
- Project page: https://evanthompson.me

#### The Meaning of the Body: Aesthetics of Human Understanding — Mark Johnson (2007)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: Meaning is felt before it is thought; the qualities of bodily experience are where sense begins.
- What it is: Johnson argues that meaning begins below words, in felt qualities, emotions and movement, and that aesthetics, the study of how things feel, is therefore the ground of all understanding.
- How it works: Pragmatist philosophy drawing on Dewey, James, Gendlin and cognitive neuroscience, with examples from music, painting and infant development.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Attunement & appreciation
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.7208/chicago/9780226026992.001.0001 (University of Chicago Press)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=HaMeGdrKnEE
- Images: https://covers.openlibrary.org/b/id/1148004-L.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Johnson_(philosopher)

#### Action in Perception — Alva Noë (2004)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Light & vision, Touch & pressure
- Idea: Perceiving is a skilful activity of the whole moving body, so changing how we move changes what we feel and see.
- What it is: Noë argues that perception is not something that happens in us but something we do: to perceive is to have practical, bodily knowledge of how sensation changes as we move.
- How it works: Philosophical argument using sensory substitution, change blindness, inverting goggles and touch as test cases for the sensorimotor theory.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://mitpress.mit.edu/9780262640633/action-in-perception/ (MIT Press)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MoOHWHEJOLU
- Images: https://covers.openlibrary.org/b/id/152218-L.jpg https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/5/56/Alva_No%C3%AB_%283419836383%29.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alva_No%C3%AB

#### Philosophy in the Flesh: The Embodied Mind and Its Challenge to Western Thought — George Lakoff, Mark Johnson (1999)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: There is no disembodied reason: the same neural and bodily systems that let us move and perceive also structure how we think.
- What it is: Lakoff and Johnson argue from cognitive science that the mind is inherently embodied, thought is mostly unconscious and abstract concepts are largely metaphorical, and re-read Western philosophy in that light.
- How it works: Synthesis of cognitive linguistics and neuroscience, applied to concepts of time, causation, mind, self and morality.
- Lens: Body-mind unity
- Paper: https://www.hachettebookgroup.com/titles/george-lakoff/philosophy-in-the-flesh/9780465056743/ (Basic Books)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=WuUnMCq-ARQ
- Images: https://covers.openlibrary.org/b/id/298974-L.jpg https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/2/25/George_Lakoff.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Lakoff

#### The Feeling of What Happens: Body and Emotion in the Making of Consciousness — Antonio Damasio (1999)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Pain & inner sensations, Heartbeat, Whole body-mind
- Idea: The sense of self begins as the felt body: consciousness is rooted in interoceptive feeling.
- What it is: Damasio traces consciousness to the brain's ongoing mapping of the body's internal state: a proto-self from which core and extended selves arise as feelings of what happens.
- How it works: Neuroscientific theory built on lesion studies, neuroanatomy and clinical observation of disorders of consciousness.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Affective loops
- Paper: https://openlibrary.org/works/OL3499048W (Harcourt Brace)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LMrzdk_YnYY
- Images: https://covers.openlibrary.org/b/id/112081-L.jpg https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/e/e4/Ant%C3%B3nio_Dam%C3%A1sio_no_Fronteiras_do_Pensamento_Porto_Alegre_2013_cropped.png
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonio_Damasio

#### Descartes' Error: Emotion, Reason, and the Human Brain — Antonio Damasio (1994)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Pain & inner sensations, Heartbeat, Skin conductance & arousal
- Idea: Descartes' error was separating mind from body: reason depends on the body's felt signals.
- What it is: Damasio's somatic marker hypothesis shows that bodily feelings tied to emotion guide decisions, so patients who lose them reason badly despite intact logic.
- How it works: Neurological case studies (Phineas Gage, patient Elliot), the Iowa Gambling Task and skin conductance measures.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Affective loops
- Paper: https://www.penguinrandomhouse.com/books/297609/descartes-error-by-antonio-damasio/ (G. P. Putnam's Sons 1994; Penguin)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=1wup_K2WN0I
- Images: https://covers.openlibrary.org/b/id/111239-L.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Descartes%27_Error

#### The Embodied Mind: Cognitive Science and Human Experience — Francisco Varela, Evan Thompson, Eleanor Rosch (1991)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Breath
- Idea: Mind is not inside the head but enacted by a living body in its world, and experience can be trained and studied from within.
- What it is: The book that named enaction: cognition as embodied action that brings forth a world, joined with Buddhist mindfulness as a disciplined way to examine lived experience.
- How it works: Brings together cognitive science, phenomenology and Madhyamaka and Abhidharma Buddhism, with mindfulness/awareness meditation as a method.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, First-person & felt experience, Attunement & appreciation
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.7551/mitpress/6730.001.0001 (MIT Press)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xhS9ffDcLOk
- Images: https://covers.openlibrary.org/b/id/152634-L.jpg https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/c/cf/Francisco_Varela.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enactivism

#### The Body in the Mind: The Bodily Basis of Meaning, Imagination, and Reason — Mark Johnson (1987)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Posture, balance & proprioception, Movement & kinesthesia, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Reason grows out of the felt patterns of a moving, balancing body.
- What it is: Johnson introduces image schemas, recurring patterns of bodily experience such as balance, containment, path and force, and shows how they structure imagination and reason.
- How it works: Philosophical analysis of image schemas and their metaphorical projection, with examples from balance, force and containment.
- Lens: Body-mind unity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.7208/chicago/9780226177847.001.0001 (University of Chicago Press)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=nda7xAIG3d0
- Images: https://covers.openlibrary.org/b/id/11011367-L.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Image_schema

#### The Tree of Knowledge: The Biological Roots of Human Understanding — Humberto Maturana, Francisco Varela (1987)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Whole body-mind
- Idea: To live is to know: cognition is not a mind representing the world but the whole organism coupling with its environment.
- What it is: An accessible account of autopoiesis: living beings are self-producing systems, and knowing is the living process by which an organism brings forth a world with others.
- How it works: Biological and philosophical argument illustrated with examples from cell biology, nervous systems, language and social coupling.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, More-than-human
- Paper: https://openlibrary.org/works/OL1418978W (New Science Library / Shambhala)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fQFlceVouPA
- Images: https://covers.openlibrary.org/b/isbn/9780877736424-L.jpg https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/0/02/Humberto_Maturana-FILSA2015.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autopoiesis

#### Metaphors We Live By — George Lakoff, Mark Johnson (1980)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Posture, balance & proprioception, Heat & temperature
- Idea: Abstract meaning is built from felt bodily experience of up and down, warm and cold, near and far.
- What it is: Lakoff and Johnson show that everyday thought is structured by conceptual metaphors grounded in bodily experience, such as HAPPY IS UP, AFFECTION IS WARMTH and ARGUMENT IS WAR.
- How it works: Linguistic analysis of systematic patterns in everyday English expressions.
- Lens: Body-mind unity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.7208/chicago/9780226470993.001.0001 (University of Chicago Press)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XWYaoAoijdQ
- Images: https://covers.openlibrary.org/b/isbn/9780226468006-L.jpg https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/a/a4/Metaphors_We_Live_By_book_cover.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metaphors_We_Live_By

### Body-Mind Oneness in East Asian Thought

Shinjin ichinyo, qi and self-cultivation: Yuasa Yasuo, Ichikawa Hiroshi, Zen, Daoist and Confucian body thought.

#### Self-Cultivation as Education Embodying Humanity — Tu Weiming (1999)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Whole body-mind
- Idea: To know is to embody: Confucian learning is judged by how one stands, speaks and relates, a first-person knowledge that lives in conduct.
- What it is: Tu presents Confucian self-cultivation (xiushen) as learning to be human through the body: knowledge that is 'embodied' (tizhi) in comportment, ritual and relationships rather than held as propositions.
- How it works: Paper for the Twentieth World Congress of Philosophy (Boston, 1998), part of Tu's long work on tizhi and New Confucianism.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, First-person & felt experience, Intercorporeality
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.5840/wcp201999349 (Proceedings of the Twentieth World Congress of Philosophy)
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/a/a9/%E6%9D%9C%E7%B6%AD%E6%98%8E_Weiming_Tu.jpg

#### The Expressiveness of the Body and the Divergence of Greek and Chinese Medicine — Shigehisa Kuriyama (1999)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Heartbeat, Whole body-mind
- Idea: How a body feels depends on how the perceiver's senses have been trained: somatic sensitivity is a cultural skill, not a neutral reading.
- What it is: Kuriyama compares how Greek doctors learned to see muscles and anatomy while Chinese doctors learned to feel the pulse and read colours and qi, arguing that trained touch and sight produce different bodies.
- How it works: Comparative history drawing on Greek medical treatises and Chinese pulse classics, with close attention to images and vocabulary of touch.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Attunement & appreciation, Body politics
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.2307/jj.20093332 (Zone Books, 1999)
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/f/fc/Chinese_meridians.JPG

#### Attunement Through the Body — Shigenori Nagatomo (1992)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Whole body-mind
- Idea: Attunement is a bodily skill of resonance that can be trained; designing for it means supporting the body's capacity to tune in, not delivering information.
- What it is: Nagatomo develops Yuasa's theory into an account of 'attunement': how a cultivated body comes into resonance with things, others and nature, drawing on Zen, Noh and Japanese aesthetics.
- How it works: Philosophical study (SUNY Series in Buddhist Studies) combining phenomenology and Japanese Buddhist texts.
- Lens: Attunement & appreciation, Body-mind unity, Intercorporeality
- Paper: https://sunypress.edu/Books/A/Attunement-Through-the-Body (SUNY Press, 1992)
- Images: https://sunypress.edu/var/site/storage/images/books/a/attunement-through-the-body/9780791412329_cover/1843173-1-eng-CA/9780791412329_cover1_rb_fullcover.jpg
- Project page: https://sunypress.edu/Books/A/Attunement-Through-the-Body

#### The Body, Self-Cultivation, and Ki-Energy — Yuasa Yasuo (1986)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Breath, Pain & inner sensations
- Idea: Ki names the felt link between body, emotion and environment: an energy that practice makes perceptible rather than a hidden substance.
- What it is: Yuasa's study of ki as it is used in acupuncture, meditation and the martial arts, with a four-layer model of the lived body that links sensory-motor circuits, emotion and the unconscious. Japanese edition 1986 (Ki, Shugyō, Shintai); English 1993.
- How it works: Reads Chinese and Japanese research on ki, acupuncture meridian measurements and meditators' reports alongside Western psychology and physiology.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Attunement & appreciation, Care & healing
- Paper: https://sunypress.edu/Books/T/The-Body-Self-Cultivation-and-Ki-Energy (SUNY Press, 1993 (Japanese: Hirakawa, 1986))
- Images: https://sunypress.edu/var/site/storage/images/books/t/the-body-self-cultivation-and-ki-energy2/9780791416242_cover/1846454-1-eng-CA/9780791416242_cover1_rb_fullcover.jpg
- Project page: https://sunypress.edu/Books/T/The-Body-Self-Cultivation-and-Ki-Energy

#### The Structure of Mi (〈Mi〉 no kōzō) — Ichikawa Hiroshi (1984)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Whole body-mind
- Idea: Ordinary language already knows the soma: mi is the body as self in relation, extending into clothes, tools, other people and place.
- What it is: Ichikawa builds a theory of the lived body on the everyday Japanese word mi, which can mean flesh, self, social position and heart at once. Kōdansha Gakujutsu Bunko edition 1993.
- How it works: Close reading of idioms built on mi (mi ni tsukeru, mi ni shimiru, mi o makaseru) as evidence of a relational, non-dualist body.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Intercorporeality
- Paper: https://bookclub.kodansha.co.jp/product?item=0000150672 (Seidosha, 1984; Kōdansha Gakujutsu Bunko, 1993)
- Images: https://dvs-cover.kodansha.co.jp/0000150672/573Em5KLyK0vdQ7Rd30RH6Hku6d3RncSBKSTgCcc.jpg
- Project page: https://bookclub.kodansha.co.jp/product?item=0000150672

#### The Body: Toward an Eastern Mind-Body Theory — Yuasa Yasuo (1977)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Whole body-mind
- Idea: Body-mind unity is an achievement of cultivation, not a starting fact: the more the body is trained, the less mind and body are felt as two.
- What it is: Yuasa reads Dōgen, Kūkai, Nishida, Watsuji and Noh theory to show how Japanese thought treats body and mind as something unified through practice. Published in Japanese in 1977 and in English (SUNY Press) in 1987.
- How it works: Comparative philosophy that pairs Japanese self-cultivation texts with Merleau-Ponty, Bergson and psychosomatic medicine, and describes a 'dark consciousness' of the body below awareness.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Attunement & appreciation
- Paper: https://sunypress.edu/Books/T/The-Body (SUNY Press, 1987 (Japanese: Sōbunsha, 1977))
- Images: https://sunypress.edu/var/site/storage/images/books/t/the-body/9780887064685_cover/1908113-1-eng-CA/9780887064685_cover1_rb_fullcover.jpg
- Project page: https://sunypress.edu/Books/T/The-Body

#### The Body as Spirit (Seishin to shite no shintai) — Ichikawa Hiroshi (1975)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: The body is already spirit: thinking, feeling and acting are one body extending itself into the world, so design shapes the mind by shaping how the body reaches.
- What it is: Ichikawa's phenomenology of the body argues that human reality cannot be separated from the concrete body, and describes the body as subject, as object and as an 'entangled' whole that reaches into tools and others. First published by Keisō Shobō in 1975; Kōdansha Gakujutsu Bunko edition 1992.
- How it works: Phenomenological analysis in dialogue with Merleau-Ponty, Gestalt psychology and physiology, organised as the body as phenomenon, as structure and as action.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://bookclub.kodansha.co.jp/product?item=0000150620 (Keisō Shobō, 1975; Kōdansha Gakujutsu Bunko, 1992)
- Images: https://dvs-cover.kodansha.co.jp/0000150620/cDG5dVkZONkUA9E7mjcJ7Fke6VVBKga9amEhM7FI.jpg
- Project page: https://bookclub.kodansha.co.jp/product?item=0000150620

#### Action-Intuition (Kōiteki chokkan) — Nishida Kitarō (1937)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: Perception is not a mind looking at the world but a body shaping and being shaped by it; designers know materials by working them.
- What it is: In essays of the late 1930s Nishida describes 'action-intuition': we see things by acting on them and are formed by what we make, so knowing and bodily making are one movement.
- How it works: Philosophical essays (1937 onward) developing the logic of basho, historical bodies and poiesis.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/nishida-kitaro/ (Stanford Encyclopedia of Philosophy (entry))
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/1/13/Portrait-of-Kitaro-Nishida.png
- Project page: https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/nishida-kitaro/

#### Dongui Suse Bowon: Sasang Constitutional Medicine — Yi Je-ma (1894)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Pain & inner sensations
- Idea: Temperament and organ are one system: excess sorrow, anger, joy or pleasure shapes the body, so healing starts from knowing one's emotional constitution.
- What it is: Yi Je-ma's treatise divides people into four constitutions (Taeyang, Soyang, Taeeum, Soeum), each with its own organ strengths, emotional tendencies and suitable foods and treatments.
- How it works: Medical treatise written in classical Chinese by a Joseon physician, grounding Korean medicine in Confucian self-cultivation.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Care & healing
- Paper: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sasang_constitutional_medicine (1894 (Wikipedia overview))
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/1/15/%EC%9D%B4%EC%A0%9C%EB%A7%88_%EC%B4%88%EC%83%81.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yi_Je-ma

#### Riken no ken: The Seeing of Detached Seeing (Kakyō) — Zeami Motokiyo (1424)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Voice, sound & listening, Whole body-mind
- Idea: A trained performer feels movement from inside and sees it from outside at the same time; this double awareness is the core skill of first-person design.
- What it is: In Kakyō (A Mirror Held to the Flower, 1424) Zeami tells the Noh actor to dance with 'eyes in front, mind behind' and to see himself as the audience sees him, uniting inner feeling and outer form.
- How it works: Secret treatise passed down within Zeami's lineage, alongside the Fūshikaden (c. 1400–1418), setting out training of body, voice and mind across an actor's life.
- Lens: First-person & felt experience, Movement & expression, Estrangement
- Paper: https://cup.columbia.edu/book/zeami-performance-notes/9780231144803 (Zeami: Performance Notes, trans. Tom Hare, Columbia University Press, 2008)
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/2/24/Sousetsubon_fushikaden_7_part.PNG https://cup-us.imgix.net/covers/9780231144803.jpg?w=350
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zeami_Motokiyo

#### Shinjin ichinyo: Body and Mind as One (Bendōwa, Shōbōgenzō) — Dōgen Zenji (1231)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Breath, Posture, balance & proprioception
- Idea: Practice and realisation are not two: the posture itself is the awakening, so attending to how you sit is already the work.
- What it is: In Bendōwa (1231), later placed at the head of the Shōbōgenzō, Dōgen rejects the idea of a mind that survives apart from the body and teaches that 'body and mind are one' (shinjin ichinyo), realised in seated practice.
- How it works: Classical Zen text written in Japanese vernacular as questions and answers, grounding its claims in the practice of zazen.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Attunement & appreciation
- Paper: https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/japanese-zen/ (Stanford Encyclopedia of Philosophy (Japanese Zen Buddhist Philosophy))
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/0/09/Soto-Zen-Master-Dogen-Zenji-Portrait.png
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/D%C5%8Dgen

#### Huangdi Neijing (Inner Classic of the Yellow Emperor) — Huangdi Neijing authors (anonymous) (100 BCE)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Breath, Pain & inner sensations
- Idea: Emotions are organ events and organs are emotional: anger, joy, worry and fear move qi, so caring for feeling and caring for the body are the same practice.
- What it is: The founding classic of Chinese medicine, in two parts (Suwen and Lingshu), describes the body as flows of qi through meridians and organs that correspond to seasons, emotions and the cosmos.
- How it works: Dialogues compiled between roughly the 3rd and 1st centuries BCE, organised around yin-yang, the five phases, meridians and pulse diagnosis.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Care & healing
- Paper: https://ctext.org/huangdi-neijing (Chinese Text Project)
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/3/39/Koteidaikei_Myodo.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huangdi_Neijing

#### Cook Ding Carves the Ox (Zhuangzi, 'Nourishing the Lord of Life') — Zhuangzi (300 BCE)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Touch & pressure
- Idea: Mastery is felt attunement to the grain of things: skill that has passed from deliberate control into the body moves with ease and without wear.
- What it is: Cook Ding has used the same knife for nineteen years because he no longer looks at the ox with his eyes but meets it with his spirit, letting the blade follow the natural gaps; Lord Wenhui learns from him how to nourish life.
- How it works: Parable in the third chapter (Yangshengzhu) of the Zhuangzi, one of many skill stories alongside the wheelwright, the swimmer and the cicada catcher.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Attunement & appreciation, Movement & expression
- Paper: https://ctext.org/zhuangzi/nourishing-the-lord-of-life (Zhuangzi, ch. 3 (Chinese Text Project))
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/9/95/Zhuangzi_Song_Edition.jpg
- Project page: https://ctext.org/zhuangzi/nourishing-the-lord-of-life

#### Fasting of the Heart-Mind and Sitting in Forgetfulness (Zhuangzi) — Zhuangzi (300 BCE)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Breath, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Listening with qi means attending with the whole body before concepts arrive; emptying habitual perception makes room to feel anew.
- What it is: Confucius teaches Yan Hui to 'listen not with the ears but with the heart-mind, not with the heart-mind but with qi' (xinzhai), and Yan Hui later reports 'sitting in forgetfulness' (zuowang), letting go of limbs, senses and knowledge.
- How it works: Dialogues in the Renjianshi and Dazongshi chapters of the Zhuangzi that later shaped Daoist meditation and neidan practice.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Attunement & appreciation, Estrangement
- Paper: https://ctext.org/zhuangzi/man-in-the-world (Zhuangzi, ch. 4 and 6 (Chinese Text Project))
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/c/c1/Dschuang-Dsi-Schmetterlingstraum-Zhuangzi-Butterfly-Dream.jpg
- Project page: https://ctext.org/zhuangzi/great-and-most-honoured-master

#### Nourishing the Flood-Like Qi (Mencius 2A2) — Mencius (300 BCE)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Breath, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Moral strength is bodily and cultivated slowly: will leads qi, qi fills the body, and forcing growth harms it.
- What it is: Asked about an unmoved heart, Mencius says he is good at nourishing his 'flood-like qi' (haoran zhi qi), which fills the body and grows from righteous action, and warns against the farmer who pulls his sprouts to help them grow.
- How it works: Dialogue in the Gongsun Chou chapter of the Mencius, a founding text for Confucian theories of qi and self-cultivation (xiushen).
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Care & healing
- Paper: https://ctext.org/mengzi/gong-sun-chou-i (Mencius 2A (Chinese Text Project))
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/2/2c/Great_Confucian_Figures_-_Painting_of_Mengzi_by_Kan%C5%8D_Sansetsu.jpg
- Project page: https://ctext.org/mengzi/gong-sun-chou-i

### Embodied Interaction Theory

How HCI turned to the body: Dourish, Klemmer, Hornecker, Loke & Robertson, Höök's somaesthetic interaction design.

#### Toward Pluralizing Reflection in HCI through Daoism — Aaron Pengyu Zhu, Janghee Cho (2026)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Breath
- Idea: Reflection need not be a mind looking back at data: in Daoist practice it is embodied, relational and emerges from stillness with the world.
- What it is: Drawing on Daoist texts and interviews with 18 Daoist practitioners, scholars and priests, the paper describes everyday reflection as Stillness, Resonance and Emergence, and proposes 'reflecting-with' rather than reflecting on oneself.
- How it works: Qualitative study combining textual analysis of Daoist literature with semi-structured interviews, analysed thematically for HCI design implications.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Attunement & appreciation, More-than-human
- Paper: https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22831 (CHI 2026)

#### Tracing Conceptions of the Body in HCI: From User to More-Than-Human — Sarah Homewood, Susan Kozel (2021)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Whole body-mind
- Idea: Which body we assume shapes what we design; the soma is one step toward bodies that are relational and more-than-human.
- What it is: Homewood, Hedemyr, Fagerberg Ranten and Kozel trace how HCI has conceived the body, from the user's body as an input device, through the lived, somatic body, to the more-than-human body entangled with other beings.
- How it works: Critical literature review of CHI and related publications, organised by conceptions of the body.
- Lens: Body politics, More-than-human, Body-mind unity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3411764.3445656 (CHI 2021)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=g4Nrd5mv_Tk

#### Embodied Cognition and the Magical Future of Interaction Design — David Kirsh (2013)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Whole body-mind
- Idea: The body is a thinking medium: moving a partial version of an action helps us reason about the whole.
- What it is: Kirsh reviews embodied cognition and reports his studies of dancers 'marking' a phrase, showing that people think by using their bodies and things as external representations.
- How it works: Review of embodied cognition plus empirical study with professional dancers at Random Dance (Wayne McGregor).
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Movement & expression
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2442106.2442109 (ACM Transactions on Computer-Human Interaction 20(1))
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Vhcubb00g6c

#### Interaction Design for and with the Lived Body: Some Implications of Merleau-Ponty's Phenomenology — Dag Svanæs (2013)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: Design for the lived body by designing with it: the designer's own felt, moving body is a source of design knowledge.
- What it is: Svanæs reads Merleau-Ponty for interaction designers: the lived body, body schema and motor intentionality, and proposes that designers use their own bodies as instruments in the design process.
- How it works: Philosophical analysis with design examples, including role-play and bodily enactment in a health-technology project.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2442106.2442114 (ACM Transactions on Computer-Human Interaction 20(1))
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=pmwt0JRdEzA

#### Kinesthetic Interaction: Revealing the Bodily Potential in Interaction Design — Maiken Hillerup Fogtmann (2008)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Posture, balance & proprioception
- Idea: The kinaesthetic sense, how we feel our own movement, is a material designers can work with directly.
- What it is: Fogtmann, Fritsch and Kortbek define kinesthetic interaction as a concept for designing with the body's kinaesthetic sense, with design parameters such as engagement, sociality and movability.
- How it works: Theoretical framework drawing on physiology and phenomenology, applied to interactive installations for movement.
- Lens: Movement & expression, Pleasure & play
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/1517744.1517770 (OZCHI 2008)

#### Move to Get Moved: A Search for Methods, Tools and Knowledge to Design for Expressive and Rich Movement-Based Interaction — Caroline Hummels (2007)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Designers must move to be moved: bodily skill and emotion are one, and designing movement requires moving yourself.
- What it is: Hummels, Overbeeke and Klooster argue that movement-based interaction should aim at expressive, rich movement that engages people's perceptual-motor, emotional and social skills, and propose design methods to get there.
- How it works: Reflection on design cases and education at TU/e, drawing on phenomenology and Gibson's ecological psychology.
- Lens: Movement & expression, Body-mind unity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1007/s00779-006-0135-y (Personal and Ubiquitous Computing 11(8))
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=GHtx5SCuYgY

#### The Feel Dimension of Technology Interaction: Exploring Tangibles through Movement and Touch — Astrid Twenebowa Larssen, Toni Robertson (2007)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Touch & pressure
- Idea: How an interaction feels in the mover's body is a design dimension in its own right.
- What it is: Larssen, Robertson and Edwards argue that interaction with technology should be understood from how it feels to the moving body, using kinaesthetic and tactile experience rather than visual appearance as the design lens.
- How it works: Conceptual paper grounded in Merleau-Ponty and illustrated with analysis of movement-based and tangible interactions.
- Lens: First-person & felt experience, Movement & expression
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/1226969.1227024 (TEI 2007)

#### Getting a Grip on Tangible Interaction: A Framework on Physical Space and Social Interaction — Eva Hornecker, Jacob Buur (2006)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: Bodies in space are always interacting, with things and with each other; design should shape that whole-body, social situation.
- What it is: Hornecker and Buur propose a framework for tangible interaction with four themes, tangible manipulation, spatial interaction, embodied facilitation and expressive representation, that treats bodily movement in space as interaction.
- How it works: Conceptual framework derived from literature and case studies of tangible and interactive installations.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Movement & expression
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/1124772.1124838 (CHI 2006)

#### How Bodies Matter: Five Themes for Interaction Design — Scott Klemmer (2006)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Touch & pressure
- Idea: People think with their hands and bodies, so interfaces that strip away bodily engagement also strip away thinking.
- What it is: Klemmer, Hartmann and Takayama distil five themes from embodied cognition for interaction design: thinking through doing, performance, visibility, risk, and thickness of practice.
- How it works: Synthesis of research from psychology, education, sociology and philosophy into design themes, with examples from tangible interfaces.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Movement & expression
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/1142405.1142429 (DIS 2006)
- Project page: https://d.ucsd.edu/srk/

#### Technology as Experience — John McCarthy, Peter Wright (2004)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Whole body-mind
- Idea: Experience with technology is felt and lived as a whole; the sensual thread is as much part of it as function.
- What it is: McCarthy and Wright build a pragmatist account of experience with technology, naming four threads, the sensual, emotional, compositional and spatio-temporal, and six sense-making processes.
- How it works: Theoretical framework drawing on Dewey and Bakhtin, illustrated with cases from online shopping to Internet banking.
- Lens: First-person & felt experience, Body-mind unity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.7551/mitpress/6687.001.0001 (MIT Press)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=V8ZnMHysJkw
- Images: https://covers.openlibrary.org/b/id/1154743-L.jpg

#### Where the Action Is: The Foundations of Embodied Interaction — Paul Dourish (2001)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Touch & pressure
- Idea: Interaction is not a mind operating a device but a body acting in a world; design for what people do, not what they represent.
- What it is: Dourish draws on Heidegger, Merleau-Ponty, Schutz and Wittgenstein to define embodied interaction: meaning arises through engaged action in the physical and social world, which tangible and social computing should support.
- How it works: Theoretical synthesis of phenomenology with tangible and social computing research, closing with design principles for embodied interaction.
- Lens: Body-mind unity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.7551/mitpress/7221.001.0001 (MIT Press)
- Images: https://covers.openlibrary.org/b/id/149713-L.jpg https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/9/97/Paul_Dourish_in_Pasadena%2C_2010.jpg
- Project page: https://www.dourish.com

## Somatic Practices

The practices soma designers train in and borrow from: movement education, meditation, breath, martial and dance traditions that treat body and mind as one.

### Movement Education & Somatics

Feldenkrais, Alexander Technique, Body-Mind Centering, Laban/Bartenieff, Hanna Somatics.

#### Sensing, Feeling, and Action — Bonnie Bainbridge Cohen (1993)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Movement & kinesthesia, Pain & inner sensations
- Idea: The book argues that perception is an action: how we move shapes what we can sense and feel, so designing a sensation also designs a way of moving.
- What it is: A collection of Bainbridge Cohen's essays and interviews from Contact Quarterly (1980 onward) on organs, glands, fluids, reflexes, the senses and developmental movement, with explorations readers can try.
- How it works: Essays, interviews and experiential exercises on the body systems of Body-Mind Centering; Contact Editions, 1993.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://contactquarterly.com/contact-editions/title/sensing-feeling-and-action-3 (Contact Editions 1993)
- Images: https://bonniebainbridgecohen.com/cdn/shop/products/sensing-feeling-and-action-bonnie-bainbridge-cohen_630x630.jpg?v=1753184330

#### Somatics: Reawakening the Mind's Control of Movement, Flexibility, and Health — Thomas Hanna (1988)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Muscle tension (EMG), Movement & kinesthesia, Posture, balance & proprioception
- Idea: Hanna argues that many problems blamed on ageing are learned body-mind habits and can be unlearned from within, which puts first-person sensing at the centre of health.
- What it is: Hanna explains 'sensory-motor amnesia', habitual muscle contractions we no longer feel, and gives a set of slow exercises to regain voluntary sensing and control.
- How it works: Theory of the reflexes of stress (red light, green light, trauma) plus illustrated self-practice exercises; Addison-Wesley, 1988.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, First-person & felt experience, Care & healing
- Paper: https://books.google.com/books/about/Somatics.html?id=sapXJRU4nH8C (Addison-Wesley 1988)
- Images: https://archive.org/services/img/somaticsreawaken00hann

#### Bartenieff Fundamentals — Irmgard Bartenieff (1980)
- Type: Somatic practice · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Breath, Posture, balance & proprioception
- Idea: Bartenieff insisted that intention and spatial imagination drive physical recovery, so exercises are about where you are going, not muscle strength; for designers, a movement is always movement toward something.
- What it is: A set of floor-based movement sequences, the 'Basic Six' such as the thigh lift, pelvic shift and arm circles, that connect breath, core and limbs through developmental patterns like head-tail, upper-lower and cross-lateral connectivity.
- How it works: Breath-supported, spatially intended floor sequences based on six patterns of total body connectivity; set out in Body Movement: Coping with the Environment (1980, with Dori Lewis).
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Movement & expression, Care & healing
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=HeQgq7L9yOo
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/3/3a/Irmgard_Bartenieff.jpg
- Project page: https://labaninstitute.org

#### Hanna Somatic Education — Thomas Hanna (1976)
- Type: Somatic practice · Body & senses: Muscle tension (EMG), Movement & kinesthesia, Pain & inner sensations
- Idea: Hanna defined soma as the body perceived from within by a first person, as distinct from the body seen from outside; soma design takes both its name and its first-person stance from this definition.
- What it is: Slow 'pandiculation' exercises, in which a person voluntarily contracts a habitually tight muscle group and then releases it very slowly, to regain sensation and control of muscles lost to what Hanna called sensory-motor amnesia.
- How it works: Guided voluntary contraction and slow release of muscle groups plus hands-on sessions, drawing on Feldenkrais; Hanna coined 'somatics' in 1976.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, First-person & felt experience, Care & healing
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=elHCMJDE1vI
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Louis_Hanna

#### Body-Mind Centering — Bonnie Bainbridge Cohen (1973)
- Type: Somatic practice · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Movement & kinesthesia, Pain & inner sensations
- Idea: In Body-Mind Centering the mind is found in each tissue: every body system has its own quality of feeling and moving, so a designer can ask which system an interaction speaks to, not just which muscle.
- What it is: Students embody one body system at a time (bones, organs, fluids, glands, cells) and the developmental movement patterns of infancy, moving and vocalising from it until they can feel how that system shapes mood and attention.
- How it works: Experiential anatomy: touch, imagery, sound and movement explorations of body systems and developmental patterns, taught through the School for Body-Mind Centering founded in 1973.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, First-person & felt experience, Attunement & appreciation
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=UuqXL-ua06g
- Images: https://www.bodymindcentering.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/08/bonnie-teaching.jpg
- Project page: https://www.bodymindcentering.com

#### Awareness Through Movement — Moshé Feldenkrais (1972)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Posture, balance & proprioception, Breath
- Idea: Its core claim, that every action involves moving, sensing, feeling and thinking together, is the practical non-dualism soma designers adopt when they design from Feldenkrais lessons.
- What it is: Feldenkrais's book explains why we act according to our self-image and gives twelve lessons, such as lifting the head or coordinating flexors and extensors, that readers do lying on the floor.
- How it works: Theory chapters on self-image and learning followed by twelve scripted Awareness Through Movement lessons; Harper & Row, 1972.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Attunement & appreciation, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://www.harpercollins.ca/9780062503220/awareness-through-movement/ (Harper & Row 1972)
- Images: https://i.harperapps.com/covers/9780062503220.jpg

#### Continuum — Emilie Conrad (1967)
- Type: Somatic practice · Body & senses: Breath, Voice, sound & listening, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: Conrad saw the body as fluid, continuous with the oceans and with early life forms; designing from this view means working with undulation and resonance instead of discrete commands.
- What it is: Practitioners lie or sit and combine breaths and sounds with very small wave-like movements, then rest and sense the aftermath, often discovering spiralling motions that feel more like water than muscle.
- How it works: Breath and sound sequences, micro-movement, and 'open attention' rests between explorations; founded by Emilie Conrad in 1967.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Attunement & appreciation, More-than-human
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=IAacwbfveys
- Images: https://continuummovement.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/12/emilie-1.png
- Project page: https://continuummovement.com

#### Iyengar Yoga — B. K. S. Iyengar (1966)
- Type: Somatic practice · Body & senses: Posture, balance & proprioception, Breath, Pain & inner sensations
- Idea: Iyengar used precise alignment to bring awareness into every part of the body, down to the skin and the breath; his props show how external objects can help a body feel from within, a precedent for supportive soma design artefacts.
- What it is: Students hold yoga postures with exact attention to alignment, using belts, blocks, blankets and chairs so that each person can feel the intended action of the pose in their own body.
- How it works: Detailed alignment instruction, long holds, sequencing and props, set out in Light on Yoga (1966); yoga as practised in Western studios.
- Lens: Attunement & appreciation, Body-mind unity, Care & healing
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Hs4ZqJwR14o
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/3/32/Eyal_Shifroni_in_Vasisthasana_with_props.jpg https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/0/01/BKS_Iyengar.jpg
- Project page: https://bksiyengar.com

#### Feldenkrais Method — Moshé Feldenkrais (1949)
- Type: Somatic practice · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Posture, balance & proprioception, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Feldenkrais held that we act according to our self-image, and that sensing, feeling, thinking and moving change together; Höök's soma design group trains in Feldenkrais before designing, and its Soma Mat and Breathing Light grew out of Feldenkrais lessons.
- What it is: A teacher talks a group through slow, small, often lying-down movement variations (Awareness Through Movement); in Functional Integration the teacher guides one person's movement by touch. People notice how a small change in one part reorganises the whole self.
- How it works: Verbally guided sequences of slow, reduced-effort movements with attention to differences, plus hands-on lessons; first set out in Body and Mature Behavior (1949).
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Attunement & appreciation, First-person & felt experience
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=e1bjUmxkf5Q
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/b/b9/Feldenkrais_Awareness_Through_Movement_Lesson.png https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/f/fc/Moshe_Feldenkrais_Demonstrates_Functional_Integration.png
- Project page: https://feldenkrais-method.org

#### Eutony — Gerda Alexander (1940)
- Type: Somatic practice · Body & senses: Muscle tension (EMG), Touch & pressure, Posture, balance & proprioception
- Idea: Eutony treats tone as a relation between person and situation: good tension is the one that fits the moment, so a design can aim to tune tension rather than to relax the user.
- What it is: People lie, sit or move while sensing skin, bones and the space inside the body, often with bamboo sticks or balls under them, to find a flexible, well-adapted tone rather than simply relaxing.
- How it works: Contact with the floor and small objects, attention to skin and bone, and 'transport' of pressure through the skeleton; Alexander founded her Copenhagen school in 1940 and named the method in 1956.
- Lens: Attunement & appreciation, Body-mind unity
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=srJWpSTGTQo
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/7/7e/Gerda_Alexander.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gerda_Alexander

#### Ideokinesis — Mabel Elsworth Todd, Lulu Sweigard (1937)
- Type: Somatic practice · Body & senses: Posture, balance & proprioception, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: Ideokinesis shows that an image is already a motor act: thinking a movement reorganises posture, so imagery is a design material for the body.
- What it is: Lying still in 'constructive rest', a person imagines movements along the skeleton, for example the spine lengthening down or the thigh bones sinking, without doing them, and the body's alignment changes in response.
- How it works: Guided imagery of 'lines of movement' in a supine rest position, from Mabel Todd's The Thinking Body (1937) and Lulu Sweigard's research on imagery and alignment.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Attunement & appreciation
- Paper: https://openlibrary.org/works/OL6916763W (Book, 1937)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=8nG1DdSkF4o
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ideokinesis

#### Contrology (Pilates) — Joseph Pilates (1934)
- Type: Somatic practice · Body & senses: Muscle tension (EMG), Breath, Posture, balance & proprioception
- Idea: Pilates wrote that Contrology 'begins with mind control over muscles', a mind-over-body model of movement education; comparing it with Feldenkrais or Gindler shows what shifts when control gives way to sensing.
- What it is: A sequence of precise floor exercises and exercises on spring-loaded apparatus such as the Reformer, done with coordinated breathing and attention to the body's centre.
- How it works: Mat and apparatus exercises with breath and core engagement, taught in Pilates's New York studio and set out in Your Health (1934) and Return to Life Through Contrology (1945).
- Lens: Body politics, Movement & expression
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=nQnLYoUHzps
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/b/b8/Pilates_reformer.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pilates

#### Laban Movement Analysis — Rudolf Laban, Irmgard Bartenieff (1928)
- Type: Somatic practice · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Laban treated effort as the inner attitude of the mover made visible, so movement quality is felt meaning; Schiphorst and Fdili Alaoui (a Certified Movement Analyst) use LMA to design and model how movement feels.
- What it is: A language for observing and doing movement in four categories, Body, Effort, Shape and Space; movers explore Effort qualities such as light or strong, sudden or sustained, direct or indirect, bound or free, and feel how each changes intention and mood.
- How it works: Observation, notation (Kinetography Laban, 1928) and embodied exploration of Effort, Shape and Space, extended by Lisa Ullmann, Irmgard Bartenieff and Warren Lamb; taught by certification programs such as LIMS.
- Lens: Movement & expression, First-person & felt experience
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=X437LVhmeeg
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/8/8d/Laban_and_pupils.jpg https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/9/98/Laban-Effort-graph-labeled.jpg
- Project page: https://labaninstitute.org

#### Alexander Technique — F. Matthias Alexander (1910)
- Type: Somatic practice · Body & senses: Posture, balance & proprioception, Movement & kinesthesia, Voice, sound & listening
- Idea: Alexander called the self a 'psycho-physical' unity and showed that sensory appreciation can be unreliable; noticing and inhibiting a habit is itself an estrangement method, and Shusterman's somaesthetics discusses it at length.
- What it is: In lessons, a teacher's light hands and verbal 'directions' help a person pause before an everyday act such as sitting, standing or speaking, and let the head-neck-back relation free up. The person discovers that what feels 'right' is often just habitual.
- How it works: One-to-one lessons with light touch, practising 'inhibition' (pausing the habitual response) and 'direction' in daily activities; first set out in Man's Supreme Inheritance (1910).
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Estrangement, Attunement & appreciation
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=lEmQHuYt404
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/2/24/Poster_made_to_advertise_services_of_F_M_Alexander.gif https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/0/06/Learning_Alexander_Technique_with_Graham_Fandrei_%2828692247726%29.jpg
- Project page: https://alexandertechniqueusa.org

### Qi, Tai Chi, Yoga & Zen

Practices of body-mind unity from Asia: qigong, tai chi, yoga, zazen, aikido, butoh roots.

#### Baduanjin Qigong (Eight Pieces of Brocade) — Chinese Health Qigong Association (2003)
- Type: Somatic practice · Body & senses: Breath, Movement & kinesthesia, Posture, balance & proprioception
- Idea: A short daily sequence can join breath, stretch and attention so that the body is felt as one moving whole.
- What it is: Eight standing movements, such as 'holding up the heavens with both hands' and 'drawing the bow to shoot the eagle', done slowly with coordinated breath; the sequence dates back to the Song dynasty and was standardised as Health Qigong in 2003.
- How it works: Twelve-minute routine of eight movements with preparatory and closing postures, each paired with slow nasal breathing and mental focus.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Care & healing, Attunement & appreciation
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=mc1jeKAb6tM
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/5/51/Baduanjin_qigong_edit1.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baduanjin_qigong

#### Zen Shiatsu — Shizuto Masunaga (1977)
- Type: Somatic practice · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Pain & inner sensations
- Idea: Therapeutic touch is a two-way listening: the practitioner's own relaxation and attention are part of what the receiver feels.
- What it is: The giver uses relaxed body weight rather than muscle, keeping one 'mother hand' still while the other explores, and feels for kyo (empty) and jitsu (full) areas along extended meridians.
- How it works: Floor-based pressure and stretches on a futon, guided by Masunaga's meridian charts and hara (abdominal) diagnosis, set out in his book Zen Shiatsu (1977).
- Lens: Care & healing, Intercorporeality, Attunement & appreciation
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Chul6S4OvsU
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/8/8d/Anma_Tebiki_%281835%2C_introduction_page%29.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shizuto_Masunaga

#### Noguchi Taisō — Noguchi Michizō (1964)
- Type: Somatic practice · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Posture, balance & proprioception, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Ask the body rather than command it: Noguchi's motto 'karada ni kiku' (listen to the body) makes sensing weight the basis of moving well.
- What it is: Practitioners hang, sway and ripple the body, imagining it as 'a leather bag filled with water' in which bones and organs float, and let weight and momentum do the moving; taught at Tokyo University of the Arts and in Noguchi's own classes from 1964.
- How it works: Relaxation and wave exercises (nyoro-nyoro, hanging forward bends), taught without fixed goals, alongside Noguchi's writings such as Genshi Seimeitai to shite no Ningen (1972).
- Lens: Attunement & appreciation, Body-mind unity, First-person & felt experience
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=GBuJICU89jQ
- Project page: https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/野口体操

#### Cheng Man-ch'ing's 37-Posture Tai Chi Form — Cheng Man-ch'ing (1950)
- Type: Somatic practice · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Posture, balance & proprioception
- Idea: Relaxation is not collapse: the practice trains a body that is soft yet structured, a state many Western somatic and dance teachers later adopted.
- What it is: A shortened Yang-style form of 37 postures that takes about ten minutes, emphasising total relaxation, a 'beautiful lady's hand' and rooting through the feet; Cheng taught it in Taipei and from 1964 at the Shr Jung school in New York.
- How it works: Short form codified in Cheng's Thirteen Chapters on Tai Chi Chuan (Zhengzi Taijiquan Shisan Pian), taught with push hands and Chinese medicine.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Attunement & appreciation
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3_BKwlpOAkk
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/c/c0/Cheng_Man-ching.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cheng_Man-ch%27ing

#### Seitai and Katsugen Undō (Spontaneous Regenerative Movement) — Noguchi Haruchika (1950)
- Type: Somatic practice · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Pain & inner sensations, Whole body-mind
- Idea: The body has its own intelligence of recovery: stepping back from control lets involuntary movement rebalance body and mood.
- What it is: After a few preparatory breaths and twists, practitioners let the body move by itself, shaking, stretching or swaying without conscious direction; Noguchi Haruchika proposed katsugen undō in the decade after 1945 as part of Seitai.
- How it works: Group or solo sessions of spontaneous movement, combined with yuki (attentive hand contact) and taiheki (body-type) observation.
- Lens: Care & healing, Body-mind unity, First-person & felt experience
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=RAXrDW1deyU
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/5/5e/Haruchika-noguchi-portrait.jpg
- Project page: https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/活元運動

#### Aikido — Morihei Ueshiba (1942)
- Type: Somatic practice · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Touch & pressure, Posture, balance & proprioception
- Idea: Harmony can be trained physically: sensing and joining another's momentum turns conflict into shared movement.
- What it is: Partners practise throws and pins in which the defender blends with the attacker's movement, leads it in circles and redirects it, rather than blocking force with force; Ueshiba adopted the name aikido in 1942.
- How it works: Paired kata practice (nage and uke) of techniques such as shihōnage and iriminage, with breathing exercises and falls (ukemi).
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Body-mind unity
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=30Sa0PLquFg
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/6/62/A%C3%AFkido-shihoo_nage.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aikido

#### Yang-Style Tai Chi Chuan — Yang Chengfu (1934)
- Type: Somatic practice · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Posture, balance & proprioception, Breath
- Idea: Softness is a form of sensing: by moving slowly and staying relaxed, practitioners learn to feel weight, breath and another person's intention through the body.
- What it is: A slow, continuous solo form of about 85–108 movements, practised with a relaxed, rooted stance and paired with push hands (tuishou), in which two partners keep contact and learn to feel and yield to each other's force.
- How it works: Form practice of weight shifts and spiralling movement from the dantian, standardised by Yang Chengfu and set down in his 1934 manual Taijiquan Tiyong Quanshu.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Attunement & appreciation, Intercorporeality
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7Twc_Zaezcg
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/7/7f/Yang-single_%28restoration%29.jpg https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/4/4c/Pushing_hands_by_yatish.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yang-style_tai_chi

#### Zhan Zhuang (Standing Like a Post) — Wang Xiangzhai (1928)
- Type: Somatic practice · Body & senses: Posture, balance & proprioception, Breath, Pain & inner sensations
- Idea: Stillness is a rich movement practice: standing reveals tensions, shifts and inner motion that busy movement hides.
- What it is: The practitioner stands still for many minutes, knees soft and arms held as if embracing a tree, attending to breath, tension and the subtle sensations of the standing body; Wang Xiangzhai made it the core training of Yiquan from the late 1920s.
- How it works: Held postures (the 'hunyuan' or tree-hugging stance and others) practised with relaxed breathing and imagery, gradually lengthened from minutes to an hour.
- Lens: Attunement & appreciation, Body-mind unity
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=IeYzjnCt5vA
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/8/85/Wang_Xiangzhai.png https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/3/3a/Nitzan_Oren_practicing_Zhan_Zhuang.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhan_zhuang

#### Zazen (Seated Zen Meditation, Fukanzazengi) — Dōgen Zenji (1227)
- Type: Somatic practice · Body & senses: Posture, balance & proprioception, Breath, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Posture is practice: aligning spine, breath and attention is already the realisation, not a means to some later insight.
- What it is: Sitting upright on a cushion in full or half lotus, hands in the cosmic mudra, eyes half open and breath left natural, just sitting (shikantaza) without chasing thoughts; Dōgen's Fukanzazengi (1227) gives the instructions.
- How it works: Daily sitting periods in a zendō, alternating with walking meditation (kinhin), guided by Dōgen's instructions on posture, breath and non-thinking.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Attunement & appreciation
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9eacYQsCvWU
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/c/c1/Kodo_Sawaki_Zazen.jpg https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/9/9e/Fukan-Zazengi-Instructions-for-Zazen-1233.png
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zazen

### Meditation, Mindfulness & Breath

Mindfulness, body scan, breathwork and contemplative practice.

#### Full Catastrophe Living — Jon Kabat-Zinn (1990)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Breath, Whole body-mind, Pain & inner sensations
- Idea: The subtitle names its stance: using the wisdom of your body and mind together; it is the standard reference for the body scan used in HCI studies.
- What it is: Kabat-Zinn's book lays out the MBSR program week by week, with the body scan, sitting meditation and mindful yoga, for people living with stress, pain and illness.
- How it works: Program description, guided practices and patient stories from the UMass Stress Reduction Clinic; Delacorte, 1990 (revised 2013).
- Lens: Attunement & appreciation, Care & healing, Body-mind unity
- Paper: https://www.penguinrandomhouse.com/books/89149/full-catastrophe-living-revised-edition-by-jon-kabat-zinn-preface-by-thich-nhat-hanh-foreword-by-joan-borysenko/ (Delacorte 1990)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ru87wojiBTo
- Images: https://images3.penguinrandomhouse.com/smedia/9780345536938

#### Body Scan — Jon Kabat-Zinn (1979)
- Type: Somatic practice · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Pain & inner sensations, Breath
- Idea: The body scan is the simplest tool for turning inward and making the body available to design; KTH's Teaching Soma Design course (DIS 2019) used body scans with Feldenkrais to widen students' design space.
- What it is: Lying down, a person moves attention slowly through the body, from the toes of one foot up to the crown of the head, noticing whatever sensations are present, including none, and breathing into each region.
- How it works: A 30–45 minute guided sweep of attention through body regions, adapted in MBSR from the Burmese Vipassana tradition of U Ba Khin and S. N. Goenka.
- Lens: Attunement & appreciation, First-person & felt experience, Body-mind unity
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_DTmGtznab4

#### Mindfulness-Based Stress Reduction (MBSR) — Jon Kabat-Zinn (1979)
- Type: Somatic practice · Body & senses: Breath, Whole body-mind, Pain & inner sensations
