- How it works: A low-fidelity inflatable prototyping tool on a racing seat, used in a soma design workshop with the manufacturer's designers.
- Lens: Technology that touches back, Attunement & appreciation, Body-mind unity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3643834.3661518 (DIS 2024)
- Images: https://figures.semanticscholar.org/6ab6d94919fb0abd93165123064d0ccffe4bbe71/6-Figure2-1.png https://figures.semanticscholar.org/6ab6d94919fb0abd93165123064d0ccffe4bbe71/8-Figure3-1.png

#### OmniFiber — Ozgun Kilic Afsar (2021)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Shape-change & inflatables, Movement & kinesthesia, Touch & pressure
- Idea: A textile that behaves like muscle lets movement be taught and shared through the fabric we already wear.
- What it is: OmniFiber is a thin fluidic fibre that contracts and senses its own strain, so it can be woven or knitted into garments that move, squeeze and guide the body.
- How it works: Fluidic artificial muscle fibres (under 1.8 mm) with integrated soft sensors, woven into on-body haptic devices for embodied learning (with the MIT Media Lab Tangible Media Group).
- Lens: Technology that touches back, Movement & expression
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3472749.3474802 (UIST 2021)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xtlL-0eb0xc

#### Preemptive Action — Shunichi Kasahara, Jun Nishida (2019)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Muscle tension (EMG), Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: The sense of 'I did it' depends on timing: assistance that arrives in step with one's intention is felt as one's own movement.
- What it is: Electrical muscle stimulation makes the user's hand react faster than it would alone, and by timing the stimulation just before the person's own reaction, users still feel that they moved.
- How it works: Reaction-time studies varying EMS onset relative to natural reaction, with sense-of-agency ratings (with Pedro Lopes, University of Chicago).
- Lens: First-person & felt experience, Body-mind unity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3290605.3300873 (CHI 2019)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=1BT8REEJibM

#### Shaking the World / Save YourSelf!!! — Taro Maeda, Hideyuki Ando (2005)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Posture, balance & proprioception, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Balance is felt, not given: when technology writes directly to the vestibular sense, the ground itself seems to move and the body's certainty becomes strange.
- What it is: A headset passes weak currents behind the ears (galvanic vestibular stimulation) so the wearer feels pulled left or right; the related art piece Save YourSelf!!! lets visitors feel their balance being shifted from outside.
- How it works: Bilateral GVS electrodes on the mastoids driven by a small current controller; the art version likely drives the current from sensor input.
- Lens: Estrangement, Technology that touches back
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/1187297.1187315 (SIGGRAPH 2005 Emerging Technologies)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jPeWwvsnaaQ

### Sound, Voice & Sonification

Hearing the body: movement sonification, singing, the voice as felt.

#### Corsetto — Ozgun Kilic Afsar, Yoav Luft, Kelsey Cotton, Claudia Núñez-Pacheco (2023)
- Type: Performance, dance & music · Body & senses: Breath, Shape-change & inflatables, Voice, sound & listening
- Idea: Haptic gestures on the torso can bridge inner and outer sensation, so listening to a voice becomes a shared intercorporeal experience.
- What it is: Corsetto is a robotic garment worn by audience members during a live opera performance; it enacts haptic gestures that echo the singer's upper-body movements, adding a felt 'fourth voice' to Morton Feldman's Three Voices.
- How it works: Soft robotic (likely fluidic fibre) actuators in a corset-like garment play composed haptic gestures synchronised with a live singer; audience experiences were studied with micro-phenomenological interviews.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Technology that touches back, Movement & expression
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3544548.3581294 (CHI 2023)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ML_A_L2u280

#### The Body Electric — Kelsey Cotton, Pedro Sanches, Vasiliki Tsaknaki, Pavel Karpashevich (2020)
- Type: Performance, dance & music · Body & senses: Breath, Voice, sound & listening
- Idea: An instrument designed from the singer's felt breathing can unsettle control and open new ways of relating to one's own voice.
- What it is: The Body Electric is a singing corset that captures the breathing movements of the singer's torso and uses them to shape the soundscape, so the instrument steers the singer's voice as much as the singer steers the instrument.
- How it works: Breathing sensors on a corset stream biofeedback to a live electronic composition; performed in Hamburg in July 2020 and documented as a NIME paper.
- Lens: First-person & felt experience, Movement & expression, Estrangement
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.21428/92fbeb44.ec9f8fdd (NIME 2021)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=L5DbL4XyBsU

#### Sarka — Ilias Bergström, Martin Jonsson (2016)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Voice, sound & listening, Posture, balance & proprioception
- Idea: Sound feedback can support attention to the body only if it stays quiet enough not to become the thing you listen to.
- What it is: Sarka is a mat that sonifies how a person lying on it distributes their weight and how intensely each part of the body moves, to support inward listening during body-awareness exercises.
- How it works: Sensing of weight distribution and movement across zones of the mat (likely pressure sensors) drives an interactive sonification designed through Somaesthetic Appreciation Design.
- Lens: Attunement & appreciation, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2948910.2948922 (MOCO 2016)
- Images: https://figures.semanticscholar.org/cd5a659b9d8a65cff778c9b909b61bcdb2e86318/4-Figure2-1.png https://figures.semanticscholar.org/cd5a659b9d8a65cff778c9b909b61bcdb2e86318/1-Figure1-1.png

### Garments, Textiles & Soft Wearables

Clothing and soft materials that sense and touch: corsets, shirts, e-textiles.

#### Inside the Mirror, Wearing My Own Body — Pavel Karpashevich, Kristina Höök (2026)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Shape-change & inflatables, Touch & pressure, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Attending to felt, ambiguous experiences reveals a monstrous category that user-experience frameworks miss; such experiences deserve design attention rather than removal.
- What it is: Looking back on four wearable systems, the authors describe felt experiences that were at once pleasant and unpleasant, comforting and frightening, human-like and machinic, and read them through monster theory.
- How it works: Retrospective first-person analysis of shape-changing and actuated wearables using concepts such as fusion, fission, massification and incompleteness (with Jeffrey Bardzell).
- Lens: Estrangement, Body-mind unity, More-than-human
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3772318.3790753 (CHI 2026)
- Images: https://figures.semanticscholar.org/46f2883425394b2f11137ff595de6bd8992ef412/6-Figure5-1.png https://figures.semanticscholar.org/46f2883425394b2f11137ff595de6bd8992ef412/5-Figure2-1.png

#### The Breathing Wings — Vasiliki Tsaknaki (2021)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Shape-change & inflatables, Touch & pressure, Breath
- Idea: A material that touches you slowly can blur the boundary between garment and body and make the shoulder blades felt again.
- What it is: The Breathing Wings is a wearable over the shoulder blades whose soft shape-changing parts swell and embrace the back, inviting the wearer to feel a neglected part of the body.
- How it works: Autobiographical soma design with soft inflatable and shape-change materials, documented as a pictorial.
- Lens: Technology that touches back, First-person & felt experience, Body-mind unity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3461778.3462054 (DIS 2021)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3BcjnEDwakU

#### Nebula — Vasiliki Tsaknaki (2015)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Voice, sound & listening
- Idea: A garment's drape can itself be the interface: implicit movement, not commands, carries the expression.
- What it is: Nebula is a heavy studded cloak that turns both intentional gestures and the incidental swing of its folds into sound, so the wearer hears how they move.
- How it works: Metal studs act as a sensing material and visual feature in a draped heavy fabric; a sound design exploits the garment's material qualities (with sound researcher Ludvig Elblaus at KTH).
- Lens: Movement & expression, Attunement & appreciation
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3294109.3295623 (TEI 2019)
- Images: https://figures.semanticscholar.org/81b83d6431712aa2e5de7e65dbf2fe04156c71e7/2-Figure1-1.png https://figures.semanticscholar.org/81b83d6431712aa2e5de7e65dbf2fe04156c71e7/6-Figure3-1.png

## Affective Loops & Biodata

Emotion as a bodily, felt process: affective loops, biosignals as design material, interoception, and the ambiguity of body data.

### Affective Loops

Systems where emotion is co-produced through gesture and response: eMoto, Affective Diary, SenToy.

#### As Light as You Aspire to Be — Ana Tajadura-Jiménez (2019)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Voice, sound & listening, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: Altering the felt body can support physical activity without numbers or targets, but the effect depends on each person's weight and body ideals and fades when the sound stops, which design must take seriously.
- What it is: Sound-altering footwear (the 'Magic Shoes' line of work) tested during exertion such as gym stepping and stair climbing: lighter footstep sounds made people feel lighter even while working hard, depending on the body they aspired to.
- How it works: A wearable senses footsteps and alters their sound in real time; two studies in exertion contexts measured body perception, emotion and after-effects (CHI 2019).
- Lens: Affective loops, Care & healing, Estrangement
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3290605.3300888 (CHI 2019)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Dw_LC-YpjFw
- Project page: https://imbodylab.com/ana-tajadura-jimenez/

#### As Light as Your Footsteps — Ana Tajadura-Jiménez (2015)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Voice, sound & listening, Movement & kinesthesia, Posture, balance & proprioception
- Idea: The felt size and weight of the body is not fixed: changing one sound of one's own movement shifts body image, emotion and gait together, showing them as one process.
- What it is: Shoes with microphones pick up the wearer's footsteps and play them back through headphones with the high frequencies raised or lowered; walkers feel lighter or heavier, walk differently and report a changed mood.
- How it works: Real-time frequency shifting of footstep sounds captured by shoe-mounted microphones; body-weight estimates, emotion ratings, galvanic skin response and gait were measured (CHI 2015).
- Lens: Estrangement, Affective loops, Body-mind unity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2702123.2702374 (CHI 2015)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=6Tcx6F5y6Rw
- Project page: https://imbodylab.com/ana-tajadura-jimenez/

#### Emotions Mediated Through Mid-Air Haptics — Marianna Obrist (2015)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Vibration & haptics
- Idea: How a touch feels, where, how fast and how strong, carries emotion in non-arbitrary ways, so felt qualities of touch are a vocabulary for affective design.
- What it is: One group of people designed mid-air ultrasound touch patterns on the palm to express emotions such as happy, sad, excited and afraid; two further groups recognised and validated them.
- How it works: An ultrasonic mid-air haptic array; participants composed haptic descriptions for emotions, which were reviewed and validated by two other groups (CHI 2015).
- Lens: Affective loops, Technology that touches back
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2702123.2702361 (CHI 2015)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=OBOxuFmsxBY

#### Incendiary Reflection — Shigeo Yoshida (2013)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Light & vision, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Emotion flows from the felt and seen face inward: changing the body's expression changes the feeling, not only the other way round.
- What it is: A mirror-like display subtly deforms the viewer's face in real time into a slight smile or frown; people who see themselves smiling report feeling more positive and rate things more favourably.
- How it works: Camera and real-time facial image warping shown on a display framed as a mirror, with studies on emotion and preference (University of Tokyo, Hirose-Narumi lab).
- Lens: Affective loops, Body-mind unity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2459236.2459243 (Augmented Human 2013)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=1ORSFameZZI
- Project page: http://www.shigeodayo.com/incendiary_reflection.html

#### Affective Loop Experiences — Kristina Höök (2009)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: Emotions are processes constructed in interaction, starting from bodily, cognitive and social experience; the user, not the system, holds the responsibility for interpreting them.
- What it is: Höök's essay defines the affective loop: a system that is influenced by and influences the user corporeally, drawing on eMoto, the Affective Diary and other SICS systems.
- How it works: Conceptual analysis of a series of built systems (eMoto, FriendSense, Affective Diary) and their user studies, published in a theme issue on computation of emotions.
- Lens: Affective loops, Body-mind unity, Movement & expression
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1098/rstb.2009.0202 (Philosophical Transactions of the Royal Society B 2009)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-_GBHgADRr4
- Images: https://figures.semanticscholar.org/a4980adb3deee93c75ff36c412b0ba0467a6a5ba/3-Figure1-1.png

#### How Emotion Is Made and Measured — Kirsten Boehner, Phoebe Sengers (2007)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Whole body-mind
- Idea: Design should help people experience, interpret and reflect on their own emotions rather than have a system decide what they feel, which is the ground of affective loops.
- What it is: A critique of affective computing's 'informational' view of emotion as an internal state to be sensed and transmitted, proposing instead an 'interactional' view in which emotion is made in social and cultural interaction.
- How it works: A theoretical argument drawing on social and cultural accounts of emotion, with design examples of interactional systems (International Journal of Human-Computer Studies, 2007; with Rogério DePaula and Paul Dourish).
- Lens: Affective loops, Body-mind unity, Body data & ambiguity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ijhcs.2006.11.016 (International Journal of Human-Computer Studies 2007)

#### eMoto — Petra Sundström, Anna Ståhl, Kristina Höök (2004)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Touch & pressure, Light & vision
- Idea: Emotion is not a label to select but a process that starts in the body; the interface should pull the user into it physically and leave the interpretation to them.
- What it is: eMoto is a mobile messaging service in which the sender expresses emotion by how they move and squeeze a stylus; the text is sent against an animated background of colours and shapes chosen that way.
- How it works: A pressure- and motion-sensing stylus maps effort and shape of gesture (from Laban analysis) to a circular space of animated colour backgrounds; tested with friends in everyday use.
- Lens: Affective loops, Movement & expression, Body-mind unity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1007/s00779-004-0301-z (Personal and Ubiquitous Computing 2004)
- Images: https://figures.semanticscholar.org/d4fc400539b03fed6e3e52b2120f9d95c2729658/2-Figure4-1.png https://figures.semanticscholar.org/a4980adb3deee93c75ff36c412b0ba0467a6a5ba/4-Figure2-1.png

#### SenToy — Ana Paiva, Kristina Höök (2003)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Touch & pressure
- Idea: Emotion can be entered into a system as a whole-body gesture, so the player has to act the feeling out to use it.
- What it is: SenToy is a plush doll with motion sensors that players move and squeeze to make game characters happy, sad, angry or afraid in the fantasy game FantasyA.
- How it works: Sensors in the doll's body and limbs (likely accelerometers and position switches) classify gesture patterns (e.g. bouncing for happy, covering the eyes for fear), derived from a Wizard of Oz study at SICS.
- Lens: Affective loops, Movement & expression, Pleasure & play
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1016/s1071-5819(03)00048-x (International Journal of Human-Computer Studies 2003)
- Images: https://figures.semanticscholar.org/73b66a6b4c4bc8e975ca3f6b446c6efe307439e5/3-Figure1-1.png https://figures.semanticscholar.org/73b66a6b4c4bc8e975ca3f6b446c6efe307439e5/5-Figure2-1.png

#### Affective Computing — Rosalind Picard (1997)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Skin conductance & arousal, Heartbeat
- Idea: It put the body's signals at the centre of computing with emotion; soma design keeps that attention to the body but questions the idea that emotion can be read off it as a label.
- What it is: The book that named and founded affective computing: computing that relates to, arises from or deliberately influences emotion, including wearable sensing of physiological signals.
- How it works: A monograph combining emotion theory, signal processing, pattern recognition and early wearable prototypes from the MIT Media Lab.
- Lens: Affective loops, Body data & ambiguity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.7551/mitpress/1140.001.0001 (MIT Press 1997)

### Biosignals as Design Material

EDA, heart rate, EMG, EEG turned into felt experience: from biodata to somadata.

#### Can You Hear My Heartbeat? — R. Michael Winters (2021)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Heartbeat, Voice, sound & listening
- Idea: A heartbeat heard, not seen, reaches the listener's body directly and can open an embodied path to empathy.
- What it is: In a lab study, 27 people recognised others' emotions while sometimes hearing that person's heartbeat; hearing it shifted their emotional perspective and raised their sense of 'feeling what the other was feeling'.
- How it works: Within-subjects emotion recognition task with auditory heartbeat conditions and empathy measures (CHI 2021; with Bruce Walker and Grace Leslie).
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Affective loops
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3411764.3445545 (CHI 2021)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7N9GOKW1mbA

#### Significant Otter — Fannie Liu (2021)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Heartbeat
- Idea: Sensing made communication feel easier and more authentic, but also raised worries about who authors a feeling; biosignals work best as suggestions the person can accept or refuse.
- What it is: A watch and phone app for romantic partners: the app reads the sender's heart rate and suggests matching animated otters (calm, excited, angry…) to send, and the partner can respond with otter gestures such as a hug.
- How it works: Apple Watch heart rate informs a small set of suggested otter animations; a one-month study with 20 couples compared sensing off and on (CHI 2021).
- Lens: Affective loops, Intercorporeality, Body data & ambiguity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3411764.3445200 (CHI 2021)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Oub3OY5j7Z8
- Images: https://www.cmu.edu/news/stories/archives/2021/june/images/otter-900x600.jpg
- Project page: https://research.snap.com/publications/significant-otter-understanding-the-role-of-biosignals-in-communication.html

#### ThermoPixels — Muhammad Umair, Miquel Alfaras (2020)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Skin conductance & arousal, Heat & temperature, Light & vision
- Idea: Making your own representation of arousal, with your hands, turns biodata into something personal you can regulate emotion with.
- What it is: ThermoPixels is a crafting toolkit of thermochromic materials, heating elements and skin conductance sensors with which people make their own colour-changing displays of physiological arousal.
- How it works: GSR sensing drives heating elements under thermochromic paint and fabric; 20 participants created personalised shapes and colours in 10 workshops.
- Lens: Body data & ambiguity, Attunement & appreciation, Pleasure & play
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3357236.3395512 (DIS 2020)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_IhgPrDsXzI

#### Animo — Fannie Liu (2019)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Heartbeat
- Idea: A biosignal shared as an expressive, abstract form lets people feel close without words, while leaving the meaning of the body's state open to both.
- What it is: A smartwatch app that turns the wearer's heart rate into a small animated shape, an 'animo', that close friends or partners can send to each other as a lightweight way of saying how they are.
- How it works: Smartwatch heart rate drives the colour and motion of an animated shape, likely with features for dyadic sending and viewing; a two-week field study with 34 participants (IMWUT 2019).
- Lens: Affective loops, Intercorporeality, Body data & ambiguity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3314405 (IMWUT 2019 (UbiComp))
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qE-H8bYmlgs

#### Breeze — Jérémy Frey (2018)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Breath, Vibration & haptics, Light & vision
- Idea: Sharing breath invites bodily attunement: matching another's breathing is itself a way of understanding their emotion, from the inside.
- What it is: A pendant worn around the neck that senses the wearer's breathing and relays it to a partner's pendant as light, vibration or sound; people often breathed along with the feedback to sense what the other felt.
- How it works: A wearable pendant measures breathing and renders digital breathing patterns through visual, audio and haptic output; patterns were evaluated for emotional interpretation in a lab study (CHI 2018).
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Affective loops
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3173574.3174219 (CHI 2018)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QPQ8-hbWKyM
- Images: https://labs.ullo.fr/wp-content/uploads/2021/04/breeze_teaser.jpg
- Project page: https://labs.ullo.fr/projects/breeze/

#### HeartChat — Mariam Hassib (2017)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Heartbeat
- Idea: Adding a live body signal to text restores some of the felt presence that written messages lose, supporting empathy as well as raising questions about privacy.
- What it is: A mobile chat app that attaches the sender's heart rate, sensed by a wearable, to messages, so friends and partners can glimpse each other's bodily state while texting.
- How it works: Heart rate from a wearable sensor is displayed in the chat interface in several forms; evaluated in a field study with pairs of users (CHI 2017).
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Body data & ambiguity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3025453.3025758 (CHI 2017)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=elTsjD4wBuE

#### Teegi — Jérémy Frey (2014)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Brain (EEG), Touch & pressure, Light & vision
- Idea: Turning an abstract brain signal into a figure one can hold makes one's own inner activity approachable and felt, rather than a chart to be decoded.
- What it is: A small tangible character on whose head a person's own EEG activity is projected in real time; by handling and turning it, novices explore what their brain is doing as they move, close their eyes or relax.
- How it works: EEG headset signals are processed in real time and projected onto a tracked figurine with spatial augmented reality; an exploratory study with novice users (UIST 2014).
- Lens: Body data & ambiguity, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2642918.2647368 (UIST 2014)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fxiARWdgSCU
- Images: https://phd.jfrey.info/images/teaser_teegi.jpg
- Project page: https://phd.jfrey.info/projects/teegi/

#### Metaphone — Vygandas Šimbelis, Kristina Höök (2013)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Skin conductance & arousal, Heartbeat, Voice, sound & listening
- Idea: A slow, machine-like response to one's pulse can feel strangely relaxing: machine aesthetics can make the living body stand out by contrast.
- What it is: Metaphone is a large machine on two wheels that rolls across a sheet of watercolour paper, dripping paint and making sound according to the visitor's skin conductance and heart rate.
- How it works: GSR and heart-rate sensors drive a rotating shaft that drips paint onto paper on the floor, with a soundscape following the biosignals; six invited commentators interacted with it.
- Lens: Body data & ambiguity, Attunement & appreciation, Estrangement
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2556288.2557152 (CHI 2014)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9BpBc7UuclI

#### Swarming Emotional Pianos — Erin Gee (2012)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Heartbeat, Skin conductance & arousal, Breath
- Idea: Visitors meet two windows onto one emotional body, the face and the physiology, and feel how much, and how little, of feeling survives the translation into data.
- What it is: Six robotic chime instruments surround a projection of an actress moving through extreme emotions; her recorded heartbeat, sweat and breathing drive the robots' musical outbursts.
- How it works: Custom biosensors recorded heart rate, skin conductance and respiration during timed emotional performances; the data drives servo-mallets on aluminium-tube robots.
- Lens: Affective loops, Body data & ambiguity
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=8crC5vGj44A
- Images: https://eringee.net/wp-content/uploads/2016/01/erin_gee-swarming_emotional_pianos-1.jpg
- Project page: https://eringee.net/swarming-emotional-pianos/

#### Understanding Heart Rate Sharing — Petr Slovák, Joris H. Janssen (2012)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Heartbeat
- Idea: A heartbeat shared between people is not neutral data but a 'physiosocial' cue: it carries intimacy and responsibility and must be designed for as a relationship.
- What it is: A qualitative study of people sharing their heart rate with others in everyday situations, which found that a shared heartbeat can feel intimate and create connection, and that its meaning depends on the relationship.
- How it works: Qualitative field study of heart rate sharing across social settings, analysed to map the dimensions of a 'physiosocial space' (CHI 2012; with Geraldine Fitzpatrick).
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Body data & ambiguity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2207676.2208526 (CHI 2012)

#### Intimate Heartbeats — Joris H. Janssen (2010)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Heartbeat, Voice, sound & listening
- Idea: The heartbeat is felt as one of the most intimate body signals; hearing it gives technology a way to mediate closeness through the body, with the care that intimacy requires.
- What it is: A set of studies, including one in an immersive virtual environment, showing that people perceive another's heartbeat as an intimate signal and that hearing it changes how close they feel to that person.
- How it works: Survey and experimental studies on the perceived intimacy of heartbeats, including an immersive virtual environment study of interpersonal distance (IEEE Transactions on Affective Computing, 2010; with Jeremy Bailenson and others).
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Affective loops
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1109/t-affc.2010.13 (IEEE Transactions on Affective Computing 2010)

#### Heart Chamber Orchestra — TERMINALBEACH (2006)
- Type: Performance, dance & music · Body & senses: Heartbeat, Voice, sound & listening
- Idea: A performance is a loop between bodies and sound: the players' excitement shapes the score, and the score shapes their excitement.
- What it is: Twelve classical musicians wear heart sensors; their heartbeats generate the musical score in real time, which they read from screens and play, so the music feeds back into the hearts that write it.
- How it works: ECG sensors stream the musicians' heartbeats to software that generates notation and visuals live on stage.
- Lens: Affective loops, Intercorporeality
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=T3FJ4YYJC0U

#### Pulse Room — Rafael Lozano-Hemmer (2006)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Heartbeat, Light & vision
- Idea: One's own heartbeat, seen as light among hundreds of others, is felt both as intimately personal and as part of a collective pulse.
- What it is: A visitor grips a sensor and a bare light bulb above starts to flash with their heartbeat; the pulse then moves along a field of hundreds of bulbs, each blinking with the heartbeat of an earlier visitor.
- How it works: A heart-rate sensor on a handle drives up to 300 incandescent bulbs through dimmers, keeping the most recent recordings; the combined pulses are also heard.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Body data & ambiguity
- Shown at: 52nd Venice Biennale, Mexican Pavilion (2007)
- Video: https://vimeo.com/33929373
- Images: https://www.lozano-hemmer.com/image_sets/pulse_room/kanazawa_2025/pulse_room_kanazawa_2025_ko_001_t.jpg https://canadianart.ca/wp-content/uploads/2014/07/pulse_room11-1000x628.jpg
- Project page: https://www.lozano-hemmer.com/pulse_room.php

### Interoception & the Inner Body

Heartbeat, gut, breath and inner sensations made perceptible or altered.

#### Mettāmatics — George Poonkhin Khut (2020)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Heartbeat, Breath
- Idea: Feelings of compassion and gratitude have a bodily signature; a slow interface lets people sense that shift in themselves, a 'being as a way of doing'.
- What it is: A meditation artwork in which a guided loving-kindness practice, a real-time visualisation and a soundscape respond to changes in the participant's heart rate variability.
- How it works: A fingertip or earlobe pulse sensor feeds heart rate variability into visuals and sound, paired with guided mettā meditation (with Callum Howard; TEI 2020).
- Lens: Attunement & appreciation, Body-mind unity, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3374920.3375286 (TEI 2020)
- Images: https://static1.squarespace.com/static/6572a8653a6cba0345e9e3cb/6572cc375a2d8a284b57b09d/65a33498eafaaf1208540946/1705202744813/george-khut-mettamatics-TEI2019-installation-view-1920x1080.jpg?format=1500w
- Project page: https://www.georgekhut.com/media-art/mettamatics

#### ambienBeat — Kyung Yun Choi (2020)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Heartbeat, Vibration & haptics, Touch & pressure
- Idea: The body entrains to rhythms it feels: a calm beat on the skin can guide the heart below the level of deliberate effort.
- What it is: A wristband that taps a gentle pulse on the skin at a rhythm slightly slower than the wearer's heart rate, helping the heart settle without the wearer having to watch a screen or follow instructions.
- How it works: Wrist-worn tactile actuator paired with heart rate sensing, delivering a personalised 'calming heartbeat' pattern (MIT Media Lab, Tangible Media Group).
- Lens: Attunement & appreciation, Care & healing
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3374920.3374938 (TEI 2020)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5SzRCJtwrxg

#### Heart Sounds Bench — Noura Howell (2019)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Heartbeat, Voice, sound & listening
- Idea: Biosensing can affirm being alive rather than diagnose or score; heart sounds felt as intimate yet anonymous invite rest and a sense of shared life, while respecting each person's right to stay opaque to data.
- What it is: A red public bench that amplifies the heart sounds of the people sitting on it into the surrounding space, and also plays back recorded heartbeats of earlier sitters.
- How it works: Heart sounds, likely picked up with stethoscope-style microphones, are amplified through speakers without labels or scores, and past sitters' recordings are replayed; a study with 19 participants in public space (CHI 2019).
- Lens: Attunement & appreciation, Intercorporeality, Body data & ambiguity
- Shown at: Extension of Self, Georgia Tech Art Gallery (2022)
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3290605.3300910 (CHI 2019)
- Video: https://vimeo.com/314159216
- Images: https://nourahowell.com/static/img/1000px_wide/red_bench.jpg https://nourahowell.com/static/img/1000px_wide/hsbv3.jpg
- Project page: https://nourahowell.com/projects/heart_sounds_bench.html

#### How Emotions Are Made — Lisa Feldman Barrett (2017)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Pain & inner sensations, Heartbeat, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Every feeling starts from the body's own budget of energy and sensation; emotion is body-mind activity, which undercuts designs that 'detect' emotions as fixed categories.
- What it is: A book presenting the theory of constructed emotion: emotions are not fixed circuits or universal expressions but are built by the brain from interoceptive sensations, concepts and context.
- How it works: Synthesis of neuroscience, psychology and anthropology on predictive processing, interoception ('body budgeting') and emotion concepts (Houghton Mifflin Harcourt).
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Affective loops
- Paper: https://lisafeldmanbarrett.com/books/how-emotions-are-made/ (Houghton Mifflin Harcourt 2017)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0gks6ceq4eQ
- Images: https://lisafeldmanbarrett.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/4/2020/06/BookTemplate_Square_HowEmotionsMade.jpg
- Project page: https://lisafeldmanbarrett.com/books/how-emotions-are-made/

#### EmotionCheck — Jean Costa (2016)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Heartbeat, Vibration & haptics
- Idea: Emotion is partly built from how we read our body: feeling a slower heartbeat can calm us, a reminder that interoceptive feedback is itself persuasive.
- What it is: A wrist device that lets anxious users feel, through vibration, a heart rate slower than their real one; believing their heart was calm, people reported less anxiety.
- How it works: A wearable renders vibrotactile heartbeats at a false, slower rate; tested in an anxiety-inducing task with physiological and self-report measures (UbiComp 2016).
- Lens: Affective loops, Technology that touches back
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2971648.2971752 (UbiComp 2016)

#### Knowing Your Own Heart — Sarah Garfinkel (2015)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Heartbeat
- Idea: Feeling the body well is not one thing: a design that trains or displays interoception should say whether it addresses accuracy, belief or the awareness between them.
- What it is: A study showing that how accurately people detect their heartbeat, how well they believe they sense their body, and how well their confidence tracks their accuracy are three separate abilities.
- How it works: Heartbeat tracking and discrimination tasks, confidence ratings and questionnaires with 80 participants (Biological Psychology, 2015; with Anil Seth, Hugo Critchley and others).
- Lens: First-person & felt experience, Body data & ambiguity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1016/j.biopsycho.2014.11.004 (Biological Psychology 2015)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hI_gG49sV2s

#### doppel — doppel, Manos Tsakiris (2015)
- Type: Product & app · Body & senses: Heartbeat, Vibration & haptics
- Idea: Rather than measuring the body and telling us about it, technology can speak to the body in its own rhythms and let it respond.
- What it is: A wristband that taps a heartbeat-like rhythm on the inside of the wrist; a slow rhythm is meant to calm and a faster one to energise, without displaying any data.
- How it works: A vibrotactile actuator produces heartbeat-like pulses at a chosen tempo; a controlled study found lower skin conductance and anxiety before public speaking (Scientific Reports, 2017).
- Lens: Technology that touches back, Care & healing
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-017-02274-2 (Scientific Reports 2017)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XU_UEcvbJW8
- Project page: https://feeldoppel.co.uk/pages/science

#### Turning Body and Self Inside Out — Jane Aspell (2013)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Heartbeat, Light & vision
- Idea: The inner heartbeat helps locate the self in space: making interoception visible outside the body can move where 'I' feel I am.
- What it is: Participants saw a projected image of their own body outlined by a silhouette that flashed with their heartbeat; when the flashing was in sync, they identified more with the virtual body and felt touch displaced toward it.
- How it works: A cardio-visual full-body illusion: real-time video of the participant's body with a heartbeat-synchronous or asynchronous illuminated silhouette, measuring self-identification, self-location and tactile perception (Psychological Science, 2013; with Olaf Blanke and others).
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Estrangement
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1177/0956797613498395 (Psychological Science 2013)

#### Distillery: Waveforming — George Poonkhin Khut (2012)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Heartbeat, Light & vision
- Idea: Directing attention quietly inward to the heartbeat reveals how mind and body answer each other moment by moment.
- What it is: A gallery version of Khut's heart-rate-controlled tablet work: visitors hold a sensor and watch forms bloom as their heart rate changes, beside video portraits of earlier participants recalling what they thought and felt.
- How it works: Heart-rate sensing on iPads drives generative visuals and sound; shown with three vertical video portraits of participants' first-person accounts.
- Lens: Attunement & appreciation, First-person & felt experience
- Shown at: 2012 National New Media Art Award (winner), Queensland Art Gallery | Gallery of Modern Art
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=I_dvtZzqK90
- Images: https://static1.squarespace.com/static/6572a8653a6cba0345e9e3cb/6572cc375a2d8a284b57b09d/65a31ea6a57eae28c787c6b7/1705194648691/Distillery-Estee-Wah-photo-by-Julia-Charles.jpg?format=1500w
- Project page: https://www.georgekhut.com/media-art/distillery-waveforming

#### BrightHearts — George Poonkhin Khut (2011)
- Type: Product & app · Body & senses: Heartbeat, Breath, Light & vision
- Idea: Relaxation can be learned as a felt skill: a gentle, playful mirror of the heart gives a child a way to act on fear from inside the body.
- What it is: An iPad app in which glowing, overlapping circles change colour and size as a child's heart rate slows, used to help children relax during painful and recurring hospital procedures.
- How it works: A pulse sensor streams heart rate to the app, which maps slowing heart rate to visual and sound changes; developed with Dr Angela Morrow at The Children's Hospital at Westmead and evaluated in clinical pilot studies.
- Lens: Care & healing, Attunement & appreciation
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1111/papr.12655 (Pain Practice 2018)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VfUeXEZ8dYE
- Images: https://static1.squarespace.com/static/6572a8653a6cba0345e9e3cb/6572cc375a2d8a284b57b09d/65a28ca6f4576c6533190d94/1755912521535/brighthearts-heart-rate-biofeedback-app-ipad-red-2048.jpg?format=1500w
- Project page: https://www.georgekhut.com/media-art/brighthearts-app

#### Just a Heartbeat Away from One's Body — Manos Tsakiris, Ana Tajadura-Jiménez (2011)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Heartbeat, Touch & pressure
- Idea: Interoception anchors the sense of owning one's body: the better we feel from inside, the less our body-image is pulled around by what we see.
- What it is: People counted their own heartbeats and then took part in the rubber hand illusion; those who sensed their heartbeat less accurately more readily felt a rubber hand as their own.
- How it works: Heartbeat perception task to measure interoceptive sensitivity, followed by the rubber hand illusion with proprioceptive drift and questionnaires (Proceedings of the Royal Society B, 2011).
- Lens: Body-mind unity, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1098/rspb.2010.2547 (Proceedings of the Royal Society B 2011)

#### Les Archives du Cœur — Christian Boltanski (2008)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Heartbeat, Voice, sound & listening, Light & vision
- Idea: The sound of a heartbeat is at once anonymous and unmistakably a living person; hearing it in the dark makes one's own life felt as fragile and shared.
- What it is: An archive of recorded human heartbeats, collected from people around the world since 2008 and housed on Teshima island, Japan, where visitors listen to strangers' hearts, record their own, and stand in a dark room where a bulb pulses with a heartbeat.
- How it works: Heartbeats are recorded in a recording room (likely with a stethoscope-style microphone), stored in a small permanent museum for listening, and visitors take their own recording home on CD; a heart room links a bulb to amplified heartbeat sound.
- Lens: Body data & ambiguity, Intercorporeality
- Shown at: Setouchi International Art Festival, Teshima (2010)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5-8OtBaeYTA
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/c/c4/Les_Archives_du_C%C5%93ur.jpg https://benesse-artsite.jp/en/art/boltanski_kv_thumb_01_pc.jpg
- Project page: https://benesse-artsite.jp/en/art/boltanski.html

#### Cardiomorphologies — George Poonkhin Khut (2004)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Heartbeat, Breath, Light & vision
- Idea: Seeing and hearing one's heart and breath in slow, simple forms turns attention inward; participants learn by doing how thought, feeling and physiology change together.
- What it is: A seated participant watches a floor-to-ceiling, mandala-like projection of concentric circles and hears a soundscape that expand and contract with their own breath and heart rate over a 20–30 minute session.
- How it works: Heart rate and chest or abdominal breathing sensors drive real-time video and sound (with Lizzie Muller, Greg Turner and John Tonkin); experience was studied through post-session interviews.
- Lens: Attunement & appreciation, First-person & felt experience, Body-mind unity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1109/mmul.2007.70 (IEEE MultiMedia 2007)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=efiDTIpiHR4
- Images: https://static1.squarespace.com/static/6572a8653a6cba0345e9e3cb/6572cc375a2d8a284b57b09d/65a25f3d60057e7829a8216b/1705151654572/cm2_Website-1038-x-576.jpg?format=1500w
- Project page: https://www.georgekhut.com/media-art/cardiomorphologies

#### How Do You Feel? Interoception — A. D. (Bud) Craig (2002)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Pain & inner sensations, Heartbeat, Heat & temperature
- Idea: Pain, temperature, itch, hunger and heartbeat belong to one sense of the body's inner state, and that sense is the stuff that subjective feeling is made of.
- What it is: A review that redefines interoception as the sense of the physiological condition of the entire body, carried by a dedicated pathway to the insular cortex, and links it to feelings and emotional awareness.
- How it works: Review of neuroanatomical and functional imaging evidence on the lamina I spinothalamocortical pathway and the insula (Nature Reviews Neuroscience, 2002).
- Lens: Body-mind unity, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1038/nrn894 (Nature Reviews Neuroscience 2002)

### Body Data, Reflection & Ambiguity

Self-tracking reconsidered: open interpretation, ambiguity, reflection rather than measurement.

#### Diffraction-in-action: Lived Data — Pedro Sanches, Noura Howell, Vasiliki Tsaknaki (2022)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Heartbeat, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: Data about a body is lived: it changes what it measures, and designers learn most by staying with its messiness over time.
- What it is: Five biodata case studies, in which design researchers lived with their own data in slow, open-ended ways, show how the data became entangled with their everyday lives rather than standing apart as objective measurement.
- How it works: Five first-person biodata case studies analysed through Karen Barad's agential realism and diffraction.
- Lens: Body data & ambiguity, First-person & felt experience, More-than-human
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3491102.3502029 (CHI 2022)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9z79h46zTKA

#### Fabulating Biodata Futures — Vasiliki Tsaknaki, Pedro Sanches, Noura Howell (2022)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Breath, Heartbeat
- Idea: Biodata need not be individual and diagnostic; it can be a medium for living and knowing together with other bodies.
- What it is: The paper synthesises design research on biodata and offers three fabulations, Weaving Alongside, Diffracting Selves and Collective Affect, that imagine biodata shared among diverse human and non-human bodies.
- How it works: Thematic synthesis of design exemplars followed by speculative fabulation written by an international group of biodata design researchers.
- Lens: Body data & ambiguity, More-than-human, Intercorporeality
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3532106.3533477 (DIS 2022)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=NLcnlKI3vHM

#### Emotional Biosensing: Exploring Critical Alternatives — Noura Howell (2018)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Skin conductance & arousal, Heartbeat
- Idea: Emotion is not a hidden state that sensors reveal; designs that return interpretation to the feeling person resist the reduction of the body-mind to a category.
- What it is: A critical study of emotion-sensing products and research that reads their promises against their assumptions, and sketches alternatives in which biosensing supports people's own emotional meaning-making.
- How it works: Critical analysis of emotional biosensing technologies, drawing on design research and critical theory (CSCW / PACM HCI 2018).
- Lens: Body data & ambiguity, Body politics
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3274338 (CSCW 2018 (PACM HCI))
- Images: https://nourahowell.com/static/img/largest/spire_feel_oura.jpg
- Project page: https://nourahowell.com/projects/emotional_biosensing.html

#### Ripple — Noura Howell (2018)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Skin conductance & arousal, Heat & temperature
- Idea: Emotional biosensing raises the question of who has interpretive authority over a feeling, the person or the data; a subtle garment display keeps that authority with the wearer's own felt sense.
- What it is: Everyday shirts with thermochromic pinstripes embroidered on the sleeve that shift colour at moments of heightened skin conductance; friends, housemates and couples wore them in daily life.
- How it works: A Bitalino skin conductance sensor feeds an Arduino that heats embroidered conductive thread coated with thermochromic pigment when a spike is detected; a field study with pairs of wearers (CHI 2018).
- Lens: Body data & ambiguity, First-person & felt experience, Body politics
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3173574.3174005 (CHI 2018)
- Video: https://vimeo.com/363031801
- Images: https://nourahowell.com/static/img/1000px_wide/ripple_shirts.jpg https://nourahowell.com/static/img/1000px_wide/inlay_embroidery.jpg
- Project page: https://nourahowell.com/projects/ripple.html

#### A Quantified Past — Chris Elsden (2016)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: Body data gains meaning when it is tied back to lived experience and memory; design should support reminiscence and ambiguity, not only goals.
- What it is: A study of how self-trackers look back on their old personal data, arguing that tracking is also a way of remembering one's life, not only of monitoring performance.
- How it works: Qualitative interviews with Quantified Self practitioners about revisiting their data, leading to design considerations for remembering (Human-Computer Interaction journal; with David Kirk and Abigail Durrant).
- Lens: Body data & ambiguity, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1080/07370024.2015.1093422 (Human-Computer Interaction 2016)

#### Hint — Noura Howell (2016)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Skin conductance & arousal, Heat & temperature
- Idea: A body signal worn in public becomes a social cue whose meaning is made together; keeping it ambiguous lets people treat arousal as joy, embarrassment or nothing, instead of a verdict on what they feel.
- What it is: A T-shirt whose screen-printed thermochromic pattern slowly changes colour when the wearer's skin conductance rises; pairs of friends wore it while talking and tried to read what the change meant.
- How it works: A skin conductance sensor and microcontroller detect sudden rises and heat conductive thread under thermochromic ink; studied with pairs of friends in conversation (DIS 2016).
- Lens: Body data & ambiguity, Affective loops, Intercorporeality
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2901790.2901850 (DIS 2016)
- Images: https://nourahowell.com/static/img/1000px_wide/hint_shirt.jpg https://biosense.berkeley.edu/files/2018/03/3Hint.jpg
- Project page: https://biosense.berkeley.edu/portfolio/biosignals-as-social-cues-designing-color-changing-clothing-displays/

#### Affective Diary — Anna Ståhl, Kristina Höök, Jarmo Laaksolahti (2006)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Skin conductance & arousal, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: Body data works best as an open mirror: vague shapes invite people to remember how the day felt instead of telling them what they felt.
- What it is: The Affective Diary pairs a biosensor armband with a tablet diary: in the evening, the day's arousal and movement appear as ambiguous, colourful body figures next to the user's text messages and photos, and the user can scribble on them.
- How it works: A SenseWear armband logs galvanic skin response and step counts; a Tablet PC renders them as abstract, posture-like figures on a timeline with mobile phone data; studied with users over several weeks (with Microsoft Research Cambridge).
- Lens: Body data & ambiguity, Affective loops, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1007/s00779-008-0202-7 (Personal and Ubiquitous Computing 2008)
- Images: https://figures.semanticscholar.org/55385d09e20b61373302e23cc8971577caa57d79/6-Figure1-1.png https://figures.semanticscholar.org/55385d09e20b61373302e23cc8971577caa57d79/9-Figure4-1.png

#### Staying Open to Interpretation — Phoebe Sengers (2006)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Whole body-mind
- Idea: Leaving body data ambiguous is a design resource: it invites people to bring their own felt sense and context to what a signal means.
- What it is: An argument that interactive systems, and their evaluations, should support many valid interpretations by users rather than one meaning fixed by the designer.
- How it works: Conceptual analysis with examples from the authors' own design projects and evaluation practices (DIS 2006; with Bill Gaver).
- Lens: Body data & ambiguity, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/1142405.1142422 (DIS 2006)

#### Bio Mapping — Christian Nold (2004)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Skin conductance & arousal, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: A skin conductance peak says little on its own; its meaning comes from the walker's memory and from collective discussion, making body data a starting point for shared stories.
- What it is: Participants walk their neighbourhood wearing a skin conductance sensor linked to GPS; afterwards they annotate the peaks of arousal on a map together, producing community 'emotion maps' of places such as Greenwich and San Francisco.
- How it works: A galvanic skin response logger and a GPS unit record arousal and position; mapping software visualises tracks, and group workshops add annotations (published as Emotional Cartography, 2009).
- Lens: Body data & ambiguity, Intercorporeality, Body politics
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=2mVYKVrIXs4
- Images: http://www.softhook.com/biomap.jpg
- Project page: http://biomapping.net/

## Movement, Dance & Somatic Art

The moving body in dance, performance, sport, play and art, and the computing built around it.

### Dance & Technology

Choreography, sensing and projection with and for dancers.

#### AISOMA / Living Archive — Wayne McGregor (2018)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: An archive of a choreographer's bodily style can become a partner that dancers answer with their own bodies.
- What it is: A machine-learning tool trained on 25 years of McGregor's filmed choreography that proposes new movement phrases in response to a dancer's pose.
- How it works: Pose estimation over archival video trains a sequence model that generates continuations; developed with Google Arts & Culture Lab.
- Lens: Movement & expression, More-than-human
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qshkvUOc35A
- Images: https://waynemcgregor.com/assets/4Research/Living-Archive/_1455x825_crop_center-center_75/GoogleLivingArchive_carousel_GAC_2.jpg
- Project page: https://waynemcgregor.com/research/aisoma

#### Radical Choreographic Object [RCO] — Sarah Fdili Alaoui (2017)
- Type: Performance, dance & music · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Whole body-mind
- Idea: A choreographic object can be shared: the audience learns by moving, not by watching.
- What it is: A participatory dance performance by Jean-Marc Matos and Sarah Fdili Alaoui in which audience members join dancers in scored, interactive movement.
- How it works: Performance with movement scores and interactive media, staged in community venues in the Toulouse area (2017–2018).
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Movement & expression, Body politics
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3170427.3181403 (CHI 2018 Extended Abstracts (Art))
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XkPGWT4btw4
- Images: https://saralaoui.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/02/RCO-50.jpg
- Project page: https://saralaoui.com/2017/02/rco/

#### MotionComposer — Palindrome (Robert Wechsler) (2016)
- Type: Product & app · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Voice, sound & listening
- Idea: When any movement can make music, people with limited mobility can feel their own movement as expressive.
- What it is: A camera device that turns any movement, from a wave of the arm to a blink, into music, designed so that people with very different abilities can dance and compose together.
- How it works: Stereo/depth and video tracking with sound environments tuned for small and large movements, developed with therapists and disabled users.
- Lens: Care & healing, Movement & expression, Pleasure & play
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4aDj7Ma_HkE
- Images: https://motioncomposer.de/wp-content/uploads/2023/06/Bild1_MotionComposer-in-Action.jpg
- Project page: https://motioncomposer.de/

#### SKIN — Sarah Fdili Alaoui (2016)
- Type: Performance, dance & music · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Muscle tension (EMG), Voice, sound & listening
- Idea: Integrating technology into dance creates tensions; experience interviews show what 'inner movement' made audible means to dancers and audience.
- What it is: An interactive dance piece in which sensors map the dancers' inner movement and bodily sensations to sound and video on stage; its making was studied through interviews with the team and audience.
- How it works: Research-through-practice: iterative cycles of choreography and interaction design with wearable sensing, then micro-phenomenological (explicitation) interviews.
- Lens: First-person & felt experience, Movement & expression, Body-mind unity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3322276.3322289 (DIS 2019)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hdin4bVr5tQ
- Project page: https://saralaoui.com/2016/02/skin/

#### Huang Yi & KUKA — Huang Yi (2015)
- Type: Performance, dance & music · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: Partnering a machine depends on timing and attention more than on sensing: the dancer's own attunement makes the robot feel like a partner.
- What it is: Huang Yi dances a duet with an industrial KUKA robot arm that he programmed himself, mirroring and answering its movements; a light on the arm's tip draws lines across his body.
- How it works: Choreography keyframed by hand, pose by pose, into the robot's controller over many hours, then performed live with dancers.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Movement & expression, More-than-human
- Shown at: TED 2017
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Xy6tPmFlN34
- Images: https://huangyistudio.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/10/huangyiandkuka_TED2017_01.jpg
- Project page: https://www.huangyistudio.com

#### Synchronous Objects for One Flat Thing, reproduced — William Forsythe (2009)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: Choreography is a system of attention between dancers; data can show how bodies cue and align with each other.
- What it is: An online set of visualisations that reveal the hidden counterpoint in Forsythe's dance for 17 dancers and 20 tables: cues, alignments and timing between bodies.
- How it works: Annotation of multi-camera video of the dance, turned into animations and interactive objects by The Forsythe Company and Ohio State University (Palazzi, Zuniga Shaw).
- Lens: Movement & expression, Intercorporeality
- Shown at: SIGGRAPH 2009 Art Gallery
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/1667265.1667306 (SIGGRAPH 2009 Art Gallery)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=v2REtWg6-bo
- Images: https://synchronousobjects.osu.edu/assets/img/portfolio/SO-1.jpg
- Project page: https://synchronousobjects.osu.edu/

#### Choreographic Language Agent — Wayne McGregor (2008)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Choreography is thinking with the body; an agent can provoke that thinking without replacing it.
- What it is: Software that generates abstract, moving visual prompts for dancers to translate into movement; its interactive form 'Becoming' was used to create the piece Atomos (2013).
- How it works: Rule-based and generative graphics developed with cognitive scientists and digital artists, projected in the studio as a creative partner during rehearsal.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Movement & expression
- Shown at: Thinking with the Body, Wellcome Collection 2013–14
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=yAgDYA7tJOo
- Images: https://waynemcgregor.com/assets/2Work/Atomos/_1455x825_crop_center-center_75/atomoscreation_carousel_ravideepres_CLA2_230118_161057.jpg
- Project page: https://waynemcgregor.com/research/choreographic-language-agent

#### Glow — Chunky Move (Gideon Obarzanek with Frieder Weiss) (2006)
- Type: Performance, dance & music · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Light & vision
- Idea: Projection that answers the body in real time can make the dancer's own kinesphere visible and strange.
- What it is: A 27-minute duet between a dancer on the floor and projected light that tracks, outlines and extends her body as it moves.
- How it works: Infrared camera tracking from above drives real-time generative graphics projected onto the floor (software by Frieder Weiss).
- Lens: Movement & expression, More-than-human
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=C4He543_a80
- Images: https://chunkymove.com/site/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/ChunkyMovesGLOW.PhotoRomAnthoni_PerformerKristyAyre_300dpi_2.webp
- Project page: https://chunkymove.com/works/glow/

#### Touching — Palindrome (Robert Wechsler) (2004)
- Type: Performance, dance & music · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Voice, sound & listening, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: Contact between bodies, not individual gestures, becomes the instrument.
- What it is: Two dancers wrestle, lean and press their hands together, and every moment of skin-to-skin contact becomes audible: the harder or longer they touch, the more the sound changes.
- How it works: A body-contact sensor worn by both dancers measures when and how firmly they touch and drives sound in real time (Palindrome's EyeCon-era systems, likely with Frieder Weiss).
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Movement & expression
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=FfjU-lbHdvo

#### Improvisation Technologies — William Forsythe (1999)
- Type: Method & toolkit · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Dancers think with imagined geometry inside and around the body; making that inner imagery visible makes it teachable.
- What it is: A video lecture series in which Forsythe demonstrates his improvisation principles while graphic lines drawn over the video show the imagined points, lines and planes his body moves through.
- How it works: Digitised lecture-demonstrations with line annotations, published as a CD-ROM by ZKM Karlsruhe for training Ballett Frankfurt dancers.
- Lens: Movement & expression, Body-mind unity
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ogsdGjAtyDc
- Images: https://zkm.de/system/files/styles/default_metatag/private/field_media_image/2023/03/01/121656/0103_1999_William-Forsythe-Improvisation-Technologies.jpg?itok=0MhVgG1P
- Project page: https://zkm.de/en/publication/william-forsythe-improvisation-technologies

#### In Plane (MidiDancer) — Mark Coniglio / Troika Ranch (with Dawn Stoppiello) (1994)
- Type: Performance, dance & music · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Posture, balance & proprioception
- Idea: Sensing from inside the joints ties media to felt bending rather than to how the body looks from outside.
- What it is: A solo in which dancer Dawn Stoppiello wears the MidiDancer suit: the bending of her own elbows, knees and hips controls the video image and music around her.
- How it works: Flex sensors on the joints transmit wirelessly to a computer that maps joint angles to MIDI-controlled video and sound.
- Lens: Movement & expression
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_MyT3E44KC4
- Project page: https://troikaranch.org/

#### Life Forms — Tom Calvert, Thecla Schiphorst, Merce Cunningham (1989)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Designing for choreographers meant building around how they think movement in the body, with dancers' bodies still testing every idea.
- What it is: A figure-animation program in which choreographers sketch and sequence body shapes on screen; Merce Cunningham used it from 1989 to compose dances such as Trackers (1991).
- How it works: Keyframe animation of articulated human figures with a stance/body editor, timeline and spatial stage view, developed iteratively with choreographers at Simon Fraser University.
- Lens: Movement & expression
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/169059.169113 (CHI 1993)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aKbWX-9HKeo

### Movement & Computing

Modelling movement quality: Laban effort, MOCO, interactive machine learning for movement.

#### Motion-Sound Mapping through Interaction — Jules Françoise, Frédéric Bevilacqua (2018)
- Type: Method & toolkit · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Voice, sound & listening
- Idea: Mapping by demonstration lets people design body-sound feedback from their own movement sense instead of programming it.
- What it is: People demonstrate a movement together with a sound they imagine for it; the system learns the relation so they can then play and feel their movement as sound.
- How it works: Interactive machine learning (Gaussian mixture and hidden Markov regression) trained on a few demonstrations of paired motion and sound.
- Lens: Movement & expression, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3211826 (ACM Transactions on Interactive Intelligent Systems)

#### Seeing, Sensing and Recognizing Laban Movement Qualities — Sarah Fdili Alaoui, Jules Françoise, Thecla Schiphorst (2017)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Muscle tension (EMG), Whole body-mind
- Idea: Movement experts' embodied knowledge should shape what the machine measures; adding physiological signals captures qualities that position alone misses.
- What it is: Certified Laban Movement Analysts helped choose sensors and features so that a computer could recognise Effort qualities such as lightness, strength and sustainment.
- How it works: Multimodal capture (motion capture, accelerometers, EMG) with features designed with LMA experts, evaluated against expert annotations of Laban Efforts.
- Lens: Movement & expression, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3025453.3025530 (CHI 2017)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cafivJpiYUE
- Images: https://saralaoui.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/03/dancer_sensors.jpg
- Project page: https://saralaoui.com/2015/03/effortmodeling/

#### Choreographic Inscriptions — Lian Loke (2016)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: Technology quietly scripts how we move; naming those scripts in movement-quality terms lets designers choose them.
- What it is: A framework for noticing how devices and social norms 'choreograph' everyday movement, described in Laban Effort and Shape terms.
- How it works: Combines actor-network inscriptions with Laban Movement Analysis, demonstrated on a case study of an affective gesture-based mobile device.
- Lens: Movement & expression, Body politics
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.17011/ht/urn.201605192619 (Human Technology)

#### DANCE: Sonifying Movement Qualities — Antonio Camurri (2016)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Voice, sound & listening
- Idea: Movement qualities can travel between senses: a felt quality in one body can be heard by another.
- What it is: Qualities such as lightness, heaviness and fragility in a dancer's movement are analysed live and turned into sound, with the aim that blind and sighted people can 'see' dance through listening.
- How it works: EyesWeb-based real-time analysis of motion-capture and inertial data into a multi-layered model of qualities, sonified with designed sound (EU H2020 DANCE project, with choreographer Virgilio Sieni).
- Lens: Movement & expression, Attunement & appreciation
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2948910.2948927 (MOCO 2016)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5HGSuuzbgAM
- Images: http://dance.dibris.unige.it/user_files/images/DANCE_Mocap-2.png
- Project page: http://dance.dibris.unige.it/

#### Vocalizing Dance Movement — Jules Françoise, Sarah Fdili Alaoui, Thecla Schiphorst, Frédéric Bevilacqua (2014)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Voice, sound & listening, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: The voice is a bodily bridge: sounds made by movement experts let others hear and learn a movement quality from inside.
- What it is: Dancers vocalize while moving; the system learns the link between their voice and Laban Effort qualities and then sonifies new movement so dancers can hear their own quality.
- How it works: Vocalizations of movement experts recorded with motion data train a probabilistic motion-sound mapping; explored in a dance pedagogy workshop.
- Lens: Movement & expression, Attunement & appreciation
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2598510.2598582 (DIS 2014)

#### A Light Touch (Movement Qualities as Interaction Modality) — Sarah Fdili Alaoui, Frédéric Bevilacqua (2012)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Light & vision
- Idea: Using movement quality, rather than trajectory, as input invites exploratory and expressive movement.
- What it is: An installation where a spot of light moves with the quality of a visitor's hand movement, how it moves, not where it goes.
- How it works: Hand motion is tracked and classified into dance-derived movement qualities that drive the behaviour of a projected light spot; evaluated in a user experiment.
- Lens: Movement & expression, Attunement & appreciation
- Shown at: DIS 2012 Art Exhibition, Newcastle
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2317956.2318071 (DIS 2012)
- Images: https://saralaoui.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/03/installation.jpg
- Project page: https://saralaoui.com/2015/03/a-light-touch/

#### MO – Modular Musical Objects — Nicolas Rasamimanana, Frédéric Bevilacqua (2011)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Touch & pressure, Voice, sound & listening
- Idea: Instruments can be 'finished' by the player: the meaning of a gesture comes from how the body uses the object.
- What it is: Small wireless motion-sensing modules that can be attached to balls, hands or everyday objects, turning shaking, tapping and throwing into music.
- How it works: Wireless inertial sensor modules with accessories, mapped to sound through gesture following and recognition software from IRCAM's Interlude project.
- Lens: Movement & expression, Pleasure & play
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/1935701.1935704 (TEI 2011)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Uhps_U2E9OM

#### Gesture Follower — Frédéric Bevilacqua (2010)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Voice, sound & listening
- Idea: Following the continuous time of a gesture, rather than only recognising it at the end, keeps the performer's timing and feel in the sound.
- What it is: A system that learns a gesture from one example and then follows it in real time, so a sound can be stretched or compressed to the pace at which the gesture is performed.
- How it works: Hidden-Markov-model-based real-time alignment of multidimensional sensor data to recorded templates, used with dancers and musicians at IRCAM.
- Lens: Movement & expression
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-642-12553-9_7 (Gesture in Embodied Communication and Human-Computer Interaction (LNCS))
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=2dSU8MeJlz4
- Project page: https://ismm.ircam.fr/

#### Understanding Movement for Interaction Design — Lian Loke, Toni Robertson (2007)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Each framework makes a different aspect of the moving body visible; designers need dance-derived vocabularies to see movement quality.
- What it is: Compares several frameworks for describing movement, from HCI models to Labanotation, by applying them to players of a camera-based body game.
- How it works: Analytic comparison of frameworks on video of EyeToy game play, with Laban-based notation of players' movements.
- Lens: Movement & expression, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1007/s00779-006-0132-1 (Personal and Ubiquitous Computing)

#### EyesWeb — Antonio Camurri (2000)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Expressive, emotional qualities of movement can be modelled directly, not only positions and gestures.
- What it is: An open software platform that extracts expressive cues from video of dancers (contraction, fluidity, impulsiveness) and maps them to music and media in real time.
- How it works: Visual-programming environment with computer-vision modules for full-body motion features (quantity of motion, contraction index), developed at InfoMus Lab, Genoa.
- Lens: Movement & expression, Affective loops
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1162/014892600559182 (Computer Music Journal)
- Project page: http://www.casapaganini.org/

### Exertion, Sport & Somatic Play

Exertion games, body-centric play, physical training and sport.

#### The Body as Its Own Best Sensor — Jakob Tholander (2025)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Breath, Heartbeat
- Idea: A trained body senses more finely than the watch; sports technology should support these somatic sensitivities rather than replace them.
- What it is: Through an autobiographical study of everyday long-distance running with a basic sports watch, Tholander describes how runners sense pace, effort and terrain with their whole body.
- How it works: Autoethnography of running practice and data use, distilled into six themes for sports technology design.
- Lens: First-person & felt experience, Body data & ambiguity, Attunement & appreciation
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3706598.3713607 (CHI 2025)

#### Boards Hit Back — Yoav Luft, Pavel Karpashevich, Kristina Höök (2023)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Touch & pressure, Posture, balance & proprioception
- Idea: Estrangement in soma design lets practitioners break habits and cultivate new ones, fused with thought and feeling.
- What it is: An interactive wooden training dummy for martial arts was designed with and by a martial arts expert, and the process changed both the expert's practice and the expert.
- How it works: Soma design process around a sensor-equipped wooden dummy, reported through a phenomenological first-person account of the practitioner.
- Lens: Estrangement, Movement & expression, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3544548.3580722 (CHI 2023)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=lTISUBb1gyc

#### Physical Warm-up Games — Elena Márquez Segura, Laia Turmo Vidal (2021)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Warm-ups tune body and mind together; light, open-ended technology can support that tuning without taking over.
- What it is: Studies warm-up games in physical education and circus training, and adds open-ended wearables to them, to show how warm-ups prepare people physically, socially and mentally.
- How it works: Collection of warm-up games from teachers online plus design interventions with wearable light/sound artefacts in a circus training course.
- Lens: Pleasure & play, Attunement & appreciation, Intercorporeality
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3411764.3445163 (CHI 2021)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xD9P-kBwDRk

#### Ari, the eBike — Josh Andres, Florian 'Floyd' Mueller (2019)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Muscle tension (EMG)
- Idea: When a machine acts on your effort, the felt question is how to share control of your own body's work.
- What it is: An electric bike that knows the traffic-light timing ahead and adds or holds back motor power so rider and bike reach the green light together.
- How it works: An eBike augmented with traffic-light data and motor control, plus light cues, studied with riders on city routes.
- Lens: More-than-human, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3322276.3322307 (DIS 2019)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7GCnU0QjGl8
- Images: http://exertiongameslab.org/wp-content/uploads/2018/06/ava.jpg
- Project page: https://exertiongameslab.org/projects/ari-the-green-light-ebike

#### Experiencing the Body as Play — Florian 'Floyd' Mueller, Richard Byrne, Josh Andres (2018)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Movement & kinesthesia, Posture, balance & proprioception
- Idea: Designing for the Leib (the lived body) as well as the Körper (the object body) turns play into a way of feeling oneself.
- What it is: Argues that games are moving from using the body as a controller to letting players experience their own body as the play itself, and offers design tactics for it.
- How it works: Two phenomenological perspectives (Körper and Leib) applied to the lab's and others' bodily games to derive a suite of design tactics.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Pleasure & play, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3173574.3173784 (CHI 2018)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5DK7pBNJEAE

#### The Guts Game — Zhuying Li (2018)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Pain & inner sensations, Heat & temperature, Whole body-mind
- Idea: The inside of the body can be played with: making core temperature a game goal turns hidden physiology into felt, shared awareness.
- What it is: Two players each swallow a temperature-sensing capsule and compete over a day or more to raise or lower their core body temperature on command; the game ends when the capsule leaves one player's body.
- How it works: Ingestible core-temperature sensor pill transmitting to a phone app with tasks; interview study with 14 players yielding themes of bodily awareness, integration, agency and discomfort (Exertion Games Lab).
- Lens: Pleasure & play, First-person & felt experience, Body data & ambiguity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3242671.3242681 (CHI PLAY 2018)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=TLtPxjy3jNQ
- Images: https://i0.wp.com/exertiongameslab.org/wp-content/uploads/2018/06/guts_game-300x244.jpg
- Project page: https://exertiongameslab.org/projects/the-guts-game

#### Designing for Bodily Interplay in Social Exertion Games — Florian 'Floyd' Mueller (2017)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Touch & pressure, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Social exertion is felt between bodies; designers can tune how much bodies meet in physical and virtual space.
- What it is: A design dimension for how players' bodies act on one another in exertion games, from parallel play side by side to interdependent pushing, blocking and cooperating.
- How it works: Conceptual framework (parallel vs. interdependent play, across physical and virtual space) derived from the lab's exertion game designs and studies.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Pleasure & play
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3064938 (ACM TOCHI)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3-BoqB6eskM

#### Balance Ninja — Richard Byrne, Florian 'Floyd' Mueller (2016)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Posture, balance & proprioception, Pain & inner sensations
- Idea: Vertigo makes the usually invisible vestibular sense felt, and can be shaped as a playful, shared experience.
- What it is: Two players balance on a line while electrodes behind their ears disturb their sense of balance; each player's movements control the vertigo felt by the opponent.
- How it works: Galvanic vestibular stimulation whose strength is likely driven by the other player's tracked movement; studied with 20 players to derive vertigo-game design strategies.
- Lens: Estrangement, Pleasure & play, Intercorporeality
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2967934.2968080 (CHI PLAY 2016)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MfHaKFiAyOQ
- Images: http://exertiongameslab.org/wp-content/uploads/2018/06/balance_ninja-300x169.jpg
- Project page: https://exertiongameslab.org/projects/balance-ninja

#### Movement-Based Game Guidelines — Florian 'Floyd' Mueller, Katherine Isbister (2014)
- Type: Method & toolkit · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Good movement games are designed for how movement feels and looks to others, not for sensing accuracy.
- What it is: Ten design guidelines for movement games, drawn from expert designers, such as 'celebrate movement articulation' and 'embrace ambiguity' in sensing.
- How it works: Guidelines derived from interviews and workshops with movement-game designers and prior work, organised by movement requirements, feedback and social aspects.
- Lens: Pleasure & play, Movement & expression
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2556288.2557163 (CHI 2014)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hQM3xg6IjD4
- Project page: https://exertiongameslab.org/projects/design-tool-movement-based-game-guidelines

#### Musical Embrace — Amy Huggard, Florian 'Floyd' Mueller (2013)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Social awkwardness and close bodily contact can be a resource for play, not something to avoid.
- What it is: Two strangers play a music game by hugging a large pillow-like controller between them and moving together to steer it.
