- How it works: A soft controller with embedded motion sensing, observed at public events to derive strategies for 'facilitating social awkwardness'.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Pleasure & play
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2493432.2493518 (UbiComp 2013)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7LmUyIqdpRo
- Images: http://exertiongameslab.org/wp-content/uploads/2012/06/musical_embrace3.jpg
- Project page: https://exertiongameslab.org/projects/musical-embrace

#### The Design Space of Body Games — Elena Márquez Segura, Jin Moen (2013)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Whole body-mind
- Idea: When sensing is limited, the room, the rules and the other players carry the felt experience.
- What it is: Defines body games as games whose main pleasure is bodily engagement, and shows how the physical and social setting can matter as much as the technology.
- How it works: Literature study plus design experiments with children playing multiplayer games on the BodyBug wearable.
- Lens: Pleasure & play, Intercorporeality
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2470654.2466461 (CHI 2013)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=zDlI2ZiUXF0

#### Hanging off a Bar — Florian 'Floyd' Mueller (2012)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Muscle tension (EMG), Posture, balance & proprioception
- Idea: Exertion can be felt without movement: sustained effort and muscle burn are the play.
- What it is: Players hang from an exercise bar above a projected river for as long as they can, like an action hero; the game is almost all effort and almost no movement.
- How it works: An exercise bar with sensing of whether the player is still hanging, with projected graphics and sound; observed at three public events.
- Lens: Pleasure & play, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2212776.2212384 (CHI 2012 Extended Abstracts)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=TLDEr4XO4wg
- Project page: https://exertiongameslab.org/projects/hanging-off-a-bar

#### Johann Sebastian Joust — Die Gute Fabrik (2011)
- Type: Product & app · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Voice, sound & listening, Posture, balance & proprioception
- Idea: Removing the screen turns attention back to bodies, balance and each other.
- What it is: A screenless game for motion controllers: players must keep their controller still while trying to jostle others'; when Bach's music slows, the controllers become more sensitive.
- How it works: PlayStation Move controllers measure acceleration against a threshold that changes with the tempo of the Brandenburg Concertos.
- Lens: Pleasure & play, Attunement & appreciation
- Shown at: IndieCade 2011 Official Selection
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=c2Ei9fgFAOs
- Images: http://jsjoust.com/images/jsjoust-photo04.jpg
- Project page: https://www.gutefabrik.com/joust.html

#### BodyBug — Jin Moen (2007)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Movement interaction should start from people's own movement experience, drawing on modern dance, rather than from sensor capabilities.
- What it is: A small creature-like device that slides along a strap worn on the body and reacts to the wearer's movement, inviting full-body, dance-like play.
- How it works: Accelerometer-equipped device that climbs a wire strapped to the body, designed from movement workshops; later sold as Oriboo.
- Lens: Movement & expression, Pleasure & play
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/1226969.1227021 (TEI 2007)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=J5PVmKfiaCY

#### Jogging over a Distance — Florian 'Floyd' Mueller (2007)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Heartbeat, Movement & kinesthesia, Voice, sound & listening
- Idea: Mapping effort, not speed, lets people of different fitness run 'together' and feel each other's exertion.
- What it is: Two runners in different places talk through headsets while jogging; if one runs harder than usual relative to their heart rate, their voice seems to come from ahead, otherwise from behind.
- How it works: Heart-rate monitors and mobile phones compare each jogger's heart rate to a personal target and place the partner's voice in spatial audio accordingly.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Pleasure & play, Body data & ambiguity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/1866029.1866062 (UIST 2010)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=NWJjIbWmaN4

#### hipDisk — Danielle Wilde (2007)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Voice, sound & listening, Posture, balance & proprioception
- Idea: An awkward, funny wearable can extend how people move and make the torso's range of motion newly felt.
- What it is: Two stiff disks worn around the hips and chest sound a note when the wearer bends their torso to bring the disks' edges together, so the body becomes a playable instrument.
- How it works: Contact points around the edges of the two disks trigger tones, so each torso tilt plays a pitch; explored in performance and workshops.
- Lens: Estrangement, Pleasure & play, Movement & expression
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1386/padm.4.1.7_1 (International Journal of Performance Arts and Digital Media)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Htsy_ldbOag

#### Kick Ass Kung-Fu — Perttu Hämäläinen (2004)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Whole body-mind
- Idea: A full-body mirror with gentle 'cheats' like slow motion lets people feel like skilled martial artists and train real moves.
- What it is: Players fight virtual opponents with real kicks and punches, seeing their own video image on a large screen with slowed-down, exaggerated jumps.
- How it works: Camera-based silhouette tracking composited into a game world projected on two screens, with time-manipulation of jumps; tested with martial-arts practitioners.
- Lens: Pleasure & play, Movement & expression
- Shown at: Pixelache 2005, Kiasma Theatre, Helsinki
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/1054972.1055081 (CHI 2005)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=lhdJM9pIsvQ

#### Breakout for Two (Exertion Interfaces) — Florian 'Floyd' Mueller (2003)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Physical effort itself creates social bonding; 'exertion interfaces' made tiring the body a design goal.
- What it is: Two players in different cities kick a real ball against a wall that shows the other player life-size; hitting projected blocks scores points, like tennis across a video net.
- How it works: Life-size videoconference projected on a wall, with sensors detecting where a real soccer ball hits; compared with a keyboard version in a study.
- Lens: Pleasure & play, Intercorporeality
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/642611.642709 (CHI 2003)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Z8cASgpFQgM

### Performance, Voice & Opera

Somatic performance, the singing body, opera with machines.

#### Wings Against the Veil of Light — Jiabao Li, Rosemary Candelario (2024)
- Type: Performance, dance & music · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Voice, sound & listening
- Idea: Butoh's practice of 'emptying the human shell' becomes a way to feel another species' body from inside.
- What it is: Butoh dancers wearing wings made of zippers and rulers chirp like crickets by rubbing them together, enacting how light pollution makes male crickets call at the wrong time.
- How it works: Site-specific performance at The Contemporary Austin, choreographed by Rosemary Candelario from a cricket butoh score adapted from Hijikata's bug notation.
- Lens: More-than-human, Movement & expression, Estrangement
- Shown at: The Contemporary Austin, Laguna Gloria, 2024
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=V-doIkBdPkg
- Images: https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/58688c8a6a496327e937e35b/c3932ec7-6a6b-4671-96ce-bc5808b54847/Jiabao+Li_Cricket_Butoh+0.jpg https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/58688c8a6a496327e937e35b/1730851572775-050B68ZON2C30S1RQKM0/Jiabao+Li+cricket+2.jpg
- Project page: https://www.jiabaoli.org/wings-against-the-veil-of-light

#### Wire Costume (Reinterpreting Schlemmer's Triadic Ballet) — Pavel Karpashevich, Pedro Sanches (2018)
- Type: Performance, dance & music · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Posture, balance & proprioception, Light & vision
- Idea: Restricting and altering the senses creates a new soma to train into; the costume's character is only revealed once it is worn and danced.
- What it is: Inspired by Oskar Schlemmer's Bauhaus Triadic Ballet, the Wire Costume restricts the dancer's lower body and turns arm movements into LED 'sparks' and 'waves' on a tutu-like wire skirt.
- How it works: Motion sensing on the arms drives LED light sequences in a wire costume; designed with cultural research, expert interviews and a dancer's rehearsals (Bauhaus-Universität Weimar with KTH).
- Lens: Estrangement, Movement & expression
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3173574.3173635 (CHI 2018)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_32Tl6zLGTU

#### Corpus Nil — Marco Donnarumma (2016)
- Type: Performance, dance & music · Body & senses: Muscle tension (EMG), Movement & kinesthesia, Light & vision
- Idea: Human and machine form a hybrid body; bodily norms are unsettled when a machine co-shapes how a body moves and sounds.
- What it is: A performer moves slowly in near darkness while an autonomous system listens to his muscles' electrical and acoustic signals and answers with sound and light.
- How it works: Biophysical sensors (Xth Sense bioacoustic and bioelectric) on the limbs feed machine-learning algorithms that autonomously drive sound and light (premiered at ZKM, 2016).
- Lens: More-than-human, Body politics, First-person & felt experience
- Shown at: Prix Ars Electronica 2017, Award of Distinction (Sound Art); Bains Numériques 2018, 1st Prize Performing Arts
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CAIh8FxLMtk
- Images: https://marcodonnarumma.com/live/wp-content/uploads/2017/03/Marco-Donnarumma_Corpus-Nil_1_web_by-onuk.jpg
- Project page: https://marcodonnarumma.com/works/corpus-nil/

#### The Vocal Chorder — Carl Unander-Scharin, Åsa Unander-Scharin (2013)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Voice, sound & listening, Touch & pressure, Posture, balance & proprioception
- Idea: Designed from the singer's own bodily experience on stage, it lets the whole singing body, not only the voice, shape the music.
- What it is: A large instrument of steel wires that an opera singer pushes, leans on and bends while singing, to control their own accompaniment.
- How it works: Sensors measure the bending of tensioned steel wires and map it to tempo and accompaniment; developed through autobiographical design and stage material explorations, with Kristina Höök.
- Lens: First-person & felt experience, Movement & expression
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2556288.2557050 (CHI 2014)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oxUGb6rmFos
- Images: https://www.operamecatronica.com/wp-content/uploads/2019/10/scharin_MB-6778_3sp_hi.jpg
- Project page: https://www.operamecatronica.com/

#### 2 — Tao Ye (2011)
- Type: Performance, dance & music · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Continuous circular movement makes the whole body a single flowing line; stamina turns into a meditative, felt state.
- What it is: Two dancers in grey, hair loose, keep moving continuously for the whole piece, their bodies circling, folding and releasing without a pause or a narrative.
- How it works: Part of TAO Dance Theater's numbered series, built on Tao Ye's 'circular movement system' of releasing weight through the spine; premiered at the M.A.D.E festival in Umeå in 2011.
- Lens: Movement & expression, Attunement & appreciation, Body-mind unity
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=A3_EzVGfxvs
- Images: https://www.taodancetheater.com/Content/assets/images/home_performances_img.jpg
- Project page: https://www.taodancetheater.com

#### Xth Sense — Marco Donnarumma (2011)
- Type: Performance, dance & music · Body & senses: Muscle tension (EMG), Voice, sound & listening, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: Music can come from inside the body: the felt effort of muscles, not visible gesture, drives the sound.
- What it is: A wearable instrument that amplifies the sound of the performer's own muscle contractions and blood flow and shapes it into live music.
- How it works: Microphone sensors on the arms capture mechanomyographic (MMG) vibrations, which software analyses and processes into sound; free and open hardware (created 2010).
- Lens: First-person & felt experience, Body-mind unity, Movement & expression
- Shown at: 1st Prize, Guthman Musical Instrument Competition 2012
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=v4jz_7PDVfo
- Images: https://marcodonnarumma.com/live/wp-content/uploads/2012/08/marco-donnarumma_xthsense_speakers_1000px.jpg
- Project page: https://marcodonnarumma.com/works/xth-sense/

#### Robocygne — Åsa Unander-Scharin, Carl Unander-Scharin (2010)
- Type: Performance, dance & music · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: Moving a dancer's felt movement into a machine body makes both the robot and the ballet strange, and asks what expression survives the transfer.
- What it is: A small robot performs the dying swan from Swan Lake, its movements recorded from the choreographer's own body.
- How it works: Humanoid robot programmed from motion recordings of Åsa Unander-Scharin, built with Mälardalen University, to Tchaikovsky's music arranged by Carl Unander-Scharin.
- Lens: More-than-human, Movement & expression, Estrangement
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6ohpNi_BpY
- Images: https://www.operamecatronica.com/wp-content/uploads/2019/12/3.png
- Project page: https://www.operamecatronica.com/robocygne/

#### Pichet Klunchun and Myself — Jérôme Bel, Pichet Klunchun (2005)
- Type: Performance, dance & music · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Voice, sound & listening
- Idea: A dance body is a body of knowledge: putting it into words, in dialogue with a foreign body, reveals assumptions that neither could see alone.
- What it is: Sitting face to face, French choreographer Jérôme Bel and Thai khon dancer Pichet Klunchun ask each other questions and demonstrate movements, explaining what their bodies know and why.
- How it works: Staged conversation and demonstration created in 2005 and toured internationally.
- Lens: Movement & expression, Body politics, First-person & felt experience
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=biR7pV-hzR4
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pichet_Klunchun

#### Moon Water — Lin Hwai-min (1998)
- Type: Performance, dance & music · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Breath, Posture, balance & proprioception
- Idea: An Eastern somatic practice can become a stage language: the audience sees qi and breath moving through bodies trained from the inside.
- What it is: Cloud Gate dancers move in slow, continuous tai chi-based flows to Bach's cello suites while water gradually covers the stage and mirrors reflect their bodies; the company trains daily in tai chi daoyin, meditation and martial arts.
- How it works: Choreography built from tai chi daoyin (developed by Hsiung Wei) and meditation training, with water and mirror scenography.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Movement & expression, Attunement & appreciation
- Shown at: New York Times best dance of 2003 (Anna Kisselgoff)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=KmTplJXenAg
- Images: https://www.cloudgate.org.tw/sites/default/files/u1005/MetaInfo_CG_1200x630.jpg https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/6/62/Cloud_Gate_Theater_Tamsui_02.jpg
- Project page: https://www.cloudgate.org.tw/en

#### Admiring La Argentina — Kazuo Ohno, Tatsumi Hijikata (1977)
- Type: Performance, dance & music · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Memory lives in the body and can be danced: an old body becomes fully expressive when it moves from felt memory rather than technique.
- What it is: A solo in which Ohno, then in his seventies, in a woman's dress and hat, evokes the Spanish dancer Antonia Mercé, whom he had seen in 1926, moving between trembling fragility and joy; directed by Hijikata.
- How it works: Solo butoh performance premiered in Tokyo in 1977 and toured internationally after its showing at the Nancy International Festival.
- Lens: Movement & expression, Body-mind unity
- Shown at: Dance Critics' Circle Award (Japan); Nancy International Festival
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cG4H4yeNHs0
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/a/af/Kazuo_Ohno.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kazuo_Ohno

### Somatic & Participatory Art

Art that works directly on the visitor's senses and body: Lygia Clark, Rebecca Horn, Char Davies.

#### Surging Verticality — Lian Loke, George Poonkhin Khut (2011)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Posture, balance & proprioception, Touch & pressure, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Feldenkrais-style guidance shows that artist care and the visitor's receptivity are part of the interface.
- What it is: A body-focused interactive artwork in which an artist guides a visitor through touch and slow movement to sense how their skeleton holds them upright.
- How it works: Prototype built by applying the Feldenkrais Method to proprioception and touch, tested with audiences and reflected on by the artist-researchers.
- Lens: First-person & felt experience, Attunement & appreciation, Body-mind unity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/1935701.1935747 (TEI 2011)

#### Electric Stimulus to Face — Daito Manabe (2008)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Muscle tension (EMG), Voice, sound & listening
- Idea: When an outside signal moves your face, the link between expression and feeling becomes strange and visible.
- What it is: Electrodes on the faces of Manabe and his friends deliver electrical muscle stimulation driven by music, so their faces twitch and grimace in rhythm while they sit still.
- How it works: Custom EMS device controlled from music software, with myoelectric sensors in later tests to pass facial movement between people.
- Lens: Estrangement, Body-mind unity
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=pLAma-lrJRM
- Project page: https://www.daito.ws/en/

#### soft(n) — Thecla Schiphorst (2007)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Vibration & haptics, Whole body-mind
- Idea: The paper that introduced Shusterman's somaesthetics to HCI: design for how touch feels, not only for what it triggers.
- What it is: A group of soft, pillow-like networked objects responds when people hold, stroke or hug them, passing touch on to each other as vibration, light and sound.
- How it works: Soft tangible objects with embedded touch sensing and vibration/light/sound actuators, networked so that touching one affects others; designed with V2_Lab.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Technology that touches back, Attunement & appreciation
- Shown at: DEAF07, Rotterdam
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/1520340.1520345 (CHI 2009 Extended Abstracts)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fsDXBxeRoOw
- Images: https://v2.nl/wp-content/uploads/2023/02/softn.png
- Project page: https://v2.nl/archive/works/soft-n

#### Célula Nave — Ernesto Neto (2004)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Posture, balance & proprioception, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Soft, weighted, body-scale sculpture makes visitors feel gravity, tension and softness with the whole body.
- What it is: A large soft architecture of stretched fabric held down by eighteen legs of sand-filled stockings, made for the central hall of Museum Boijmans Van Beuningen.
- How it works: Stretch fabric and eighteen legs made of eight sand-filled socks each (about eight kilos per sock), installed from the artist's written instructions.
- Lens: Attunement & appreciation, Pleasure & play
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=GSl5QJCuOlE

#### Ephémère — Char Davies (1998)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Breath, Posture, balance & proprioception, Light & vision
- Idea: Moving between landscape and body interior suggests that the felt body and the world share one living process.
- What it is: A breath-and-balance VR work in three layers (landscape, earth and the inside of the body), where organs and seeds appear and fade with the seasons.
- How it works: Same breath-sensing vest and balance interface as Osmose, with time-varying generative imagery and spatial sound.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Attunement & appreciation, More-than-human
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XCWaMll0leI
- Images: https://www.immersence.com/images/ephemere/Eph_Autumn_Flux_I_600.jpg https://www.immersence.com/images/ephemere/016_Ephemere_Installation_View_2.jpg
- Project page: https://www.immersence.com/ephemere/

#### Bodymaps: Artifacts of Touch — Thecla Schiphorst (1996)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Touch is a two-way, intimate act: a surface that 'wants' contact makes visitors aware of how and why they touch.
- What it is: A white velvet table shows a projected image of the artist's body; when visitors stroke, press or hover over it, the image and sound respond in ways that are hard to predict.
- How it works: Fifteen electric-field sensors and eight force-sensitive resistors under the velvet sense proximity, touch and pressure and drive video and sound through a deliberately opaque rule set.
- Lens: Technology that touches back, First-person & felt experience, Intercorporeality
- Shown at: SIGGRAPH 97 Art Gallery
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/259081.259172 (SIGGRAPH 97 Visual Proceedings)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3DrCM8pJVYI

#### Ping Body — Stelarc (1996)
- Type: Performance, dance & music · Body & senses: Muscle tension (EMG), Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: Being moved from outside makes the felt boundary between voluntary and involuntary movement vivid.
- What it is: Internet traffic moves Stelarc's body: network ping times are turned into electrical muscle stimulation, so his arms and legs jerk without his intention.
- How it works: Ping values to global internet domains are mapped to a muscle-stimulation system on his limbs; his own movements also drive a robotic Third Hand.
- Lens: Estrangement, More-than-human, Body politics
- Shown at: DEAF96, Rotterdam
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=45MOlsFGSHI
- Project page: https://stelarc.org/

#### Osmose — Char Davies (1995)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Breath, Posture, balance & proprioception, Light & vision
- Idea: Tying navigation to breath and balance turns VR into a meditative, embodied experience instead of hand-and-eye control.
- What it is: An immersive virtual space of trees, clouds and code navigated by breath and balance: breathe in to float up, breathe out to sink, lean to drift.
- How it works: Head-mounted display with a vest that senses chest expansion (breath) and tilt sensors for balance, inspired by scuba diving; real-time graphics on SGI computers.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Attunement & appreciation, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/240806.240808 (ACM SIGGRAPH Computer Graphics)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=54O4VP3tCoY
- Images: https://www.immersence.com/images/osmose/Osm_Tree_Pond_600.jpg https://www.immersence.com/images/osmose/Osm_Subt_Earth_600_v2.jpg
- Project page: https://www.immersence.com/osmose/

#### Very Nervous System — David Rokeby (1986)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Voice, sound & listening, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Rokeby: the loop is fast enough that control dissolves into 'encounter and involvement', a felt partnership rather than command.
- What it is: Video cameras watch a person moving in an empty space and a computer answers every movement with sound, so the body plays the room like an instrument.
- How it works: Custom video-motion analysis of camera images, mapped in real time to synthesised and sampled sound (developed 1982–1991).
- Lens: Affective loops, Movement & expression, Body-mind unity
- Shown at: Ars Electronica 2009, Lentos Kunstmuseum Linz
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=FsR7EQgG7N0
- Images: http://www.davidrokeby.com/VNS_Potsdam_web.jpg
- Project page: http://www.davidrokeby.com/vns.html

#### Third Hand — Stelarc (1980)
- Type: Performance, dance & music · Body & senses: Muscle tension (EMG), Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: An added limb has to be learned from inside: the body remaps which muscles 'mean' a hand, a slow first-person training of body schema.
- What it is: A robotic right hand attached to Stelarc's arm is driven by muscle signals from his abdomen and legs; he spent years learning to write with three hands.
- How it works: Electromyographic (EMG) electrodes on abdominal and leg muscles control the grasp, rotation and release of a mechanical hand built in Japan.
- Lens: First-person & felt experience, Estrangement, More-than-human
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Or1CVc6A9YA
- Images: https://stelarc.org/media/img/projects-overview/3rdhand2.jpg
- Project page: https://stelarc.org/_activity-20265.php

#### Estruturação do Self (Structuring of the Self) — Lygia Clark (1976)
- Type: Somatic practice · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Heat & temperature, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Objects exist only in relation to a body: weight, warmth and texture on the skin can reorganise body memory and sense of self.
- What it is: A therapeutic practice in which Clark placed 'relational objects' (air bags, stones, shells, bags of water and seeds) on the body of a person lying down, in sessions lasting months.
- How it works: Individual sessions with hand-made relational objects applied to the client's body, developed from 1976 until her death in 1988.
- Lens: Care & healing, Technology that touches back, First-person & felt experience
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ckQEQ4HKCE8

#### Videoplace — Myron Krueger (1975)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Light & vision, Whole body-mind
- Idea: The whole, unencumbered body can be the interface; no gloves, goggles or controllers are needed to feel present.
- What it is: Visitors see their live silhouettes on a large screen, where they can play with graphic creatures, draw with their fingers and touch the silhouettes of people in another room.
- How it works: Real-time camera silhouette extraction on custom hardware, composited with graphics and linked between separate rooms.
- Lens: Movement & expression, Pleasure & play, Intercorporeality
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/317456.317463 (CHI 1985)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=dqZyZrN3Pl0

#### Pencil Mask — Rebecca Horn (1972)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Touch & pressure
- Idea: The body becomes a drawing instrument; the drawing is a trace of how the head and neck felt their way across the wall.
- What it is: A mask of fabric straps with pencils sticking out from the face; by rocking her head in front of a wall, Horn draws marks that record the rhythm of her movement.
- How it works: Wearable mask of straps and pencils, used in a filmed performance; the mask is in the Tate collection.
- Lens: Estrangement, Movement & expression
- Images: https://media.tate.org.uk/aztate-prd-ew-dg-wgtail-st1-ctr-data/images/.width-600_lfxgvMS.jpg
- Project page: https://www.tate.org.uk/art/artworks/horn-pencil-mask-t07847

#### Einhorn (Unicorn) — Rebecca Horn (1970)
- Type: Performance, dance & music · Body & senses: Posture, balance & proprioception, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: A small addition to the head changes posture, balance and pace completely; extension makes the whole body newly felt.
- What it is: A woman walks slowly through fields and woods wearing a tall white horn strapped to her head with bandages around her upper body.
- How it works: Wearable body sculpture of wood and fabric straps, performed outdoors and recorded on 16 mm film.
- Lens: Estrangement, First-person & felt experience, Body politics
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=8-uShAVpDLM

#### Éden — Hélio Oiticica (1969)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Oiticica called it a place for 'crelazer' (creative leisure): sensing with the whole body, slowly, is the work.
- What it is: A walk-through environment of sand, straw, water, leaves and small shelters where visitors go barefoot, lie down and rest.
- How it works: Installation of natural materials and enclosures (Ninhos, Bólides) first built for his exhibition at the Whitechapel Gallery, London.
- Lens: Attunement & appreciation, Pleasure & play, Body-mind unity
- Shown at: Whitechapel Gallery, London 1969; To Organize Delirium, Carnegie Museum of Art 2016
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=pm6tvOfN80Y

#### Máscaras Sensoriais (Sensorial Masks) — Lygia Clark (1967)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Light & vision, Voice, sound & listening, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Blocking and altering the everyday senses turns the wearer inward and makes perception itself felt.
- What it is: Cloth hoods that cover the whole head, fitted with small bags of herbs at the nose, sound devices at the ears and lenses at the eyes, change how the wearer smells, hears and sees.
- How it works: Fabric masks in different colours, each with its own combination of scent sachets, ear devices and optical eyepieces.
- Lens: Estrangement, First-person & felt experience
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0kZ-WIORsZg
- Images: https://i.ytimg.com/vi/0kZ-WIORsZg/hqdefault.jpg

#### O Eu e o Tu (The I and the You) — Lygia Clark (1967)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Knowing another person through touch alone reorganises the felt sense of self and other, including gendered expectations.
- What it is: Two people, hooded and unable to see, wear heavy suits joined by a tube; zippers on each suit open onto textures the other can explore by touch.
- How it works: Industrial plastic overalls lined with materials such as foam, rubber and water bags, with hoods and a connecting tube.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Estrangement, Body politics
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5oxfmJpfVY4

#### Respire Comigo (Breathe with Me) — Lygia Clark (1966)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Breath, Voice, sound & listening, Touch & pressure
- Idea: An object can lend the body a breath to follow; noticing its rhythm turns attention to one's own breathing.
- What it is: A rubber tube, closed at both ends, is stretched and released by the participant's hands so that it rhythmically 'breathes' next to their ear.
- How it works: A length of diving-hose rubber tube; its air sound and elasticity respond to hand pressure.
- Lens: Attunement & appreciation, First-person & felt experience, Body-mind unity
- Shown at: Lygia Clark: The Abandonment of Art, 1948–1988, MoMA 2014
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XG2qmCYQ7Ps

#### Cut Piece — Yoko Ono (1964)
- Type: Performance, dance & music · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Stillness and exposure make the audience feel their own agency: the work is felt as much by those who cut as by the one who is cut.
- What it is: Ono kneels motionless on stage with a pair of scissors in front of her, and audience members come up one by one to cut away pieces of her clothing; first performed in Kyoto in 1964.
- How it works: Event score that Ono performed six times between 1964 and 2003; the 1965 New York performance was filmed by Albert and David Maysles.
- Lens: Body politics, Intercorporeality
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aGMVqbEl-Y0
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cut_Piece_1964

#### Parangolé — Hélio Oiticica (1964)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Whole body-mind, Light & vision
- Idea: Colour and structure are completed by the dancing body; the wearer is at once artwork, performer and viewer.
- What it is: Layered capes, banners and tents of fabric and plastic that only come alive when worn and danced in, first by samba dancers of the Mangueira community in Rio.
- How it works: Wearable fabric constructions with painted colour fields and written phrases, activated through samba movement.
- Lens: Movement & expression, Body politics, Pleasure & play
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=iaQBW7FitQE
- Images: https://mam.rio/wp-content/uploads/2020/12/2016-65-16-687x1024.jpg https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/a/ae/Gerardo_Mosquera_wearing_H%C3%A9lio_Oiticica%27s_parangol%C3%A9_Da_adversidade_vivemos.JPG
- Project page: https://mam.rio/historia/parangole-em-opiniao-65/

#### Caminhando (Walking) — Lygia Clark (1963)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: The work is the act: meaning lives in the time and attention of the participant's hands, not in an object.
- What it is: The participant cuts a paper Möbius strip lengthwise with scissors, again and again, choosing where to go until the strip becomes too thin to cut.
- How it works: A paper strip, glue and scissors; instructions invite anyone to perform it.
- Lens: First-person & felt experience, Body-mind unity
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3sP-uT5DQLM
- Images: http://www.bauhaus-imaginista.org/images/20243.jpg?w=2000&h=2000
- Project page: https://www.bauhaus-imaginista.org/articles/3822/walking-on-a-mobius-strip/

#### Electric Dress — Atsuko Tanaka (1956)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Light & vision, Heat & temperature, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Wearing technology is a bodily risk and a felt state: heat, weight, light and danger enclose the wearer inside the machine.
- What it is: A garment of about 200 painted light bulbs and tubes, weighing over 50 kg, that flashed in sequence while Tanaka wore it at the 2nd Gutai Art Exhibition in Tokyo in 1956.
- How it works: Hand-painted incandescent and fluorescent lamps wired to a flashing controller, worn as a kimono-like costume.
- Lens: Body politics, Estrangement
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=1DcNEpJMBbY
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atsuko_Tanaka_(artist)

#### Challenging Mud — Kazuo Shiraga (1955)
- Type: Performance, dance & music · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Touch & pressure, Whole body-mind
- Idea: The whole body can be the brush: making becomes a struggle of flesh with material, felt from inside and left as trace.
- What it is: Stripped to his underwear, Shiraga threw himself into a pit of mud, clay and cement and wrestled with it, leaving the churned surface as the work; performed at the First Gutai Art Exhibition in Tokyo in 1955.
- How it works: Action performed three times in the exhibition yard, documented by photographs and film.
- Lens: Movement & expression, Body-mind unity
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=1xCg79GrWaM
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kazuo_Shiraga

## Intimate, Feminist & Queer Soma

Designing for bodies that design has ignored or made taboo: menstruation, pleasure, fertility, menopause, queer and trans bodies.

### Menstruation & Menarche

Touching and being in touch with the menstruating body.

#### Kuddi — Guðrún Margrét Ívansdóttir, Joo Young Park, Anna Ståhl, Madeline Balaam (2025)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Shape-change & inflatables, Touch & pressure, Pain & inner sensations
- Idea: Data felt through touch, 'visceralised' rather than visualised, can change one's relation to menstrual pain.
- What it is: Kuddi is a soft pillow with 12 inflatable pockets that touches and is touched in relation to the changing menstruating body; its designer lived with it for two menstrual cycles.
- How it works: Haptic data physicalisation with 12 inflatable pockets, evaluated autoethnographically over two cycles and analysed through narrative thick descriptions.
- Lens: Body data & ambiguity, Technology that touches back, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3706598.3713221 (CHI 2025)

#### Touchware — Joo Young Park, Caroline Yan Zheng, Nadia Campo Woytuk, Madeline Balaam, Marianela Ciolfi Felice (2025)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Shape-change & inflatables, Touch & pressure, Pain & inner sensations
- Idea: Technology can touch a body in a vulnerable state with care, when it is designed for the diffuse, lingering qualities of discomfort.
- What it is: Touchware is a set of on-body touch probes that inflate or add weight, worn at work to engage with and care for menstrual discomfort.
- How it works: Pneumatic shape-change and weighted components developed through participatory soma design, deployed for two weeks with six participants in a workplace.
- Lens: Technology that touches back, Care & healing, Body politics
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3706598.3714032 (CHI 2025)

#### Critiquing Menstrual Pain Technologies — Joo Young Park, Nadia Campo Woytuk, Marianela Ciolfi Felice, Madeline Balaam (2024)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Pain & inner sensations, Intimate & hormonal body
- Idea: Most products try to hide menstrual pain; design should instead attend to it as a cyclical, chronic lived experience.
- What it is: An interaction critique of FemTech products for menstrual pain, read through feminist disability studies, ends in three design provocations.
- How it works: Interaction critique of FemTech market exemplars using feminist disability studies as the analytical lens.
- Lens: Body politics, Care & healing
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3613904.3642691 (CHI 2024)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SPOFbrspHgY

#### Biomenstrual: Menstrual Care for More-than-Human Bodies — Marie Louise Juul Søndergaard, Nadia Campo Woytuk (2023)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Intimate & hormonal body, Touch & pressure
- Idea: Menstrual care can extend to soil and other species when the body is understood as more-than-human.
- What it is: Workshop participants made biodegradable menstrual artefacts from biomaterials that can be composted, returning the nutrients in menstrual blood to soil.
- How it works: Research through Design with three workshops and 20 participants, making tools and biomaterial artefacts.
- Lens: More-than-human, Body politics, Care & healing
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3544548.3581083 (CHI 2023)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r9TaL5jPR50

#### Tactful Feminist Sensing — Nadia Campo Woytuk, Joo Young Park, Marianela Ciolfi Felice, Madeline Balaam (2023)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Intimate & hormonal body, Touch & pressure
- Idea: Sensing intimate fluids can stay close to the fleshy, tactile body instead of abstracting it into numbers.
- What it is: Design explorations, four concepts and one working prototype sense the conductivity of cervical mucus, adding touch-based bodily knowledge to fertility tracking.
- How it works: A conductivity sensor prototype for vaginal fluids, presented as a pictorial with material explorations of proximity, visibility and temporality.
- Lens: Body politics, Body data & ambiguity, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3563657.3595966 (DIS 2023)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=t5bukX1SJhQ

#### Menstrual Garden — Jiabao Li (2022)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Intimate & hormonal body, Pain & inner sensations
- Idea: Giving menstrual pain a smell makes an inner, often hidden sensation shareable and discussable.
- What it is: A multisensory installation with scent bottles holding the smells of fresh menstrual blood, stagnant menstrual blood and menstrual pain, beside 3D-printed sculptures of proteins found only in menstrual blood, identified from the artist's own samples.
- How it works: Mass spectrometry of the artist's menstrual effluent over many cycles to build a menstrual blood proteome, translated into perfumes and 3D-printed protein models.
- Lens: Body politics, First-person & felt experience
- Shown at: Olfactory Art Keller, New York, 2022
- Images: https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/58688c8a6a496327e937e35b/1669072958670-Y5OZPGTKSBGKPQFVU1L0/Jiabao+Li+menstrual+garden+4-3.jpg https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/58688c8a6a496327e937e35b/1669072954462-P6HVTCDOE9M9M4TRT58F/Jiabao+Li+menstrual+garden+2-2.jpg
- Project page: https://www.jiabaoli.org/menstrual-garden

#### Rethinking Menstrual Trackers Towards Period-Positive Ecologies — Anupriya Tuli, Neha Kumar (2022)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Intimate & hormonal body
- Idea: A cycle is lived, not just predicted; period-positive design treats the menstruating body as something to be felt and talked about without shame.
- What it is: A critique of menstrual tracking apps and a proposal for period-positive design that supports how people feel and live with their cycles, not only how they log them.
- How it works: Review of menstrual tracking apps and design research with users in India, leading to a period-positive ecology framework.
- Lens: Body data & ambiguity, Body politics, Care & healing
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3491102.3517662 (CHI 2022)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=G8JXJ76HghU

#### Menstrual Technologies Designed with Adolescents — Marie Louise Juul Søndergaard, Marianela Ciolfi Felice, Madeline Balaam (2021)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Intimate & hormonal body, Heat & temperature, Shape-change & inflatables
- Idea: Participatory soma design lets young people listen to and care for newly menstruating bodies instead of hiding them.
- What it is: Seven adolescents aged 16 to 18 used Menarche Bits in two workshops to design technologies that respond to menstrual cramps and to anxious feelings before a period, so they can keep playing sport.
- How it works: Two participatory soma design workshops with the Menarche Bits toolkit, run with sports clubs and a high school as part of menstrual advocacy.
- Lens: Body politics, Care & healing, Technology that touches back
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3411764.3445471 (CHI 2021)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=6smyCA51U_g

#### Curious Cycles — Nadia Campo Woytuk, Marie Louise Juul Søndergaard, Marianela Ciolfi Felice, Madeline Balaam (2020)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Intimate & hormonal body, Touch & pressure
- Idea: Touching and looking at one's own fluids builds self-knowledge and a nurturing appreciation of the changing body, against the stigma that teaches disgust.
- What it is: Curious Cycles is a set of objects that invite people to look at and touch their menstrual blood, saliva and cervical mucus over a full cycle.
- How it works: Research through Design: a kit of objects and interactions deployed with five participants over a menstrual cycle, with diaries and interviews.
- Lens: Body politics, First-person & felt experience, Care & healing
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3313831.3376471 (CHI 2020)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=NJaDG-SHa4w

#### Menarche Bits — Marie Louise Juul Søndergaard, Ozgun Kilic Afsar, Marianela Ciolfi Felice, Nadia Campo Woytuk, Madeline Balaam (2020)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Heat & temperature, Shape-change & inflatables, Intimate & hormonal body
- Idea: Warmth and pressure on the body can open conversations about menstruation and help young people trust their menstruating bodies.
- What it is: Menarche Bits is an open-ended kit of heat pads and soft shape-changing actuators, worn on the skin or in pockets of a stretchy garment, for young people to create body-worn technologies around their first periods.
- How it works: Soft robotic inflatables and heat elements that stimulate, press, heat and massage the skin and muscles; later used in participatory workshops with adolescents in sports clubs.
- Lens: Technology that touches back, Body politics, Care & healing
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3357236.3395592 (DIS 2020)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=DcHno9yU3ws
- Images: https://www.kth.se/polopoly_fs/1.1486487.1782821190!/image/DSC_0172%20small.jpg
- Project page: https://www.kth.se/hct/mid/research/interaction-design/projects/menarche-bits-1.921962

#### Menstrual (Im)Mobilities and Safe Spaces — Anupriya Tuli, Shaan Chopra, Neha Kumar (2020)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Intimate & hormonal body, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: The menstruating body is felt in public space: a missing toilet or a hostile room becomes pain, worry and a smaller world.
- What it is: A study of how menstruating people in urban India plan where they can go, change and rest, and how the lack of safe toilets and spaces restricts their movement during periods.
- How it works: Interviews and design activities with menstruators in India, analysed through mobility and safe-space lenses.
- Lens: Body politics
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3313831.3376653 (CHI 2020)

#### Vivewell — Sarah Fox, Noura Howell (2019)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Intimate & hormonal body
- Idea: Body data about menstruation is never neutral; imagining who else reads it makes the politics of tracking a felt, not abstract, concern.
- What it is: A speculative workplace wellness product that tracks employees' menstrual cycles, presented through a fictional brochure and artefacts to show how current data practices could turn intimate body data into a tool of control.
- How it works: Speculative design: a fictional company's product materials built from existing menstrual-tracking and workplace-wellness data practices, used to provoke discussion.
- Lens: Body data & ambiguity, Body politics
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3322276.3323695 (DIS 2019)

#### Learning from and with Menstrupedia — Anupriya Tuli, Shaan Chopra, Neha Kumar (2018)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Intimate & hormonal body
- Idea: Where the menstruating body is hidden, education that is friendly, visual and local is the first technology of care.
- What it is: A study of Menstrupedia, an Indian comic and website that teaches girls about periods, and of how young people, parents and teachers use it to talk about a taboo body.
- How it works: Qualitative study of Menstrupedia's content and of interviews with its users and creators in India.
- Lens: Body politics, Care & healing
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3274443 (CSCW 2018 (PACM HCI))
- Images: https://www.menstrupedia.com/images/comic/preview/english/h9szlx5gx1el70x8wy9d.jpg
- Project page: https://www.menstrupedia.com

#### Menstruation Machine – Takashi's Take — Sputniko! (Hiromi Ozaki) (2010)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Intimate & hormonal body, Pain & inner sensations
- Idea: Feeling another body's cycle in your own body turns a gendered experience into shared, felt knowledge and questions who gets to have it.
- What it is: A metal device resembling a chastity belt, with a blood-dispensing system and electrodes that stimulate the lower abdomen, reproduces the pain and bleeding of a five-day period; in the music video a boy, Takashi, wears it to feel what menstruation is like.
- How it works: Wearable device with electrical abdominal stimulation and a blood reservoir, presented with a pop music video; made at the Royal College of Art.
- Lens: Body politics, Estrangement
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gnb-rdGbm6s
- Images: https://storage.googleapis.com/production-os-assets/assets/6afd6133-e6f0-48e5-95f7-1b17034f2529
- Project page: https://sputniko.com/Menstruation-Machine

### Pleasure & Sexual Health

Pleasure, intimacy and sexual wellbeing as design topics.

#### Sentient Clit: The Pussification of BioTech — Jiabao Li, WhiteFeather Hunter (2024)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Intimate & hormonal body, Touch & pressure
- Idea: Biotech can be designed around felt pleasure: asking who owns an organ that can sense opens questions of consent and sentience.
- What it is: Video performances, lab experiments and digital art around growing clitoral tissue by 3D bioprinting stem cells from menstrual fluid, centring pleasure rather than reproduction.
- How it works: Primary cell culture from a menstrual cup to petri dish, 3D bioprinting and distribution of the work through social platforms such as OnlyFans.
- Lens: Pleasure & play, Body politics
- Shown at: Exploratorium: Sexplorations
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=DkvH62jYCjM
- Images: https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/58688c8a6a496327e937e35b/714f6ccf-9ea2-4a1b-b5fd-38214abb33dd/Clitoris+bio+print+1-2.jpg
- Project page: https://www.jiabaoli.org/sentient-clit

#### Embodied Embroidery — Dianya Mia Hua, Jeffrey Bardzell, Shaowen Bardzell (2023)
- Type: Method & toolkit · Body & senses: Intimate & hormonal body, Touch & pressure
- Idea: Embroidery can articulate felt pleasure where words fail, turning first-person sexual experience into shareable design knowledge.
- What it is: The first author, a woman with an invisible disability, tried other women's masturbation techniques on her own body and articulated each felt experience as an embroidered piece.
- How it works: First-person somaesthetic study guided by first-person perspective, intersubjectivity and articulation, with handmade embroidery as the articulation medium (pictorial).
- Lens: First-person & felt experience, Pleasure & play, Body politics
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3569009.3572803 (TEI 2023 (Pictorial))

#### What Can HCI Learn from Sexual Consent? — Yolande Strengers, Florian 'Floyd' Mueller (2021)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Consent is felt and negotiated moment to moment between bodies; technologies that touch or sense the body should be designed to ask, listen and stop.
- What it is: A feminist model of sexual consent (voluntary, ongoing, informed, specific and revocable) carried into interaction design, especially for technologies that act on or read the body.
- How it works: Conceptual transfer of feminist and sexual consent literature into design principles, with examples from bodily and intimate technologies.
- Lens: Body politics, Intercorporeality
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3411764.3445107 (CHI 2021)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=g_9fklT2nLM

#### Open-Source 3D Clitoris Model — Odile Fillod (2016)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Intimate & hormonal body, Touch & pressure
- Idea: An organ of pleasure that textbooks drew as a dot becomes knowable when it can be held; tactile knowledge corrects a political omission.
- What it is: A free, life-size, 3D-printable model of the whole clitoris, including its internal bulbs and crura, released for schools and sex education.
- How it works: Anatomically based 3D model released under an open licence for anyone to print; used in French classrooms and exhibitions.
- Lens: Body politics, Pleasure & play
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/7/7e/Clitoris_3D_%28v2%29.jpg
- Project page: https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Clitoris_3D_(v2).jpg

#### How HCI Talks about Sexuality — Gopinaath Kannabiran, Jeffrey Bardzell, Shaowen Bardzell (2011)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Intimate & hormonal body, Whole body-mind
- Idea: What a field will not say about bodies shapes what it can design for; naming the blind spot is the first step toward designing with desire and pleasure.
- What it is: A discourse analysis of HCI papers that mention sex and sexuality, showing how the field frames sexuality as risk, identity or health data while avoiding the sexual body itself.
- How it works: Critical reading of the HCI literature on sexuality, identifying discursive strategies, blind spots and research opportunities.
- Lens: Body politics, Pleasure & play
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/1978942.1979043 (CHI 2011)

#### Pleasure is Your Birthright: Digitally Enabled Designer Sex Toys — Jeffrey Bardzell, Shaowen Bardzell (2011)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Intimate & hormonal body, Vibration & haptics, Touch & pressure
- Idea: Designing for pleasure is designing for the whole felt body; leaving sexuality out of HCI is itself a norm about which bodies and experiences count.
- What it is: A study of how designers of high-end digital sex toys work, arguing that these vibrators are sophisticated interactive artefacts for embodied pleasure that HCI had refused to study.
- How it works: Interviews with sex toy designers and analysis of their process (critical immersion, conversations with users and sexual health experts, iterated 3D prototypes), framed as third-wave HCI.
- Lens: Pleasure & play, Body politics
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/1978942.1978979 (CHI 2011)

#### Sex Toys and Designing for Sexual Wellness — Anna Eaglin, Shaowen Bardzell (2011)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Intimate & hormonal body, Vibration & haptics
- Idea: Sexual pleasure belongs to health: a toy that helps someone know what their body enjoys is a wellness device, not a joke.
- What it is: A user study of how people choose, use and talk about sex toys, turned into design principles for sexual wellness technology.
- How it works: Survey and interviews with sex toy users, analysed for experiences, barriers and preferences, leading to design principles.
- Lens: Pleasure & play, Care & healing
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/1979742.1979879 (CHI 2011 Extended Abstracts)

### Feminist HCI & Women's Health

Pelvic floor, fertility, pregnancy and the politics of tracking women's bodies.

#### Pelvic Chair — Anna Ståhl, Madeline Balaam, Marianela Ciolfi Felice (2022)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Shape-change & inflatables, Touch & pressure, Intimate & hormonal body
- Idea: Rhythmic, technology-initiated touch can build somatic literacy of a hidden muscle group: feeling tension and release where words fail.
- What it is: The Pelvic Chair uses soft latex air pockets in the seat to touch the outer pelvic floor in gentle rhythms, drawing attention to its anatomy, muscles and connections.
- How it works: Inflatable latex pockets with tuned layers, volume and timing, developed through an annotated soma design process (Ståhl et al., DIS 2022) and studied with 14 participants (Yadav et al., CHI 2025).
- Lens: Technology that touches back, Attunement & appreciation, Care & healing
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3706598.3713223 (CHI 2025)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ouG7d90Hp-o

#### Removal as a Method — Sarah Homewood, Anna Vallgårda (2020)
- Type: Method & toolkit · Body & senses: Intimate & hormonal body, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Taking a tracker away shows what it was doing to the felt body; removal lets designers notice how data had replaced bodily attention.
- What it is: A first-person design method: the researcher stops using fertility and menstrual self-tracking tools and records how the felt sense of one's own cycle and body changes once the data is gone.
- How it works: Autoethnographic study in which the designer removes self-tracking technologies from daily life and documents the experience over time, proposed as a fourth-wave HCI method.
- Lens: First-person & felt experience, Body data & ambiguity, Body politics
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3357236.3395425 (DIS 2020)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=GvNc-aeR6nA

#### HCI and Intimate Care as an Agenda for Change in Women's Health — Teresa Almeida, Rob Comber, Madeline Balaam (2016)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Intimate & hormonal body, Muscle tension (EMG)
- Idea: Feeling better starts from knowing and feeling the pelvic floor: designing for women's health means designing against the silence that keeps this part of the body unfelt.
- What it is: A study of urinary incontinence from the side of women and of pelvic floor therapists, which argues that HCI has ignored the intimate, leaking, ageing female body and sets out an agenda for designing intimate care.
- How it works: Interviews with women and physiotherapists about pelvic floor training, read through feminist HCI to derive knowledge, esteem and agency as design goals.
- Lens: Body politics, Care & healing
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2858036.2858187 (CHI 2016)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BXY-7MmEYjo

#### Make the Breast Pump Not Suck: A Feminist HCI Approach to Postpartum Technologies — Catherine D'Ignazio (2016)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Intimate & hormonal body, Touch & pressure
- Idea: A device clamped to the breast for hours a day is a somatic experience; asking lactating people what it feels like reveals design that had ignored their bodies.
- What it is: An account of the Make the Breast Pump Not Suck hackathon at the MIT Media Lab, where parents, designers and clinicians redesigned the breast pump and the postpartum experience around it.
- How it works: Hackathon and participatory design with parents and experts, analysed through feminist HCI to reflect on process, inclusion and outcomes.
- Lens: Body politics, Care & healing
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2858036.2858460 (CHI 2016)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SnGcXgzJ9EA

#### Labella — Teresa Almeida, Rob Comber, Madeline Balaam (2015)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Intimate & hormonal body, Light & vision, Muscle tension (EMG)
- Idea: Looking at one's own genitals through a playful interface turns a taboo into first-person bodily knowledge, from the 'very basics' to how the pelvic muscles feel when they work.
- What it is: A pair of marked underwear and a smartphone app: the woman points the phone at her own body and sees an augmented view of the vulva, then learns anatomy and pelvic floor exercises through it.
- How it works: Fiducial markers printed on underwear let a phone camera overlay 3D anatomy models on the body; the app guides identification of body parts and pelvic floor exercises; evaluated with women at home.
- Lens: Body politics, Attunement & appreciation, Estrangement
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2858036.2858119 (CHI 2016)
- Images: https://imgix.bustle.com/mic/0svztmysgezjm7soktefkfbtfwdorjpmg4yhadm4zt6iecv8rt9ftth0ifnegl1n.jpg?w=1200&h=630&fit=crop&crop=faces&fm=jpg
- Project page: https://www.mic.com/articles/143637/labella-is-an-app-that-will-teach-you-to-get-to-know-your-vagina

#### Intimate Care: eTextiles for Pelvic Fitness — Teresa Almeida, Rob Comber, Madeline Balaam (2014)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Intimate & hormonal body, Muscle tension (EMG), Touch & pressure
- Idea: A muscle most people cannot see or name becomes trainable once a soft material gives it a felt, readable response.
- What it is: Soft e-textile prototypes worn close to the body that help women locate and feel the pelvic floor muscles and learn to contract them correctly.
- How it works: Textile sensors and conductive fabric worn against the perineum pick up pelvic floor contraction and give feedback during training (early design exploration).
- Lens: Care & healing, Attunement & appreciation, Body politics
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2598784.2602768 (DIS 2014 Companion)

#### Feminist HCI: Taking Stock and Outlining an Agenda for Design — Shaowen Bardzell (2010)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Whole body-mind
- Idea: Design always assumes a user's body and life; feminist HCI makes that assumption visible and asks designers to answer for it.
- What it is: The paper that named feminist HCI: it proposes qualities such as pluralism, participation, advocacy, ecology, embodiment and self-disclosure to guide interaction design.
- How it works: Theoretical synthesis of feminist theory for interaction design, yielding a set of design qualities with examples.
- Lens: Body politics
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/1753326.1753521 (CHI 2010)

### Menopause, Ageing & Life Transitions

Bodies changing over a life: menopause, ageing, illness.

#### What Makes Us Most Human Is Also So Animal — Jiabao Li (2026)
- Type: Performance, dance & music · Body & senses: Intimate & hormonal body, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Breastfeeding, the most intimate bodily act, is also what we share with other mammals: tasting it crosses the line between human and animal.
- What it is: A blind tasting in which visitors drink a line of milks from mammals, including the artist's own breast milk, cow, goat, camel, yak, bat and coyote, and try to guess the source.
- How it works: Performance and tasting made after the artist's pregnancy, part of the solo exhibition Womb Wetware (2026).
- Lens: More-than-human, First-person & felt experience, Body politics
- Shown at: Womb Wetware, tiat, San Francisco, 2026; Fusebox Festival, Austin, 2026
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=8k3acKrF-o8
- Project page: https://www.jiabaoli.org/what-makes-us-most-human-is-also-so-animal

#### Reclaiming the Body through Design: Menopause — Marianela Ciolfi Felice, Marie Louise Juul Søndergaard, Madeline Balaam (2021)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Intimate & hormonal body, Heat & temperature, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Menopause is an entanglement of physical and psychosocial change; design can recognise and transform it rather than only treat symptoms.
- What it is: Interviews with twelve women and a design workshop produced concepts that bring design close to the body during menopause, resisting its framing as a medical problem.
- How it works: Interviews with 12 women and a participatory design workshop with three, analysed from a holistic, body-first perspective.
- Lens: Body politics, Care & healing, Body-mind unity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3411764.3445153 (CHI 2021)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Qr9K9ScP6BI

#### Parting the Red Sea: Lived Experiences of Menopause — Amanda Lazar, Jeffrey Bardzell, Shaowen Bardzell (2019)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Intimate & hormonal body, Heat & temperature, Pain & inner sensations
- Idea: A body in transition is not a list of symptoms; its changes become meaningful through shared stories, so design should support that sense-making rather than only track signs.
- What it is: A study of a menopause forum on Reddit, where women make sense of hot flushes, bleeding and mood together, showing that the experience of menopause is social as much as bodily.
- How it works: Qualitative analysis of posts in a menopause subreddit, informed by feminist social science methods that centre women's lived experience.
- Lens: Body politics, Care & healing
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3290605.3300710 (CHI 2019)

### Queer & Trans Bodies

Soma design with and for queer, trans and non-normative bodies.

#### The Bodies of TEI — Katta Spiel (2021)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: Designing 'for the body' always means designing for some bodies; soma design has to ask whose felt experience it takes as the norm.
- What it is: A review of papers at the TEI conference that asks which bodies embodied interaction design assumes, and finds a default body that is able, binary-gendered and normatively sized.
- How it works: Systematic analysis of TEI papers for how bodies are described, sensed and addressed, from a critical, queer and disability-studies stance.
- Lens: Body politics, Body-mind unity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3430524.3440651 (TEI 2021)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-UKwgW2Ok6Q

#### Designing Trans Technology — Oliver L. Haimson (2020)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Intimate & hormonal body, Voice, sound & listening
- Idea: Trans communities name body change as a design space of their own; design that centres them treats transition as care for a felt self.
- What it is: Participatory design sessions with 21 trans people that produced ideas for technologies for changing bodies, changing appearance, safety and finding resources.
- How it works: Participatory design workshops with trans participants, analysed into four categories of envisioned technologies and challenges.
- Lens: Body politics, Care & healing
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3313831.3376669 (CHI 2020)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ih3gPbmk4p8

#### Reimagining (Women's) Health: HCI, Gender and Essentialised Embodiment — Os Keyes, Katta Spiel (2020)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Intimate & hormonal body
- Idea: Tying care to a presumed body leaves out the people whose felt bodies differ; health design should start from lived embodiment, not from categories.
- What it is: A critique of 'women's health' research in HCI that ties health needs to assumed female anatomy, and a call to design for trans, intersex and non-binary people whose bodies do not fit that model.
- How it works: Critical review of women's health literature in HCI, informed by trans and queer theory.
- Lens: Body politics, Body data & ambiguity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3404218 (ACM TOCHI 2020)

#### Patching Gender: Non-binary Utopias in HCI — Katta Spiel, Os Keyes (2019)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Whole body-mind
- Idea: Systems that fix gender also fix how bodies may be felt and read; non-binary utopias let design imagine bodies otherwise.
- What it is: A short speculative piece imagining technologies that treat gender as open and changeable rather than as a fixed binary field in a system.
- How it works: Speculative, utopian writing grounded in non-binary experience, proposing ways to 'patch' gender in computing.
- Lens: Body politics
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3290607.3310425 (CHI 2019 Extended Abstracts)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SQozcPAftps

#### Project Spectra — Alex A. Ahmed (2018)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Voice, sound & listening, Breath
- Idea: The voice is a felt part of gendered embodiment; training it is somatic practice, and the trainee, not the software, decides what sounds right.
- What it is: A free, open-source voice training app built with an online community of trans people, with exercises, warm-ups, pitch tracking and goals each user sets for their own voice.
- How it works: Mobile app with real-time pitch tracking and guided exercises, developed through community-based participatory design on Discord and GitHub.
- Lens: Body politics, First-person & felt experience, Care & healing
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3434167 (CSCW 2021 (PACM HCI))
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9I6x4mlwChQ

## Health, Care & Wellbeing

Somatic approaches to pain, rehabilitation, stress and mental health, where feeling better starts from feeling more.

### Chronic Pain & Rehabilitation

Movement confidence, pain and physical rehabilitation.

#### Self-Tracking to Do Less — Sarah Homewood (2023)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Heartbeat, Pain & inner sensations
- Idea: With a chronically ill body, the goal is to feel limits early; tracking can support listening to the body instead of pushing past it.
- What it is: An autoethnography of living with long COVID, in which the author used self-tracking to pace activity and rest, turning tracking from a tool for doing more into one for doing less.
- How it works: Longitudinal first-person account of self-tracking heart rate and activity for pacing, analysed to derive design implications for pacing technologies.
- Lens: First-person & felt experience, Body data & ambiguity, Care & healing
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3544548.3581505 (CHI 2023)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4FkcETHdxNc

#### Supporting Everyday Function in Chronic Pain Using Wearable Technology — Aneesha Singh, Nadia Bianchi-Berthouze (2017)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Voice, sound & listening
- Idea: Rehabilitation lives in ordinary movement; sensing the body during daily chores helps people pace and trust it where pain actually happens.
- What it is: A home study in which people with chronic pain used a movement-sonification wearable during everyday tasks such as cooking and cleaning, not only during exercise.
- How it works: Wearable sonification device deployed at home with people with chronic pain, studied through interviews and diaries.
- Lens: Care & healing, Body data & ambiguity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3025453.3025947 (CHI 2017)

#### Musically Informed Sonification for Chronic Pain Rehabilitation — Joseph W. Newbold, Nadia Bianchi-Berthouze (2016)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Voice, sound & listening
- Idea: Musical expectation is felt in the body; a phrase that wants to resolve can guide movement without the anxiety of numbers.
- What it is: A sonification that plays a musical phrase as a person with chronic pain stretches, resolving at the target point so the listener feels when to stop and when progress was made.
- How it works: Movement sensors drive harmonic musical phrases whose cadence resolves at a set range of motion; tested with people with chronic pain.
- Lens: Care & healing, Movement & expression
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2858036.2858302 (CHI 2016)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=OrIKRs5FeQI

#### Go-with-the-Flow — Aneesha Singh, Nadia Bianchi-Berthouze (2015)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Breath, Voice, sound & listening
- Idea: Sound gives a fearful body a new way to feel itself; hearing movement and breath rebuilds confidence to move, so feeling better starts from feeling more.
- What it is: A wearable that turns movement and breathing into sound while people with chronic pain exercise, so they can hear how far they have stretched and when they hold their breath.
- How it works: Smartphone-based wearable and Kinect versions track trunk movement and breathing and sonify them within user-defined exercise spaces; studied with people with chronic pain and physiotherapists.
- Lens: Care & healing, Attunement & appreciation
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1080/07370024.2015.1085310 (Human–Computer Interaction (journal))
- Project page: https://discovery.ucl.ac.uk/1472796/

#### Motivating People with Chronic Pain to Do Physical Activity — Aneesha Singh, Nadia Bianchi-Berthouze (2014)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Pain & inner sensations
- Idea: Pain is a felt state of body and mind together; technology for rehabilitation should rebuild trust in the moving body rather than count steps.
- What it is: Interviews with people living with chronic pain and with physiotherapists about why moving is hard, which found that fear, anxiety and lost trust in the body, not lack of information, hold people back.
- How it works: Qualitative interviews and focus groups with people with chronic pain and clinicians, leading to design opportunities for affect-aware activity support.
- Lens: Care & healing, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2556288.2557268 (CHI 2014)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gNmVlW9gcFY

#### The Virtual Meditative Walk — Diane Gromala (2011)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Breath, Pain & inner sensations, Light & vision
- Idea: Instead of distracting from pain, the VR teaches a different relationship to it, so pain is attended to calmly rather than escaped.
- What it is: An immersive VR walk through a forest with guided mindfulness for people living with chronic pain; the fog lifts as the person's physiological arousal drops.
- How it works: Head-mounted display VR environment with mindfulness-based stress reduction audio and biofeedback from skin conductance controlling fog density; studied with chronic pain patients.
- Lens: Care & healing, Attunement & appreciation
- Shown at: Shown at CHI 2011
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2702123.2702344 (CHI 2015)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=IX9Mukjf3n8

#### SnowWorld — Hunter G. Hoffman (2000)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Pain & inner sensations, Light & vision
- Idea: Pain is shaped by attention: when the mind is absorbed in a cold, playful world, the same wound hurts less. It is the classic case of VR as distraction, and a contrast to designs that teach people to attend to pain.
- What it is: An icy virtual canyon with snowmen, penguins and snowballs to throw, experienced in a head-mounted display by burn patients while their wounds are cleaned and dressed.
- How it works: Head-mounted display VR used during burn wound care and physical therapy, compared with standard care in clinical studies; the first 2000 studies likely used an earlier VR world, with SnowWorld built for burn patients soon after.
- Lens: Care & healing, Body-mind unity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1016/s0304-3959(99)00275-4 (Pain 2000)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Nh4K_dZ7LvQ
- Project page: https://www.hitl.washington.edu/projects/vrpain/

#### Mirror Box — V. S. Ramachandran (1995)
- Type: Method & toolkit · Body & senses: Light & vision, Pain & inner sensations, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: The felt body is a living construction of vision, movement and expectation; changing what one sees can change what one feels.
- What it is: A box with a mirror down the middle: an amputee places the intact arm beside the mirror, sees its reflection where the missing limb should be, and moves both, which can relieve cramping phantom pain.
- How it works: A vertical mirror in a cardboard box creates the visual illusion of two intact limbs; patients perform symmetric movements while watching the reflection.
- Lens: Estrangement, Care & healing, Body-mind unity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1038/377489a0 (Nature 1995)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jNTCFSuKEMA
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/4/4a/Mirror-box-comic.jpg

### Stress, Anxiety & Mental Health

Biofeedback, breath and body awareness for stress and mental health.

#### Conversations With The Stressed Body — Xinglin Sun (2025)
- Type: Method & toolkit · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Touch & pressure, Pain & inner sensations
- Idea: Starting from bodily sensation makes stress easier to speak about: the body opens a path to self-disclosure that words alone block.
- What it is: A co-design workshop in which nine girls aged 15–18 used Embodied Probes, artefacts and bodily activities, to locate, express and discuss the sensations of stress, then designed prototypes for their own stress relief.
- How it works: Workshop sessions with body mapping, tangible expression materials and embodied prototyping, analysed qualitatively.
- Lens: First-person & felt experience, Care & healing, Body politics
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3715336.3735707 (DIS 2025)

#### HCI and Affective Health — Pedro Sanches, Pavel Karpashevich, Muhammad Umair, Kristina Höök (2019)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Skin conductance & arousal
- Idea: Producing more body data is not care; mental health technology should help people live with and act on what they feel.
- What it is: A review of 139 CHI-family papers on depression, anxiety and bipolar disorder finds most innovation in automated diagnosis and self-tracking, and calls for interventions and ethical involvement of people living with these conditions.
- How it works: Systematic literature review of ten years of SIGCHI proceedings, coded for condition, approach, technology and evaluation.
- Lens: Care & healing, Body data & ambiguity, Body politics
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3290605.3300475 (CHI 2019)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=IlAAXSIb4WA

#### Purrble — Nikki Theofanopoulou, Petr Slovák, Katherine Isbister (2019)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Vibration & haptics, Heartbeat
- Idea: Soothing another body in one's lap is a way of soothing one's own; emotion regulation is practised through the hands, in the moment it is needed.
- What it is: A plush creature whose heartbeat races when a child picks it up; stroking it slows the heartbeat until it purrs, so the child calms the toy and, in doing so, calms down.
- How it works: Soft toy with touch sensors and a vibration motor that simulates heartbeat and purring; deployed at home with children in a feasibility study, later commercialised.
- Lens: Care & healing, Technology that touches back, Affective loops
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.2196/14029 (JMIR Mental Health 2019)
- Project page: https://purrble.com/

#### Inner Garden — Joan Sol Roo, Martin Hachet (2017)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Breath, Heartbeat, Touch & pressure
- Idea: Tending a small world with one's hands and breath turns inner states into something to care for rather than to control.
- What it is: A sandbox landscape shaped by hand and brought to life with projected water and weather that respond to the person's breathing and heart rhythm, used as a space for mindfulness practice.
- How it works: Spatial augmented reality projects onto a physical sandbox tracked by a depth camera; breathing and heart-rate sensors drive the simulated weather and sea; optional VR view for meditation.
- Lens: Attunement & appreciation, Care & healing
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3025453.3025743 (CHI 2017)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=6a4URQLt2Yw

#### Life Tree — Rakesh Patibanda, Florian 'Floyd' Mueller (2017)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Breath
- Idea: Breath practice sticks when it feels like play; slowing the breath becomes a pleasure rather than a prescription.
- What it is: A virtual reality game in which slow, deep breaths make a tree grow and bloom; a study examined what makes breathing exercise engaging enough to return to.
- How it works: Breath sensed through a chest-worn sensor drives the growth of a tree in a VR scene; field study with players to derive design strategies for breathing games.
- Lens: Attunement & appreciation, Pleasure & play
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3116595.3116621 (CHI PLAY 2017)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7khAiBdbLmg

#### Fidget Widgets — Michael Karlesky, Katherine Isbister (2013)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: Fidgeting is the body regulating the mind; small objects for the hands can support emotional self-regulation without a screen.
- What it is: A set of small tangible objects to click, squeeze, spin and roll at the desk, designed from a study of how people fidget to manage stress, focus and boredom during digital work.
- How it works: Survey of fidgeting habits and a range of physical prototypes with distinct tactile qualities; later NordiCHI study of their use.
- Lens: Attunement & appreciation, Care & healing
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2971485.2971557 (NordiCHI 2016)

#### Sonic Cradle — Jay Vidyarthi, Bernhard E. Riecke, Diane Gromala (2012)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Breath, Voice, sound & listening
- Idea: Hearing one's own breath as music makes attention settle in the body without instruction; calm arrives through feeling, not effort.
- What it is: A person lies suspended in a hammock-like chair in a dark chamber; a respiration belt turns each breath into layers of ambient music, and people without meditation experience drift into calm attention.
- How it works: Chest respiration sensor maps breathing patterns to generative ambient sound in a light-free, suspended setup; studied with first-time users through interviews.
- Lens: Attunement & appreciation, Body-mind unity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2317956.2318017 (DIS 2012)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kB8qfJ1M0gg
- Project page: https://ispace.iat.sfu.ca/project/sonic-cradle/

#### Affective Health — Pedro Sanches, Kristina Höök (2010)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Skin conductance & arousal, Heartbeat, Movement & kinesthesia
