- Idea: Stress cannot be read off the body as a diagnosis; it is better to mirror bodily reactions and let people interpret them in the context of their lives.
- What it is: Affective Health is a mobile stress-management service that mirrors short-term stress reactions measured by wearable sensors back to users as a moving spiral of colour, so they can reflect on their own patterns.
- How it works: Skin conductance, heart rate and movement sensors stream to a phone that renders arousal and activity as layered and spiral visualisations over time; designed through a multidisciplinary process at SICS.
- Lens: Body data & ambiguity, Care & healing, Affective loops
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/1858171.1858182 (MobileHCI 2010)
- Images: https://figures.semanticscholar.org/352cfe0ae89771b68d9f0cbe9e9ec13d503ae0e1/7-Figure4-1.png https://figures.semanticscholar.org/352cfe0ae89771b68d9f0cbe9e9ec13d503ae0e1/5-Figure1-1.png

### Neurodiversity & Motor Conditions

Parkinson's, stroke, autism, sensory differences.

#### Expressive Bodies — Katta Spiel (2022)
- Type: Method & toolkit · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Disabled bodies carry expert knowledge of how technology feels; letting critique happen through movement values that knowledge.
- What it is: A design critique method drawn from disability dance and embodied disability cultures, in which disabled participants use movement to respond to and critique technology designs.
- How it works: Workshops pairing embodied practices from disability arts with collaborative design critique.
- Lens: Body politics, Movement & expression
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3517428.3551350 (ASSETS 2022)

#### Magic Room — Franca Garzotto (2018)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Light & vision, Voice, sound & listening, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: A responsive room lets each child explore the senses at their own pace; the environment adapts to the body instead of the other way round.
- What it is: A smart multisensory room where lights, projections, sounds, bubbles and smart objects respond to children's movement and touch, used in therapy for neurodevelopmental conditions.
- How it works: Room-scale installation with projections, smart lights, sensors and connected toys, configurable by therapists; deployed in therapy centres and schools.
- Lens: Care & healing, Pleasure & play
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1109/mprv.2018.011591060 (IEEE Pervasive Computing 2018)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=yDbFbPbD3do

#### Embodied Companion Technologies for Autistic Children — Katta Spiel (2016)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: Designing with autistic children's own sensory pleasures respects their embodied experience instead of normalising it.
- What it is: Smart objects co-designed with autistic children, each built around what that child enjoys and how they move and sense, rather than around deficits to be corrected.
- How it works: Participatory design over several months with individual autistic children, producing bespoke sensor-actuator objects; evaluated through the children's own experience.
- Lens: Body politics, Care & healing, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2839462.2839495 (TEI 2016)

#### Emma Watch — Haiyan Zhang (2016)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Vibration & haptics, Muscle tension (EMG), Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: A rhythm felt on the skin can change how a body moves: designing with one person's lived experience produced care that feels like getting one's hand back.
- What it is: A wrist-worn device with vibrating motors that reduced the hand tremor of Emma Lawton, a graphic designer with early-onset Parkinson's, enough for her to write and draw straight lines again.
- How it works: Wrist wearable with an array of vibration motors running a pattern (likely distracting the brain's feedback loop), designed with Emma Lawton for the BBC's Big Life Fix at Microsoft Research.
- Lens: Care & healing, Technology that touches back, First-person & felt experience
- Shown at: BBC Big Life Fix (2016); Microsoft Build 2017
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=R6rAlFYDffQ
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haiyan_Zhang

#### Lands of Fog — Joan Mora-Guiard, Narcís Parés (2016)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Whole body-mind, Light & vision
- Idea: Social connection can grow from moving in the same space; the body opens the encounter before words do.
- What it is: A floor-projected world covered in fog that two children, an autistic child and a neurotypical peer, clear by moving together, discovering creatures that invite shared play.
- How it works: Full-body interaction on a floor projection tracked by overhead sensors, with a butterfly-net tangible; studied with pairs of children with and without autism.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Pleasure & play, Care & healing
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2930674.2930695 (IDC 2016)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=klWT4csu8WI
- Images: https://www.upf.edu/documents/8512687/8513142/Lands+of+Fog+2+Users/3ac024f2-543f-5ab1-9065-128b45070eee?t=1536586692812
- Project page: https://www.upf.edu/web/fubintlab/lands-of-fog

#### CueS — Amey Holden, Madeline Balaam (2015)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Vibration & haptics
- Idea: A gentle touch on the wrist can bring a neglected limb back into awareness; rehabilitation happens between sessions, in the body's daily habits.
- What it is: A wrist-worn device that senses how much a stroke survivor uses the affected arm and gives vibration cues to use it more during everyday life.
- How it works: Wrist-worn accelerometers measure arm activity and trigger vibrotactile prompts; studied with stroke survivors at home.
- Lens: Care & healing, Technology that touches back
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2783446.2783576 (British HCI 2015)

#### SensoryPaint — Kathryn E. Ringland (2014)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Touch & pressure, Light & vision
- Idea: Sensory integration works best when it feels like play; the whole body becomes the brush.
- What it is: A large projected canvas where children with neurodevelopmental conditions paint by moving textured balls and their own bodies, turning sensory therapy into play.
- How it works: Depth camera tracks balls of different textures and body movement in front of a projection; colour and sound respond; studied in a sensory therapy setting.
- Lens: Care & healing, Pleasure & play
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2632048.2632065 (UbiComp 2014)

#### Motivating Mobility: Lived Motivation in Stroke Rehabilitation — Madeline Balaam (2011)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: Rehabilitation is lived in a whole life, not in exercise counts; motivation comes from what movement means to the person.
- What it is: A study of how stroke survivors actually find motivation to rehabilitate at home, and the design of home rehabilitation technologies around their lived, changing motivations.
- How it works: Participatory design and home deployment of rehabilitation prototypes with stroke survivors, analysed for lived motivation.
- Lens: Care & healing, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/1978942.1979397 (CHI 2011)

#### Dance for PD — Mark Morris Dance Group (2001)
- Type: Somatic practice · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Posture, balance & proprioception, Voice, sound & listening
- Idea: People with Parkinson's are addressed as dancers; music and imagery can unlock movement that effortful control cannot.
- What it is: Dance classes for people with Parkinson's disease taught by professional dancers, using live music, imagery and choreography rather than exercise drills.
- How it works: Seated and standing dance classes with live music taught worldwide; teacher training programme run by the Mark Morris Dance Group with the Brooklyn Parkinson Group.
- Lens: Care & healing, Movement & expression
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4LwWkFHpve0
- Images: https://danceforparkinsons.org/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/DancePD-8069-by-Amber-Star-Merkens-scaled.jpg
- Project page: https://danceforparkinsons.org/

#### Snoezelen — Jan Hulsegge, Ad Verheul (1978)
- Type: Somatic practice · Body & senses: Light & vision, Touch & pressure, Voice, sound & listening
- Idea: A space that asks nothing lets a body settle and explore at its own pace; well-being comes through sensing, not performance.
- What it is: A multisensory room with soft light, bubble tubes, music, textures and scents where people with intellectual disabilities, autism or dementia can rest and explore without a task.
- How it works: Controlled multisensory environment with an accompanying therapist; the name combines Dutch 'snuffelen' (to sniff, explore) and 'doezelen' (to doze).
- Lens: Attunement & appreciation, Care & healing
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Lhel1pU5kq8
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/8/84/Snoezelroom.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snoezelen

### Ageing, Care & Caregiving

Bodies in care: older adults, caregivers, touch in care.

#### HUG — Cathy Treadaway (2018)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Heartbeat, Voice, sound & listening
- Idea: When words fail, care continues through touch, rhythm and sound; a held heartbeat can calm a body that is anxious.
- What it is: A soft, cuddly companion with long arms, a beating heart and a speaker that plays the person's favourite music, made for people living with advanced dementia.
- How it works: Weighted soft body with an embedded vibration heartbeat and music player activated by hugging; co-designed with people with dementia and carers and studied in care settings.
- Lens: Care & healing, Technology that touches back
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.3390/ijerph20054410 (IJERPH 2023)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hRQ8VaAMzI0
- Images: https://www.laughproject.info/wp-content/uploads/2020/09/HUG-crowdfunder-1024x577.png
- Project page: https://www.laughproject.info

#### Tovertafel — Hester Anderiesen-Le Riche (2015)
- Type: Product & app · Body & senses: Light & vision, Movement & kinesthesia, Touch & pressure
- Idea: Play invites apathetic bodies to reach and touch again; activation comes through the hands and shared laughter, not instructions.
- What it is: A ceiling-mounted projector that throws interactive light games onto a care-home table: residents with dementia sweep leaves, catch fish or bat a ball of light with their hands.
- How it works: Projector with infrared sensors above a table detects hand and arm movements and updates the projected games; developed from a TU Delft PhD with people with dementia.
- Lens: Care & healing, Pleasure & play
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=RIK53oFgyms
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/8/81/Tovertafel_%282015%29.jpg
- Project page: https://www.tovertafel.com/

## Social & Collective Soma

Intercorporeality: feeling each other's bodies through mediated touch, shared breath and heartbeat, and collective movement.

### Social & Mediated Touch

Touch at a distance, hugs, holding hands through technology.

#### Embodied Telepresent Connection (ETC) — John Desnoyers-Stewart, Ekaterina R. Stepanova, Kristina Höök (2023)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Light & vision, Heartbeat
- Idea: Social touch in VR need not imitate physical tactility; an ephemeral, suggested touch can still carry play and intimacy.
- What it is: A VR artwork where two people meet as ethereal, glowing bodies and 'touch' each other through visual and sound effects rather than any device on the skin, with subtle biosignals in the scene.
- How it works: Pseudo-haptic visual and audio feedback at virtual contact points between networked avatars; developed in soma design-inspired workshops with 14 HCI experts and shown to 300+ visitors.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Technology that touches back
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3800645.3812937 (DIS 2026)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3OW05odYNHI

#### SymbioSinging (ADA) — Kelsey Cotton, Ozgun Kilic Afsar, Yoav Luft (2021)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Breath, Shape-change & inflatables, Voice, sound & listening
- Idea: Somatic knowledge can be partly transposed from one body to another through touch.
- What it is: SymbioSinging is a soft robotic garment, ADA, that re-enacts the felt movements of singing on the body of someone who is not singing.
- How it works: A pneumatically controlled, fibre-based air-driven actuator garment developed through a soma design process with a singer.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Technology that touches back
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3450741.3466718 (Creativity and Cognition 2021)
- Images: https://figures.semanticscholar.org/5578691d7f746c8e071db6559a505de3a54bbf15/2-Figure1-1.png https://figures.semanticscholar.org/5578691d7f746c8e071db6559a505de3a54bbf15/3-Figure2-1.png

#### Interdisciplinary Insights for Digital Touch Communication — Carey Jewitt (2020)
- Type: Book & essay · Body & senses: Touch & pressure
- Idea: Touch is social and cultural before it is technical: designing digital touch means designing who may touch whom, how and when.
- What it is: An open-access book from the IN-TOUCH project that brings together design, social science and computing to ask how digital touch changes intimacy, care and social norms.
- How it works: Speculative design workshops, interviews and case studies with artists, engineers and the public, analysed through multimodal social semiotics.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Body politics
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-24564-1 (Springer HCI Series 2020)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CiVFakYPlQo

#### Social Touch Technology: A Survey of Haptic Technology for Social Touch — Gijs Huisman (2017)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Vibration & haptics
- Idea: Designs for touch at a distance work when they respect what skin evolved to feel: slow, warm, gentle stroking reads as care.
- What it is: A survey of devices that mediate social touch, from hugging shirts to robot pets, read against the psychology and neuroscience of affective touch.
- How it works: Literature review organised by touch type, device and study outcome, drawing on C-tactile afferent research.
- Lens: Intercorporeality
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1109/toh.2017.2650221 (IEEE Transactions on Haptics 2017)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qXoQ4r5BhCs

#### Frebble — Holland Haptics, Alexander Toet (2014)
- Type: Product & app · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Vibration & haptics
- Idea: Holding hands is a small, continuous touch; a mediated version can change how shared moments feel even at a distance.
- What it is: A palm-sized device you hold like a hand: squeezing it makes the partner's Frebble press against the back of their hand, so two people can hold hands during a call or a film.
- How it works: Pressure sensors and vibration actuators in a hand-shaped shell, linked over the internet; TNO tested it during a remotely shared sad film.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Technology that touches back
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.7717/peerj.1297 (PeerJ 2015)
- Images: https://dfzljdn9uc3pi.cloudfront.net/2015/1297/1/fig-1-2x.jpg

#### TaSST: Tactile Sleeve for Social Touch — Gijs Huisman (2013)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Vibration & haptics
- Idea: Social touch is carried by its pattern in time and space, a stroke versus a tap, more than by force alone.
- What it is: A pair of forearm sleeves: stroking or squeezing the pressure-sensitive layer of one sleeve is played back as a pattern of vibration on the other person's arm.
- How it works: A grid of conductive-foam pressure sensors on one sleeve maps onto a matching grid of vibration motors on the other.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Technology that touches back
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1109/whc.2013.6548410 (IEEE World Haptics Conference 2013)

#### Hug over a Distance — Florian 'Floyd' Mueller (2005)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Shape-change & inflatables
- Idea: An embrace is felt as pressure closing around the torso; the vest designs that closure rather than a signal of affection.
- What it is: An air-inflatable vest lets couples in long-distance relationships send a hug: when one partner squeezes a koala-shaped soft toy, the other's vest inflates around the torso.
- How it works: A PDA-linked koala with a pressure sensor triggers a pump that inflates air pockets in the partner's vest; tested with couples in a field study.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Technology that touches back
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/1056808.1056994 (CHI 2005 Extended Abstracts)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XtG4UzezQWk

#### The Hug Shirt — CuteCircuit (2004)
- Type: Product & app · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Heat & temperature, Heartbeat
- Idea: A hug is a whole-body event of pressure, warmth and rhythm, so sending one means designing for where and how it lands on the skin.
- What it is: A shirt with sensors and actuators where a hug would land: a hug sent from a phone is replayed on the wearer's body as pressure, warmth and the sender's heartbeat.
- How it works: Sensors record the strength, duration and location of a hug; Bluetooth and a phone app relay it to actuators and heating elements in the partner's shirt.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Technology that touches back
- Shown at: TIME Best Inventions of the Year 2006
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4MbzMiEGn5s
- Images: https://cutecircuit.com/images/cute2/archive/7d18d69954180842.webp https://cutecircuit.com/images/cute2/archive/b940be00f887ec0b.webp
- Project page: https://cutecircuit.com/hugshirt/

#### The Hug — Carl DiSalvo (2003)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Vibration & haptics, Heat & temperature
- Idea: A robot for intimacy should first be shaped to be held; its form invites the embrace before any function does.
- What it is: A soft, arm-shaped robotic pillow for elders and their families: hugging it sends a hug to a matching pillow, which then vibrates, warms and plays the caller's voice.
- How it works: Pressure and stroke sensors in the pillow's arms connect over a phone line to vibration motors, heating pads and audio in the paired pillow.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Technology that touches back
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1109/roman.2003.1251879 (IEEE RO-MAN 2003)

#### ComTouch — Angela Chang, Scott Brave (2002)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Vibration & haptics, Voice, sound & listening
- Idea: People invent their own tactile vocabulary, emphasis, turn-taking and play, as soon as touch is added to talk.
- What it is: A sleeve on a mobile phone turns the pressure of each finger into vibration under the other caller's fingers, so a conversation carries squeezes as well as words.
- How it works: Force-sensitive resistors under the fingers drive vibrotactile actuators on the remote device, tested with pairs of users in a lab study.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Technology that touches back
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/778712.778755 (DIS 2002)
- Project page: https://tangible.media.mit.edu/project/comtouch/

#### inTouch — Scott Brave (1997)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: Touch at a distance works best as a shared, two-way physical medium that both bodies can push and feel at once, not as a message sent from one to the other.
- What it is: Two sets of three wooden rollers are linked by motors: when one person turns a roller, the matching roller under the other person's hand turns too, and both can push against each other.
- How it works: Force-feedback rollers with position sensors and motors synchronise two remote objects so that both users feel the same state and each other's resistance.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Technology that touches back
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/289444.289491 (CSCW 1998)
- Project page: https://tangible.media.mit.edu/project/intouch/

### Shared Breath, Heartbeat & Synchrony

Sharing biosignals and falling into rhythm with others.

#### Cell Space — Rem RunGu Lin, Botao 'Amber' Hu (2024)
- Type: Performance, dance & music · Body & senses: Heartbeat, Touch & pressure, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: Making biosignals visible between touching bodies extends intercorporeality: partners sense each other's inner state as part of the dance.
- What it is: Dancers and visitors practising contact improvisation each occupy a mixed reality 'cell' whose fluid boundary shows their heartbeat and pressure in real time, so the cells merge and split as bodies meet.
- How it works: Co-located AR headsets rendering Voronoi cells around tracked participants, fed by heart rate and pressure sensors (neurofeedback).
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Body data & ambiguity, Movement & expression
- Shown at: SIGGRAPH 2024 Art Papers; TEI 2025; IEEE VR 2025 XR Gallery
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3689050.3707678 (TEI 2025 Art and Performance)
- Video: https://vimeo.com/915076535
- Images: https://reality.design/media/project-cell-space-gallery-01.jpg
- Project page: https://reality.design/project/cell-space

#### Shared User Interfaces of Physiological Data — Clara Moge (2022)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Heartbeat, Breath, Skin conductance & arousal
- Idea: Shared body signals change relationships at three levels, within oneself, between people and around a shared task, and their meaning depends on timing and social context.
- What it is: A systematic review of 64 studies of social biofeedback, systems in which two or more people see, hear or feel each other's heart rate, breath or arousal.
- How it works: PRISMA-style systematic review of HCI literature, synthesised into the Social Biofeedback Interactions framework.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Body data & ambiguity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3491102.3517495 (CHI 2022)

#### Evoking Physiological Synchrony and Empathy Using Social VR with Biofeedback — Mikko Salminen, Simo Järvelä (2019)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Breath, Brain (EEG), Whole body-mind
- Idea: Seeing another body's rhythm alongside one's own can pull two people into sync and toward empathy.
- What it is: Pairs do a compassion meditation together in social VR while seeing each other's breathing rate and an EEG-based empathy signal; the more their signals synchronised, the more empathy they reported.
- How it works: Lab study (N = 72) in the DYNECOM VR meditation environment with respiration and EEG frontal-asymmetry biofeedback, shown solo or shared; a companion paper (Järvelä et al. 2021) reports respiratory synchrony and social presence.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Body data & ambiguity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1109/taffc.2019.2958657 (IEEE Transactions on Affective Computing)

#### Synchrony and Social Connection in Immersive Virtual Reality — Bronwyn Tarr, Mel Slater (2018)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Moving in time with others is itself a source of felt closeness, even when the others are virtual and no shared intention is involved.
- What it is: Participants in VR move in time with two virtual co-participants whose timing is secretly set to be in or out of sync with them; those in sync feel closer to their virtual partners.
- How it works: Immersive VR with full-body avatars in a joint movement task; timing of virtual co-participants covertly manipulated; social closeness measured by questionnaire.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Movement & expression
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-018-21765-4 (Scientific Reports 2018)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9GQTLwUaA1I

#### bioSync — Jun Nishida (2017)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Muscle tension (EMG), Movement & kinesthesia, Touch & pressure
- Idea: Empathy can be kinesthetic: feeling another's movement in your own muscles gives an understanding that watching cannot.
- What it is: A pair of wearables that read muscle activity from one person and play it into another person's muscles through electrical stimulation, so that, for example, a therapist can feel a patient's tremor or stiffness in their own arm.
- How it works: Wearable electrodes that switch between EMG sensing and EMS actuation, linking two people bidirectionally in real time.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Care & healing
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3025453.3025829 (CHI 2017)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=vOyt4pA6wow

#### Bounden — Game Oven (2014)
- Type: Product & app · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Touch & pressure
- Idea: A shared object held by two hands can choreograph bodies into closeness, so the game designs the dance rather than the screen.
- What it is: A two-player game on one phone: both players hold an end of the phone and follow a path on a sphere, which turns them into a couple dancing around each other.
- How it works: The phone's gyroscope tracks rotation along choreographies developed with the Dutch National Ballet.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Pleasure & play, Movement & expression
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Gete61IxkPo
- Project page: https://playbounden.com

#### Yamove! — Katherine Isbister (2012)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Rewarding synchrony rather than individual skill choreographs social connection: partners must watch and feel each other to win.
- What it is: A dance-battle game for pairs: each dancer holds a phone, and the game scores how energetic, varied and above all synchronised the partners move.
- How it works: Phone accelerometers track both dancers; an emcee and audience frame the play; studies observed how pairs coordinate.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Pleasure & play
- Shown at: IndieCade 2012 Nominee
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.17011/ht/urn.201605192621 (Human Technology 2016)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BimobsCaGzo

#### United-pulse — Julia Werner (2008)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Heartbeat, Touch & pressure
- Idea: A pulse felt on the skin is a quiet way of being with someone, closer to presence than to messaging.
- What it is: A pair of rings for lovers apart: each ring measures its wearer's pulse and taps it softly onto the partner's finger.
- How it works: A pulse sensor and a small actuator in each ring, likely linked wirelessly through the wearers' phones.
- Lens: Intercorporeality
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/1409240.1409338 (MobileHCI 2008)

#### exhale: breath between bodies — Thecla Schiphorst (2005)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Breath, Vibration & haptics, Touch & pressure
- Idea: Breath can be a shared, felt medium between bodies; the garment makes the space between people breathable.
- What it is: A group of networked skirts that respond to their wearers' breath: small fans and vibrations let each wearer feel the breath of others in the room.
- How it works: Breath sensors on each wearer drive fans and vibrotactile actuators embedded in the skirts of the other wearers over a wireless network.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Attunement & appreciation
- Shown at: SIGGRAPH 2005 Emerging Technologies
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/1187297.1187304 (SIGGRAPH 2005 Emerging Technologies)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_7XKOa8JH1U

### Collective & Participatory Somatics

Groups moving, breathing and sensing together.

#### Body Oracle — Ke Huang, Danlin Huang, Botao 'Amber' Hu (2024)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Posture, balance & proprioception, Movement & kinesthesia, Light & vision
- Idea: A language that has words for postures and for how bodies stand together could make people more aware of their own and each other's bodies.
- What it is: A camera reads the poses of one or several visitors and an AI model turns them into new pictograms styled on Chinese oracle-bone inscriptions, a written vocabulary for bodily and inter-bodily positions.
- How it works: MoveNet keypoint detection feeding an image-to-image model trained on paired examples of volunteers re-enacting oracle-bone characters.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Attunement & appreciation, Movement & expression
- Shown at: Ars Electronica Expanded 2025; SIGGRAPH Asia 2025 XR; IEEE VR 2026 XR Gallery
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3689050.3707688 (TEI 2025 Art and Performance)
- Video: https://vimeo.com/1020163664
- Images: https://reality.design/media/_resources/Body%20Oracle/project-body-oracle-figure-01.jpg
- Project page: https://reality.design/project/body-oracle

#### GravField — Botao 'Amber' Hu (2024)
- Type: Performance, dance & music · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Voice, sound & listening, Light & vision
- Idea: Virtual objects with felt 'digital physics' can nudge a group into moving together without choreography, while leaving each person their agency.
- What it is: Participants wearing mixed reality headsets move together while an 'object jockey' live-codes virtual ropes, springs and magnetic fields between their bodies; distance, formation and synchrony between the bodies generate music and visuals.
- How it works: Co-located HoloKit AR headsets tracking head poses, mapped to live-coded parameters (weight, elasticity, force) and sound; studied through video, interviews, soma trajectories and field notes.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Movement & expression, Pleasure & play
- Shown at: NIME 2025; SIGGRAPH Asia 2024 XR; IEEE VR 2025 XR Gallery
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3772318.3790651 (CHI 2026)
- Video: https://vimeo.com/955522087
- Images: https://reality.design/media/_resources/GravField/project-gravfield-figure-01.jpg
- Project page: https://reality.design/project/gravfield

#### Body RemiXer — John Desnoyers-Stewart, Ekaterina R. Stepanova (2020)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Movement & kinesthesia, Light & vision
- Idea: Blurring where one body ends and another begins can lower social boundaries, but it also unsettles one's sense of owning a body.
- What it is: A mixed-reality installation where participants' bodies appear as abstract, light-like avatars that stretch toward and mix with each other, inviting touch and synchronised movement between strangers.
- How it works: Asymmetric XR with body tracking, projection and a headset; body-mixing avatars; studied with phenomenological interviews.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Body-mind unity
- Shown at: SIGGRAPH 2020 Art Gallery
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3772318.3791023 (CHI 2026)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LHddyzvMYCY

#### JeL — John Desnoyers-Stewart, Ekaterina R. Stepanova (2019)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Breath, Light & vision
- Idea: Synchronised breath can link people to each other and to a living world at once.
- What it is: Two people in VR breathe to grow a coral reef of jellyfish-like forms; the reef grows most when their breathing falls into sync.
- How it works: Two VR headsets with breathing sensors; a shared virtual reef responds to each breath and to their synchrony; evaluated through interviews.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, More-than-human, Attunement & appreciation
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3357236.3395532 (DIS 2020)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jp91I0BuGhY
- Images: http://ispace.iat.sfu.ca/wp-content/uploads/2019/02/JeL_Diagram_Horizontal-1024x228.png
- Project page: https://ispace.iat.sfu.ca/project/jel/

#### Fairground: Thrill Laboratory — Brendan Walker, Steve Benford (2006)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Heartbeat, Skin conductance & arousal, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: Thrill is social: making a rider's bodily state visible turns spectators into participants in someone else's fear and joy.
- What it is: Riders on real fairground rides wear sensors and cameras; their heart rate, face and screams are shown live to the crowd, who watch the ride from the inside of another body.
- How it works: Telemetry of heart rate, acceleration, audio and video from riders streamed to a spectator display; studied as a performance frame.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Pleasure & play, Body data & ambiguity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/1357054.1357238 (CHI 2008)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=HUDJg-bZEmE

#### whisper[s] — Thecla Schiphorst, Susan Kozel (2003)
- Type: Performance, dance & music · Body & senses: Breath, Heartbeat, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: Somatic practice can be the design method: the garments grew from exercises in listening to one's own and others' inner rhythms.
- What it is: A participatory performance in which visitors wear networked garments that sense their breath and heartbeat and pass them to each other as touch, sound and light.
- How it works: Wireless wearables with breath and heart sensors plus vibration and sound outputs, developed through workshops of body-based listening; shown at DEAF03.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, First-person & felt experience
- Shown at: DEAF03, V2_ Rotterdam (2003)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=GEofDFj8WFY

## Soma with Machines, AI & XR

Soma design meets drones, robots, machine learning, virtual bodies and more-than-human others.

### Somaesthetic Robots & Drones

Drone Chi, soft and inflatable robots, robots felt through the body.

#### Somatic Safety — Steve Benford, Paul Tennent (2025)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Movement & kinesthesia, Posture, balance & proprioception
- Idea: Safety with robots can be felt and learned through contact, a mind-body skill, not only calculated as distance and force limits.
- What it is: Dancers use contact improvisation to dance with collaborative robot arms, pushing, leaning on and being moved by them, and learn through the body how to do so safely.
- How it works: Artistic exploration with dancers and cobots in iterative sessions of contact improvisation exercises and reflection, analysed by a multidisciplinary team.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, First-person & felt experience, Intercorporeality
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1109/hri61500.2025.10973822 (HRI 2025)

#### Drone Arena — Mousa Sondoqah, Kristina Popova, Joseph La Delfa, Rachael Garrett (2024)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Touch & pressure
- Idea: Shaping a machine's behaviour needs perception-action loops you can feel; programming becomes a bodily negotiation rather than pure instruction.
- What it is: In a three-day drone challenge at KTH, teams moved from aiming for seamless control to treating small drones as fragile, wilful and crash-prone, and used intimate bodily interactions to probe what their drone could do.
- How it works: Observation, interviews, field notes, video and inspection of each team's code from the Drone Arena challenge (Crazyflie nano-drones with an external positioning system), analysed through bricolage.
- Lens: More-than-human, Intercorporeality, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3643834.3661636 (DIS 2024)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kDzwTJadit8
- Images: https://figures.semanticscholar.org/469f723c597d62dd98e6fef227ffd4545a59e410/4-Figure1-1.png https://figures.semanticscholar.org/469f723c597d62dd98e6fef227ffd4545a59e410/6-Figure4-1.png

#### How to Train Your Drone — Joseph La Delfa, Rachael Garrett, Kristina Höök (2024)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Thinking of a drone's umwelt, its own sensory world, opens a design space where human and machine come to understand each other somatically.
- What it is: Three people sharing a home spent a month shaping what their drones could sense and how they responded, forming intimate, individual human-drone couplings.
- How it works: Drones whose sensing and behaviour can be trained through bodily interaction, deployed for a month in a shared household; umwelt used as a design metaphor within soma design.
- Lens: More-than-human, Intercorporeality, Movement & expression
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3643834.3660737 (DIS 2024)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4EagWgeGESI

#### Mechanical Sympathy — Joseph La Delfa, Rachael Garrett, Kristina Höök (2024)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Like a driver who feels an engine, people can cultivate appreciative, idiosyncratic relationships with machines that complement technical understanding.
- What it is: Drawing on How to Train Your Drone, the paper proposes mechanical sympathy, the feel a person develops for a machine's agency, oscillations and aesthetic possibilities, as a design concept for somaesthetic relations with machines.
- How it works: Descriptive and reflective accounts of design strategies and of co-creators living with the drones, distilled into three qualities: machine agency, oscillations and aesthetic pursuits.
- Lens: More-than-human, Attunement & appreciation, Intercorporeality
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3643834.3661514 (DIS 2024)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=pYzybU66dGE
- Images: https://figures.semanticscholar.org/bd75a121dc19350017f538dacc1b47e1d9ff7d5a/5-Figure2-1.png

#### A Calming Hug — Alice Haynes (2022)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Breath, Touch & pressure, Shape-change & inflatables
- Idea: The body entrains to a rhythm it can feel against it; calm can be passed on through touch without any screen or voice.
- What it is: A soft cushion held against the chest that slowly expands and contracts like breathing; people hugging it before a stressful test tended to slow their own breath to match.
- How it works: A shape-changing soft robotic cushion with an internal mechanism simulating breathing, validated in a lab study with an anxiety-inducing maths test.
- Lens: Technology that touches back, Care & healing, Attunement & appreciation
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0259838 (PLOS ONE 2022)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=66h4R4BL0W4

#### HuggieBot — Alexis E. Block (2021)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Heat & temperature, Shape-change & inflatables
- Idea: A good robot hug is defined by how it feels: soft, warm, the right size, responsive, and above all willing to release.
- What it is: A human-sized robot with a soft, warm, inflated chest and padded arms that sees a person approach, fits its embrace to their body and lets go when they want.
- How it works: HuggieBot 2.0 uses an inflated, heated torso with pressure sensing, a camera to see the user and arm control adapted to user size; evaluated in a user study that proposed six 'hug commandments'.
- Lens: Technology that touches back, Care & healing, Intercorporeality
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3434073.3444656 (HRI 2021)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jtEh0ZC32gA

#### Designing Drone Chi — Joseph La Delfa, Mehmet Aydın Baytaş, Florian 'Floyd' Mueller (2020)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Designing a robot for the soma is slow work that moves between the designer's own practice, product design and engineering.
- What it is: A pictorial that retraces how the sensory experience of tai chi was carried, step by step, into the form, flight and feel of the Drone Chi drone.
- How it works: Quasi-chronological design account with sketches, prototypes and first-person tai chi practice, reflecting on somaesthetic, product design and engineering perspectives.
- Lens: First-person & felt experience, Body-mind unity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3357236.3395589 (DIS 2020)

#### Drone Chi — Joseph La Delfa, Mehmet Aydın Baytaş, Florian 'Floyd' Mueller (2020)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Touch & pressure, Whole body-mind
- Idea: A drone can be a partner in a meditative movement practice rather than a remote-controlled tool: slowness, closeness and airflow let it be felt, not piloted.
- What it is: A person moves slowly in a tai chi-like practice with a small drone hovering close to their hands; the drone follows and leads their movement, and its downdraft is felt on the skin.
- How it works: A micro-quadcopter in a custom protective shell, likely tracked by motion capture, responds to the practitioner's hand movements; designed with soma design and Somaesthetic Appreciation principles and studied with practitioners.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Attunement & appreciation, More-than-human
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3313831.3376786 (CHI 2020)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w6CFGl-Plug

#### Ethics in Movement — Sara Eriksson, Kristina Höök (2020)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Voice, sound & listening, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Ethics is not only rules 'out there': how a technology informs or dictates movement shapes what we can feel and freely choose, and somatic awareness widens that freedom.
- What it is: A video analysis of a choreographer, an opera singer and a choir of five drones performing together shows how the human-drone assemblage limits or frees movement against the norms of operatic performance.
- How it works: Detailed video analysis of rehearsals and performances of a drone opera, read through Shusterman's somaesthetic perspective on ethics (with Shusterman as co-author).
- Lens: Body politics, Intercorporeality, Attunement & appreciation
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3313831.3376678 (CHI 2020)
- Images: https://figures.semanticscholar.org/c3ecbe7da0bc4d9175884fd5a540c973cd809a75/2-Figure1-1.png

#### Next Steps for Human-Computer Integration — Florian 'Floyd' Mueller, Pedro Lopes (2020)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Muscle tension (EMG)
- Idea: When computers act inside the body, design must ask how agency, ownership and the felt self are shared between person and machine.
- What it is: An agenda written by 29 researchers after a five-day workshop on systems that merge with the body, from muscle stimulation to implants, and the ethical questions they raise.
- How it works: Structured workshop of experts in body-integrated systems, synthesised into a set of research challenges.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, More-than-human
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3313831.3376242 (CHI 2020)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qpwl2QiGhAI

#### Dancing With Drones — Sara Eriksson, Kristina Höök (2019)
- Type: Performance, dance & music · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Expressive machine movement is shaped through the designer's own moving body: an intercorporeal understanding between human and drone.
- What it is: For a commissioned opera with custom drones on stage, the choreographer used their own moving body to feel the drones' 'otherness', showed the engineer how the drones' behaviour should change, and re-choreographed as the drones improved.
- How it works: Analysis of months of artistic development of drone behaviours for an opera, based on observation, video and interviews with choreographer Åsa Unander-Scharin and the team.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Movement & expression, More-than-human
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3290605.3300847 (CHI 2019)
- Images: https://figures.semanticscholar.org/f01cfce1209f9699f692049d9556e3f8c0ee4b8d/3-Figure1-1.png https://figures.semanticscholar.org/f01cfce1209f9699f692049d9556e3f8c0ee4b8d/7-Figure3-1.png

#### Tai Chi in the Clouds — Joseph La Delfa, Florian 'Floyd' Mueller (2018)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Light & vision
- Idea: Tai chi teaches through metaphor; technology can help a practitioner live the metaphor instead of measuring posture.
- What it is: Micro drones act as the 'clouds' of the tai chi image 'wave hands like clouds': they lead or follow the practitioner's hands and glow to show how smooth the movement is.
- How it works: Micro UAVs with LEDs, likely tracked in a motion-capture space, lead or follow hand movements and give live light feedback on smoothness.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Attunement & appreciation
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3270316.3271511 (CHI PLAY 2018 Extended Abstracts)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=wUeW4OAXGVQ

#### CuddleBits — Paul Bucci (2017)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Breath, Touch & pressure, Shape-change & inflatables
- Idea: A robot's felt emotion comes from the coupling of body and behaviour, so both have to be prototyped at the same time and by feel.
- What it is: Small, simple robots held in the hands that 'breathe' by expanding and contracting; designers sketch their bodies and their breathing behaviours together to express emotions.
- How it works: Paper, foam and fur bodies driven by a single servo or voice-coil actuator; behaviours sketched with a custom tool and rated by participants.
- Lens: Technology that touches back, Affective loops, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3025453.3025774 (CHI 2017)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=EywcxQTkfDo

#### MetaLimbs — Tomoya Sasaki, Kouta Minamizawa (2017)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Posture, balance & proprioception
- Idea: The body schema is plastic: with direct, continuous control, new limbs start to feel like part of one's own body rather than a tool.
- What it is: Two robotic arms mounted on the wearer's back are controlled by the wearer's own legs and feet, so that after practice the person uses four arms to hold, reach and solder at once.
- How it works: Backpack-mounted robot arms mapped to foot and knee positions tracked by motion sensors, with toe-controlled grippers and haptic feedback at the feet (Keio KMD with Masahiko Inami's lab).
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Estrangement
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3084822.3084837 (SIGGRAPH 2017 Emerging Technologies)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=NIuIiI5mVhI

#### Affordance++ — Pedro Lopes (2015)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Muscle tension (EMG), Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: An object's use can be communicated through the user's own movement, which raises the question of who is acting when the body moves.
- What it is: When a user reaches for an object, muscle stimulation moves their hand to show how to use it: to shake a spray can, or to let go of a hot teapot.
- How it works: Optical tracking detects which object is being reached for and EMS electrodes on the arm trigger the matching hand and wrist movement.
- Lens: Technology that touches back, Estrangement
- Shown at: CHI 2015 Best Paper Award
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2702123.2702128 (CHI 2015)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Gz4dphzBb6I

#### Proprioceptive Interaction — Pedro Lopes (2015)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Muscle tension (EMG), Posture, balance & proprioception, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: Proprioception can be an output channel: a computer can speak to you by gently moving your own body.
- What it is: Electrical muscle stimulation tilts the user's wrist in response to a device's state, so information is felt through one's own posture and muscles rather than seen on a screen.
- How it works: EMS electrodes on the forearm actuate wrist muscles against the user's own movement; users read values by feeling the resulting force and position.
- Lens: Technology that touches back, Estrangement
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2702123.2702461 (CHI 2015)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=KMxIfj7zhlw

#### Being the Machine — Laura Devendorf (2014)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Touch & pressure
- Idea: Performing a machine's motions with one's own hands makes both the machine and one's bodily skill strange, and puts control back in negotiation.
- What it is: A portable system projects a 3D printer's toolpath as a laser point, and the person follows it by hand with found materials, becoming the printer.
- How it works: A laser guide driven by G-code shows the next point to 'print'; participants chose when to follow or deviate, studied in field sessions.
- Lens: Estrangement, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2702123.2702547 (CHI 2015)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sLfwvSHMD9s

#### Broncomatic — Joe Marshall, Brendan Walker, Steve Benford (2011)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Breath, Movement & kinesthesia, Posture, balance & proprioception
- Idea: Breath is both voluntary and involuntary, so using it as a control under physical stress makes the rider feel the struggle between will and body.
- What it is: A mechanical bucking bronco that riders steer with their breath, while the ride itself throws the body and makes controlled breathing hard.
- How it works: A breathing sensor on the rider's chest likely drives the rotation of a rodeo machine; studied with riders at public events.
- Lens: Pleasure & play, Estrangement
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/1978942.1978955 (CHI 2011)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ql4ResSvoD4

#### The Haptic Creature — Steve Yohanan (2011)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Breath, Vibration & haptics
- Idea: Emotion between human and robot can live entirely in touch: how a person strokes, and how the creature breathes back.
- What it is: A furry, lap-sized robot animal without face or eyes that breathes, purrs and stiffens its ears under a person's hand, communicating only through touch.
- How it works: Touch sensors under fur detect gestures; actuators control breathing, purring vibration and ear stiffness; a study mapped touch gestures to intended emotions.
- Lens: Technology that touches back, Affective loops
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1007/s12369-011-0126-7 (International Journal of Social Robotics 2012)

#### ExoBuilding — Holger Schnädelbach (2010)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Breath, Heartbeat, Shape-change & inflatables
- Idea: When architecture takes on the rhythm of the body, a person can feel their own physiology from the outside, as the space around them.
- What it is: A tent-like room that breathes with the person inside: its fabric rises and falls with their abdominal breathing, their heartbeat plays as sound and light, and skin conductance is projected on the walls.
- How it works: A breathing belt, ECG and EDA sensors drive actuators that change the tent's shape, plus sound, lights and projection; iterated from scale model to full size.
- Lens: Body data & ambiguity, Attunement & appreciation, Technology that touches back
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/1868914.1868965 (NordiCHI 2010)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=NAZPQDqr8aQ

#### PARO Therapeutic Robot — Takanori Shibata (2004)
- Type: Product & app · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Voice, sound & listening, Heat & temperature
- Idea: A robot designed to be touched and held can calm the body the way animal contact does, without asking for any cognitive effort.
- What it is: A furry baby harp seal robot that responds to being stroked, held and spoken to by moving its head and flippers, blinking and crying, used in hospitals and nursing homes, especially with people living with dementia.
- How it works: Tactile, light, sound, temperature and posture sensors under soft fur drive actuators and recorded harp seal cries; developed at AIST from 1993 and sold since 2004.
- Lens: Care & healing, Technology that touches back, More-than-human
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=KhyCbtqnEYI
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/6/60/Paro_robot.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paro_(robot)

### Soma Design with AI & Machine Learning

Machine learning as a somatic material; AI that listens to the body.

#### Intelligence Is Corporeal — Kristina Höök (2025)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Whole body-mind
- Idea: Why we turn toward what is good and away from harm is felt in the body; AI that ignores the soma leaves out the ground of value itself.
- What it is: In a 2025 lecture and a 2026 interactions article, Höök argues that current AI lives mostly in language, while intelligence, aesthetics and ethics are rooted in bodily existence, and asks how soma design can shape embodied AI.
- How it works: Position lecture and essay drawing on a decade of soma design with shape-changing garments, drones and robots.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, More-than-human, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3801726 (ACM interactions 2026)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Y1_ySUJvTMA

#### Words that Feel Right: Haiku, Felt Sense and LLMs — Claudia Núñez-Pacheco, Pedro Sanches (2025)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Voice, sound & listening
- Idea: A language model can support finding words for a felt sense only if designers respect the time it takes and each person's own idiom.
- What it is: Over three months, the authors wrote haiku with ChatGPT as a tool for somatic introspection, searching for words that 'feel right' to the body and its emotions.
- How it works: First-person collaborative process combining Gendlin's Focusing, haiku writing and ChatGPT dialogues, yielding two design qualities: ongoing temporalities and idiolectic resonance.
- Lens: First-person & felt experience, Attunement & appreciation, Body-mind unity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3715336.3735751 (DIS 2025)

#### Understanding the Role of Interactive Machine Learning in Movement Interaction Design — Marco Gillies (2019)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Movement knowledge is tacit and felt, so the best specification of a movement interaction is a body performing it.
- What it is: An essay arguing that movement interaction should be designed by moving: the designer performs examples and machine learning captures the embodied knowledge that cannot be written as rules.
- How it works: Synthesis of theories of embodied and tacit knowledge with case studies of interactive machine learning tools for movement.
- Lens: First-person & felt experience, Movement & expression
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3287307 (ACM TOCHI 2019)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=l2C6aAWzpyw

#### Understanding Gesture Expressivity through Muscle Sensing — Baptiste Caramiaux (2015)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Muscle tension (EMG), Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: Expressivity is felt from inside as effort and tension; sensing the muscles gets closer to what the mover feels than tracking the limb.
- What it is: Performers repeat gestures with different expressive intentions while EMG and motion are recorded; the muscle signal captures variation in effort that motion data misses.
- How it works: Combined EMG and accelerometer recordings of repeated gestures, analysed with machine learning for expressive variation; collaboration with Marco Donnarumma and Atau Tanaka.
- Lens: Movement & expression, Body data & ambiguity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2687922 (ACM TOCHI 2015)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=c9uEeNGQdM4

#### Wekinator — Rebecca Fiebrink (2009)
- Type: Method & toolkit · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Voice, sound & listening
- Idea: Teaching a model by moving lets designers keep the knowledge in their body, tuning a mapping by feel rather than by code.
- What it is: Free software for interactive machine learning: a person demonstrates gestures with a sensor or camera, pairs them with sounds or visuals, and trains a model on the spot.
- How it works: Real-time supervised learning (classification, regression) from OSC inputs to outputs; the user records examples, trains, plays and re-trains in a loop.
- Lens: Movement & expression, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1007/978-981-10-2951-6_10 (Musical Instruments in the 21st Century (Springer 2016))
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=yc5CL5EoPqg
- Project page: http://www.wekinator.org/

### Virtual Bodies & XR

Body ownership, embodiment swaps, breath- and biosignal-driven VR/AR.

#### Soma Design and Sensory Misalignment — Paul Tennent, Joe Marshall (2020)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Posture, balance & proprioception, Movement & kinesthesia, Light & vision
- Idea: Deliberately misaligning the senses is a technological form of estrangement: it disrupts the habitual body so that it can be felt anew.
- What it is: A workshop report that brings together soma designers and researchers of sensory misalignment in VR and rides, arguing that each practice can extend the other.
- How it works: Joint workshop of the Nottingham Mixed Reality Lab and KTH soma design researchers, with hands-on sessions on misalignment prototypes, synthesised into design themes.
- Lens: Estrangement, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3313831.3376812 (CHI 2020)

#### Egocentric Smaller-Person Experience — Jun Nishida (2019)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Light & vision, Movement & kinesthesia, Posture, balance & proprioception
- Idea: Changing only the height of the eyes changes the felt size of the body, and with it how people act toward objects and others.
- What it is: A wearable visual translator lowers the wearer's eye level to waist height, so adults see and move through a nursing school or museum from a child's viewpoint.
- How it works: Head-mounted display fed by a waist-mounted stereo camera that follows head movements, tested in field studies with nursing students and museum visitors.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Estrangement
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3290605.3300926 (CHI 2019)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ZkZjfqo6h3I

#### Attending to Breath — Mirjana Prpa, Thecla Schiphorst (2018)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Breath, Voice, sound & listening, Light & vision
- Idea: Mapping breath directly into sound and image can hold attention on the body from within, and micro-phenomenology can show how that awareness unfolds.
- What it is: An immersive virtual environment for focused-attention meditation in which sound and visuals follow the user's breathing, helping them keep attention on the breath.
- How it works: A breathing sensor drives a generative soundtrack and visuals in VR; experience studied with micro-phenomenological interviews.
- Lens: Attunement & appreciation, First-person & felt experience
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3196709.3196765 (DIS 2018)

#### My Tai-Chi Coaches — Ping-Hsuan Han (2017)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Posture, balance & proprioception, Light & vision
- Idea: Learning a form is learning from many viewpoints; placing the teacher's body around the learner keeps attention on one's own movement instead of on turning to look.
- What it is: A head-mounted augmented reality tool in which several virtual tai chi coaches surround the learner, so that whichever way the learner turns during the form, a coach is in view to follow.
- How it works: Virtual coach avatars rendered in a head-mounted display around the learner's tracked position, likely evaluated with novice tai chi learners at National Taiwan University (Yi-Ping Hung's group).
- Lens: Movement & expression, Attunement & appreciation
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3041164.3041194 (Augmented Human 2017)

#### VR Playground — Brendan Walker, Paul Tennent, Steve Benford (2017)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Posture, balance & proprioception, Light & vision
- Idea: Deliberately mismatching what the eyes see and what the inner ear feels can be a source of delight, and a way to make one's own motion strange.
- What it is: Riders on a real playground swing wear VR headsets and travel through four virtual worlds, each of which amplifies, reverses or distorts the felt motion of swinging.
- How it works: A sensor on the swing measures its motion and drives four 'abstract machines' that map physical swing to virtual movement; toured to over 5,000 riders.
- Lens: Estrangement, Pleasure & play
- Shown at: Norfolk and Norwich Festival 2017
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3290605.3300811 (CHI 2019)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=WmP79khSrQc
- Images: https://paultennent.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/06/swing.png https://www.nottingham.ac.uk/research/groups/mixedrealitylab/images/works/vr-playground-cropped-950x300.png
- Project page: https://www.nottingham.ac.uk/research/groups/mixedrealitylab/works/vr-playground.aspx

#### DEEP — Owen Harris (2016)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Breath, Light & vision
- Idea: When breath is the only control, the practice of slowing down becomes the game itself, and calm is felt rather than instructed.
- What it is: A meditative VR world under the sea that you move through only by breathing: slow, deep belly breaths carry you along and change the glowing landscape.
- How it works: A custom stretch-sensor belt around the diaphragm drives movement and visuals in a VR headset; later tested as biofeedback for anxious children.
- Lens: Attunement & appreciation, Care & healing
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2851581.2892452 (CHI 2016 Extended Abstracts)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qIhZhdmPQ_8
- Images: https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/56a1092da128e65fa42a11fa/1585418439909-AH0BEHDJN736S99KKYEG/Screenshot_102.3696.jpg
- Project page: https://www.exploredeep.com

#### Human Tails — William Steptoe, Mel Slater (2013)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Movement & kinesthesia, Posture, balance & proprioception, Whole body-mind
- Idea: The body schema is plastic: with the right sensorimotor loop, a mover can come to feel a limb that humans have never had.
- What it is: In VR, participants have an avatar with a tail they swing by moving their hips; after using it to block falling objects, many react as if the tail were part of their body.
- How it works: Full-body tracked avatar in immersive VR with a tail driven by hip movement (synchronous or random) and a task of blocking objects with body and tail; questionnaires and behaviour measured.
- Lens: More-than-human, Estrangement
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1109/tvcg.2013.32 (IEEE TVCG (IEEE VR 2013))
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=d2zbZLBUqwU

#### Illusory Ownership of a Virtual Child Body — Domna Banakou, Mel Slater (2013)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Light & vision, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: The felt body shapes perception and self-concept at once: change the body, and both the world and the self look different.
- What it is: Adults embodied in the virtual body of a four-year-old child overestimated the size of objects and associated themselves more with child-like traits.
- How it works: Immersive VR with full-body motion capture and synchronous visuomotor feedback in a child or scaled adult body; size estimation and implicit association tests.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Estrangement
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1073/pnas.1306779110 (PNAS 2013)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=8Oy83OVgbSM

#### Putting Yourself in the Skin of a Black Avatar — Tabitha C. Peck, Mel Slater (2013)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Light & vision, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: Owning a different body for a while can loosen the boundary between self and other that bias depends on, though such effects raise their own ethical questions.
- What it is: Light-skinned participants embodied in a dark-skinned virtual body, seen in a virtual mirror, showed reduced implicit racial bias afterwards.
- How it works: Immersive VR with full-body tracking and a virtual mirror, comparing light-skinned, dark-skinned, alien and no-body conditions with pre/post implicit association tests.
- Lens: Body politics, Intercorporeality
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1016/j.concog.2013.04.016 (Consciousness and Cognition 2013)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=HliN3iOX090

#### The Machine to Be Another — BeAnotherLab (2012)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Touch & pressure, Movement & kinesthesia
- Idea: Embodiment is choreographed between people: the illusion of being another depends on two bodies attending to each other and moving together.
- What it is: Two people wear headsets showing each other's first-person view; they move in agreed synchrony and touch the same objects, so each feels, for a while, in the other's body.
- How it works: Head-mounted cameras stream each participant's view to the other's HMD; performers mirror movements and apply matching touch; shown as an installation and used in workshops.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, First-person & felt experience
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=F0dfA1DsLRk
- Images: https://beanotherlab.org/wp-content/uploads/2019/04/bal_tmtba01.jpg
- Project page: https://beanotherlab.org/home/work/tmtba/

#### The Sense of Embodiment in Virtual Reality — Konstantina Kilteni, Mel Slater (2012)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Movement & kinesthesia, Posture, balance & proprioception
- Idea: Feeling embodied is several felt senses working together; designers can tune each one rather than treating 'presence' as a single switch.
- What it is: A theory paper that splits the sense of embodiment in a virtual body into three parts, self-location, agency and body ownership, and reviews how each can be induced.
- How it works: Conceptual review of body-ownership, agency and self-location research in neuroscience and VR.
- Lens: First-person & felt experience, Body-mind unity
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1162/pres_a_00124 (Presence: Teleoperators and Virtual Environments 2012)

#### First Person Experience of Body Transfer in Virtual Reality — Mel Slater (2010)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Light & vision, Touch & pressure, Whole body-mind
- Idea: A first-person perspective alone can transfer ownership to a very different body, which opens VR as a tool for living in another's body.
- What it is: Adult men in VR saw, from a first-person view, the body of a young girl in place of their own, and felt it as theirs, with bodily reactions when the girl was threatened.
- How it works: Immersive VR with a virtual female body seen from first-person or third-person perspective, with visuotactile stroking; heart-rate deceleration to a threat measured.
- Lens: First-person & felt experience, Body politics
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0010564 (PLoS ONE 2010)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=pzCxjN8hyvg

#### If I Were You: Perceptual Illusion of Body Swapping — Valeria I. Petkova, H. Henrik Ehrsson (2008)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Light & vision, Whole body-mind
- Idea: The boundary between my body and yours is held by synchrony of sight and touch; change it, and bodies can be swapped.
- What it is: Seeing through cameras on another person's head while shaking hands with them, participants felt the other person's body as their own and their own as someone else's.
- How it works: Head-mounted cameras on a mannequin or experimenter stream to the participant's HMD; synchronous squeezing and handshakes, with threat-evoked skin conductance.
- Lens: Intercorporeality, Estrangement
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0003832 (PLoS ONE 2008)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AuzCSejk0Bw

#### The Experimental Induction of Out-of-Body Experiences — H. Henrik Ehrsson (2007)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Light & vision, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Where 'I' am is felt, not known: with the right sensory match the self can step out of its own body.
- What it is: Participants see themselves from behind through goggles fed by cameras while their chest is touched, and report feeling located outside their own body, where the cameras are.
- How it works: Stereo cameras behind the participant feed an HMD; a rod touches the real chest while moving toward the illusory body; skin conductance to a threat measured.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Estrangement
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1126/science.1142175 (Science 2007)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ee4-grU_6vs

#### Video Ergo Sum — Bigna Lenggenhager (2007)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Light & vision, Whole body-mind
- Idea: The felt 'I am here, in this body' can be shifted with a camera and a brush: self-location is part of the living, adjustable soma.
- What it is: Wearing a head-mounted display, participants see a video of their own back being stroked two metres in front of them while their real back is stroked, and begin to feel that body as theirs.
- How it works: Full-body illusion with a camera filming the participant from behind into an HMD, synchronous or asynchronous back-stroking, then drift in self-location measured.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Estrangement
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1126/science.1143439 (Science 2007)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5jg0sSwOtLI

#### Rubber Hands 'Feel' Touch That Eyes See — Matthew Botvinick (1998)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Light & vision, Posture, balance & proprioception
- Idea: Ownership of the body is not given but continuously made from matching sight and touch, which is why a felt body can be extended or displaced.
- What it is: A participant's hidden hand and a visible rubber hand are stroked in synchrony; after a few minutes the rubber hand feels like one's own and the real hand seems to drift toward it.
- How it works: Synchronous versus asynchronous brush strokes on hidden real hand and visible rubber hand; questionnaires and proprioceptive drift measured.
- Lens: Body-mind unity, Estrangement
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1038/35784 (Nature 1998)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sxwn1w7MJvk

### More-than-Human Soma

Feeling with plants, animals, weather and ecosystems.

#### FeltSight — Danlin Huang, Botao 'Amber' Hu, Rem RunGu Lin (2025)
- Type: Research prototype · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Vibration & haptics, Light & vision
- Idea: Reducing sight and extending touch slows people down and makes them probe the world with the hand as an organ of knowing.
- What it is: Inspired by the star-nosed mole, soft haptic gloves worn with a mixed reality headset let wearers feel nearby surfaces through their fingertips before touching them, while vision fades to darkness and small clouds of light surround the hands.
- How it works: Apple Vision Pro scene meshes and hand tracking drive vibrotactile actuators in splayed soft gloves according to distance to surfaces.
- Lens: More-than-human, Estrangement, Attunement & appreciation
- Shown at: SIGGRAPH Asia 2025 Art Papers; CHI 2026 Interactivity; IEEE VR 2026 XR Gallery
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3800645.3812822 (DIS 2026 Pictorial)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7Pq6s3VnD0A
- Images: https://reality.design/media/feltsight-cover-forest-closeup.webp
- Project page: https://reality.design/project/feltsight

#### EchoVision — Botao 'Amber' Hu, Jiabao Li, Danlin Huang (2024)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Voice, sound & listening, Light & vision
- Idea: Using one's own voice to 'see' turns a familiar place strange and makes perception feel like an active, bodily act.
- What it is: Visitors lift a bat-shaped mixed reality mask, make a sound with their voice and see waves of light travel over the people and objects around them, a simulation of bat echolocation.
- How it works: Smartphone-based HoloKit headset in a custom mask; LiDAR scene reconstruction and microphone input trigger expanding echo shaders.
- Lens: More-than-human, Estrangement, Pleasure & play
- Shown at: SIGGRAPH Asia 2024 Art Papers; Augmented Humans 2025 Best Demo Award; Plásmata 3, Athens, 2025
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3706599.3721192 (CHI 2025 Interactivity)
- Video: https://vimeo.com/955577972
- Images: https://reality.design/media/_resources/echovision-jiabao-01.webp
- Project page: https://reality.design/project/echovision

#### Making Energy Matter — Thórhildur Ásgeirsdóttir, Rob Comber (2023)
- Type: Paper · Body & senses: Heat & temperature, Touch & pressure, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Sustainability framed rationally breeds fatalism; somatic relations with energy open a more ethical, felt way of being with it.
- What it is: A first-person soma design engagement with household energy moved from restrictive 'sustainability' designs to enacting material relations with energy, such as feeling residual heat and running water with the hands.
- How it works: Autobiographical design over months, with body maps, sketches and co-performed kitchen experiments.
- Lens: First-person & felt experience, More-than-human, Body politics
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3544548.3581160 (CHI 2023)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=nIpaQ5-uQVs
- Images: https://figures.semanticscholar.org/93d1703784186205708c39ae166a2a894032fbbc/9-Figure4-1.png

#### We Live in an Ocean of Air — Marshmallow Laser Feast (2018)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Breath, Heartbeat, Light & vision
- Idea: Breath is an exchange between bodies and species; making it visible turns breathing into a felt relation with plants.
- What it is: A multi-person VR installation around a giant sequoia in which visitors see their own breath leave the body as particles and pass into the tree, and the tree's exhalation return to them.
- How it works: Untethered VR with breath and heart-rate sensors; each visitor's breath and pulse are rendered as particles in a shared scene.
- Lens: More-than-human, Intercorporeality, Attunement & appreciation
- Shown at: Saatchi Gallery, London (2018–2019)
- Video: https://vimeo.com/303589503
- Images: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2023/11/Copy-of-OceanOfAir_Saatchi_-12-of-34_sml.jpg
- Project page: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com

#### Tree — Milica Zec, Winslow Porter (2017)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Whole body-mind, Heat & temperature, Vibration & haptics
- Idea: Taking on a tree's body and slow life through touch, heat and rumble makes deforestation felt as something happening to a body.
- What it is: In VR you grow from a seed into a rainforest tree, your arms become branches, and you feel wind, heat and vibration until the forest burns.
- How it works: Room-scale VR with haptic vibration, wind and heat (and likely scent), synchronised to the tree's life stages.
- Lens: More-than-human, Estrangement
- Shown at: Sundance Film Festival New Frontier 2017
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ERffRXjTAqM
- Images: https://docubase.mit.edu/wp-content/uploads/2018/11/Tree-02-Still.jpg
- Project page: https://www.treeofficial.com

#### GoatMan — Thomas Thwaites (2016)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Posture, balance & proprioception, Movement & kinesthesia, Whole body-mind
- Idea: Trying to live in another species' body shows how deeply thought, mood and worry are tied to one's own posture and gait.
- What it is: A designer builds prosthetic legs and an artificial rumen and lives for days with a herd of goats in the Alps, walking on four limbs.
- How it works: Prosthetic limbs designed with prosthetists and animal experts, a helmet and an external digestion device; tested in a first-person expedition.
- Lens: More-than-human, First-person & felt experience, Estrangement
- Shown at: Ig Nobel Prize 2016 (Biology)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-IPub-Fipz8
- Images: https://www.thomasthwaites.com/folio5/wp-content/uploads/2016/03/Goat8-Thomas_Thwaites-photo-Tim_Bowditch.jpg
- Project page: https://www.thomasthwaites.com/a-holiday-from-being-human-goatman/

#### Treehugger: Wawona — Marshmallow Laser Feast (2016)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Touch & pressure, Light & vision, Breath
- Idea: Hugging a tree, physically and in VR, invites a slower, bodily sense of a life on another timescale.
- What it is: Visitors put their heads into a sculpted tree trunk and see and hear the inside of a giant sequoia, following water as it rises through the tree.
- How it works: A physical tree-trunk sculpture with embedded VR headset and haptics, using scan data of a giant sequoia turned into a particle visualisation.
- Lens: More-than-human, Attunement & appreciation
- Video: https://vimeo.com/195539105
- Images: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2024/08/5.jpg
- Project page: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com

#### In the Eyes of the Animal — Marshmallow Laser Feast (2015)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Light & vision, Vibration & haptics, Voice, sound & listening
- Idea: Borrowing another species' senses unsettles the human body as the default measure of perception.
- What it is: Visitors in a forest wear headsets and a vibrating backpack and see the same forest through the senses of a mosquito, a dragonfly, a frog and an owl.
- How it works: LiDAR and CT scans of the forest rendered as particle worlds in VR, with a binaural soundtrack and a subwoofer backpack delivering vibration to the body.
- Lens: More-than-human, Estrangement
- Video: https://vimeo.com/140057053
- Images: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2024/07/Screenshot-2024-07-24-at-10.52.46.jpg
- Project page: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com

#### Birdly — Somniacs, Max Rheiner (2014)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Posture, balance & proprioception, Movement & kinesthesia, Touch & pressure
- Idea: Becoming another creature starts with posture: lying prone with arms as wings is what makes the flight felt.
- What it is: You lie face down on a machine, spread your arms as wings and fly as a bird over a city; flapping, tilting and a fan-driven headwind make the flight felt through the whole body.
- How it works: A motorised platform responds to arm flapping and body tilt, with a VR headset, a fan for headwind and spatial sound.
- Lens: More-than-human, Pleasure & play
- Shown at: SIGGRAPH 2014 Emerging Technologies
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2614066.2614101 (SIGGRAPH 2014 Emerging Technologies)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gWLHIusLWOc
- Images: https://birdlyvr.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/3/2017/03/homepage-birdly-BHP-2868-2.jpg
- Project page: https://www.birdlyvr.com

#### May the Horse Live in Me — Art Orienté Objet (2011)
- Type: Performance, dance & music · Body & senses: Intimate & hormonal body, Posture, balance & proprioception, Pain & inner sensations
- Idea: Letting another species' blood act inside one's body is an extreme first-person test of where one's own body ends.
- What it is: After months of immunising herself, artist Marion Laval-Jeantet is injected with horse blood plasma and then walks with a horse on stilts shaped like hooves.
- How it works: Progressive immunisation to horse immunoglobulins, followed by a live injection and a walk with a horse on hoof-stilts; documented as performance.
- Lens: More-than-human, First-person & felt experience, Body politics
- Shown at: Prix Ars Electronica 2011, Golden Nica, Hybrid Art
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=yx_E4DUWXbE
- Images: https://archive.aec.at/asset/730673/preview/
- Project page: https://archive.aec.at/prix/169875/

#### Animal Superpowers — Chris Woebken, Kenichi Okada (2008)
- Type: Artwork & installation · Body & senses: Light & vision, Touch & pressure, Voice, sound & listening
- Idea: Changing the scale and senses of a child's body is a playful way to feel that other creatures live in different worlds.
