- What it is: Wearable devices give children animal senses: an 'ant' device magnifies what their hands touch 50 times, a 'bird' device lets them feel magnetic fields, a 'giraffe' device raises their eyes.
- How it works: Hand-worn cameras with helmet displays, a vibrating GPS/compass device and periscope-like goggles, made as playful critical-design objects.
- Lens: More-than-human, Pleasure & play, Estrangement
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=L9oTcez2CXU
- Images: https://freight.cargo.site/w/1200/i/ad1784c02faa9d2afb6614fa878b4982436d2d58292e1349ebee13b3d8c6a682/2232862656_cba3f094c1_o.jpg
- Project page: https://www.chriswoebken.com/animal-superpowers

## Resources & links: people, institutions and websites

### Soma design & HCI researchers

- **Ana Tajadura-Jiménez** (Madrid, Spain) — Head of the i_mBODY Lab at UC3M who uses sound and multisensory feedback to change how people feel their own body, as in 'As Light as your Footsteps', where altered walking sounds change perceived body weight. Known for: Embodied auditory perception: the emotional impact of approaching and receding sound sources (Emotion, 2010); Action sounds recalibrate perceived tactile distance (2012); As Light as your Footsteps: altering walking sounds to change perceived body weight (CHI 2015); BODYinTRANSIT project on multisensory body transformation. https://imbodylab.com/about/
- **Anders Lundström** (Umeå, Sweden) — Umeå University associate professor and Höök's PhD student (KTH 2016) on energy-sensitive interaction, now on human-powered interaction; co-author of Metaphone and co-supervisor of Windlin's Soma Bits PhD. Known for: Metaphone (CHI 2014); Designing Energy-Sensitive Interactions (PhD thesis, KTH, 2016). https://www.umu.se/en/staff/anders-lundstrom/
- **Anna Ståhl** (Gothenburg, Sweden) — Interaction designer at RISE and Höök's longest-standing collaborator, from the affective interaction of eMoto and Affective Diary to somaesthetic appreciation design, the Soma Mat and Breathing Light. Her PhD (KTH 2014) framed design for interactional empowerment. Known for: eMoto (2004–2005); Affective Diary (CHI EA 2006; PUC 2009); Somaesthetic Appreciation Design (CHI 2016); The Soma Mat and Breathing Light (CHI EA 2016). https://www.digitalfutures.kth.se/profile/anna-stahl/
- **Atau Tanaka** (London, UK) — Goldsmiths professor and performer who plays the body as an instrument through muscle-tension (EMG) sensing and machine learning, from Sensorband to Myogram. Known for: Sensorband (with Zbigniew Karkowski and Edwin van der Heide); Myogram (EMG performance, 2015); Gestural Musical Performance with Physiological Sensors, Focusing on the Electromyogram (Routledge Companion to Embodied Music Interaction, 2017). https://www.gold.ac.uk/computing/people/tanaka-atau/
- **Baptiste Caramiaux** (Paris, France) — CNRS researcher at ISIR, Sorbonne, who works on machine learning for expressive gesture and muscle sensing in musical interaction, and on human-centred AI in the arts; co-author of the 2018 first-person soma paper. Known for: Adaptive Gesture Recognition with Variation Estimation for Interactive Systems (ACM TiiS, 2014); Understanding Gesture Expressivity through Muscle Sensing (TOCHI 2015); Mogees gesture-based musical product (Mogees Ltd., 2015); Embracing First-Person Perspectives in Soma-Based Design (Informatics, 2018). https://baptistecaramiaux.com/
- **Bin Zhu** (Hangzhou, China) — KTH PhD (2021, 'Designing for Mindfulness', co-supervised by Höök) on how digital technology can support mindfulness, distinguishing presence-in, presence-with and presence-through; now at Hangzhou City University. Known for: Designing Digital Mindfulness: Presence-In and Presence-With versus Presence-Through (CHI 2017); Designing for Mindfulness (PhD thesis, KTH, 2021). https://orcid.org/0000-0001-9960-1639
- **Carl Unander-Scharin** (Karlstad, Sweden, 1964–) — Opera tenor and composer whose KTH PhD with Höök ('Extending Opera', 2015) built interactive instruments such as the Vocal Chorder and led to the drone opera experiments with her group. Known for: The Vocal Chorder (CHI 2014); Extending Opera – Artist-led Explorations in Operatic Practice through Interactivity and Electronics (PhD thesis, KTH, 2015); Dancing With Drones (CHI 2019). https://www.kau.se/en/carl-unander-scharin-professor-musical-performance-specialising-classical-vocal-studies
- **Caroline Hummels** (Eindhoven, Netherlands) — TU Eindhoven professor whose 'Move to get moved' and seven principles for embodied sensemaking (with Jelle van Dijk) shaped the Dutch school of movement-based, embodied interaction design; co-author of the 2018 first-person soma paper. Known for: Move to get moved: methods, tools and knowledge to design for expressive and rich movement-based interaction (PUC, 2006); Seven Principles to Design for Embodied Sensemaking (TEI 2015, with Jelle van Dijk). https://www.tue.nl/en/research/researchers/caroline-hummels
- **Charles Windlin** (Stockholm, Sweden) — Höök's PhD student (KTH 2023) who built the Soma Bits, a kit of shapes with heat, vibration and shape-change actuators placed on the body so designers can sketch soma experiences hands-on. Known for: Soma Bits (2019); SKETCHING SOMA BITS (DIS 2022); Shape and Being Shaped: Sketching with Haptics in Soma Design (PhD thesis, KTH, 2023). https://orcid.org/0000-0002-4481-4298
- **Christine Li** (Nottingham, UK) — Mixed Reality Lab PhD researcher designing VR for bodily training in performance arts and sport; she co-authored the collision design and 'Tangles' soma trajectories work and the non-dualistic design paper. Known for: Unpacking Non-Dualistic Design: The Soma Design Case (TOCHI 2021); Collision Design (CHI EA 2023); Tangles: Unpacking Extended Collision Experiences with Soma Trajectories (TOCHI 2025). https://www.nottingham.ac.uk/research/beacons-of-excellence/smart-products/training/christine-li/index.aspx
- **Claudia Daudén Roquet** (London, UK) — King's College London researcher on mindfulness and emotion-regulation technologies; co-author of 'HCI and Affective Health' and of the soma design workshop study in 'Unpacking Non-Dualistic Design'. Known for: HCI and Affective Health (CHI 2019); Unpacking Non-Dualistic Design: The Soma Design Case (TOCHI 2021); Designing for Emotion Regulation Interventions: An Agenda for HCI Theory and Research (TOCHI 2022). https://kclpure.kcl.ac.uk/portal/en/persons/claudia-dauden-roquet
- **Claudia Núñez-Pacheco** (Malmö, Sweden) — Malmö University design researcher trained in Focusing and micro-phenomenology who uses body maps and felt-sense articulation as design tools. She co-authored the soma design ethics papers on vulnerability and felt ethics with Höök's group. Known for: Body Maps: A Generative Tool for Soma-based Design (TEI 2022); Vulnerability as an ethical stance in soma design processes (CHI 2022); Felt Ethics: Cultivating Ethical Sensibility in Design Practice (CHI 2023). https://claudianunezpacheco.com/
- **Corina Sas** (Lancaster, UK) — Lancaster University professor of HCI and digital health who led the AffecTech network on personal technologies for affective health, the setting for 'From Biodata to Somadata' and much of the biodata work with Höök. Known for: AffectCam (CHI EA 2013); HCI and Affective Health: Taking stock of a decade of studies (CHI 2019); From Biodata to Somadata (CHI 2020); AffecTech: Personal Technologies for Affective Health (Marie Skłodowska-Curie ITN, 2017–2021). https://www.lancaster.ac.uk/scc/about-us/people/corina-sas
- **Cumhur Erkut** (Copenhagen, Denmark) — Aalborg University Copenhagen associate professor in sonic and embodied interaction who teaches somaesthetics-informed embodied interaction courses (with Sofia Dahl); co-author of the 2018 first-person soma paper. Known for: Embracing First-Person Perspectives in Soma-Based Design (Informatics, 2018); Embodied Interaction through Movement in a Course Work (MOCO 2017, with S. Dahl); Incorporating Virtual Reality in an Embodied Interaction Course (MOCO 2018, with S. Dahl); Workshop 'Training, Sharing, and Enacting: Somaesthetics of Rhythmic Interaction' (2017, with S. Dahl). https://vbn.aau.dk/en/persons/cer/
- **Dag Svanæs** (Trondheim, Norway, 1959–) — NTNU professor whose writing on kinaesthetic thinking and 'interaction design for and with the lived body' (TOCHI 2013) brought Merleau-Ponty's phenomenology into HCI, a core theoretical source for soma design. Known for: Kinaesthetic thinking: the tacit dimension of interaction design (Computers in Human Behavior, 1997); Understanding Interactivity: Steps to a Phenomenology of Human-Computer Interaction (PhD, NTNU, 2000); Interaction design for and with the lived body: some implications of Merleau-Ponty's phenomenology (TOCHI 2013). https://www.ntnu.edu/employees/dag.svanes
- **Danielle Lottridge** (Auckland, New Zealand) — University of Auckland associate professor and registered dance/movement therapist who works on affective interaction and remote intimacy (Sharing Empty Moments); co-author of the 2018 first-person soma paper. Known for: Sharing Empty Moments: design for remote couples (CHI 2009); Affective Interaction: understanding, evaluating, and designing for human emotion (Reviews of Human Factors and Ergonomics, 2011); Embracing First-Person Perspectives in Soma-Based Design (Informatics, 2018). https://profiles.auckland.ac.nz/d-lottridge
- **Danielle Wilde** (Umeå, Sweden) — Professor at Umeå Institute of Design and SDU who made performative body devices such as hipDisk and wrote on embodying embodied design research techniques; she now applies embodied research-through-design to food systems. Known for: hipDisk (CHI EA 2012); Embodying Embodied Design Research Techniques (2015). https://www.daniellewilde.com/wilde/
- **Deepika Yadav** (Espoo, Finland) — Aalto University assistant professor who, as a Digital Futures postdoc in the KTH intimate-health group, studied the mixed feelings of digital contraception and fertility tracking. Known for: Ambivalences in Digital Contraception: Designing for Mixed Feelings and Oscillating Relations (DIS 2023); Relational Aspects of Care in Intimate Digital Health Technologies (Digital Futures postdoc project). https://www.aalto.fi/en/people/deepika-yadav
- **Edward Melcer** (Ottawa, Canada) — Carleton University associate professor who, with Katherine Isbister, developed a design framework for embodied learning games; co-author of the 2018 first-person soma paper. Known for: Bridging the Physical Learning Divides: A Design Framework for Embodied Learning Games and Simulations (DiGRA 2016, with K. Isbister); Bots & (Main)Frames: tangible blocks and collaborative play in an educational programming game (CHI 2018, with K. Isbister); Embracing First-Person Perspectives in Soma-Based Design (Informatics, 2018). https://carleton.ca/scs/people/edward-melcer/
- **Ekaterina R. Stepanova** (Stockholm, Sweden) — Cognitive scientist and KTH postdoc who uses embodied cognition and somaesthetics to design immersive VR and bio-responsive installations for felt connection, such as JeL, where two people breathe together to grow a virtual ecosystem. Known for: Creating AWE (2018); JeL: Breathing Together to Connect with Others and Nature (DIS 2020); Strategies for Fostering a Genuine Feeling of Connection in Technologically Mediated Systems (CHI 2022); Corsetto (CHI 2023). https://www.kth.se/profile/katstep
- **Elena Márquez Segura** (Madrid, Spain) — UC3M researcher (PhD Uppsala 2016, co-supervised by Höök) who created embodied design methods such as embodied sketching and bodystorming for movement-based play; co-author of the 2018 first-person soma paper. Known for: The Design Space of Body Games (CHI 2013); Embodied Sketching (CHI 2016); Bodystorming for movement-based interaction design (Human Technology, 2016); Yamove! (Human Technology, 2016, with Isbister et al.). https://imbodylab.com/about/
- **Elsa Kosmack Vaara** (Gothenburg, Sweden) — Designer and former musician at RISE whose KTH PhD (2017) explored the aesthetics of felt time, for instance through sourdough baking. She co-authored early somaesthetic and affective-health work with Höök at SICS and Mobile Life. Known for: Mirroring bodily experiences over time (CHI EA 2009); Experiential artifacts as a design method for somaesthetic service development (2011); Exploring the Aesthetics of Felt Time (PhD thesis, KTH, 2017). https://www.researchcatalogue.net/profile/?person=556703
- **Eva Hornecker** (Weimar, Germany) — Bauhaus-Universität Weimar professor whose frameworks for tangible interaction, physical space and the role of physicality are core references for embodied and whole-body interaction. Known for: Getting a Grip on Tangible Interaction: a framework on physical space and social interaction (CHI 2006, with Jacob Buur); Tangible User Interfaces: Past, Present, and Future Directions (2010, with Orit Shaer); The Role of Physicality in Tangible and Embodied Interactions (Interactions, 2011); Human-Computer Interactions in Museums (2019, with Luigina Ciolfi). http://www.ehornecker.de/
- **Feng Zhou** (Nottingham, UK) — Research fellow at the University of Nottingham who leads Somabotics' Soma Skins, a plug-and-play kit of wearable sensor and actuator tiles for embodied interaction; co-author of 'Unpacking Non-Dualistic Design'. Known for: Unpacking Non-Dualistic Design: The Soma Design Case (TOCHI 2021); Soma Skins toolkit (Somabotics, 2025). https://www.somabotics.ac.uk/soma-skins-toolkit/
- **Florian 'Floyd' Mueller** (Melbourne, Australia) — Monash University professor and director of the Exertion Games Lab, which designs bodily play, from exertion interfaces and Jogging over a Distance to human-computer integration where technology works inside and with the body. Known for: Exertion Interfaces: sports over a distance for social bonding and fun (CHI 2003); Jogging over a Distance between Europe and Australia (UIST 2010); Designing Sports: a framework for exertion games (CHI 2011); Next Steps for Human-Computer Integration (CHI 2020). https://www.florianfloydmueller.com/
- **Frédéric Bevilacqua** (Paris, France) — Head of IRCAM's Sound Music Movement Interaction team, whose gesture following and movement sonification (e.g. Interlude) underpin much movement-sound interaction, including sound-guided rehabilitation. Known for: Interlude project (2008–2010; 2011 Guthman Prize for new musical instruments); Continuous Realtime Gesture Following and Recognition (LNCS, 2010); Sensori-Motor Learning with Movement Sonification (Frontiers in Neuroscience, 2016). https://www.stms-lab.fr/person/frederic-bevilacqua/
- **George Poonkhin Khut** (Sydney, Australia, 1969–) — Chinese-Australian artist at UNSW who since 2004 has made participatory biofeedback artworks driven by heartbeat and breath (Cardiomorphologies) and brought them into hospitals with the BrightHearts app for children's procedural pain. Known for: Cardiomorphologies (biofeedback artwork, from 2004; IEEE MultiMedia 2007); Development and Evaluation of Participant-Centred Biofeedback Artworks (Doctor of Creative Arts exegesis, Western Sydney University, 2006); Designing Biofeedback Artworks for Relaxation (CHI EA 2016); BrightHearts biofeedback app for children's procedural pain (Pain Practice, 2018). https://georgekhut.com/
- **Helena Tobiasson** (Stockholm, Sweden) — Ergonomist and KTH PhD (2015, 'Traces of Movement') who studied physical activity in eldercare, schools and offices and proposed Physical Movement Sketching; co-author of the 2018 first-person soma paper. Known for: Traces of Movement: Exploring physical activity in societal settings (PhD thesis, KTH, 2015); Physical Movement Sketching and Movement Probes (design methods, 2015 thesis); Embracing First-Person Perspectives in Soma-Based Design (Informatics, 2018). https://scholar.google.com/citations?user=mjctlakAAAAJ
- **Hiroshi Ishii / 石井裕** (Cambridge, US, 1956–) — MIT Media Lab professor who founded the Tangible Media Group; his Tangible Bits and Radical Atoms visions established tangible and shape-changing interfaces, a strand of the material and embodied turn in HCI. Known for: Tangible Bits (CHI 1997, with B. Ullmer); Radical Atoms (ACM Interactions, 2012); inFORM shape display (UIST 2013). https://www.media.mit.edu/people/ishii/overview/
- **Jakob Tholander** (Stockholm, Sweden) — Stockholm University professor of HCI who studied whole-body interaction in golf, skateboarding and sports watches ('Snot, Sweat, Pain, Mud, and Snow') and co-authored the 2018 first-person soma design paper. Known for: Mirroring bodily experiences over time (CHI EA 2009); Design qualities for whole body interaction: learning from golf, skateboarding and BodyBugging (NordiCHI 2010); Snot, Sweat, Pain, Mud, and Snow: Performance and Experience in the Use of Sports Watches (CHI 2015). https://people.dsv.su.se/~jakobth/
- **Jarmo Laaksolahti** (Stockholm, Sweden) — Höök's PhD student (2008) and long-time collaborator at SICS and KTH. He co-developed the Sensual Evaluation Instrument for non-verbal, bodily evaluation and, with Windlin and Höök, the Soma Bits prototyping kit. Known for: The sensual evaluation instrument (CHI 2006); Plot, Spectacle, and Experience (PhD thesis, Stockholm University, 2008); SKETCHING SOMA BITS (DIS 2022). https://www.kth.se/profile/jarmola
- **Jiabao Li** (Oakland, US) — Artist-researcher at Northeastern University working across wearables, robots, performance and biotechnology on interspecies and climate themes and on the female body, from Glacier's Lament to Menstrual Garden. Known for: Glacier's Lament (Fast Company World Changing Ideas finalist, 2021); Body-focused works Menstrual Garden, Womb Wetware and Sentient Clit (years not verified). https://www.jiabaoli.org/about
- **Jodi Forlizzi** (Pittsburgh, US) — Carnegie Mellon professor who helped define experience-centred design and research through design in HCI, and studies social robots; co-author of the 2018 first-person soma paper. Known for: Understanding Experience in Interactive Systems (DIS 2004, with Katja Battarbee); Research through Design as a Method for Interaction Design Research in HCI (CHI 2007, with Zimmerman and Evenson); How robotic products become social products (2007). https://www.jodiforlizzi.com/
- **Joe Marshall** (Nottingham, UK) — Mixed Reality Lab researcher who builds full-body experiences such as computerised fairground rides and physical games; co-author of 'Soma Design and Sensory Misalignment' and the non-dualistic design paper. Known for: Soma Design and Sensory Misalignment (CHI 2020); Unpacking Non-Dualistic Design: The Soma Design Case (TOCHI 2021). https://people.cs.nott.ac.uk/pszjm2/
- **Jonas Fritsch** (Copenhagen, Denmark) — IT University of Copenhagen associate professor who brought affect theory into interaction design through experiments with interactive sound and physical interfaces, and now applies affective interaction design to care. Known for: Understanding Affective Engagement as a Resource in Interaction Design (Nordes 2009); Affective Interaction Design at the End of the World (DRS 2018); Problematizing Affective Interaction Design (in Affects, Interfaces, Events, 2021). http://www.fritsch.dk/
- **Jonas Löwgren** (Norrköping, Sweden) — Linköping University professor of interaction design whose work on interaction aesthetics and, with Höök, 'strong concepts' as intermediate-level design knowledge frames how soma design generalises. Known for: Thoughtful Interaction Design (MIT Press, 2004, with E. Stolterman); Inspirational patterns for embodied interaction (Nordes 2005); Strong concepts: Intermediate-level knowledge in interaction design research (ACM TOCHI, 2012, with K. Höök). https://jonas.lowgren.info/
- **Jordi Solsona Belenguer** (Stockholm, Sweden) — Höök's PhD student (KTH 2015) on designerly conversations with digital materials; co-author of the somaesthetic service design and Metaphone biodata work, and later co-supervisor of Karpashevich's soma design PhD. Known for: Experiential artifacts as a design method for somaesthetic service development (2011); Metaphone (CHI 2014); Engineering through Designerly Conversations with the Digital Material (PhD thesis, KTH, 2015). https://www.su.se/english/profiles/jsols
- **Joseph La Delfa** (Stockholm, Sweden) — Designer and Höök's PhD student (KTH 2023, 'Cultivating Mechanical Sympathy') who made Drone Chi, where a drone follows the hand in tai chi-like movement, and developed 'mechanical sympathy' for somaesthetic relations with machines. Known for: Drone Chi: Somaesthetic Human-Drone Interaction (CHI 2020); How to Train Your Drone (DIS 2024); Articulating Mechanical Sympathy for Somaesthetic Human-Machine Relations (DIS 2024); Cultivating Mechanical Sympathy (PhD thesis, KTH, 2023). https://www.bitcraze.io/author/joseph/
- **Josh Andres** (Canberra, Australia) — UNSW Canberra senior lecturer whose award-winning PhD at the Exertion Games Lab built eBikes that respond to the rider's brain and body states, articulating human-computer integration during exertion. Known for: Integrated Exertion: Understanding the Design of Human–Computer Integration in an Exertion Context (PhD thesis, RMIT, 2020; ACM SIGCHI Outstanding Dissertation Award); Ena, Ari and Ava eBikes (EEG- and sensor-driven integrated eBikes); Introducing Peripheral Awareness as a Neurological State for Human-computer Integration (CHI 2020). https://www.joshandres.com/
- **Juan Pablo Martinez Avila** (Nottingham, UK) — Nottingham HCI researcher in embodied and music interaction who brought soma design to musical instruments ('Soma Design for NIME', 2020) and took part in the workshop analysed in 'Unpacking Non-Dualistic Design'. Known for: Soma Design for NIME (NIME 2020); Unpacking Non-Dualistic Design: The Soma Design Case (TOCHI 2021); Encumbered Interaction: a Study of Musicians Preparing to Perform (CHI 2019). https://sites.google.com/view/pszjm3/about
- **Jules Françoise** (Paris-Saclay, France) — CNRS researcher at LISN working on movement sonification and interactive machine learning; his work with Schiphorst and Fdili Alaoui on kinesthetic awareness brought second-person, micro-phenomenological inquiry into somatic interaction design. Known for: Motion-Sound Mapping by Demonstration (PhD thesis, UPMC/IRCAM, 2015); Sensori-Motor Learning with Movement Sonification (Frontiers in Neuroscience, 2016); Designing for Kinesthetic Awareness: revealing user experiences through second-person inquiry (CHI 2017); Seeing, Sensing and Recognizing Laban Movement Qualities (CHI 2017). https://julesfrancoise.com/
- **Karey Helms** (Umeå, Sweden) — Designer and associate professor at Umeå Institute of Design whose KTH PhD ('Designing with care', 2023) worked self-centred and first-person with intimate, somatic data: urine, leaky objects and breastfeeding bodies. Known for: Leaky Objects (DIS 2017); Do You Have to Pee?: A Design Space for Intimate and Somatic Data (DIS 2019); Entangled Reflections on Designing with Leaky Breastfeeding Bodies (DIS 2021); Designing with care (PhD thesis, KTH, 2023). https://www.kareyhelms.com/
- **Katherine Isbister** (Santa Cruz, US) — UC Santa Cruz professor of computational media who co-developed the Sensual Evaluation Instrument with Höök and designs movement games and playful wearables for emotion and social connection, from Yamove! to the self-soothing toy Purrble. Known for: The Sensual Evaluation Instrument (2006, with Höök et al.); Yamove! A Movement Synchrony Game That Choreographs Social Interaction (Human Technology, 2016); How Games Move Us (MIT Press, 2016); Playful Wearables (MIT Press, 2024). https://www.katherineinterface.com/
- **Katta Spiel** (Vienna, Austria) — TU Wien researcher on critical access in embodied computing who centres disabled, neurodivergent and non-binary bodies; 'The Bodies of TEI' audits the normative body assumptions of embodied interaction research. Known for: Agency of Autistic Children in Technology Research—A Critical Literature Review (TOCHI 2019); Patching Gender: Non-binary Utopias in HCI (CHI EA 2019); The Bodies of TEI – Investigating Norms and Assumptions in the Design of Embodied Interaction (TEI 2021). https://katta.mere.st/
- **Kelsey Cotton** (Gothenburg, Sweden) — Vocalist and researcher at Chalmers whose soma design work centres on the singing body and breath, including the Body Electric NIME and Corsetto, made with Höök's group. Known for: The Body Electric: A NIME designed through and with the somatic experience of singing (NIME 2021); 'Feeling the Sensor Feeling you': A Soma Design Exploration on Sensing Non-habitual Breathing (CHI 2021); Corsetto (CHI 2023). https://kelseycotton.com
- **Kristina Andersen** (Eindhoven, Netherlands) — TU Eindhoven associate professor and Höök's PhD student (KTH 2017, 'Making Magic Machines') whose Magic Machine workshops use embodied making with found materials to surface people's desires about technology. Known for: Making Magic Machines (PhD thesis, KTH, 2017); The Magic Machine Workshops: Making Personal Design Knowledge (CHI 2019); Ten Permissions for Facilitating (Live) Design Activities (DIS 2024). https://www.tue.nl/en/research/researchers/kristina-andersen
- **Kristina Höök** (Stockholm, Sweden, 1964–) — Professor of Interaction Design at KTH who formulated soma design: designing from the designer's own trained, felt body-mind, drawing on Shusterman's somaesthetics and Feldenkrais practice. Her lab's work, from the affective loops of eMoto to the Soma Mat and Drone Chi, is the core lineage of this gallery. Known for: Designing with the Body: Somaesthetic Interaction Design (MIT Press, 2018); Somaesthetic Appreciation Design (CHI 2016); The Soma Mat and Breathing Light (CHI EA 2016); Unpacking Non-Dualistic Design: The Soma Design Case (TOCHI 2021). https://www.digitalfutures.kth.se/profile/kristina-hook/
- **Kristina Popova** (Stockholm, Sweden) — KTH researcher (PhD 2026, co-supervised by Höök) who studies the affective and felt dimensions of ethics in technology work, drawing on STS, feminist epistemology and soma design; co-author of the felt ethics papers. Known for: Vulnerability as an ethical stance in soma design processes (CHI 2022); Felt Ethics: Cultivating Ethical Sensibility in Design Practice (CHI 2023); Ethical Reasoning in Tech Work: From Individual Responsibility to Collective Action (PhD thesis, KTH, 2026). https://www.kth.se/profile/kpopova
- **Laia Turmo Vidal** (Stockholm, Sweden) — Assistant professor at KTH whose PhD (Uppsala 2021, 'Designing for Intercorporeality') studied technology for learning movement together, e.g. BodyLights; she now designs soft-robotic and haptic wearables for lived health with feminist and disability perspectives. Known for: Embodied Sketching (CHI 2016); BodyLights (CHI 2020); Designing for Intercorporeality (PhD thesis, Uppsala University, 2021); Aesthetics of Felt Asymmetry (CHI 2026). https://laiatv.github.io/
- **Lian Loke** (Sydney, Australia) — University of Sydney associate professor, dancer and performance artist whose 'Moving and Making Strange' (with Toni Robertson) gave interaction design a method for estranging habitual movement; co-author of 'The Somatic Turn in HCI'. Known for: Understanding movement for interaction design: frameworks and approaches (Personal and Ubiquitous Computing, 2006); Moving and Making Strange: a design methodology for movement-based interactive technologies (PhD thesis, UTS, 2009; TOCHI 2013 with Toni Robertson); Intimate Aesthetics and Facilitated Interaction (2014, with George Khut); The somatic turn in human-computer interaction (Interactions, 2018, with Thecla Schiphorst). https://www.lianloke.com/
- **Madeline Balaam** (Stockholm, Sweden) — KTH professor of interaction design who designs for intimate bodies and health: pelvic fitness (Labella), breastfeeding (FeedFinder) and menstruation (Menarche Bits). She co-authored soma design papers with Höök and supervises several soma-oriented PhD students. Known for: FeedFinder (CHI 2015); On Looking at the Vagina through Labella (CHI 2016); Touching and Being in Touch with the Menstruating Body (CHI 2020); Exploring the Somatic Possibilities of Shape Changing Car Seats (DIS 2024). https://www.digitalfutures.kth.se/profile/madeline-balaam/
- **Marco Gillies** (London, UK) — Goldsmiths professor whose 'embodied design' lets designers create full-body interactions by moving and demonstrating with interactive machine learning rather than by coding. Known for: Embodied design of full bodied interaction with virtual humans (MOCO 2015); Understanding the Role of Interactive Machine Learning in Movement Interaction Design (ACM TOCHI, 2019); VR and ML for All MOOCs (Coursera). https://www.gold.ac.uk/computing/people/m-gillies/
- **Marianna Obrist** (London, UK) — UCL professor of multisensory interfaces who works to make touch, taste and smell interaction modalities, from mid-air haptics that convey emotion to scent-delivery devices. Known for: Opportunities for odor: experiences with smell and implications for technology (CHI 2014); Emotions Mediated Through Mid-Air Haptics (CHI 2015); Scent-delivery technology shown at WEF Davos 2019 and 2020, commercialised via OWidgets Ltd (co-founded 2019). http://www0.cs.ucl.ac.uk/people/M.Obrist.html
- **Marianne Graves Petersen** (Aarhus, Denmark) — Aarhus University professor whose 'Aesthetic Interaction' (DIS 2004) framed interaction through Dewey's pragmatist aesthetics, a precursor of somaesthetic interaction design; co-author of the CHI 2012 review of shape-changing interfaces. Known for: Aesthetic Interaction: A Pragmatist's Aesthetics of Interactive Systems (DIS 2004); Shape-changing interfaces: a review of the design space and open research questions (CHI 2012); Embracing First-Person Perspectives in Soma-Based Design (Informatics, 2018). https://pure.au.dk/portal/en/persons/mgraves@cs.au.dk
- **Marie Louise Juul Søndergaard** (Oslo, Norway) — Danish designer who uses speculative and feminist design for intimate technologies around menstruation and desire (PeriodShare, Intimate Futures); as a KTH postdoc she co-made the Menarche Bits and 'Soma Design and Politics of the Body'. Known for: PeriodShare: A Bloody Design Fiction (NordiCHI 2016); Intimate Futures (DIS 2018); Designing with Intimate Materials and Movements: Making 'Menarche Bits' (DIS 2020); Soma Design and Politics of the Body (HttF 2019). https://www.fabulationforfuture.net/dr-marie-louise-juul-sondergaard/
- **Martin Jonsson** (Stockholm, Sweden) — Södertörn University researcher in tangible and ubiquitous interaction who co-designed the Soma Mat, Breathing Light and the heat-based somaesthetic prototypes with Höök and Ståhl. Known for: Somaesthetic Appreciation Design (CHI 2016); The Aesthetics of Heat (TEI 2016); The Soma Mat and Breathing Light (CHI EA 2016). https://www.sh.se/english/sodertorn-university/contact/researchers/martin-jonsson
- **Masahiko Inami / 稲見昌彦** (Tokyo, Japan) — University of Tokyo professor of human augmentation who led the ERATO JIZAI Body project on bodies whose natural and artificial parts can be freely controlled or shared, drawing on the Japanese notion of jizai (freedom of the body). Known for: JIZAI Body project (JST ERATO, Oct 2017 – Mar 2023); MetaLimbs: foot-controlled extra robotic arms (SIGGRAPH 2017 Emerging Technologies); Theory of JIZAI Body: Towards Mastery Over the Extended Self (Springer, 2023). https://www.ais.rcast.u-tokyo.ac.jp/en/research/labs/inami.html
- **Mehmet Aydın Baytaş** — Designer-engineer, formerly at Chalmers, who co-created Drone Chi with Joseph La Delfa, a somaesthetic human-drone interaction inspired by tai chi, and Azalea for somaesthetic remote co-experience. Known for: The Design of Social Drones (CHI 2019); Drone Chi: Somaesthetic Human-Drone Interaction (CHI 2020); Designing Drone Chi (DIS 2020); Azalea: Co-experience in Remote Dialog through Diminished Reality and Somaesthetic Interaction Design (CHI 2021). https://www.designdisciplin.com/about
- **Michael Haller** (Bolzano, Italy) — Professor at the Free University of Bozen-Bolzano and founder of the Media Interaction Lab, known for touch- and deformation-sensitive smart textiles (SmartSleeve, RESi); co-author of the 2018 first-person soma paper. Known for: SmartSleeve: sensing gestures on interactive textiles (UIST 2017); RESi: pressure-sensitive textile interface based on resistive yarns (UIST 2018); Embracing First-Person Perspectives in Soma-Based Design (Informatics, 2018). https://www.mediainteractionlab.eu/?page_id=561
- **Miquel Alfaras** (Spain) — Physicist and biosignals researcher who, as a Marie Skłodowska-Curie fellow at PLUX, worked with Höök's group on making biosensing accessible to designers; first author of 'From Biodata to Somadata' (CHI 2020). Known for: From Biodata to Somadata (CHI 2020); Biosensing and Actuation—Platforms Coupling Body Input-Output Modalities for Affective Technologies (Sensors, 2020); Exploring Awareness of Breathing through Deep Touch Pressure (CHI 2021). https://orcid.org/0000-0002-8942-5843
- **Mirjana Prpa** (Guangzhou, China) — Designer-researcher (PhD SFU, now HKUST Guangzhou) who creates breath-based VR and biofeedback and applies micro-phenomenology to design research ('Articulating Experience', CHI 2020). Known for: Attending to Breath: breath-based VR experiences (DIS 2018); Inhaling and Exhaling: How Technologies Can Perceptually Extend our Breath Awareness (CHI 2020); Articulating Experience: reflections from experts applying micro-phenomenology to design research in HCI (CHI 2020). https://orcid.org/0000-0003-0368-6581
- **Muhammad Umair** (Lancaster, UK) — Lancaster University researcher whose PhD built wearable biofeedback that turns arousal into visual, vibrotactile and thermal patterns for emotion regulation; co-author of 'From Biodata to Somadata' and the non-dualistic design paper. Known for: From Biodata to Somadata (CHI 2020); Exploring Personalized Vibrotactile and Thermal Patterns for Affect Regulation (DIS 2021); Unpacking Non-Dualistic Design: The Soma Design Case (TOCHI 2021). https://orcid.org/0000-0002-0017-9827
- **Nadia Bianchi-Berthouze** (London, UK) — UCL Interaction Centre professor of affective computing and the body who studies emotion in movement and touch and designs movement and breathing sonification to build confidence for people with chronic pain. Known for: Does Body Movement Engage You More in Digital Game Play? and Why? (ACII 2007); Affective Body Expression Perception and Recognition: A Survey (IEEE Transactions on Affective Computing, 2013); As Light as your Footsteps (CHI 2015, with Tajadura-Jiménez). https://orcid.org/0000-0001-8921-0044
- **Nadia Campo Woytuk** (Stockholm, Sweden) — Designer and artist at KTH (PhD 2026, 'Feminist Sensing') who designs with the menstruating body through textiles, biomaterials and touch, from Menarche Bits to tactful sensing of vaginal fluids. Known for: Touching and Being in Touch with the Menstruating Body (CHI 2020); Designing with Intimate Materials and Movements: Making 'Menarche Bits' (DIS 2020); Tactful Feminist Sensing: Designing for Touching Vaginal Fluids (DIS 2023); Feminist Sensing (PhD thesis, KTH, 2026). https://nadiacw.com/About
- **Nassrin Hajinejad** (Berlin, Germany) — Professor of media informatics at HTW Berlin whose GangKlang project sonified walking to renew walkers' sense of the embodied qualities of their gait; co-author of the 2018 first-person soma paper. Known for: GangKlang: sonic interaction for walking experiences (Audio Mostly 2016); Embracing First-Person Perspectives in Soma-Based Design (Informatics, 2018). https://www.fokus.fraunhofer.de/en/dps/news/hajinejad_professur_2024_10.html
- **Noura Howell** (Vejle, Denmark) — Designer-researcher (now at SDU, formerly Georgia Tech's Future Feelings Lab) who critically designs emotional biosensing, showing how ambiguous skin conductance and heart sounds can open reflection instead of fixing emotions as data. Known for: Biosignals as Social Cues: ambiguity and emotional interpretation in social displays of skin conductance (DIS 2016); Tensions of Data-Driven Reflection: a case study of real-time emotional biosensing (CHI 2018); Emotional Biosensing: exploring critical alternatives (CSCW 2018). https://nourahowell.com/
- **Ozgun Kilic Afsar** (Cambridge, US) — Designer-engineer of soft artificial muscles and fluidic fibre actuators (OmniFiber) who worked with Höök's group; Corsetto, a kinesthetic corset that lets a singer's voice be felt on the listener's body, came out of this collaboration. Known for: OmniFiber: Integrated Fluidic Fiber Actuators for Weaving Movement based Interactions into the 'Fabric of Everyday Life' (UIST 2021); Wearable Choreographer: Designing Soft-Robotics for Dance Practice (DIS 2022); Corsetto: A Kinesthetic Garment for Designing, Composing for, and Experiencing an Intersubjective Haptic Voice (CHI 2023). https://ozgun.io/
- **Patrizia Marti** (Siena, Italy) — University of Siena associate professor in experience design, formerly at TU Eindhoven, who designs for health and inclusion, including socially assistive robots in dementia care; co-author of the 2018 first-person soma paper. Known for: Socially Assistive Robotics in the Treatment of Behavioural and Psychological Symptoms of Dementia (BioRob 2006); Embracing First-Person Perspectives in Soma-Based Design (Informatics, 2018). https://docenti.unisi.it/it/marti
- **Paul Dourish** (Irvine, US, 1966–) — UC Irvine professor whose 'Where the Action Is' (2001) introduced embodied interaction, grounding HCI in the phenomenology of Heidegger, Schutz and Merleau-Ponty; the theoretical ground soma design builds on. Known for: Embodied Interaction: Exploring the Foundations of a New Approach to HCI (1999 paper); Where the Action Is: The Foundations of Embodied Interaction (MIT Press, 2001); Divining a Digital Future (MIT Press, 2011, with Genevieve Bell); The Stuff of Bits (MIT Press, 2017). https://ics.uci.edu/people/paul-dourish/
- **Paul Tennent** (Nottingham, UK) — Nottingham associate professor who builds breath-controlled games, VR swings and rides, and led the Nottingham–KTH work on soma trajectories and sensory misalignment. Known for: Breathalising Games (2011); Soma Design and Sensory Misalignment (CHI 2020); Articulating Soma Experiences using Trajectories (CHI 2021); Unpacking Non-Dualistic Design: The Soma Design Case (TOCHI 2021). https://www.nottingham.ac.uk/computerscience/people/paul.tennent
- **Pavel Karpashevich** (Oldenburg, Germany) — Höök's PhD student (KTH 2023, 'Designing Monstrous Experiences Through Soma Design') who built shape-changing, on-body interfaces around breathing and deep touch pressure, reading their unsettling effects through monster theory. Known for: Exploring Awareness of Breathing through Deep Touch Pressure (CHI 2021); Touching Our Breathing through Shape-Change: Monster, Organic Other, or Twisted Mirror (TOCHI 2022); Designing Monstrous Experiences Through Soma Design (PhD thesis, KTH, 2023). https://uol.de/en/media-informatics/team/pavel-karpashevich
- **Pedro Ferreira** (Copenhagen, Denmark) — Höök's PhD student (KTH 2015) who studied play and leisure in ICT for development and co-authored studies of the bodily and playful use of mobile phones; now at IT University of Copenhagen. Known for: Bodily orientations around mobiles (CHI 2011); Appreciating plei-plei around mobiles (CHI 2012); Play as Freedom: Implications for ICT4D (PhD thesis, KTH, 2015). https://bacilo.github.io/
- **Pedro Lopes** (Chicago, US) — University of Chicago associate professor who designs electrical muscle stimulation interfaces that move the user's own muscles, and studies with neuroscientists how such integration affects the sense of agency. Known for: Affordance++: allowing objects to communicate dynamic use via electrical muscle stimulation (CHI 2015); Providing Haptics to Walls & Heavy Objects in Virtual Reality by Means of Electrical Muscle Stimulation (CHI 2017); Context-aware AI + EMS movement guidance (CHI 2026 Best Paper, students Yun Ho and Romain Nith). https://plopes.org/
- **Pedro Sanches** (Umeå, Sweden) — Umeå University researcher who worked for over a decade in Höök's group on biodata and affective health. He co-authored 'From Biodata to Somadata' and 'Teaching Soma Design', and brings feminist epistemologies to designing biodata and AI for bodies. Known for: From Biodata to Somadata (CHI 2020); Teaching Soma Design (DIS 2019); Touching Our Breathing through Shape-Change: Monster, Organic Other, or Twisted Mirror (TOCHI 2022); Making New Worlds – Transformative Becomings with Soma Design (CHI 2022). https://www.umu.se/en/staff/pedro-sanches/
- **Philippe Pasquier** (Vancouver, Canada) — SFU professor and director of the Metacreation Lab for Creative AI who founded the MOCO conference on movement and computing and co-supervised breath and micro-phenomenology work with Schiphorst. Known for: Founder of the ACM International Conference on Movement and Computing (MOCO); Mova: interactive movement analytics platform (MOCO 2014); Inhaling and Exhaling (CHI 2020, with Prpa, Schiphorst et al.); Articulating Experience (CHI 2020, with Prpa, Fdili Alaoui, Schiphorst). https://www.sfu.ca/siat/people/research-faculty/philippe-pasquier.html
- **Phoebe Sengers** (Ithaca, US) — Cornell professor whose 'Reflective Design' and work on openness and ambiguity in evaluating experience (with Höök and colleagues) shaped the non-reductive approach to emotion that led to affective loops. Known for: Sense and sensibility: evaluation and interactive art (CHI 2003, with K. Höök and G. Andersson); Reflective Design (Critical Computing 2005, with Boehner, David, Kaye). https://infosci.cornell.edu/people/phoebe-sengers
- **Rachael Garrett** (Nottingham, UK) — Höök's PhD student (KTH 2026, 'Somatic Freedoms') who treats ethics as something felt in the body during design, through 'felt ethics' and vulnerability in soma design. At Nottingham's Somabotics she works on dancing with robots and the Drone Gymnasium. Known for: Vulnerability as an ethical stance in soma design processes (CHI 2022); Felt Ethics: Cultivating Ethical Sensibility in Design Practice (CHI 2023); Somatic Freedoms: Reconfiguring Human-Machine Ethical Relations (PhD thesis, KTH, 2026); The Digital Futures Drone Gymnasium (2024–). https://rachaelgarrett.com/
- **Rebecca Fiebrink** (London, UK) — UAL professor of creative computing whose Wekinator lets musicians and artists train machine learning by demonstration to map body movement to sound and media. Known for: Wekinator interactive machine learning software (2008–); Real-time interaction with supervised learning (CHI EA 2010); Machine Learning for Musicians and Artists (first online course on ML for music and art). https://www.arts.ac.uk/creative-computing-institute/people/rebecca-fiebrink
- **Rohit Ashok Khot** (Melbourne, Australia) — RMIT senior lecturer from the Exertion Games Lab who materialises bodily data as edible or printed artefacts, such as SweatAtoms and EdiPulse's heart-rate chocolate, and designs playful eating experiences. Known for: SweatAtoms: materializing physical activity as 3D-printed artefacts (OzCHI 2013); EdiPulse: self-monitoring through 3D-printed chocolate treats (CHI 2017); Playful human-food interaction projects (e.g., GustaCine, SWAN). https://rohitashokkhot.com/
- **Rosalind Picard** (Cambridge, US, 1962–) — MIT professor who founded affective computing (1997) and wearable electrodermal sensing; soma design and affective loops developed partly in response to her recognition-based model of emotion. Known for: Affective Computing (MIT Press, 1997); Toward machine emotional intelligence: analysis of affective physiological state (IEEE TPAMI, 2001); Co-founder of Affectiva and Empatica (wearable biosignal sensing). https://web.media.mit.edu/~picard/
- **Sarah Fdili Alaoui** (London, UK) — Dancer and professor at UAL's Creative Computing Institute who integrates computation into choreography and somatic practice through Laban movement qualities, questioning how technology enters dance-making. Known for: From dance to touch: movement qualities for interaction design (CHI EA 2011); Seeing, Sensing and Recognizing Laban Movement Qualities (CHI 2017); Making an Interactive Dance Piece: tensions in integrating technology in art (DIS 2019). https://saralaoui.com/
- **Sarah Homewood** (Copenhagen, Denmark) — Former contemporary dancer and University of Copenhagen researcher who studies how self-tracking shapes views of bodies (fertility, menstrual cycles, long COVID), proposing removal as a design method and tracing conceptions of the body in HCI. Known for: Ovum: Designing for Fertility Tracking as a Shared and Domestic Experience (DIS 2019); Removal as a Method (DIS 2020); Tracing Conceptions of the Body in HCI: From User to More-Than-Human (CHI 2021). https://sarahhomewood.com/bio
- **Scott Klemmer** (San Diego, US) — UC San Diego professor and co-founder of its Design Lab whose 'How Bodies Matter: Five Themes for Interaction Design' (DIS 2006) is a widely cited statement of embodied cognition for interaction design. Known for: Papier-Mâché: toolkit support for tangible input (CHI 2004); How Bodies Matter: Five Themes for Interaction Design (DIS 2006). https://d.ucsd.edu/srk/
- **Sha Xin Wei** (Tempe, US) — ASU professor and founder of the Topological Media Lab and Synthesis Center whose responsive environments and 'Poiesis and Enchantment in Topological Matter' treat gesture and movement as processual, felt experience. Known for: Poiesis and Enchantment in Topological Matter (MIT Press, 2013); Topological Media Lab (Concordia University, founder-director); Synthesis Center for responsive environments (ASU). https://search.asu.edu/profile/2202759
- **Shaowen Bardzell** (Atlanta, US) — Taiwanese-American HCI scholar at Georgia Tech who set out the feminist HCI agenda (CHI 2010) and wrote on pleasure, sexuality and care, groundwork for intimate and feminist soma design. Known for: Feminist HCI: taking stock and outlining an agenda for design (CHI 2010); Pleasure is your birthright: digitally enabled designer sex toys as a case of third-wave HCI (CHI 2011, with J. Bardzell); Humanistic HCI (Morgan & Claypool, 2015, with J. Bardzell). https://shaowenbardzell.com/
- **Steve Benford** (Nottingham, UK) — Co-founder of Nottingham's Mixed Reality Lab whose trajectories framework for performance and cultural experiences was extended to soma design with Höök and Tennent; co-author of 'Unpacking Non-Dualistic Design' and lead of Somabotics. Known for: Performing Mixed Reality (MIT Press, 2011, with Gabriella Giannachi); Uncomfortable Interactions (CHI 2012); Articulating Soma Experiences using Trajectories (CHI 2021); Unpacking Non-Dualistic Design: The Soma Design Case (TOCHI 2021). https://www.nottingham.ac.uk/computerscience/people/steve.benford
- **Susan Kozel** (Malmö, Sweden) — Dancer-philosopher at Malmö University whose 'Closer: Performance, Technologies, Phenomenology' (2007) reworks Merleau-Ponty from inside dance with interactive systems; she now develops somatic archiving with mixed reality. Known for: Closer: Performance, Technologies, Phenomenology (MIT Press, 2007); Somatic Archiving with AR/MR (ongoing research); Ten Permissions for Facilitating (Live) Design Activities (DIS 2024, with Höök and Andersen). https://mau.se/en/persons/susan.kozel/
- **Teresa Almeida** (Lisbon, Portugal) — Instituto Superior Técnico assistant professor whose feminist design research on intimate care created Labella for pelvic fitness and treats the body as an ecosystem. Known for: Labella: looking at the vagina through an interactive pelvic-fitness system (CHI 2016 Interactivity); Woman-Centered Design through Humanity, Activism, and Inclusion (ACM TOCHI, 2020); Gut-Tracking as Cultivation (2020). https://iti.larsys.pt/member/teresa-almeida/
- **Thecla Schiphorst** (Vancouver, Canada, 1955–) — Dancer, computer scientist and SFU professor who brought somatic practice and Laban analysis into HCI; a member of the original LifeForms team with Merce Cunningham, she made soft(n), whisper[s] and exhale and wrote on somatic connoisseurship. Known for: Life Forms choreography software (collaboration with Merce Cunningham, 1990–2005); soft(n): toward a somaesthetics of touch (CHI EA 2009); Self-evidence: applying somatic connoisseurship to experience design (CHI EA 2011); The somatic turn in human-computer interaction (Interactions, 2018, with Lian Loke). https://www.sfu.ca/siat/people/research-faculty/thecla-schiphorst.html
- **Toni Robertson** (Sydney, Australia) — Retired UTS professor whose taxonomy of embodied actions (1997) was an early phenomenological account of the body in cooperative work; she led the UTS movement-based interaction line with Loke and Larssen. Known for: Cooperative Work and Lived Cognition: A Taxonomy of Embodied Actions (ECSCW 1997); Understanding movement for interaction design: frameworks and approaches (PUC, 2006); The feel dimension of technology interaction (TEI 2007); Moving and Making Strange (TOCHI 2013, with Lian Loke). https://scholar.google.com/citations?user=mOh-30QAAAAJ
- **Vasiliki Tsaknaki** (Copenhagen, Denmark) — Associate professor at IT University of Copenhagen who came through KTH with craft-based interaction design. She co-developed soma design pedagogy and the politics of the body with Höök's group and proposed 'breathing-with' as a more-than-human design tactic. Known for: Making Preciousness: Interaction Design Through Studio Crafts (PhD thesis, KTH, 2017); Soma Design and Politics of the Body (HttF 2019); Teaching Soma Design (DIS 2019); 'Breathing-with': a design tactic for the more-than-human (HCI journal, 2023). http://vasilikitsaknaki.com/about
- **Vygandas Šimbelis** (Stockholm, Sweden) — Lithuanian-Swedish artist ('Das Vegas') and Höök's PhD student (KTH 2018) whose post-digital art repurposes devices; Metaphone turned biodata into sound and image. Known for: Metaphone (CHI 2014); Repurposing Bits and Pieces of the Digital (CHI 2016); Humanizing Technology Through Post-Digital Art (PhD thesis, KTH, 2018). http://simbelis.com/biography/
- **Ylva Fernaeus** (Umeå, Sweden) — Professor at Umeå Institute of Design and KTH (PhD co-supervised by Höök) working on tangible and embodied interaction and design philosophy, from robotic pets to wabi-sabi and joyful movement; she supervised Tsaknaki and Helms. Known for: How do you play with a robotic toy animal? A long-term study of Pleo (IDC 2010); The material move: how materials matter in interaction design research (DIS 2012); Expanding on Wabi-Sabi as a Design Resource in HCI (CHI 2016); Designing for Joyful Movement (2018). https://www.umu.se/en/staff/ylva-fernaeus/
- **Yoav Luft** (Stockholm, Sweden) — KTH PhD candidate studying how designers can programme touch on the body with shape-changing garments; co-author of 'Boards Hit Back', which reflects on martial arts practice through soma design. Known for: Touching Our Breathing through Shape-Change (TOCHI 2022); Boards Hit Back: Reflecting on Martial Arts Practices Through Soma Design (CHI 2023); Corsetto (CHI 2023). https://www.kth.se/profile/luft
- **Zhuying Li** (Nanjing, China) — Southeast University associate professor whose Exertion Games Lab PhD invented 'ingestible play', games such as The Guts Game and HeatCraft built around swallowable sensors that engage people with their inner body. Known for: The Guts Game: Towards Designing Ingestible Games (CHI PLAY 2018); HeatCraft: experiencing body temperature from an ingestible sensor as localised heat; A Design Framework for Ingestible Play (ACM TOCHI, 2023). https://zhuyingli.info/
- **Åsa Unander-Scharin** (Piteå, Sweden) — Choreographer and professor at Luleå University of Technology who choreographs robots and drones, from the industrial robot of The Lamentations of Orpheus to Robocygne. She co-authored the drone opera studies 'Dancing With Drones' and 'Ethics in Movement' with Höök. Known for: The Lamentations of Orpheus (1998, choreography for an industrial robot); Robocygne (2010); Dancing With Drones (CHI 2019); Ethics in Movement (CHI 2020). https://www.ltu.se/en/staff/a/asa-unander-scharin

### Philosophers & theorists

- **Alva Noë / 阿尔瓦·诺伊** (Berkeley, US, 1964–) — Berkeley philosopher who argues that perception is something we do with our moving bodies (the enactive, sensorimotor view) and that art and dance are 'strange tools' that reorganise our embodied habits. Known for: Action in Perception (2004); Out of Our Heads (2009); Strange Tools: Art and Human Nature (2015). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alva_No%C3%AB
- **Antonio Damasio / 安东尼奥·达马西奥** (Los Angeles, US, 1944–) — Neuroscientist at USC whose somatic marker hypothesis argues that feelings rooted in body states guide reason, challenging Cartesian mind-body dualism; a key source for designing with emotion as a bodily process. Known for: Descartes' Error (1994); The Feeling of What Happens (1999); Looking for Spinoza (2003). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonio_Damasio
- **Claire Petitmengin** (Paris, France) — French researcher who developed micro-phenomenology from Vermersch's explicitation interview and Varela's neurophenomenology: a guided interview that helps people describe fine-grained lived experience. Soma designers use it to articulate felt experience. Known for: Describing one's subjective experience in the second person (2006); The Validity of First-Person Descriptions as Authenticity and Coherence (2013); Discovering the structures of lived experience (2019). https://www.clairepetitmengin.fr
- **Dan Zahavi / 丹·扎哈维** (Copenhagen, Denmark, 1967–) — Danish philosopher at the University of Copenhagen who directs the Center for Subjectivity Research and argues for the minimal, embodied self given in first-person experience. Known for: Subjectivity and Selfhood (2005); The Phenomenological Mind (2008, with Gallagher); Self and Other (2014). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dan_Zahavi
- **Don Hanlon Johnson** (San Francisco, US, 1934–) — Founder of the first graduate programme in somatics, at the California Institute of Integral Studies, whose anthologies documented the pioneers of body-based practice and argued for the body as a source of knowledge. Known for: Body: Recovering Our Sensual Wisdom (1983); Bone, Breath and Gesture (1995); Diverse Bodies, Diverse Practices (2018). https://www.ciis.edu/profiles/don-hanlon-johnson
- **Donna Haraway / 唐娜·哈拉维** (Santa Cruz, US, 1944–) — Feminist scholar of science whose cyborg and situated knowledges unsettle boundaries between human, animal and machine; her work underpins feminist and more-than-human strands of soma design. Known for: A Cyborg Manifesto (1985); Situated Knowledges (1988); Staying with the Trouble (2016). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donna_Haraway
- **Drew Leder** (Baltimore, US, 1954–) — Philosopher and physician at Loyola University Maryland whose 'The Absent Body' explains why the body recedes from awareness in smooth action and 'dys-appears' in pain or illness, a frame soma design uses to bring the body back into attention. Known for: The Absent Body (1990); The Distressed Body (2016). https://www.loyola.edu/academics/philosophy/faculty/leder-drew.html
- **Dōgen / 道元** (Echizen, Japan, 1200–1253) — Japanese Zen master who founded the Sōtō school and taught 'just sitting' (shikantaza) and the dropping off of body and mind (shinjin datsuraku), where practice with the whole body-mind is itself realisation. Known for: Shōbōgenzō (正法眼蔵); Fukanzazengi (普勧坐禅儀); Shinjin datsuraku (身心脱落). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/D%C5%8Dgen
- **Edmund Husserl / 埃德蒙德·胡塞尔** (Freiburg, Germany, 1859–1938) — Founder of phenomenology whose distinction between the body as object (Körper) and as lived, sensing body (Leib) and whose method of describing experience from the first person underlie phenomenology of the body and first-person design research. Known for: Logical Investigations (1900–01); Ideas II (posthumous, 1952); The Crisis of European Sciences (1936). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edmund_Husserl
- **Eleanor Rosch / 埃莉诺·罗什** (Berkeley, US, 1938–) — Berkeley psychologist known for prototype theory of categories and co-author of 'The Embodied Mind', which brought Buddhist mindfulness practice into cognitive science as a disciplined first-person method. Known for: Prototype theory of categorisation (1970s); The Embodied Mind (1991, with Varela and Thompson). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eleanor_Rosch
- **Elizabeth Grosz / 伊丽莎白·格罗兹** (Durham, US, 1952–) — Australian feminist philosopher whose 'Volatile Bodies' argues for a corporeal feminism that refuses the split between mind and body and between biology and culture. Known for: Volatile Bodies: Toward a Corporeal Feminism (1994); Becoming Undone (2011). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elizabeth_Grosz
- **Erin Manning / 艾琳·曼宁** (Montréal, Canada, 1969–) — Artist and philosopher at Concordia University who founded the SenseLab and writes on movement, relation and neurodiversity, thinking movement as relational before it is a body's property. Known for: Relationscapes: Movement, Art, Philosophy (2009); Always More Than One (2013); SenseLab. https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erin_Manning
- **Eugene Gendlin / 尤金·简德林** (Chicago, US, 1926–2017) — Philosopher and psychotherapist who described the 'felt sense', the bodily, not-yet-verbal sense of a situation, and developed Focusing to attend to it. Soma designers and micro-phenomenologists use his felt sense to articulate experience from within. Known for: Experiencing and the Creation of Meaning (1962); Focusing (1978); A Process Model (2018). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eugene_Gendlin
- **Evan Thompson / 伊凡·汤普森** (Vancouver, Canada, 1962–) — University of British Columbia philosopher and co-author of 'The Embodied Mind' who develops enactive cognition in dialogue with phenomenology and contemplative traditions, arguing for the deep continuity of life and mind. Known for: The Embodied Mind (1991, with Varela and Rosch); Mind in Life (2007); Waking, Dreaming, Being (2015); Why I Am Not a Buddhist (2020). https://evanthompson.me/
- **Francisco Varela / 弗朗西斯科·瓦雷拉** (Paris, France, 1946–2001) — Chilean biologist and neuroscientist who co-founded enactivism, arguing that cognition is embodied action, and joined Buddhist meditation with cognitive science. His neurophenomenology and first-person methods shaped micro-phenomenology and first-person design research. Known for: Autopoiesis and Cognition (1980, with Maturana); The Embodied Mind (1991, with Thompson and Rosch); Neurophenomenology (1996). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francisco_Varela
- **George Lakoff / 乔治·莱考夫** (Berkeley, US, 1941–) — Berkeley cognitive linguist whose theory of conceptual metaphor, developed with Mark Johnson, argues that thought is structured by bodily experience; widely used in embodied interaction to design with image schemas. Known for: Metaphors We Live By (1980, with Johnson); Women, Fire, and Dangerous Things (1987); Philosophy in the Flesh (1999, with Johnson). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Lakoff
- **Hanne De Jaegher** — Enactive philosopher who, with Ezequiel Di Paolo, proposed participatory sense-making: meaning is made together in the coordination of moving bodies, a key idea for designing shared and social soma experiences. Known for: Participatory sense-making (2007, with Di Paolo); Linguistic Bodies (2018, with Di Paolo and Cuffari). https://hannedejaegher.net/
- **Hubert Dreyfus / 休伯特·德雷福斯** (Berkeley, US, 1929–2017) — Berkeley philosopher who used Heidegger and Merleau-Ponty to argue that skilled coping is embodied and cannot be captured by rule-based AI; his critique influenced embodied interaction and the skill-acquisition model. Known for: What Computers Can't Do (1972); Being-in-the-World (1991); Mind over Machine (1986, with Stuart Dreyfus). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hubert_Dreyfus
- **Ichikawa Hiroshi / 市川浩** (Tokyo, Japan, 1931–2002) — Japanese philosopher of the body at Meiji University who developed a theory of 'mi', the living body-self that is at once body, mind and social being, in dialogue with Merleau-Ponty and Japanese language. Known for: 精神としての身体 (The Body as Spirit, 1975); 〈身〉の構造 (The Structure of Mi, 1984). https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/市川浩
- **Iris Marion Young / 艾丽斯·马瑞恩·杨** (Chicago, US, 1949–2006) — Political philosopher whose essay 'Throwing Like a Girl' showed how women's bodily comportment and motility are shaped by social constraint; a founding text of feminist phenomenology of the body. Known for: Throwing Like a Girl (1980); On Female Body Experience (2005). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iris_Marion_Young
- **John Dewey / 约翰·杜威** (New York, US, 1859–1952) — American pragmatist whose 'Art as Experience' grounds aesthetics in the lived, embodied flow of everyday experience; a student of F.M. Alexander, he is a root of Shusterman's somaesthetics and of experience-centred HCI. Known for: Experience and Nature (1925); Art as Experience (1934). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Dewey
- **Lisa Feldman Barrett / 丽莎·费德曼·巴瑞特** (Boston, US, 1963–) — Northeastern University neuroscientist whose theory of constructed emotion holds that emotions are made from interoceptive body budgeting and concepts, not triggered by fixed circuits, undercutting recognition-based affective computing. Known for: How Emotions Are Made (2017); Seven and a Half Lessons About the Brain (2020). https://lisafeldmanbarrett.com/
- **Marcel Mauss / 马塞尔·莫斯** (Paris, France, 1872–1950) — French anthropologist whose lecture 'Techniques of the Body' showed that walking, swimming, sleeping and breathing are learned, culturally specific techniques, opening the study of how cultures train bodies. Known for: Techniques of the Body (1935); The Gift (1925). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcel_Mauss
- **Mark Johnson / 马克·约翰逊** (Eugene, US, 1949–) — University of Oregon philosopher who showed that abstract meaning grows out of bodily image schemas and felt, aesthetic experience; his embodied aesthetics of meaning is a basis for designing with felt qualities. Known for: Metaphors We Live By (1980, with Lakoff); The Body in the Mind (1987); Philosophy in the Flesh (1999, with Lakoff); The Meaning of the Body (2007). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Johnson_(philosopher)
- **Maurice Merleau-Ponty / 莫里斯·梅洛-庞蒂** (Paris, France, 1908–1961) — French phenomenologist who argued that perception and meaning are rooted in the lived body (corps propre) and its motor intentionality, not in a detached mind. His notions of body schema, flesh and intercorporeality run through embodied interaction and soma design. Known for: Phenomenology of Perception (1945); The Visible and the Invisible (1964); Eye and Mind (1961). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maurice_Merleau-Ponty
- **Maxine Sheets-Johnstone** (Eugene, US, 1930–) — Dancer, choreographer and philosopher at the University of Oregon who argues for the primacy of movement: we first come to know ourselves and the world as animate, moving bodies, and thinking in movement comes before thinking in words. A key reference for kinesthetic and movement-based design. Known for: The Phenomenology of Dance (1966); The Primacy of Movement (1999; 2nd ed. 2011); The Corporeal Turn (2009). https://benjamins.com/catalog/aicr.82
- **Nishida Kitarō / 西田几多郎** (Kyoto, Japan, 1870–1945) — Founder of the Kyoto School whose 'pure experience', prior to the split of subject and object, and 'acting intuition', where seeing and making are one bodily act, bring Zen-informed body-mind thought into philosophy. Known for: An Inquiry into the Good (1911); Place (1926); Acting intuition (kōiteki chokkan). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kitar%C5%8D_Nishida
- **Pierre Vermersch** (Paris, France) — French psychologist who created the explicitation interview (entretien d'explicitation) for eliciting detailed descriptions of lived action, the basis of micro-phenomenology and of many first-person methods in design research. Known for: L'entretien d'explicitation (1994); Groupe de Recherche sur l'Explicitation (GREX). https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pierre_Vermersch
- **Richard Shusterman / 理查德·舒斯特曼** (Boca Raton, US, 1949–) — Pragmatist philosopher at Florida Atlantic University who founded somaesthetics, the study of the body as the site of sensory appreciation and creative self-fashioning. Höök's soma design takes its name and its call for cultivated bodily awareness from his work, which also draws on Confucian and Zen self-cultivation. Known for: Pragmatist Aesthetics (1992); Performing Live (2000); Body Consciousness (2008); Thinking through the Body (2012). https://www.shusterman.net
- **Shaun Gallagher / 肖恩·加拉格尔** (Memphis, US, 1948–) — Philosopher at the University of Memphis who clarified the difference between body schema and body image and developed enactivist and phenomenological accounts of embodied cognition used in HCI and design. Known for: How the Body Shapes the Mind (2005); The Phenomenological Mind (2008, with Zahavi); Enactivist Interventions (2017). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaun_Gallagher
- **Shigehisa Kuriyama / 栗山茂久** (Cambridge, US, 1954–) — Harvard historian of medicine whose comparison of Greek and Chinese medicine shows how touching the pulse and seeing the muscles produced different felt bodies, a reminder that body experience is culturally trained. Known for: The Expressiveness of the Body and the Divergence of Greek and Chinese Medicine (1999). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shigehisa_Kuriyama
- **Thomas Fuchs** (Heidelberg, Germany) — Psychiatrist and philosopher at Heidelberg University who develops phenomenological psychiatry and an ecological view of the brain as an organ of a living, embodied being, including work on body memory and intercorporeality. Known for: Ecology of the Brain (2018); In Defence of the Human Being (2021). https://de.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Fuchs_(Mediziner)
- **Thomas Hanna / 托马斯·汉纳** (Novato, US, 1928–1990) — Philosopher and Feldenkrais practitioner who coined 'somatics' and defined the soma as the body perceived from within by first-person perception, the sense of 'soma' that soma design adopts; founder of Hanna Somatic Education. Known for: Bodies in Revolt (1970); The Body of Life (1980); Somatics (1988). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Louis_Hanna
- **Tu Weiming / 杜维明** (Beijing, China, 1940–) — New Confucian philosopher at Harvard and Peking University who describes Confucian learning as embodied knowing (tizhi) and self-cultivation (xiushen), a Chinese frame for the knowing body in soma design. Known for: Confucian Thought: Selfhood as Creative Transformation (1985); Centrality and Commonality (1989); Humanity and Self-Cultivation (1979). http://tuweiming.net
- **Vittorio Gallese / 维托里奥·加莱塞** (Parma, Italy, 1959–) — Neuroscientist at the University of Parma, co-discoverer of mirror neurons, whose embodied simulation theory explains how we understand others' actions and feelings by re-enacting them in our own bodies. Known for: Discovery of mirror neurons (1990s, Parma group); Embodied simulation theory. https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vittorio_Gallese
- **Watsuji Tetsurō / 和辻哲郎** (Tokyo, Japan, 1889–1960) — Japanese philosopher who described human being as 'betweenness' (aidagara), always embodied in relations and in climate (fūdo), a counterpoint to individualist views of the body. Known for: Fūdo: Climate and Culture (1935); Ethics (Rinrigaku, 1937–49). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tetsur%C5%8D_Watsuji
- **William James / 威廉·詹姆斯** (Cambridge, US, 1842–1910) — American pragmatist and psychologist who argued that emotion is the feeling of bodily changes ('we feel sorry because we cry'), an early body-first theory of emotion that affective loops return to. Known for: What Is an Emotion? (1884); The Principles of Psychology (1890). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_James
- **Yang Rur-bin / 杨儒宾** (Hsinchu, Taiwan, 1956–) — Philosopher at National Tsing Hua University in Taiwan whose studies of the Confucian view of the body trace how qi, form and mind were understood as one cultivated whole in classical Chinese thought. Known for: 儒家身体观 (The Confucian View of the Body, 1996); 中国古代思想中的气论及身体观 (ed., 1993). https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/楊儒賓
- **Yuasa Yasuo / 汤浅泰雄** (Tokyo, Japan, 1925–2005) — Japanese philosopher who argued that in Eastern thought body-mind unity is not a given but an achievement of self-cultivation (shugyō) through practices such as meditation and the martial arts, a source for soma design's attention to training the body. Known for: The Body: Toward an Eastern Mind-Body Theory (1977; English 1987); The Body, Self-Cultivation, and Ki-Energy (1993). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yasuo_Yuasa

### Somatic practitioners & teachers

- **B. K. S. Iyengar / B·K·S·艾扬格** (Pune, India, 1918–2014) — Indian yoga teacher whose precise, prop-supported Iyengar Yoga and 'Light on Yoga' made asana and pranayama widely teachable as practices of alignment and breath awareness. Known for: Light on Yoga (1966); Light on Pranayama (1981); Iyengar Yoga. https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B._K._S._Iyengar
- **Bonnie Bainbridge Cohen** (El Sobrante, US, 1941–) — Movement artist and therapist who founded Body-Mind Centering, an embodied study of the body's systems, from organs and fluids to developmental movement patterns, experienced from the inside. Known for: Body-Mind Centering (founded 1973); Sensing, Feeling, and Action (1993). https://www.bodymindcentering.com
- **Charlotte Selver / 夏洛特·塞尔弗** (New York, US, 1901–2003) — Gindler's student who brought Sensory Awareness to the United States, teaching at Esalen and with Zen teachers; her simple experiments in noticing sensation are close cousins of soma design's attunement exercises. Known for: Sensory Awareness (taught in the US from 1938); Reclaiming Vitality and Presence (2007). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlotte_Selver
- **Cheng Man-ch'ing / 郑曼青** (Taipei, Taiwan; New York, US, 1902–1975) — Chinese tai chi master, painter and physician who created the Yang-style short form and brought tai chi to Taiwan and New York, where it spread as a practice of relaxed, rooted body-mind unity. Known for: Yang-style short form (37 postures); Cheng Tzu's Thirteen Treatises on T'ai Chi Ch'uan. https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cheng_Man-ch%27ing
- **Chungliang Al Huang / 黄忠良** (1937–) — Chinese-American dancer and tai chi teacher who taught at Esalen with Alan Watts and blended tai chi, dance and calligraphy into a practice of Tao as moving, felt experience. Known for: Embrace Tiger, Return to Mountain (1973); Tao: The Watercourse Way (1975, with Alan Watts); Living Tao Foundation. https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chungliang_Al_Huang
- **Elsa Gindler / 埃尔莎·金德勒** (Berlin, Germany, 1885–1961) — Berlin teacher whose practice of attentive, experimental sensing of breathing, standing and moving, rather than exercises, seeded Sensory Awareness and influenced many later somatic methods. Known for: Gymnastik für Berufsmenschen (1926); Sensory awareness work (Berlin, 1910s–1961). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elsa_Gindler
- **Emilie Conrad** (Los Angeles, US, 1934–2014) — Dancer who founded Continuum, a practice of breath, sound and wave-like micro-movement that explores the body as fluid process, informing somatic dance and fluid, undulating forms of interaction. Known for: Continuum Movement (founded 1967); Life on Land (2007). https://www.continuummovement.com
- **F. M. Alexander / F·M·亚历山大** (London, UK, 1869–1955) — Australian actor who developed the Alexander Technique to unlearn habitual tension through 'inhibition' and conscious direction of head, neck and back; John Dewey was his student, and the technique is a root of somatics. Known for: The Alexander Technique; The Use of the Self (1932); Man's Supreme Inheritance (1910). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F._Matthias_Alexander
- **Gerda Alexander / 格尔达·亚历山大** (Copenhagen, Denmark, 1908–1994) — Danish teacher who created Eutony, a practice of sensing and adjusting muscle tone through contact with the floor, objects and one's own skin. Known for: Eutony (Eutonie, from 1940); Eutony: The Holistic Discovery of the Total Person (1985). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gerda_Alexander
- **Ida Rolf / 艾达·罗尔夫** (New York, US, 1896–1979) — Biochemist who created Structural Integration (Rolfing), hands-on work on fascia to reorganise the body in gravity; one of the founding figures of somatic bodywork. Known for: Structural Integration (Rolfing); Rolfing: The Integration of Human Structures (1977). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ida_Rolf
- **Irmgard Bartenieff / 伊姆加德·巴特尼耶夫** (New York, US, 1900–1981) — Laban's student, dancer and physical therapist who developed the Bartenieff Fundamentals of connected, whole-body movement and brought Laban analysis to dance therapy in the United States. Known for: Bartenieff Fundamentals; Body Movement: Coping with the Environment (1980). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irmgard_Bartenieff
- **Jon Kabat-Zinn / 乔·卡巴金** (Massachusetts, US, 1944–) — Founder of Mindfulness-Based Stress Reduction at the University of Massachusetts, whose body scan and mindful movement brought Buddhist-derived practice into medicine; the body scan is a standard soma design exercise. Known for: Mindfulness-Based Stress Reduction (1979); Full Catastrophe Living (1990); Wherever You Go, There You Are (1994). https://jonkabat-zinn.com/
- **Mabel Elsworth Todd / 梅布尔·托德** (New York, US, 1880–1956) — Teacher whose 'The Thinking Body' used anatomical imagery to change posture and movement, the origin of ideokinesis in dance and somatic education. Known for: The Thinking Body (1937); Ideokinesis. https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mabel_Elsworth_Todd
- **Mary Starks Whitehouse** (Los Angeles, US, 1911–1979) — Dancer and Jungian-trained therapist whose 'movement in depth' became Authentic Movement: a mover follows inner impulses with eyes closed while a witness attends, a model of first- and second-person bodily attention. Known for: Authentic Movement ('movement in depth', 1950s–60s); Physical Movement and Personality (1963). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Authentic_Movement
- **Moshé Feldenkrais / 摩谢·费登奎斯** (Tel Aviv, Israel, 1904–1984) — Physicist and judoka who created the Feldenkrais Method, gentle, attentive movement lessons that refine self-awareness. Höök and her lab trained in Feldenkrais, and its lessons became a core soma design practice for sensitising the designer. Known for: Awareness Through Movement (1972); The Feldenkrais Method (Awareness Through Movement and Functional Integration); Body and Mature Behavior (1949). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mosh%C3%A9_Feldenkrais
- **Nancy Stark Smith / 南希·史塔克·史密斯** (Northampton, US, 1952–2020) — Dancer who co-developed Contact Improvisation with Steve Paxton and co-edited Contact Quarterly for decades, spreading the practice and its underscore worldwide. Known for: Contact Quarterly (co-founded 1975); Caught Falling (2008, with David Koteen). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nancy_Stark_Smith
- **Noguchi Michizō / 野口三千三** (Tokyo, Japan, 1914–1998) — Japanese physical educator who created Noguchi Taisō, a practice of feeling the body as a water-filled sack moved by gravity and ease rather than muscular effort, influential in Japanese theatre and somatics. Known for: Noguchi Taisō (野口体操); 原初生命体としての人間 (The Human as Primordial Life Form, 1972). https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/野口三千三
- **Peter A. Levine / 彼得·列文** (Boulder, US) — Psychologist who developed Somatic Experiencing, a body-based approach to trauma that tracks sensation and lets arrested survival responses complete, widely used in somatic therapy. Known for: Waking the Tiger (1997); In an Unspoken Voice (2010); Somatic Experiencing. https://www.somaticexperiencing.com/about-peter
- **Ron Kurtz** (Ashland, US, 1934–2011) — Psychotherapist who created the Hakomi Method, a body-centred, mindfulness-based therapy that uses gentle experiments in present-moment bodily experience. Known for: Hakomi Method (1970s); Body-Centered Psychotherapy: The Hakomi Method (1990). https://ronkurtz.com
- **Rudolf Laban / 鲁道夫·拉班** (Weybridge, UK, 1879–1958) — Dance theorist who created Labanotation and the effort and space theory that became Laban Movement Analysis, the vocabulary movement computing and MOCO researchers use to model movement qualities. Known for: Labanotation (1928); Effort (1947, with Lawrence); The Mastery of Movement (1950). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rudolf_von_Laban
- **Shunryū Suzuki / 铃木俊隆** (San Francisco, US, 1904–1971) — Sōtō Zen priest who founded the San Francisco Zen Center; 'Zen Mind, Beginner's Mind' taught zazen posture and breath as the practice itself, shaping Western contemplative practice. Known for: Zen Mind, Beginner's Mind (1970); San Francisco Zen Center (founded 1962). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shunry%C5%AB_Suzuki
- **Steve Paxton / 史蒂夫·帕克斯顿** (Vermont, US, 1939–2024) — Dancer and Judson Dance Theater member who initiated Contact Improvisation, where partners move through shared weight, touch and momentum; a model for designing with touch and intercorporeality. Known for: Contact Improvisation (1972); Material for the Spine (2008); Magnesium (1972). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Paxton
- **Thích Nhất Hạnh / 释一行** (Hue, Vietnam, 1926–2022) — Vietnamese Zen monk who founded Plum Village and taught mindful breathing and walking meditation worldwide; his practices of coming home to the body through breath inform breath-based design. Known for: The Miracle of Mindfulness (1975); Plum Village (founded 1982); Walking meditation. https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Th%C3%ADch_Nh%E1%BA%A5t_H%E1%BA%A1nh
- **Ueshiba Morihei / 植芝盛平** (Iwama, Japan, 1883–1969) — Founder of aikido, a martial art of blending with an attacker's movement and ki, framed as a spiritual practice of harmony; a reference for designing with partnered movement and yielding. Known for: Aikido; Budō (1938); The Art of Peace (teachings). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morihei_Ueshiba
- **Wang Xiangzhai / 王芗斋** (Beijing, China, 1885–1963) — Chinese martial artist who founded Yiquan and championed zhan zhuang (standing post), a practice of cultivating whole-body awareness and intent through stillness. Known for: Yiquan (意拳); Zhan zhuang standing practice (站桩). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wang_Xiangzhai
- **Wilhelm Reich / 威廉·赖希** (Rangeley, US, 1897–1957) — Austrian psychoanalyst who argued that emotional history is held in the body as 'muscular armour', founding body psychotherapy; later lineages include bioenergetics and Hakomi. Known for: Character Analysis (1933); Body armour (muscular armouring) concept. https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilhelm_Reich

### Artists, dancers & choreographers

- **Anna Halprin / 安娜·哈尔普林** (Kentfield, US, 1920–2021) — Dancer who pioneered postmodern and community dance on her outdoor deck, using dance for healing and collective ritual (Planetary Dance); a reference for participatory, somatic movement. Known for: Parades and Changes (1965); Planetary Dance (from 1981); Tamalpa Institute (1978, with Daria Halprin). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anna_Halprin
- **Char Davies / 夏尔·戴维斯** (Montréal, Canada, 1954–) — Canadian artist whose VR works Osmose and Ephémère are navigated by breath and balance, letting the 'immersant' float through translucent landscapes; a founding example of breath-based, embodied VR. Known for: Osmose (1995); Ephémère (1998). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Char_Davies
- **Deborah Hay / 黛博拉·海** (Austin, US, 1941–) — Choreographer whose practice asks performers questions such as 'what if every cell in my body at once has the potential to perceive?', making perception itself the dance.
