Known for: Solo Performance Commissioning Project (1998–); My Body, The Buddhist (2000); Using the Sky (2015). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deborah_Hay
- **Eiko Otake** (New York, US) — Japanese-born movement artist who, with Koma, made slow, earth-bound dances shaped by butoh study with Ohno and Hijikata; her solo 'A Body in Places' brings the fragile body into sites of disaster and memory. Known for: Eiko & Koma (1972–2014); A Body in Places (2014–); Duet Project. https://www.eikootake.org/
- **Ernesto Neto / 埃内斯托·内托** (Rio de Janeiro, Brazil, 1964–) — Brazilian artist whose soft, spice-scented, walk-in crocheted installations invite visitors to touch, smell and lie down, extending the Neo-Concrete tradition of sensorial participation. Known for: Leviathan Thot (2006); anthropodino (2009); GaiaMotherTree (2018). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ernesto_Neto
- **Hijikata Tatsumi / 土方巽** (Tokyo, Japan, 1928–1986) — Japanese dancer who founded butoh, a dance of the 'body that has died', using imagery-driven transformation that rejects Western dance form; a radical source for somatic performance. Known for: Kinjiki (1959); Revolt of the Body (1968); Butoh-fu (butoh notation). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tatsumi_Hijikata
- **Hélio Oiticica / 埃利奥·奥蒂西卡** (Rio de Janeiro, Brazil, 1937–1980) — Brazilian artist whose Parangolés, capes worn and danced in by participants, and immersive environments such as Tropicália made art something completed by the moving body. Known for: Parangolés (1964); Tropicália (1967); Éden (1969). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/H%C3%A9lio_Oiticica
- **Lin Hwai-min / 林怀民** (Taipei, Taiwan, 1947–) — Taiwanese choreographer who founded Cloud Gate Dance Theatre and trained dancers in tai chi daoyin, meditation, martial arts and calligraphy, building a contemporary dance from East Asian body-mind practice. Known for: Cloud Gate Dance Theatre (founded 1973); Songs of the Wanderers (1994); Cursive trilogy (2001–05). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lin_Hwai-min
- **Lisa Park** (New York, US) — Korean-American artist whose Eunoia uses an EEG headset to turn her brainwaves and emotional states into vibrations rippling pools of water, making inner states visible through the body. Known for: Eunoia (2013); Eunoia II (2014); Blooming (2018). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lisa_Park_(artist)
- **Lygia Clark / 莉吉亚·克拉克** (Rio de Janeiro, Brazil, 1920–1988) — Brazilian artist who moved from Neo-Concrete sculpture to relational objects handled on the body, and finally to therapeutic sessions; her sensorial works are touchstones for art felt through the skin. Known for: Bichos (1960); Sensorial masks and Relational Objects (1960s–70s); Structuring of the Self (1976–88). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lygia_Clark
- **Marco Donnarumma / 马可·多纳鲁马** (Berlin, Germany, 1984–) — Italian performer and researcher who plays the sounds of his own muscles with the Xth Sense biosensor and performs with AI-driven prostheses, staging the body as unstable and hybrid. Known for: Xth Sense (2011); Corpus Nil (2016); Amygdala (2018). http://marcodonnarumma.com
- **Marina Abramović / 玛丽娜·阿布拉莫维奇** (New York, US, 1946–) — Serbian performance artist whose durational works test the limits of body and presence, and whose Abramović Method trains audiences in slow walking, stillness and attention. Known for: Rhythm 0 (1974); The Artist Is Present (2010); Abramović Method. https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marina_Abramovi%C4%87
- **Merce Cunningham / 默斯·坎宁汉** (New York, US, 1919–2009) — Choreographer who used chance procedures and, from 1989, the LifeForms software co-developed with Thecla Schiphorst to compose dance, an early meeting of choreography and computing. Known for: Merce Cunningham Dance Company (1953); Trackers (1991, made with LifeForms); BIPED (1999). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merce_Cunningham
- **Min Tanaka / 田中泯** (Yamanashi, Japan, 1945–) — Japanese dancer who developed Body Weather, a training that treats the body as weather, changing with its environment, practised on his farm; it shaped Lian Loke and many dance-tech practitioners. Known for: Body Weather (from 1978); Hyperdance; Body Weather Farm (1985). http://www.min-tanaka.com
- **Myron Krueger / 迈伦·克鲁格** (US, 1942–) — Computer artist whose VIDEOPLACE let people's silhouettes play with graphics and with each other through whole-body movement, decades before motion sensing; an origin of responsive environments. Known for: VIDEOPLACE (1975); Artificial Reality (1983). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myron_W._Krueger
- **Ohno Kazuo / 大野一雄** (Yokohama, Japan, 1906–2010) — Co-founder of butoh with Hijikata, whose improvised, deeply interior dancing into his hundreds made the felt inner life of the body visible. Known for: Admiring La Argentina (1977); My Mother (1981). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kazuo_Ohno
- **Rafael Lozano-Hemmer / 拉斐尔·洛萨诺-亨默** (Montréal, Canada, 1967–) — Mexican-Canadian artist whose biometric installations, such as Pulse Room, where each visitor's heartbeat makes a bulb flicker among hundreds, turn shared bodily rhythms into public space. Known for: Pulse Room (2006); Pulse Park (2008); Body Movies (2001). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Lozano-Hemmer
- **Rebecca Horn / 丽贝卡·霍恩** (Berlin, Germany, 1944–2024) — German artist whose early body extensions, prosthetic gloves, fans and masks, altered how wearers touch and sense space, early examples of wearable estrangement. Known for: Unicorn (1970–72); Finger Gloves (1972); Arm Extensions (1968). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rebecca_Horn
- **Shen Wei / 沈伟** (New York, US, 1968–) — Chinese choreographer and painter who created Natural Body Development, a movement system drawing on Chinese opera, tai chi and anatomy, and choreographed the 2008 Beijing Olympics opening scroll. Known for: Natural Body Development; Folding (2000); Connect Transfer (2004). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shen_Wei
- **Stelarc / 斯特拉克** (Perth, Australia, 1946–) — Cypriot-Australian performance artist who extends and exposes the body with robotic arms, internet-actuated muscles and a cell-grown ear, provoking questions about the obsolete and augmented body. Known for: Third Hand (1980); Ping Body (1996); Ear on Arm (2006–). http://stelarc.org/
- **Tehching Hsieh / 谢德庆** (New York, US, 1950–) — Taiwanese-American artist whose year-long performances, punching a time clock every hour or being tied to another person, make lived time and bodily endurance the material of art. Known for: Time Clock Piece (1980–81); Rope Piece (1983–84, with Linda Montano); Thirteen Year Plan (1986–99). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tehching_Hsieh
- **Trisha Brown / 崔莎·布朗** (New York, US, 1936–2017) — Postmodern choreographer whose release-based, fluid movement and gravity-defying equipment pieces changed how dancers use weight, momentum and ease. Known for: Man Walking Down the Side of a Building (1970); Accumulation (1971); Set and Reset (1983). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trisha_Brown
- **Wayne McGregor / 韦恩·麦格雷戈** (London, UK, 1970–) — British choreographer who works with cognitive scientists and AI, from studies of choreographic thinking to Living Archive, a tool trained on his archive that proposes movement to dancers. Known for: Company Wayne McGregor (founded 1992); Living Archive: An AI Performance Experiment (2019, with Google Arts & Culture). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wayne_McGregor
- **Wen Hui / 文慧** (Beijing, China, 1960–) — Beijing choreographer who co-founded Living Dance Studio, China's first independent dance company, and makes documentary dance from women's and everyday bodies and their memories. Known for: Living Dance Studio (co-founded 1994 with Wu Wenguang); Report on the Body (2002); Memory (2008). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wen_Hui_(dancer)
- **William Forsythe / 威廉·福赛斯** (Frankfurt, Germany, 1949–) — Choreographer whose Improvisation Technologies and Synchronous Objects made the principles of movement visible and analysable, bridging choreography and computing. Known for: Improvisation Technologies (CD-ROM, 1999); Choreographic Objects; Synchronous Objects (2009). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Forsythe_(choreographer)
- **Yvonne Rainer / 伊冯娜·雷纳** (New York, US, 1934–) — Judson Dance Theater choreographer whose Trio A stripped dance to task-like, everyday movement, turning attention to the ordinary moving body. Known for: Trio A (1966); No Manifesto (1965). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yvonne_Rainer
- **Zhang Huan / 张洹** (Shanghai, China, 1965–) — Chinese artist from Beijing's East Village whose endurance performances, such as sitting covered in honey in a public latrine, use the body to register physical and social conditions. Known for: 12 Square Meters (1994); To Add One Meter to an Anonymous Mountain (1995); Family Tree (2000). http://www.zhanghuan.com

### Labs & research groups

- **ACCAD (Ohio State University)** (Columbus, US) — The Advanced Computing Center for the Arts and Design at Ohio State, a hub for motion capture, dance and technology, where Synchronous Objects with William Forsythe and later dance-tech work were produced. https://accad.osu.edu
- **BeAnotherLab** (Barcelona, ES) — An interdisciplinary collective based in Barcelona that created The Machine to Be Another, an embodiment system in which two people swap first-person perspectives and move in synchrony to 'be in another's body', used in art, research and dialogue work. https://beanotherlab.org
- **Centre for Dance Research (C-DaRE), Coventry University** (Coventry, UK) — Coventry University's dance research centre, directed by Sarah Whatley, working on somatic practices, dance documentation, digital performance and disability; it hosts the Journal of Dance & Somatic Practices and the International Dance and Somatic Practices Conference. https://www.coventry.ac.uk/research/areas-of-research/centre-for-dance-research/
- **Embodied Media (Keio University KMD) / 庆应义塾大学 Embodied Media 实验室** (Yokohama, JP) — Kouta Minamizawa's lab at the Keio University Graduate School of Media Design in Yokohama, creating media that record, share and enhance bodily experience, from haptic design and the TECHTILE toolkit to telexistence. It is a centre of Japan's 'haptic design' community. https://www.embodiedmedia.org/
- **ex)situ (LISN, Université Paris-Saclay / Inria)** (Paris-Saclay, FR) — The Extreme Situated Interaction team at LISN, Université Paris-Saclay and Inria, led by Wendy Mackay; Sarah Fdili Alaoui developed her research on dance, somatics and first-person methods in HCI here. https://ex-situ.lisn.upsaclay.fr/
- **Exertion Games Lab** (Melbourne, AU) — Florian 'Floyd' Mueller's lab at Monash University in Melbourne, designing 'bodily play': exertion games, integrated human-computer bodies and playful interactions with the body. It co-organised the CHI 2017 soma-based design theory workshop and the Dagstuhl seminar on body-centric computing. https://exertiongameslab.org
- **Human Computer Integration Lab (University of Chicago)** (Chicago, US) — Pedro Lopes's lab at the University of Chicago, which builds interfaces that borrow parts of the user's body, such as electrical muscle stimulation that moves the hand, chemical haptics and temperature illusions. It raises questions of agency and ownership central to soma design with machines. https://lab.plopes.org
- **i_mBODY Lab (Universidad Carlos III de Madrid)** (Madrid, ES) — The Interactive Multisensory Body-centred Experiences lab at UC3M Madrid, led by Ana Tajadura-Jiménez, which uses sound, touch and vision to change how people perceive their own bodies (e.g. Magic Feet, body-transformation experiences in BODYinTRANSIT). Elena Márquez Segura works here on embodied ideation and sketching. https://imbodylab.com/
- **IRCAM Sound Music Movement Interaction (ISMM)** (Paris, FR) — The team at IRCAM / STMS Lab in Paris that studies movement–sound interaction: gesture following, interactive sonification and movement learning, with tools such as MuBu and CoMo used by dancers, musicians and soma designers. Frédéric Bevilacqua leads it; Jules Françoise worked here on movement sonification and somatic learning. https://www.stms-lab.fr/team/interaction-son-musique-mouvement/
- **JEDI Lab (National University of Singapore)** (Singapore, SG) — The Joyful Experiences in Design and Interaction lab in NUS's Department of Design, which studies technology for reflection, care and rest. Its CHI 2026 paper 'Toward Pluralizing Reflection in HCI through Daoism' draws on Daoist priests and practitioners to describe embodied, relational reflection (stillness, resonance, emergence). https://joyfuldesignlab.com
- **JST ERATO Inami JIZAI Body Project / 稻见自在化身体项目** (Tokyo, JP) — Masahiko Inami's research project (University of Tokyo, with Keio, Waseda and other partners) on 'body editing': how perception, action and social behaviour change when bodies are augmented, e.g. JIZAI ARMS, a wearable set of detachable robot arms. It frames augmentation through the Japanese idea of jizai, moving freely and at ease. https://www.jst.go.jp/erato/inami/en/index.html
- **KTH Interaction Design group (Soma Design)** (Stockholm, SE) — Kristina Höök's research group at KTH's Division of Media Technology and Interaction Design in Stockholm, where soma design was formulated and where Soma Mat, Breathing Light, the Soma Bits and much feminist intimate-health design were made. The group works in 'the IxD Jungle' at the top of KTH's main building and trains designers through Feldenkrais-based somaesthetic practice. https://www.kth.se/mid/research/interaction-design
- **MIT Media Lab Fluid Interfaces** (Cambridge, US) — Pattie Maes's group at the MIT Media Lab building wearable and physiological interfaces for attention, memory, sleep and wellbeing, such as Dormio for dream incubation and biofeedback wearables for calm. https://www.media.mit.edu/groups/fluid-interfaces/overview/
- **MIT Media Lab Tangible Media Group / MIT 可触媒体组** (Cambridge, US) — Hiroshi Ishii's group at the MIT Media Lab, which founded tangible user interfaces and made early remote-touch works such as inTouch (1997), later shape displays (inFORM) and 'radical atoms'. Its work is a starting point for touch and shape-change in soma design. https://tangible.media.mit.edu
- **Mixed Reality Laboratory (Nottingham)** (Nottingham, UK) — The University of Nottingham's interdisciplinary HCI lab led by Steve Benford, known for performance-led research with artists (Blast Theory) and for body-centred works such as the breath-controlled Exobuilding and the Broncomatic breathing ride. It is a key partner of Höök's group in soma design and somatic play. https://www.nottingham.ac.uk/research/groups/mixedrealitylab/
- **Mobile Life Centre** (Stockholm, SE) — A VINN Excellence Centre (2007–2017) in Kista, Stockholm, hosted by Stockholm University with SICS (now RISE) and the Interactive Institute, co-founded and directed by Kristina Höök. Its studios produced affective-loop and early somaesthetic prototypes (eMoto, Affective Diary, Brightr, Soma Mat) that seeded soma design. https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mobile_Life_Centre
- **NTNU Department of Computer Science (IDI)** (Trondheim, NO) — The Norwegian University of Science and Technology's computer science department in Trondheim, home of Dag Svanæs, whose work on Merleau-Ponty and interaction design and on 'kinaesthetic thinking' made phenomenology of the body usable for HCI. Svanæs co-organised the Dagstuhl seminar on body-centric computing. https://www.ntnu.edu/idi
- **Open Lab (Newcastle University)** (Newcastle upon Tyne, UK) — Newcastle University's digital civics and HCI lab, where Madeline Balaam led feminist research on women's intimate health and pelvic-floor technologies before moving to KTH. It is a reference point for feminist and care-oriented soma design. https://openlab.ncl.ac.uk
- **Reality Design Lab / 现实设计实验室** — Botao 'Amber' Hu's lab designing speculative and immersive realities (HoloKit, EchoVision) and teaching 'Somaesthetic Realities'; it makes this gallery, Somatic Reality Inspire. https://reality.design
- **SFU School of Interactive Arts & Technology (SIAT)** (Surrey, CA) — Simon Fraser University's school in Surrey, BC, where Thecla Schiphorst brought somatics and dance into interaction design (whisper, soft(n), somatic connoisseurship), Philippe Pasquier's Metacreation Lab works on movement and generative art, and Diane Gromala built pain-focused VR. https://www.sfu.ca/siat.html
- **Social Emotional Technology Lab (UC Santa Cruz)** (Santa Cruz, US) — Katherine Isbister's lab at UC Santa Cruz designing games and wearables for social and emotional connection, including physical co-located play and the Sensual Evaluation Instrument lineage. Isbister is a long-time collaborator of Höök on affective and somaesthetic design. https://setlab.soe.ucsc.edu
- **Synthesis Center (Arizona State University)** (Tempe, US) — Sha Xin Wei's research centre at ASU building responsive environments in which movement, sound and light co-evolve, grounded in phenomenology and process philosophy; it continues the Topological Media Lab's work from Concordia University. https://synthesiscenter.net
- **Tsinghua University Future Laboratory / 清华大学未来实验室** (Beijing, CN) — An interdisciplinary laboratory at Tsinghua University in Beijing whose Center for Perception and Consciousness Research designs multisensory, touch-based experiences such as tactile music appreciation and dream visualisation, and which publishes widely at CHI on haptics and human-computer interaction. https://thfl.tsinghua.edu.cn/index.htm
- **TU/e Department of Industrial Design** (Eindhoven, NL) — Eindhoven University of Technology's design department, where Caroline Hummels and colleagues developed embodied, phenomenology-informed design education and research (skills-based design, embodied sensemaking, transformative practices). Hummels co-organised the CHI 2017 soma-based design theory workshop. https://www.tue.nl/en/our-university/departments/industrial-design
- **UCL Interaction Centre (UCLIC)** (London, UK) — University College London's HCI centre, home of Nadia Bianchi-Berthouze's research on affective body movement and touch, including movement sonification for people with chronic pain (Go-with-the-Flow) and body-perception work with Ana Tajadura-Jiménez. https://www.ucl.ac.uk/uclic/
- **University of Sydney Design Lab** (Sydney, AU) — The research arm of design at the University of Sydney's School of Architecture, Design and Planning (formerly the Key Centre of Design Computing and Cognition). Lian Loke, a former head, translates dance and somatic knowledge into interaction design, e.g. Labanotation for movement-based interaction and the 'moving and making strange' approach with Toni Robertson. https://design.sydney.edu.au/about/

### Somatic schools & trainings

- **Aikikai Foundation / 合气会** (Tokyo, JP) — The Tokyo headquarters (Hombu Dōjō) of aikido founded by Morihei Ueshiba, whose practice of blending with a partner's movement is used in somatic education and embodied design. https://aikikai.or.jp/
- **American Society for the Alexander Technique (AmSAT) / 美国亚历山大技巧协会** (US) — The largest US association of certified Alexander Technique teachers, setting teacher-training standards for the practice of noticing and inhibiting habitual patterns of use, which soma designers cite as a model of estrangement and re-education. https://alexandertechniqueusa.org/
- **Chinese Health Qigong Association / 中国健身气功协会** (Beijing, CN) — China's national body for health qigong, which standardises and teaches forms such as Baduanjin, Wuqinxi, Yijinjing and Liuzijue that regulate body, breath and mind together. https://www.chqa.org.cn
- **Continuum Movement / Continuum 连续体动作** — The practice founded by Emilie Conrad that uses breath, sound and slow, wave-like movement to explore the body as fluid; teachers continue it through workshops and trainings. https://continuummovement.com
- **Dr. Ida Rolf Institute / 罗尔夫研究所** (Boulder, US) — The school of Rolfing Structural Integration, the manual and movement education method developed by Ida Rolf, one of the founding somatic disciplines taught at Esalen in the 1960s. https://rolf.org
- **Feldenkrais Guild of North America / 北美费登奎斯协会** — The professional association of Feldenkrais practitioners in the US and Canada, which lists accredited trainings, teachers and Awareness Through Movement classes. https://feldenkrais.com
- **Hakomi Institute / Hakomi 研究所** — The training organisation for Hakomi, Ron Kurtz's mindfulness-centred somatic psychotherapy that studies how beliefs are held in posture, gesture and felt sensation. https://hakomiinstitute.com
- **International Feldenkrais Federation (IFF) / 国际费登奎斯联合会** — The international umbrella body of Feldenkrais Method guilds and training accreditation boards, which maintains the Method's standards, archives and research resources. Feldenkrais lessons (Awareness Through Movement) are the somatic training most used in Höök's soma design. https://feldenkrais-method.org
- **International Yang Style Tai Chi Chuan Association / 国际杨氏太极拳协会** — The association led by Yang Jun, a fifth-generation lineage holder of Yang-style tai chi, which teaches the traditional Yang forms through affiliated centres worldwide. https://yangfamilytaichi.com
- **Kazuo Ohno Dance Studio / 大野一雄舞踏研究所** (Yokohama, JP) — The butoh studio of Kazuo Ohno and Yoshito Ohno in Yokohama, which still holds regular workshops and keeps the Kazuo Ohno Archives of films, photographs and notes. http://www.kazuoohnodancestudio.com/english/
- **Laban/Bartenieff Institute of Movement Studies (LIMS) / 拉班/巴特尼夫动作研究所** (New York, US) — The New York institute founded by Irmgard Bartenieff that certifies Laban Movement Analysts and teaches Bartenieff Fundamentals, the vocabulary of Effort, Shape and Space that movement-based interaction designers use to describe felt movement qualities. https://labaninstitute.org
- **Noguchi Taisō Association / 野口体操之会** (Tokyo, JP) — The group that continues Noguchi Michizō's (1914–1998) Noguchi Taisō, a Japanese movement practice of releasing unnecessary effort and letting weight and fluid move the body, taught for decades at Tokyo University of the Arts. https://www.noguchi-taisou.jp/
- **Plum Village / 梅村** (Dordogne, FR) — The monastery and practice centre founded by Zen master Thich Nhat Hanh in south-west France, teaching mindful breathing, walking meditation and 'interbeing' to a worldwide lay community. https://plumvillage.org
- **San Francisco Zen Center / 旧金山禅修中心** (San Francisco, US) — The Sōtō Zen community founded by Shunryu Suzuki, author of Zen Mind, Beginner's Mind, which runs City Center, Green Gulch Farm and Tassajara; a main route by which zazen reached Western designers and scholars. https://www.sfzc.org
- **Seitai Kyokai / 整体协会** (Tokyo, JP) — The Japanese association founded by Haruchika Noguchi to teach seitai, including katsugen undō, spontaneous 'regenerating movement' that lets the body move by itself, and yukiho, attentive laying-on of hands. https://www.seitai.org
- **Society of Teachers of the Alexander Technique (STAT) / 亚历山大技巧教师协会** (London, UK) — The oldest professional body for the Alexander Technique, founded in London in 1958 by teachers trained by F. M. Alexander; it accredits teacher-training courses and runs an extensive public guide to the Technique. https://alexandertechnique.co.uk
- **Somatic Experiencing International / 国际身体经验疗法协会** — The organisation that trains practitioners in Peter Levine's Somatic Experiencing, a body-based approach to trauma that works with felt sensation, titration and the nervous system's capacity to settle. https://traumahealing.org
- **Tamalpa Institute / 塔玛尔帕学院** (California, US) — Founded in 1978 by Anna and Daria Halprin in Marin County, California, the institute trains people in the Life/Art Process, a movement-based expressive arts therapy that links body, feeling and imagery. https://tamalpa.org/
- **The Discipline of Authentic Movement / 真实动作修习** — Janet Adler's development of Mary Starks Whitehouse's Authentic Movement: a mover moves with eyes closed while a witness attends, training embodied presence and first-person articulation. https://disciplineofauthenticmovement.com
- **The International Focusing Institute / 国际聚焦研究所** (New York, US) — The institute founded by Eugene Gendlin to teach Focusing, attending to the bodily 'felt sense' of a situation before words, and his Thinking at the Edge. Soma designers use Focusing to articulate felt experience. https://focusing.org
- **The School for Body-Mind Centering / 身心平衡技法学校** — The school founded by Bonnie Bainbridge Cohen to teach Body-Mind Centering, an experiential study of body systems, developmental movement and embodied anatomy that shapes somatic dance and several soma design methods. https://www.bodymindcentering.com
- **Trinity Laban Conservatoire of Music and Dance / 圣三一拉班音乐舞蹈学院** (London, UK) — The London conservatoire whose dance faculty descends from Rudolf Laban's Art of Movement Studio and the Laban Centre; it houses the Laban Archive and teaches somatic approaches alongside contemporary dance. https://www.trinitylaban.ac.uk
- **UMass Memorial Center for Mindfulness / 麻省大学正念中心** (Worcester, US) — The centre that grew from Jon Kabat-Zinn's Stress Reduction Clinic (1979) at the University of Massachusetts Medical School, where Mindfulness-Based Stress Reduction and its body scan were developed and taught. https://www.ummhealth.org/services-treatments/center-mindfulness
- **Vipassana Meditation (dhamma.org) / 内观禅修（葛印卡传承）** — The worldwide network of centres offering S. N. Goenka's free ten-day Vipassana courses, in which practitioners systematically scan bodily sensations with equanimity, one of the most widespread body-scan practices. https://www.dhamma.org/en/index

### Conferences & workshops

- **ACM CHI Conference on Human Factors in Computing Systems** — The largest HCI conference, where most soma design papers, workshops and first-person research have appeared (e.g. Somaesthetic Appreciation Design, 2016; the soma-based design theory workshop, 2017). https://chi2027.acm.org
- **ACM CHI PLAY** — The ACM conference on play and games research, where exertion games, bodily play and somatic play design are presented. https://chiplay.acm.org
- **ACM Designing Interactive Systems (DIS)** — The ACM conference on design practice and research in interaction, a frequent venue for soma design methods such as Teaching Soma Design (2019) and Sketching Soma Bits (2022). https://dis.acm.org
- **ACM Tangible, Embedded and Embodied Interaction (TEI)** — The ACM conference on tangible, embedded and embodied interaction, with studios and an arts track where soma designers demonstrate and share material and bodily explorations. https://tei.acm.org
- **Affective Computing and Intelligent Interaction (ACII)** — The main international conference of the affective computing community (AAAC), covering emotion sensing, physiological signals and affective interaction, the field that Höök's 'affective loops' responded to. https://acii-conf.net
- **Augmented Humans Conference** — An international conference on technologies that extend human perception, action and body, from supernumerary limbs to sensory substitution; useful for seeing where augmentation meets felt embodiment. https://augmented-humans.org
- **Dagstuhl Seminar 17392: Body-Centric Computing** (Wadern, DE) — A week-long seminar at Schloss Dagstuhl in September 2017 where 23 researchers mapped the future of body-centric computing; organised by Steve Benford, Kristina Höök, Joe Marshall, Florian Mueller and Dag Svanæs and reported in ACM Interactions (2018). https://www.dagstuhl.de/17392
- **Design Culture & Somaesthetics Conference** (Budapest, HU) — A 2019 conference at Moholy-Nagy University of Art and Design in Budapest, organised with the Hungarian Forum of Somaesthetics, with Richard Shusterman as keynote, on how somaesthetics can inform design culture. https://www.designresearchsociety.org/events/design-culture-somaesthetics-conference
- **ICHEC (formerly Chinese CHI)** — The International Conference on Human-Engaged Computing of the International Association of Chinese HCI (ICACHI), formerly Chinese CHI; it gathers Chinese-speaking and Asian HCI researchers around technology, culture and lived experience. ICHEC 2025 was held at Singapore Management University. https://ichec.icachi.org/
- **International Dance and Somatic Practices Conference** (Coventry, UK) — The conference series hosted by C-DaRE at Coventry University that brings together dance artists, somatic educators and researchers; its sixth edition took place in 2023. https://www.coventry.ac.uk/events/2023/dance-and-somatic-practices-conference-2023/
- **MOCO: International Conference on Movement and Computing** — A conference that brings together dancers, somatic practitioners, artists and researchers working on movement and computation, with papers, performances and workshops. MOCO'26 was held in Montpellier. https://www.movementcomputing.org/
- **NIME: New Interfaces for Musical Expression** — The international conference on new musical instruments and interfaces, where bodily, gestural and biosignal instruments (e.g. muscle-sensing and breath instruments) are presented and performed. https://nime.org/
- **NordiCHI** — The Nordic conference on human-computer interaction, a home of the Scandinavian design tradition from which Höök's group and many soma design papers come. http://www.nordichi.org/
- **Soma-Based Design Theory (CHI 2017 workshop)** — A CHI 2017 workshop that followed the CHI 2016 meeting on aesthetics in soma-based design and moved from philosophy to design theory, asking how practical, pragmatic and analytical work can build soma design knowledge. https://exertiongameslab.org/wp-content/uploads/2017/02/soma_chi2017.pdf

### Art & dance venues, festivals

- **Ars Electronica / 林茨电子艺术节** (Linz, AT) — The festival, centre and Prix for art, technology and society in Linz, where many body-based works, from Stelarc's performances to JIZAI ARMS, have been shown. https://ars.electronica.art
- **Cloud Gate Dance Theatre / 云门舞集** (New Taipei, TW) — Taiwan's leading contemporary dance company, founded by Lin Hwai-min, whose dancers train in tai chi daoyin, qigong, martial arts and meditation; works such as Songs of the Wanderers and the Cursive trilogy grow from this body training. https://www.cloudgate.org.tw
- **Dance Umbrella** (London, UK) — London's international festival of contemporary dance, presenting dance in theatres and public space each October. https://danceumbrella.co.uk
- **Esalen Institute / 伊莎兰学院** (Big Sur, US) — The retreat centre at Big Sur, California, where Moshe Feldenkrais, Ida Rolf, Charlotte Selver and others taught in the 1960s–70s; the word and field of 'somatics' grew in its human potential movement milieu. https://www.esalen.org
- **ImPulsTanz Vienna International Dance Festival** (Vienna, AT) — Europe's largest contemporary dance festival, held every summer in Vienna, with hundreds of workshops in somatic practices, contact improvisation and dance technique alongside performances. https://www.impulstanz.com
- **Jacob's Pillow** (Becket, US) — The dance centre and summer festival founded by Ted Shawn on a farm in Becket, Massachusetts, a National Historic Landmark with an extensive dance archive. https://www.jacobspillow.org
- **Judson Memorial Church (Judson Dance Theater)** (New York, US) — The Greenwich Village church whose gymnasium hosted Judson Dance Theater (1962–1964), where Yvonne Rainer, Steve Paxton, Trisha Brown and others turned pedestrian and task-based movement into dance, the ground from which contact improvisation grew. https://www.judson.org
- **Movement Research** (New York, US) — A New York laboratory for dance and movement-based practice founded in 1978, offering classes, residencies, the Movement Research at Judson Church series and the Performance Journal; many somatic and release techniques circulated through it. https://movementresearch.org/
- **NTT InterCommunication Center (ICC)** (Tokyo, JP) — A media art centre in Tokyo Opera City run by NTT East, showing interactive and sensory works on perception, communication and the body. https://www.ntticc.or.jp/en/
- **Taiwan Contemporary Culture Lab (C-LAB) / 台湾当代文化实验场** (Taipei, TW) — A cultural laboratory on a former air-force base in Taipei with a Future Vision Lab and technology-art residencies, showing immersive, sound and body-based work. https://clab.org.tw
- **Tanzfabrik Berlin** (Berlin, DE) — A Berlin production house and dance school with a long-standing programme of contact improvisation, release and somatic classes, residencies and performances. https://www.tanzfabrik-berlin.de
- **Tanzquartier Wien** (Vienna, AT) — Vienna's centre for contemporary dance and performance in the MuseumsQuartier, combining performances with theory, research and a public dance library. https://www.tqw.at/
- **V2_ Lab for the Unstable Media** (Rotterdam, NL) — The Rotterdam centre for art and technology that has long supported body-centred media art, from sensor-based performance to wearable and interactive installations. https://v2.nl
- **Yamaguchi Center for Arts and Media (YCAM) / 山口情报艺术中心** (Yamaguchi, JP) — An art centre in Yamaguchi, Japan, whose in-house YCAM InterLab develops media technology with artists, including Reactor for Awareness in Motion (RAM), a dance-creation toolkit made with Forsythe dancer Yoko Ando. https://www.ycam.jp/en/

### Journals, books & podcasts

- **ACM Interactions** — The ACM's magazine for the HCI community, where accessible pieces on soma design and body-centric computing have appeared (e.g. the Dagstuhl 'Body-centric computing' report, 2018). https://interactions.acm.org
- **ACM Transactions on Computer-Human Interaction (TOCHI)** — The ACM's flagship HCI journal, which published key soma design papers such as 'Moving and Making Strange' (2013), 'Unpacking Non-Dualistic Design: The Soma Design Case' (2021). https://dl.acm.org/journal/tochi
- **Body & Society** — A SAGE journal of the social and cultural analysis of the body, spanning phenomenology, feminist theory, affect and technology. https://journals.sagepub.com/home/bod
- **Body, Space & Technology** — An open-access journal on performance, the body and digital technology, publishing research on dance-tech, telematic performance and embodied media. https://www.bstjournal.com
- **Choreographic Practices** — An Intellect journal on choreography as an expanded practice, including choreographic thinking in research, technology and everyday life. https://www.intellectbooks.com/choreographic-practices
- **Constructivist Foundations** — An open-access journal on constructivist and enactive approaches that publishes micro-phenomenology and second-person methods research, with open peer commentary. https://constructivist.info
- **Contact Quarterly** — The dance and improvisation journal that began in 1975 as the newsletter of the contact improvisation community, edited for decades by Nancy Stark Smith and Lisa Nelson; also a publisher of books and videos on somatic practice. https://contactquarterly.com
- **Journal of Consciousness Studies** — The interdisciplinary journal whose 1999 special issue 'The View from Within' (eds. Francisco Varela and Jonathan Shear) set out first-person methods for the study of experience. https://www.imprint.co.uk/product/jcs/
- **Journal of Dance & Somatic Practices** — A peer-reviewed journal published by Intellect and edited from Coventry University's C-DaRE, covering somatic practices in dance, performance, education and research. https://www.intellectbooks.com/journal-of-dance-somatic-practices
- **Journal of Embodied Research** — An open-access, peer-reviewed journal whose articles are videos, publishing embodied and practice-led research in which the body carries the argument. https://jer.openlibhums.org
- **Phenomenology and the Cognitive Sciences** — A Springer journal linking phenomenology with cognitive science, home of much work on embodiment, enaction and first-person methods (founded by Shaun Gallagher and Natalie Depraz). https://link.springer.com/journal/11097
- **Somatechnics** — An Edinburgh University Press journal on how bodies and technologies co-constitute each other, with a strong feminist and queer focus. https://www.euppublishing.com/loi/soma
- **The Journal of Somaesthetics / 身体美学期刊** (Aalborg, DK) — The open-access journal of somaesthetics published by Aalborg University Press with Florida Atlantic University's Center for Body, Mind, and Culture; its 2019 issue 'Somaesthetics and Technology' gathered soma design work. https://journals.aau.dk/index.php/JOS

### Networks & associations

- **ACM SIGCHI** — The ACM Special Interest Group on Computer-Human Interaction, which sponsors CHI, DIS, TEI, CHI PLAY and other venues where soma design is published. https://sigchi.org
- **Association for Hanna Somatic Education / Hanna 身心教育协会** — The association of practitioners of Thomas Hanna's Hanna Somatic Education, the method of the thinker who coined 'soma' as the body experienced from within and founded the Novato Institute and Somatics magazine-journal. https://www.associationforhannasomaticeducation.com/
- **Body-Mind Centering Association (BMCA) / 身心平衡技法协会** — The international membership association of Body-Mind Centering practitioners and teachers, with a professional locator, conferences and recordings. https://www.bmcassociation.org
- **Center for Body, Mind, and Culture (Florida Atlantic University) / 身体、心智与文化中心** (Boca Raton, US) — Richard Shusterman's centre at Florida Atlantic University, the international hub of somaesthetics: it co-publishes the Journal of Somaesthetics, runs conferences such as 'Bodies of Culture: Somaesthetic Explorations' (2026) and hosts visiting scholars. https://www.fau.edu/artsandletters/bodymindculture/
- **ISMETA (International Somatic Movement Education and Therapy Association) / 国际身体动作教育与治疗协会** — The professional association, active since 1988, that sets standards for and registers somatic movement educators and therapists across many methods (BMC, Laban/Bartenieff, Hanna Somatics, Continuum and others). https://ismeta.org
- **Mind & Life Institute / 心智与生命研究所** — The institute co-founded by Francisco Varela, Adam Engle and the Dalai Lama to bring contemplative practice and science into dialogue; its dialogues and summer research institutes shaped contemplative and first-person science. https://www.mindandlife.org

### Archives & resources

- **Dance Notation Bureau** (New York, US) — The New York organisation holding the world's largest collection of Labanotation dance scores and training notators, a key resource behind Laban-based descriptions of movement in HCI. https://www.dancenotation.org
- **Gendlin Online Library / Gendlin 在线文库** — The International Focusing Institute's free library of Eugene Gendlin's writings, including A Process Model and his papers on the felt sense and Thinking at the Edge. https://focusing.org/gendlin
- **Jacob's Pillow Dance Interactive** — An online collection of dance videos filmed at Jacob's Pillow from the 1930s to today, with illustrated essays. https://danceinteractive.jacobspillow.org
- **KTH DiVA publication archive** (Stockholm, SE) — KTH's open repository, where the doctoral theses and many papers of Höök's soma design students (e.g. Vasiliki Tsaknaki, Pedro Sanches, Pavel Karpashevich) can be read in full. https://kth.diva-portal.org
- **Micro-phenomenology / 微观现象学** — The hub of the micro-phenomenology community (the 'psychological microscope' developed from Claire Petitmengin's and Pierre Vermersch's interview methods), listing research axes, trainings and events. https://www.microphenomenology.com
- **Motion Bank** — A research platform begun by The Forsythe Company (2010–2013) to create online digital scores of choreographers' thinking, continued at Hochschule Mainz as tools for annotating and sharing dance. https://motionbank.org
- **Numeridanse** (Lyon, FR) — A free international dance video library run from the Maison de la Danse in Lyon, with performances, documentaries, dance films and masterclasses. https://numeridanse.com/
- **Soma Bits (KTH)** (Stockholm, SE) — KTH's page for the Soma Bits, a prototyping toolkit of soft shapes with heat, vibration and shape-changing actuators that designers place on and around the body to feel and share what a design might feel like. https://www.kth.se/mid/research/interaction-design/projects/soma-bits-1.921861
- **Synchronous Objects** — An online project by William Forsythe and Ohio State University (2009) that translates the choreographic structure of One Flat Thing, reproduced into data visualisations and animated objects. https://synchronousobjects.osu.edu

## Creators

- **Kristina Höök** (44) — Professor of Interaction Design, KTH Royal Institute of Technology. Kristina Höök moved from affective computing at SICS (eMoto, Affective Diary, Affective Health) to founding soma design at KTH. The book Designing with the Body (MIT Press, 2018) sets out the programme. https://www.kth.se/profile/khook
- **Madeline Balaam** (19) — Professor of Interaction Design, KTH Royal Institute of Technology (previously Open Lab, Newcastle University). HCI researcher working on women's health, intimate care, rehabilitation and feminist approaches to designing for the body.
- **Anna Ståhl** (17) — Senior researcher, RISE Research Institutes of Sweden. Anna Ståhl is an interaction designer who has worked with Höök since eMoto and the Affective Diary, and co-designed the Soma Mat, Breathing Light and Pelvic Chair.
- **Florian 'Floyd' Mueller** (16) — Professor, Monash University; directs the Exertion Games Lab. HCI researcher on exertion games, bodily play and human-computer integration. https://exertiongameslab.org
- **Thecla Schiphorst** (16) — Professor, School of Interactive Arts and Technology, Simon Fraser University. Media artist, dancer and Certified Laban Movement Analyst who brought somatics into HCI. https://www.sfu.ca/siat/people/research-faculty/thecla-schiphorst.html
- **Lian Loke** (12) — Associate Professor, Design Lab, The University of Sydney. Interaction designer and performance-maker who works with the moving, feeling body in design. https://www.lianloke.com/
- **Vasiliki Tsaknaki** (12) — Associate Professor, IT University of Copenhagen. Vasiliki Tsaknaki did a PhD at KTH on craft and materials in interaction design and then worked on shape-change, breathing and biodata in the soma design group.
- **Claudia Núñez-Pacheco** (9) — Design researcher, Malmö University (formerly KTH). Claudia Núñez-Pacheco develops Focusing-based methods for articulating felt sense in design, and worked as a postdoc in the KTH soma design group.
- **Pedro Sanches** (9) — Interaction design researcher, Umeå University (formerly KTH). Pedro Sanches designed Affective Health at SICS and later worked on soma design, biodata and lived data at KTH.
- **Katherine Isbister** (8) — Professor of Computational Media, University of California, Santa Cruz. Game and HCI researcher on emotion, movement and social play; author of How Games Move Us (2016). https://www.isbister.org
- **Marianela Ciolfi Felice** (8) — Researcher, KTH Royal Institute of Technology. Marianela Ciolfi Felice works on participatory, body-centred design for women's health, including menopause, menstruation and contraception.
- **Pavel Karpashevich** (8) — Researcher, Carl von Ossietzky University of Oldenburg (PhD, KTH). Pavel Karpashevich designed interactive costumes at Bauhaus-Universität Weimar and shape-changing garments such as the Soma Corset during doctoral work at KTH.
- **Sarah Fdili Alaoui** (8) — Professor of Art, Science and Technology, Université Paris-Saclay; choreographer. Dancer, Laban Movement Analyst and HCI researcher working on movement qualities and dance with technology. https://saralaoui.com/
- **Elena Márquez Segura** (7) — Associate Professor, Universidad Carlos III de Madrid. Interaction designer working on embodied and social play, movement and physical training.
- **Noura Howell** (7) — Assistant Professor, School of Literature, Media, and Communication, Georgia Tech. HCI researcher and designer who builds emotional biosensing garments and installations; PhD from UC Berkeley's School of Information, now directs the Future Feelings Lab at Georgia Tech. https://nourahowell.com
- **Rachael Garrett** (7) — Interaction design researcher (PhD, KTH), felt ethics and soma design. Rachael Garrett studied how ethical sensibility is felt in the body during design work, and co-designed shape-changing garments and drone interactions at KTH.
- **Joseph La Delfa** (6) — Interaction design researcher, KTH Royal Institute of Technology (formerly RMIT Exertion Games Lab). Joseph La Delfa designs somaesthetic human-drone interactions, from Drone Chi at RMIT to How to Train Your Drone at KTH.
- **Marie Louise Juul Søndergaard** (6) — Associate Professor, Oslo School of Architecture and Design (AHO). Marie Louise Juul Søndergaard designs feminist intimate technologies; as a postdoc at KTH, Søndergaard made Menarche Bits and worked on menstrual and menopause design.
- **Mel Slater** (6) — Distinguished Investigator, University of Barcelona (Event Lab); formerly UCL. Mel Slater pioneered research on presence and virtual body ownership, from body transfer to extended avatars.
- **Nadia Campo Woytuk** (6) — Interaction design researcher, KTH Royal Institute of Technology. Nadia Campo Woytuk works on feminist HCI for intimate health: menstrual blood, cervical mucus and the vaginal microbiome.
- **Shaowen Bardzell** (6) — Professor, School of Interactive Computing, Georgia Institute of Technology. HCI scholar known for feminist HCI and humanistic approaches to interaction design.
- **Steve Benford** (6) — Professor of Collaborative Computing, Mixed Reality Lab, University of Nottingham. Co-founder of the Mixed Reality Lab, known for performance-led research with artists and the trajectories framework. https://www.nottingham.ac.uk/research/groups/mixedrealitylab/people/steve.benford
- **Botao 'Amber' Hu** (5) — Researcher, University of Oxford; founder of Reality Design Lab; inventor of HoloKit. Designer and researcher (Tsinghua BS, Stanford MS in AI) who leads Reality Design Lab's work on embodied, social mixed reality and XR soma design; his group's works have been shown at SIGGRAPH, CHI, TEI and Ars Electronica. https://reality.design
- **Frédéric Bevilacqua** (5) — Head of Sound Music Movement Interaction team (ISMM), IRCAM. Researcher who has led IRCAM's work on gesture following, motion-sound interaction and embodied music tools since the 2000s. https://ismm.ircam.fr/
- **George Poonkhin Khut** (5) — Artist; Senior Lecturer, UNSW Art & Design. Australian artist and interaction designer who has made heart-rate, breath and brainwave biofeedback artworks since the early 2000s, for galleries and for children's hospitals. https://www.georgekhut.com
- **Jiabao Li** (5) — Artist, designer and technologist; Associate Professor, Northeastern University. Chinese-born artist and inventor (Harvard GSD alum, former Apple designer) whose installations, wearables, XR, bio-art and performances deal with perception, feminist biotech and the more-than-human; co-directs work with Reality Design Lab. https://www.jiabaoli.org
- **Jules Françoise** (5) — CNRS researcher, LISN, Université Paris-Saclay. Researcher in movement-sound interaction and interactive machine learning. https://julesfrancoise.com/
- **Katta Spiel** (5) — Assistant Professor, Human-Computer Interaction group, TU Wien. HCI researcher working on neurodivergent, disabled, non-binary and trans perspectives on embodied interaction; SIGCHI Outstanding Dissertation Award 2020.
- **Kelsey Cotton** (5) — Singer, composer and HCI researcher (KTH; Chalmers University of Technology). Kelsey Cotton is a classically trained singer who designs and performs with breathing-driven instruments and robotic garments, first at KTH and then in a PhD on AI and voice at Chalmers.
- **Laia Turmo Vidal** (5) — Researcher, Universidad Carlos III de Madrid / KTH. Interaction designer researching technology for physical training, movement learning and embodied design methods. https://www.laiaturmovidal.com
- **Lygia Clark** (5) — Artist and therapist (1920–1988). Brazilian Neo-Concrete artist who moved from painting to 'propositions' completed by participants' bodies, and finally to a therapy with relational objects. https://portal.lygiaclark.org.br/
- **Paul Tennent** (5) — Associate Professor, Mixed Reality Lab, University of Nottingham. HCI researcher on amusement rides, bodily experience and biosensing in extreme experiences. https://paultennent.wordpress.com
- **Richard Shusterman** (5) — Dorothy F. Schmidt Eminent Scholar in the Humanities, Florida Atlantic University. American pragmatist philosopher who founded somaesthetics, the study of the body as the site of sensory appreciation and self-fashioning, and a trained Feldenkrais practitioner. https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Shusterman
- **Toni Robertson** (5) — Professor Emerita of Interaction Design, University of Technology Sydney. Interaction design researcher who brought Merleau-Ponty's phenomenology into the study of cooperative work and movement-based interaction.
- **Caroline Hummels** (4) — Professor of Design and Theory for Transformative Qualities, Eindhoven University of Technology. Design researcher who works on embodied, movement-based and first-person design, and on design for societal transformation.
- **Jeffrey Bardzell** (4) — Professor of Informatics, Pennsylvania State University. HCI theorist working on design criticism, aesthetics and feminist and humanistic perspectives on interaction design.
- **Joo Young Park** (4) — Interaction design researcher, KTH Royal Institute of Technology. Joo Young Park designs touch technologies for menstrual discomfort and critiques FemTech through feminist disability studies.
- **Kristina Popova** (4) — Researcher, KTH Royal Institute of Technology. HCI researcher on vulnerability, ethics and intimate data in soma design.
- **Mark Johnson** (4) — Philip H. Knight Professor of Liberal Arts and Sciences, University of Oregon. Pragmatist philosopher who developed image schemas and argues that meaning is rooted in bodily, felt, aesthetic experience. https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Johnson_(philosopher)
- **Nadia Bianchi-Berthouze** (4) — Professor of Affective Computing and Interaction, UCL Interaction Centre (UCLIC). Researcher on body movement, touch and affect, who led the EmoPain project on technology for chronic pain rehabilitation.
- **Ozgun Kilic Afsar** (4) — Researcher, MIT Media Lab and KTH Royal Institute of Technology. Ozgun Kilic Afsar develops fluidic fibre actuators and soft robotic garments, combining a KTH soma design PhD with work in MIT's Tangible Media Group.
- **Phoebe Sengers** (4) — Professor of Information Science and Science & Technology Studies, Cornell University. Cultural theorist and technologist whose work on reflective design, affect and ambiguity shaped critical and experiential HCI.
- **Rob Comber** (4) — Associate Professor of Communication, KTH Royal Institute of Technology. HCI researcher on civic, feminist and food technologies, formerly at Open Lab, Newcastle University.
- **Susan Kozel** (4) — Professor, School of Arts and Communication, Malmö University. Dancer, philosopher and researcher whose phenomenology of performance with technology shaped somatic approaches in HCI.
- **Yoav Luft** (4) — Researcher and martial arts practitioner, KTH Royal Institute of Technology. Yoav Luft builds sensing and actuation systems for the soma design group and brings a long martial arts practice into design.
- **Ana Tajadura-Jiménez** (3) — Associate Professor, Universidad Carlos III de Madrid; lead of the i_mBODY Lab. Researcher in multisensory body perception who uses sound to change how people feel their own body; led the ERC project BODYinTRANSIT. https://imbodylab.com/ana-tajadura-jimenez/
- **Aneesha Singh** (3) — Professor of Human-Computer Interaction, UCL Interaction Centre (UCLIC). HCI researcher on chronic pain, body awareness and technology for health and wellbeing.
- **Anupriya Tuli** (3) — HCI researcher, Indraprastha Institute of Information Technology Delhi (IIIT-Delhi). HCI researcher on menstrual health, education and mobility in India, working with feminist and period-positive approaches.
- **Brendan Walker** (3) — Artist and 'thrill engineer'; founder of Thrill Laboratory; collaborator of the Mixed Reality Lab, University of Nottingham. Brendan Walker designs amusement rides and VR swings as artworks about thrill and the felt body.
- **Danlin Huang** (3) — Artist, designer-researcher and AR developer; Reality Design Lab. Trained in industrial design and media art at the China Academy of Art; uses XR, biosensors and AI to enrich bodily experience; lead designer of FeltSight.
- **Ekaterina R. Stepanova** (3) — Researcher, iSpace Lab, Simon Fraser University. Ekaterina Stepanova studies transformative and self-transcendent experiences in VR.
- **Eugene Gendlin** (3) — Philosopher and psychotherapist, University of Chicago (1926–2017). American philosopher who described the 'felt sense', a bodily, pre-verbal sense of a whole situation, and developed Focusing and Thinking at the Edge from it. https://focusing.org
- **John Desnoyers-Stewart** (3) — Artist-researcher, iSpace Lab, Simon Fraser University. John Desnoyers-Stewart makes social VR installations about connection, breath and touch.
- **Jon Kabat-Zinn** (3) — Molecular biologist, founder of Mindfulness-Based Stress Reduction. Jon Kabat-Zinn (born 1944) holds a PhD in molecular biology and founded the Stress Reduction Clinic and MBSR at the University of Massachusetts Medical School in 1979. https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jon_Kabat-Zinn
- **Jun Nishida** (3) — Assistant Professor, University of Maryland; director of the Embodied Dynamics Lab. Japanese researcher who designs wearables that let people borrow each other's bodies: EMS devices that share muscle activity and rigs that give you a child's eye height; previously at the University of Tsukuba and the University of Chicago. https://junnishida.net
- **Marshmallow Laser Feast** (3) — Experiential art collective. Marshmallow Laser Feast is a London collective (Barney Steel, Robin McNicholas and others) making multisensory VR and installations about perception and the natural world. https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com
- **Martin Jonsson** (3) — Interaction design researcher, Södertörn University. Martin Jonsson (also published as Jönsson) co-developed the heated Soma Mat and the Sarka sound mat with Höök's group.
- **Miquel Alfaras** (3) — Biomedical engineer and HCI researcher (PLUX Wireless Biosignals; Universitat Jaume I). Miquel Alfaras worked on biosignal hardware at PLUX and on soma design with KTH in the AffecTech network.
- **Neha Kumar** (3) — Associate Professor, School of Interactive Computing, Georgia Institute of Technology. HCI researcher on technology and global development, with a focus on women's health in the Global South.
- **Pedro Lopes** (3) — Associate Professor of Computer Science, University of Chicago; director of the Human Computer Integration Lab. Pedro Lopes builds devices that integrate with the body, such as electrical muscle stimulation that moves the wearer's limbs. https://plopes.org
- **Sara Eriksson** (3) — Interaction design researcher (PhD, KTH), drones and choreography. Sara Eriksson studied how a choreographer and engineers shaped the movement of drones for an opera, and the ethics of movement.
- **Sarah Homewood** (3) — Design researcher, feminist and somatic HCI. Design researcher working on feminist, somatic and more-than-human perspectives on the body and self-tracking.
- **Teresa Almeida** (3) — Interaction design researcher; PhD from Open Lab, Newcastle University; later at Umeå University. Portuguese designer-researcher who works on women's intimate health, pelvic fitness and self-knowledge of the body through wearables and interactive systems.
- **Thomas Hanna** (3) — Philosopher and founder of Hanna Somatic Education (1928–1990). American philosopher who coined 'somatics' and defined the soma as the body experienced from within by first-person perception; he trained with Moshe Feldenkrais. https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Louis_Hanna
- **Antonio Camurri** (2) — Professor, DIBRIS, University of Genoa; founder of Casa Paganini–InfoMus. Italian computer scientist who built EyesWeb and has studied expressive movement qualities in dance for three decades. http://www.casapaganini.org/
- **Antonio Damasio** (2) — University Professor of Neuroscience, University of Southern California. Neuroscientist whose somatic marker hypothesis showed that bodily feeling is part of reasoning, and who traces consciousness to the body's homeostatic feelings. https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonio_Damasio
- **Audrey Desjardins** (2) — Associate Professor, School of Art + Art History + Design, University of Washington. Design researcher who works through autobiographical design and living with prototypes at home. https://desjardins.design
- **Bonnie Bainbridge Cohen** (2) — Movement artist, occupational therapist, founder of Body-Mind Centering. Bonnie Bainbridge Cohen is an occupational therapist, dancer and educator who founded the School for Body-Mind Centering in 1973. https://www.bodymindcentering.com
- **Carl Unander-Scharin** (2) — Composer and opera singer; Professor, Karlstad University. Swedish tenor and composer who builds interactive instruments for singers, co-author with Kristina Höök. https://www.operamecatronica.com/
- **Carman Neustaedter** (2) — Professor, School of Interactive Arts and Technology, Simon Fraser University. HCI researcher on domestic and family technologies and telepresence.
- **Caroline Yan Zheng** (2) — Researcher in soft robotics and affective touch, KTH Royal Institute of Technology. Caroline Yan Zheng designs soft robotic touch and studies how technologies can touch people well.
- **Char Davies** (2) — Painter and virtual reality artist; founder of Immersence. Canadian artist who made Osmose (1995) and Ephémère (1998), VR works navigated by breath and balance. https://www.immersence.com
- **Dag Svanæs** (2) — Professor of Interaction Design, Norwegian University of Science and Technology (NTNU). Interaction design researcher who has worked since the 1990s on what Merleau-Ponty's phenomenology means for interaction design.
- **Danielle Wilde** (2) — Professor of Embodied Design, Umeå Institute of Design / University of Southern Denmark. Designer and researcher working with the body, performance, wearables and participatory futures.
- **Diane Gromala** (2) — Professor, School of Interactive Arts and Technology, Simon Fraser University; former Canada Research Chair in Computational Technologies for Transforming Pain. Artist and researcher who has worked on VR and the body since the early 1990s, and on immersive technology for people living with chronic pain.
- **Dōgen Zenji** (2) — Zen master (1200–1253), founder of Japanese Sōtō Zen. Japanese Buddhist monk who brought Caodong Chan from Song China to Japan, founded Eiheiji and wrote the Shōbōgenzō and the Fukanzazengi. https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/D%C5%8Dgen
- **Evan Thompson** (2) — Professor of Philosophy, University of British Columbia. Philosopher of mind who co-authored The Embodied Mind and developed the deep continuity of life and mind, working across phenomenology, biology and Buddhist thought. https://evanthompson.me
- **Fannie Liu** (2) — HCI researcher; research scientist at Snap Research during Significant Otter. HCI researcher who studied expressive biosignals in a PhD at Carnegie Mellon's HCI Institute and built heart-rate sharing apps for close relationships. https://research.snap.com/publications/significant-otter-understanding-the-role-of-biosignals-in-communication.html
- **Francisco Varela** (2) — Biologist, neuroscientist and philosopher, CNRS Paris (1946–2001). Chilean biologist who co-developed autopoiesis, founded enactivism and neurophenomenology, and brought Buddhist mindfulness practice into cognitive science. https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francisco_Varela
- **George Lakoff** (2) — Professor Emeritus of Cognitive Science and Linguistics, UC Berkeley. Cognitive linguist who, with Mark Johnson, showed that abstract thought is structured by metaphors grounded in bodily experience. https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Lakoff
- **Gijs Huisman** (2) — Researcher in social touch technology; formerly University of Twente, later TU Delft. Gijs Huisman builds and studies devices for touch at a distance, such as the TaSST tactile sleeve.
- **Guðrún Margrét Ívansdóttir** (2) — Interaction design researcher, KTH Royal Institute of Technology. Guðrún Margrét Ívansdóttir designs haptic data physicalisations and shape-changing interfaces in the soma design group.
- **H. Henrik Ehrsson** (2) — Professor of Cognitive Neuroscience, Karolinska Institutet; Brain, Body and Self Laboratory. Henrik Ehrsson studies how the brain builds the feeling of owning a body, using illusions of body swap and out-of-body experience.
- **Hélio Oiticica** (2) — Artist (1937–1980). Brazilian Neo-Concrete artist whose Parangolés, Penetrables and environments turned viewers into wearers, dancers and inhabitants.
- **Ichikawa Hiroshi** (2) — Philosopher (1931–2002); professor at Meiji University. Japanese phenomenologist of the body who proposed the 'body as spirit' and the concept of mi, the lived body-self that extends into tools, others and the world. https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/市川浩_(哲学者)
- **Irmgard Bartenieff** (2) — Dance theorist, physical therapist, pioneer of dance therapy. Irmgard Bartenieff (1900–1981), a student of Laban, applied his spatial concepts to polio rehabilitation in the 1940s and developed the body-level exercises known as Bartenieff Fundamentals. https://labaninstitute.org
- **Jin Moen** (2) — Interaction designer; founder of Movinto Fun (Oriboo); PhD KTH. Swedish designer who developed kinaesthetic movement interaction from modern dance and built the BodyBug wearable.
- **Joe Marshall** (2) — Associate Professor, Mixed Reality Lab, University of Nottingham. Joe Marshall designs breath- and body-controlled experiences, from the Broncomatic ride to VR.
- **Jonas Löwgren** (2) — Professor of Interaction and Information Design, Linköping University. Interaction design researcher known for work on design knowledge and interaction aesthetics.
- **Joris H. Janssen** (2) — Researcher in affective computing (formerly Philips Research / TU Eindhoven). Researcher who studied heartbeats as social signals and physiological empathy at Philips Research and Eindhoven University of Technology.
- **Josh Andres** (2) — Researcher in human-computer integration; Australian National University (formerly Exertion Games Lab). Designer-researcher of integrated exertion systems such as eBikes that act with the rider.
- **Jérémy Frey** (2) — Co-founder, Ullo; HCI and physiological computing researcher. Researcher in physiological computing and tangible interfaces (Inria, IDC Herzliya) who co-founded Ullo to make biofeedback objects. https://phd.jfrey.info
- **Kouta Minamizawa** (2) — Professor, Keio University Graduate School of Media Design (KMD). Haptics researcher who leads the Embodied Media Project at Keio KMD, working on telexistence, haptic transmission and shared bodies. https://embodiedmedia.org
- **Kristina Mah** (2) — Design researcher, The University of Sydney. Design researcher who studies contemplative and compassion practices as a way to train designers' self-observation.
- **Kyung Yun Choi** (2) — Researcher; PhD, MIT Media Lab. Korean designer-researcher who builds tactile and pneumatic wearables that guide heart rate and breathing through gentle rhythms on the skin.
- **Manos Tsakiris** (2) — Professor of Psychology, Royal Holloway, University of London. Psychologist and neuroscientist of the bodily self, interoception and body ownership; leads the Lab of Action & Body. https://www.royalholloway.ac.uk/
- **Marco Donnarumma** (2) — Performance artist, composer and researcher. Italian artist who makes biophysical music and performances in which muscle sounds and bioelectric signals drive sound, light and machines. https://marcodonnarumma.com/
- **Maurice Merleau-Ponty** (2) — Philosopher, Collège de France (1908–1961). French phenomenologist who argued that perception is the body's lived engagement with the world, and later described body and world as one 'flesh'. https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maurice_Merleau-Ponty
- **Mehmet Aydın Baytaş** (2) — HCI researcher and designer; worked on human-drone interaction at Koç University and Chalmers University of Technology. Mehmet Aydın Baytaş studies the design of social drones and co-authored the Drone Chi work.
- **Mirjana Prpa** (2) — Researcher, Simon Fraser University. Interaction designer working on breath, attention and immersive experiences.
- **Moshé Feldenkrais** (2) — Physicist, judo teacher and founder of the Feldenkrais Method. Moshé Feldenkrais (1904–1984) was a physicist and judo black belt who developed a method of learning through slow, attentive movement, taught as Awareness Through Movement group lessons and hands-on Functional Integration. https://feldenkrais-method.org
- **Muhammad Umair** (2) — HCI researcher, Lancaster University (AffecTech network). Muhammad Umair designed materials-based affective interfaces for emotion regulation in the EU AffecTech network, working with Höök's group on biodata and actuation.
- **Nancy Stark Smith** (2) — Dancer, co-founder of Contact Improvisation and Contact Quarterly. Nancy Stark Smith (1952–2020) was a founding participant of Contact Improvisation, co-editor of Contact Quarterly and creator of the Underscore. https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nancy_Stark_Smith
- **Os Keyes** (2) — Researcher in HCI and science and technology studies (PhD, University of Washington). Writer and researcher on gender, disability and technology, known for critical work on automated gender recognition.
- **Palindrome (Robert Wechsler)** (2) — Interactive dance company led by choreographer Robert Wechsler; co-developers of EyeCon and MotionComposer. Founded in New York in 1982 and based in Germany since 1991, Palindrome made early dance pieces in which camera tracking, skin contact and body signals play music and light, mostly with engineer Frieder Weiss. https://www.palindrome.de
- **Peter A. Levine** (2) — Psychologist, developer of Somatic Experiencing. Peter A. Levine (born 1942) holds doctorates in medical biophysics and psychology and developed Somatic Experiencing, a body-based approach to trauma. https://traumahealing.org
- **Petr Slovák** (2) — Reader in Human-Computer Interaction, King's College London. HCI researcher working on social-emotional learning, mental health and technologies that support felt, embodied skills.
- **Rebecca Horn** (2) — Artist (1944–2024). German artist who began with wearable body extensions (horns, feathers, long gloves) and moved on to kinetic sculpture.
- **Rem RunGu Lin** (2) — Media artist and researcher; Reality Design Lab collaborator. Media artist and researcher who combines contact improvisation, neurofeedback and mixed reality; co-teaches XR soma design with Botao Amber Hu.
- **Richard Byrne** (2) — Researcher, Exertion Games Lab (RMIT / Monash). Game designer and researcher on digital vertigo play using galvanic vestibular stimulation.
- **Scott Brave** (2) — Former researcher, Tangible Media Group, MIT Media Lab. Scott Brave built inTouch with Andrew Dahley and Hiroshi Ishii in the Tangible Media Group.
- **Shaan Chopra** (2) — HCI researcher (IIIT-Delhi; later University of Washington). HCI researcher on menstrual health and chronic illness self-management.
- **Stelarc** (2) — Performance artist; Distinguished Research Fellow, Curtin University. Cyprus-born Australian artist who since the 1970s has extended, suspended and wired his own body with robots, prostheses and networks. https://stelarc.org/
- **Tatsumi Hijikata** (2) — Dancer and choreographer (1928–1986), founder of butoh. Japanese choreographer whose 1959 Kinjiki started Ankoku Butoh, the 'dance of darkness' built from images, memory and the collapsing body. https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tatsumi_Hijikata
- **Thomas Fuchs** (2) — Karl Jaspers Professor of Philosophical Foundations of Psychiatry, Heidelberg University. Psychiatrist and phenomenologist who describes the brain as an organ of a living body in relation with others, and developed the idea of intercorporeality. https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Fuchs_(psychiatrist)
- **Wayne McGregor** (2) — Choreographer; Artistic Director, Studio Wayne McGregor. British choreographer who has worked with cognitive scientists and technologists on how movement ideas are made. https://waynemcgregor.com/
- **William Forsythe** (2) — Choreographer. American choreographer who directed Ballett Frankfurt and The Forsythe Company and developed digital tools to teach and analyse improvisation. https://www.williamforsythe.com/
- **Yuasa Yasuo** (2) — Philosopher (1925–2005); professor at Osaka, Tsukuba and Obirin universities. Japanese philosopher, a student of Watsuji Tetsurō, who wrote on the body, ki, self-cultivation and Jungian psychology; his 1977 book on the body became the main English-language source on Eastern mind-body theory. https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yasuo_Yuasa
- **Yves Candau** (2) — Researcher and somatic movement practitioner, Simon Fraser University. Movement practitioner and researcher who connects somatic practices, embodied cognition and interaction design.
- **Zhuangzi** (2) — Daoist philosopher (c. 369–286 BCE). Warring States thinker whose book, the Zhuangzi, uses stories of craftsmen, butchers and swimmers to show skill as effortless, attuned action. https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhuang_Zhou
- **Åsa Unander-Scharin** (2) — Choreographer; Professor, Luleå University of Technology. Swedish choreographer and dancer who choreographs robots and mechatronic opera with Carl Unander-Scharin (Opera Mecatronica). https://www.operamecatronica.com/
- **A. D. (Bud) Craig** (1) — Neuroanatomist; formerly Barrow Neurological Institute. Neuroanatomist who mapped the interoceptive pathway from the body to the insular cortex and wrote How Do You Feel? (2015).
- **Aaron Pengyu Zhu** (1) — HCI researcher. HCI researcher studying reflection, contemplative traditions and non-Western epistemologies in interaction design.
- **Ad Verheul** (1) — Therapist, De Hartenberg Institute, Ede (Netherlands). Dutch therapist and co-founder of Snoezelen with Jan Hulsegge.
- **Alex A. Ahmed** (1) — HCI researcher; founder of Project Spectra (PhD, Northeastern University). Researcher and developer who built free, open-source voice training tools with and for trans communities.
- **Alexander Toet** (1) — Senior scientist, TNO (Netherlands Organisation for Applied Scientific Research). Alexander Toet studies perception, emotion and mediated social touch.
- **Alexis E. Block** (1) — Roboticist; PhD at Max Planck Institute for Intelligent Systems and ETH Zurich (HuggieBot). Alexis Block built the HuggieBot series of soft, warm hugging robots with Katherine J. Kuchenbecker.
- **Alice Haynes** (1) — Researcher in soft robotics and haptics; PhD at the University of Bristol. Alice Haynes designs soft, shape-changing objects for emotional support, such as a cushion that breathes.
- **Alva Noë** (1) — Professor of Philosophy, University of California, Berkeley. Philosopher who argues that perceiving is something we do with our moving bodies, through our practical grasp of how sensation changes as we move. https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alva_No%C3%AB
- **Amanda Lazar** (1) — Associate Professor, College of Information, University of Maryland. HCI researcher working on ageing, dementia, menopause and the lived experience of changing bodies.
- **Amey Holden** (1) — HCI researcher (PhD, Open Lab, Newcastle University). Researcher on wearable technology for upper limb rehabilitation after stroke.
- **Amy Huggard** (1) — Designer; Exertion Games Lab alumna (RMIT University). Game designer who led Musical Embrace, a game played by strangers hugging a pillow-like controller.
- **Ana Paiva** (1) — Professor, Instituto Superior Técnico, University of Lisbon (GAIPS, INESC-ID). Ana Paiva leads research on affective agents and social robots in Lisbon. In the EU SAFIRA project, Paiva's team built SenToy with Höök's SICS team.
- **Andrés Lucero** (1) — Professor of Interaction Design, Aalto University. Interaction design researcher working on participatory, playful and first-person design methods.
- **Angela Chang** (1) — Designer and researcher; formerly Tangible Media Group, MIT Media Lab. Angela Chang designed ComTouch, a vibrotactile device that adds touch to phone calls.
- **Anna Eaglin** (1) — HCI design researcher (Indiana University at the time of the work). Design researcher who studied sex toys and their users with Shaowen Bardzell.
- **Anna Halprin** (1) — Choreographer, pioneer of postmodern dance and dance for healing. Anna Halprin (1920–2021) founded the San Francisco Dancers' Workshop, developed the RSVP Cycles with Lawrence Halprin and co-founded the Tamalpa Institute with her daughter Daria in 1978. https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anna_Halprin
- **Anna Vallgårda** (1) — Associate Professor, IT University of Copenhagen. Interaction design researcher on computational composites, materiality and the body.
- **Annkatrin Jung** (1) — Interaction designer, KTH Royal Institute of Technology. Annkatrin Jung designed the Breathing Garment, which applies deep touch pressure in time with breathing.
- **Antti Oulasvirta** (1) — Professor of Computer Science, Aalto University. HCI researcher, early on in mobile and contextual design, later in computational models of interaction.
- **Art Orienté Objet** (1) — Artist duo of Marion Laval-Jeantet and Benoît Mangin. Art Orienté Objet is a French duo working at the edges of art, ethology and biology since 1991.
- **Astrid Twenebowa Larssen** (1) — Interaction design researcher, University of Technology Sydney (formerly). Interaction design researcher who studied how movement-based interaction feels from the mover's own perspective.
- **Atsuko Tanaka** (1) — Gutai artist (1932–2005). Japanese artist of the Gutai group, known for Electric Dress, Bell and paintings of circles and wires. https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atsuko_Tanaka_(artist)
- **B. K. S. Iyengar** (1) — Yoga teacher, founder of Iyengar Yoga. B. K. S. Iyengar (1918–2014) learned yoga from Tirumalai Krishnamacharya, taught in Pune and, after meeting Yehudi Menuhin in 1952, in Europe and the US; Light on Yoga (1966) made his method known worldwide. https://bksiyengar.com
- **Baptiste Caramiaux** (1) — CNRS researcher, ISIR, Sorbonne Université; formerly IRCAM and Goldsmiths. Baptiste Caramiaux studies machine learning for expressive gesture, music and movement.
- **BeAnotherLab** (1) — Interdisciplinary collective for embodiment and empathy research. BeAnotherLab is a collective founded in Barcelona in 2012 that builds low-cost embodiment systems for swapping perspectives and bodies. https://beanotherlab.org
- **Bernhard E. Riecke** (1) — Professor, School of Interactive Arts and Technology, Simon Fraser University; leads the iSpace Lab. Researcher on self-motion perception, VR and technology for transformative, connected experiences.
- **Bessel van der Kolk** (1) — Psychiatrist and trauma researcher. Bessel van der Kolk (born 1943) is a Dutch-born psychiatrist and trauma researcher, author of The Body Keeps the Score (2014). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bessel_van_der_Kolk
- **Bigna Lenggenhager** (1) — Professor of Cognitive Psychology and Neuropsychology, University of Konstanz; formerly EPFL (Blanke lab). Bigna Lenggenhager led the 2007 full-body illusion study with Olaf Blanke and Thomas Metzinger.
- **Bronwyn Tarr** (1) — Evolutionary anthropologist and dancer; formerly Institute of Cognitive and Evolutionary Anthropology, University of Oxford. Bronwyn Tarr studies how dancing and moving in synchrony bond people together.
- **Carey Jewitt** (1) — Professor of Learning and Technology, UCL; director of the UCL Knowledge Lab. Carey Jewitt led the ERC project IN-TOUCH on how digital technologies reshape touch and communication.
- **Carl DiSalvo** (1) — Professor, School of Interactive Computing, Georgia Institute of Technology. Carl DiSalvo works on design, robots and public life; at Carnegie Mellon he co-designed The Hug, a robotic pillow for intimate communication.
- **Catherine D'Ignazio** (1) — Associate Professor of Urban Science and Planning, MIT; director of the Data + Feminism Lab. Scholar, artist and designer working on feminist data practices; co-author of Data Feminism (2020).
- **Cathy Treadaway** (1) — Professor of Creative Practice, Cardiff Metropolitan University; leads the LAUGH project. Design researcher on compassionate design, playful objects and wellbeing in advanced dementia.
- **Charles Windlin** (1) — Interaction design researcher, KTH Royal Institute of Technology. Charles Windlin designed and built the Soma Bits, the lab's kit of heat, vibration and shape-change actuators for sketching with the body.
- **Charlotte Selver** (1) — Teacher of Sensory Awareness. Charlotte Selver (1901–2003) studied with Elsa Gindler in Berlin, emigrated to the US in 1938 and taught the work as Sensory Awareness, including at Esalen from 1963. https://www.sensoryawareness.org
- **Cheng Man-ch'ing** (1) — Tai chi teacher, painter, poet and physician (1902–1975). Student of Yang Chengfu who created the 37-posture short form and taught it in Taiwan and, from 1964, in New York. https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cheng_Man-ch%27ing
- **Chinese Health Qigong Association** (1) — National association for Health Qigong under the General Administration of Sport of China. Body that in the early 2000s standardised traditional qigong sequences such as Baduanjin, Yijinjing, Wuqinxi and Liuzijue as 'Health Qigong'. https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baduanjin_qigong
- **Chris Elsden** (1) — Research Fellow in Design Informatics, University of Edinburgh. HCI researcher who studies personal data, memory and speculative design of data-driven futures.
- **Chris Woebken** (1) — Designer and researcher; co-founder of the Extrapolation Factory. Chris Woebken is a German-born designer working on speculative and participatory futures. https://www.chriswoebken.com
- **Christian Boltanski** (1) — Artist (1944–2021). French artist whose installations about memory, absence and death used photographs, clothing, light bulbs and sound.
- **Christian Nold** (1) — Artist, designer and researcher. Artist and researcher who invented the Bio Mapping device and has run participatory emotion-mapping projects in more than 25 cities. http://biomapping.net/
- **Chunky Move (Gideon Obarzanek with Frieder Weiss)** (1) — Contemporary dance company. Australian dance company; its piece Glow (2006) was made by choreographer Gideon Obarzanek with software artist Frieder Weiss. https://chunkymove.com/
- **Claire Petitmengin** (1) — Professor Emerita, Institut Mines-Télécom; member of the Archives Husserl, Paris. Philosopher and researcher who developed the micro-phenomenological interview from Varela's neurophenomenology and Vermersch's explicitation interview. https://www.microphenomenology.com
